To 

 Harrow School, 

 as I remember it, when I had the 

 honour to wear “The Harrow Hat.”

===Original===
To 

 Harrow School, 

 as I remember it, when I had the 

 honour to wear “The Harrow Hat.”

===Chinese===
谨以此书

献给哈罗公学（Harrow School）——

我记忆中的它，那时我曾有幸

佩戴「哈罗帽」（The Harrow Hat）。

## I First Blood / 一 初战

===Original===
It was in October, 1926, that George Hanbury and I first set up house in Wiltshire; and, since for the next six months we hunted four days a week, yet would commit to no one the pleasant task of setting our home in order, I do not think we slept out of Maintenance﻿—for from time immemorial that has been the name of the place﻿—more than seven times. But two of the visits I paid stand out of my memory, and, as they bear upon the matters which I am to tell, I will set them down.

In the first week of December the wedding of one of my cousins took me to town.

Now neither Hanbury nor I would have dreamed of visiting London without calling on Jonathan Mansel, whose flat was in Cleveland Row; for we three had made our fortune together and together had proved the stuff of which friendship is made. That apart, Mansel was the very finest gentleman that ever I knew: his ways were quiet, and his address was simple; but there was a natural royalty about him such as, I think, few monarchs have been able to boast.

===Chinese===
1926 年10月，乔治·汉伯里（George Hanbury）和我初次在威尔特郡（Wiltshire）安下家来；此后半年，我们每星期狩猎四天，却不肯把「布置新居」这桩美差托付给任何人，所以依我记忆所及，我们睡在赡养庄（Maintenance）以外的时候，统共不超过七回。但我所出的两次门，在记忆里格外分明；因它们同我要讲的种种大有干系，我便把它们记下来。

十二月头一个礼拜，一位表亲完婚，把我带进了城。

汉伯里也好，我也罢，到伦敦而竟不去拜望乔纳森·曼塞尔（Jonathan Mansel），那是做梦也想不到的事；他的公寓就在克利夫兰罗（Cleveland Row）：因为我们三个一同挣下过一份家业，也一同验证了交情是何等成色。撇开这个不谈，曼塞尔也是我平生所见最完美的绅士：举止安详，谈吐质朴；可他身上自有一种天生的王者气象，我想，戴王冠的君王里，夸耀得起的也没有几个。

===Original===
I started betimes and travelled to London by road, and the clock of St. James’s Palace was striking nine as I turned out of Marlborough Gate into Cleveland Row. Except for my servant, Bell, I was alone.

Here let me say that it was Mansel who had taught me the virtue of being early abroad and, particularly, of taking a journey before the world was awake; “for,” said he, “the dawn you may nearly always have to yourself, and, since it is the fairest of the hours, that a free man should lose it is more than lamentable.”

I had no need to ring, for, when I had mounted the stairs, I found Mansel’s hall-door open and his body-servant, Carson, watching two workmen who were busy about its lock. He took me directly to the study, where Mansel was standing before a cheerful fire.

===Chinese===
我一早动身，由公路前往伦敦；从马尔伯勒门（Marlborough Gate）拐进克利夫兰罗时，圣詹姆斯宫（St. James's Palace）的钟正敲九点。除了我的仆人贝尔（Bell），只我一人。

这里不妨交代一句：早早出门的好处，尤其是趁世人未醒便上路的好处，是曼塞尔教给我的；「因为，」他说，「黎明你几乎总能独享，而它又是一天里最美的时辰，一个自由的人竟把它失掉，岂止可惜而已。」

我不必按铃，因为上了楼，便见曼塞尔家的入户门敞着，他的贴身仆人卡森（Carson）正看着两个工匠在门锁上忙活。他径直把我带进书房，曼塞尔正站在旺旺的炉火前。

===Original===
“Ah, William,” says he, “I’m glad to see you. How was it you didn’t ring?”

I told him.

“That’s right,” said he. “Those fellows are changing the lock. Yesterday this flat was entered﻿—by some person or persons unknown.”

“Thieves?” said I.

“Thieves,” said Mansel.

At once I looked at the wall, where I knew there had hung a monstrance. This was golden and jewelled, and, though there was plate-glass about it, I could have forced the case in two minutes of time. But the monstrance was there.

===Chinese===
「啊，威廉（William），」他说，「见着你真高兴。你怎么没按铃？」

我告诉了他。

「这就对了，」他说。「那两个人在换锁。昨天这套公寓被人进了——来者一人或数人，无从知晓。」

「窃贼？」我说。

「窃贼，」曼塞尔说。

我立刻望向墙上——我知道那里原挂着一具圣体匣（monstrance）。那东西金身珠宝，虽说四周罩着平板玻璃，若叫我动手，两分钟便能撬开那匣子。可圣体匣还在原处。

===Original===
“And they missed that?” said I, pointing.

“They didn’t come for that,” said Mansel.

For a moment we looked at each other: then I sat down in a chair and took out a cigarette.

“They came for my papers,” said Mansel. “And got them.” He pointed to his writing-table. “In the right-hand pedestal of that is a little safe. They cut it open and took my papers away. There were fifty sovereigns there and five hundred pounds in notes: but they didn’t take them: so it looks as though they meant me to understand that they came for my papers alone.”

===Chinese===
「他们竟漏了它？」我指着问。

「他们不是冲它来的，」曼塞尔说。

我们对望了片刻：随后我在椅子里坐下，掏出一支烟。

「他们是冲我的文件来的，」曼塞尔说。「而且得手了。」他指了指自己的写字台。「右侧那根柜柱里有只小保险柜。他们把它割开，把我的文件拿走了。柜里还有五十金镑、五百镑纸币：他们分文未动：看来是有意叫我明白，他们单为文件而来。」

===Original===
“Were there papers of value?” said I.

Mansel frowned. Then he moved swiftly to a window and stood, looking down upon the street. So he stayed for some moments, because, I am sure, he would not trust his voice.

Presently﻿—

“They were of interest,” he said, “to no one but me.”

===Chinese===
「那些文件很值钱么？」我说。

曼塞尔皱起眉头。随即他疾步走到一扇窗前，立定，俯望着街面。他这样立了片刻——我敢说，是因为他不肯让自己的嗓音露馅。

少顷——

「它们的趣味，」他说，「除我之外，于谁都谈不上。」

===Original===
I was concerned, for Mansel was plainly moved, and, though I knew no more than the man in the moon the nature of the stolen papers, I had never before seen him betray himself.

At length﻿—

“What’s to be done?” said I.

“Nothing,” said Mansel, turning. “But, as you know, Chandos, I have a dangerous enemy, and, if he should study those papers, he might see a line of attack which would hit me hard.”

“Us,” I said quickly.

“Us,” said Mansel, and smiled.

===Chinese===
我心里不安，因为曼塞尔分明动了情；虽说失窃文件的性质，我同月中人一样一无所知，可他这样自泄其情，我此前从未见过。

终于——

「该怎么办？」我说。

「不必怎么办，」曼塞尔转过身来说。「不过你是知道的，钱多斯（Chandos），我有一个危险的仇敌；他若细读那些文件，也许看得出一条攻我的路子，一击便叫我重伤。」

「伤我们，」我急忙说。

「伤我们，」曼塞尔说，莞尔一笑。

===Original===
And there we left the business, for that was clearly his will: but, though we spoke of it no more, I could not get it out of my mind, for I knew as well as did Mansel that the theft was the work of Rose Noble and that it was not to be thought of that he would fail to perceive the significance of his spoil.

Rose Noble was a sinister man, and, though he came but seldom into the light, was undoubtedly concerned in some of the greatest robberies of his time. He was never taken, and the police of more than one country feared his name, for he had a reputation so evil as to be almost fabulous and was commonly believed by those who knew him to be gifted with second sight. That he deserved this fame I can testify, for Mansel, Hanbury and I had made our fortune in his teeth: we had more than one brush with him and found him a monster of iniquity, bold, swift and strong, in whom there was no pity at all. I do not say that he had second sight; but his instinct was supernatural, and I do not think that any living being could deceive this terrible man.

Now, had we not made our fortune, Rose Noble would have made his; and, since nine hundred thousand pounds is a huge sum of money to forego, I was not greatly surprised that he was, so to speak, returning to the charge: and, as it was Mansel that had captained our enterprise, it was, I suppose, natural that Rose Noble should pitch upon him. How he would use the papers which he had stolen I could not think, but Mansel had said plainly that they could be turned to his hurt, and his demeanour had shown me that this hurt might be very sore.

===Chinese===
事情便到此搁下，因为他的意思显然如此：可是，虽说我们再没有提起，我却始终不能把它从心里赶出去——我和曼塞尔一样清楚，这桩盗窃出自罗斯·诺布尔（Rose Noble）之手，而指望他看不出这批赃物的分量，那想都不必想。

罗斯·诺布尔是个阴鸷的人，虽难得走到亮处，他那个时代几桩最大的劫案里，无疑都有他的份。他从未落网，不止一个国家的警察惮于他的名头：他的凶名恶得近乎传奇，认识他的人多半相信，他天生一双未卜先知的眼睛。这份名声他当之无愧，我可以作证——曼塞尔、汉伯里和我那份家业，就是当着他的面挣下的：我们同他交手不止一回，识得他是万恶的怪物，大胆、迅疾、强悍，心里没有半分怜悯。我不说他真有预知的异眼；可他的直觉近乎超自然，我想，世上没有一个活人骗得过这个可怕的人。

话说回来，倘若我们没有挣下那份家业，挣下的便是罗斯·诺布尔；九十万镑毕竟是一笔放弃不得的巨款，所以，可以这么说吧，他此番卷土重来，我并不十分惊讶：而我们那桩事业执掌帅印的是曼塞尔，罗斯·诺布尔挑中他下手，我想也是顺理成章。他要把偷去的文件派什么用场，我想象不出；但曼塞尔说得明白，它们可以被用来伤他，而他的神色也告诉我，这一伤兴许伤得很重。

===Original===
On my return to Maintenance I told George Hanbury my news, to find that he shared my concern: but, since Mansel had promised to apprise us if trouble came, yet week after week went by and brought no message, we began to believe our apprehension baseless and the robbery nothing more signal than a flash in the pan.

It was early in the following April that Hanbury and I spent three fine days at White Ladies in the New Forest, that is to say, at Mansel’s country home. This he shared with his cousins, whose name was Pleydell. It was not our first visit, but the Pleydells were absent when we had been there before, and, though the four Sargents in the gallery had told us what to expect, I do not think that either of us believed that all five members of one family could prove so charming.

Adèle Pleydell was the youngest and had married Captain Pleydell five years before: I learned later that she was American by birth. She and her husband seemed very young for their age. Major and Daphne Pleydell were clearly older and were by one consent treated as the heads of the house. Yet all were equal; and, when once Adèle Pleydell affirmed that she was an interloper and the only one of them whose ancestors had not known White Ladies, there was an amicable uproar, and Major Pleydell said gravely, “That I regard as one of the misfortunes of our House.”

She was a tall, slim girl, very graceful and wonderfully and beautifully made. Her face was lovely; her thick, dark hair, lustrous; the light in her fine, brown eyes, a glorious thing. She was quiet, yet tireless and seemed to do all things well: she could drive a car and could ride with any man: yet she was always maidenly and looked as delicate a-cock-horse as when, in silk and satin, she sat to a piano and lifted her exquisite voice. She was naturally eager and responsive, and I shall always see her, as did Sargent﻿—with her beautiful lips parted and her soft, brown eyes alight.

===Chinese===
回到赡养庄，我把消息告诉了乔治·汉伯里，发现他同我一样挂心：但曼塞尔许诺过，祸事一来便知会我们，而一星期又一星期过去，音信全无，我们便渐渐相信，忧虑并无根由，那桩盗窃也不过是虚晃一枪。

次年四月上旬，汉伯里和我在白女士庄（White Ladies）盘桓了三个晴美的日子——那是新森林（the New Forest）里曼塞尔的乡宅。宅子他同他的表亲共有，表亲家姓普莱德尔（Pleydell）。这并不是我们头回登门，但前几回去时普莱德尔一家人都不在；虽说画廊里那四幅萨金特（Sargent）画像早把我们该见到怎样的人家交代分明，我们俩谁也不大肯信，一家五口，竟能个个这样叫人心醉。

阿黛尔·普莱德尔（Adèle Pleydell）年纪最小，五年前嫁了普莱德尔上尉（Captain Pleydell）：我后来才知道她生在美国。她和丈夫看上去比年纪还要年轻。普莱德尔少校（Major Pleydell）和达夫妮·普莱德尔（Daphne Pleydell）显然年长些，被众口一词地当作一家的当家。然而五人一体，并无高下；有一回阿黛尔·普莱德尔自称是个外来的，是他们中间唯一祖上不曾识得白女士庄的，登时引起一场其乐融融的喧哗，普莱德尔少校正色道：「这一点，我视作本门的不幸之一。」

她是高挑纤瘦的姑娘，仪态优雅，身段生得精妙绝伦。她的脸庞可爱；一头浓密的黑发莹润生辉；那双明媚的棕眸里的光彩，更是一件夺目的珍宝。她娴静，却不知疲倦，样样事情似乎都做得漂亮：汽车开得，马也骑得，同任何一个男子并辔都不逊色：可她永远带着少女气，跨在马上也像骑竹马的小姑娘一般娇柔，一如她身着绫罗绸缎、坐到钢琴跟前、扬起那清妙歌喉的时候。她天性热忱，一唤即应；她的模样我将永远记得，一如萨金特笔下的她——美丽的双唇微启，温柔的棕眸灼灼发亮。

===Original===
If the others were less attractive, that was no fault of theirs, for I think Adèle Pleydell would have diminished anyone. She was plainly their darling, yet did not seem to know this; and, since she was very quick-witted, this one simplicity made her the more worshipful.

Hanbury and I, as was natural, fell down at her feet, and I am proud to remember that she made us free of her friendship, before we had known her an hour.

So, indeed, did they all: and I do not think I ever paid a visit one half so agreeable.

Much was made of the adventure to which I have already referred, and, when Major Pleydell proposed that they should go fishing in Carinthia later that year and then explore the very scenes of our endeavour, the idea was heartily received.

===Chinese===
倘若旁几位逊色些，那可不是他们的错，因为我想，阿黛尔·普莱德尔站在谁的身旁，谁都要黯然失色。她分明是全家的心肝，自己却浑然不觉；而她又那样颖悟，这唯一的天真，愈发叫人敬爱。

汉伯里和我自然而然拜倒在她脚下；说来我也与有荣焉——相识还不到一个钟头，她便许了我们同她的友谊。

一家人个个如此：平生做客，我再没有一回，有这里一半的惬意。

前文提到的那场冒险，被大家谈论了个痛快；待普莱德尔少校提议，年内晚些时候全家去克恩滕（Carinthia）钓鱼，随后再实地踏看我们当年冒险的故地，这主意立刻大得人心。

===Original===
“And you’ll come and stay with us,” said Daphne Pleydell, addressing Hanbury and me. “If they will let us the farm we rented before, we shall have plenty of room, and Jonah’s a poor showman when it comes to talking of himself.”

That this was so I proved the very next day, for I rode with Adèle in the morning at six o’clock and found she knew next to nothing of what Mansel had done. Be sure I enlightened her, if only for Justice’ sake.

When I had finished, she set her chin in the air.

“Tell me,” she said. “Is Rose Noble the man to take this lying down? I mean, nine hundred thousand is a bag of money to lose.”

===Chinese===
「你们俩也来，住在我们那儿，」达夫妮·普莱德尔对汉伯里和我说。「要是先前租的那座农庄还肯租给我们，地方尽宽裕——何况讲到自己的事，约拿（Jonah）最不会表功。」

这话不假，第二天我便验证了：早晨六点我陪阿黛尔骑马，发现曼塞尔干过什么，她几乎一无所知。不必说，我替她开蒙了，哪怕只是为了公道二字。

我讲完，她把下巴一扬。

「你说说，」她道。「罗斯·诺布尔是肯咽下这口气的人么？我是说，九十万镑——丢了这么大一口袋钱哪。」

===Original===
I suppose I hesitated, for her head was round in an instant, and her steady, brown eyes were on mine.

“Didn’t you know,” I said, “that Mansel was lately robbed?”

She let out a cry of excitement and checked her horse.

“I never knew,” she said. And presently, “Go on.”

I shook my head.

“It’s not my secret,” I said. “As it is, I’ve said too much.”

===Chinese===
想必我迟疑了一下，她的头倏地转过来，那双沉静的棕眸直望进我的眼睛。

「你还不知道么，」我说，「曼塞尔前些日子失窃了？」

她兴奋地叫出声来，勒住了马。

「我一直不知道，」她说。少顷：「接着讲。」

我摇了摇头。

「这不是我的秘密，」我说。「事到如今，我已经说多了。」

===Original===
With that, I would have gone forward, but she leaned down and caught my rein.

“Tell me,” she pleaded, “tell me. I swear I’ll not breathe a word.”

In the end I yielded, and, before we were back at White Ladies, she knew as much as I.

When I said I was concerned, she laughed.

“I snuff a romance,” she cried. “Jonah, the celibate, has had some passionate affair, and he’s frightened to death that Rose Noble will bring it out. But he never will. You can’t blackmail a man for playing the game: and Jonathan Mansel’s never done anything else.”

===Chinese===
说着我便要纵马前行，她却俯身一把攥住我的缰绳。

「告诉我，」她央求道，「告诉我。我发誓一个字也不往外吐。」

到底我让了步；还没回到白女士庄，她知道的已经同我一样多。

我说我不安，她笑了。

「我嗅出一桩罗曼史了，」她叫道。「约拿这个独身汉，准有过什么炽烈的情事，如今吓得半死，生怕罗斯·诺布尔把它抖搂出来。可他抖搂不出来。一个人行事光明正大，你敲诈不着他：而乔纳森·曼塞尔行事，从来光明正大。」

===Original===
“That I believe,” said I. “But why was he troubled?”

The lady shrugged her shoulders.

“A celibate sees a scandal in every bow. The memory of the most harmless flirtation is a millstone round Jonah’s neck.”

Her interpretation relieved me, for I was sure she was wise; but, though I was greatly tempted to share it with George, I did not care to admit that I, and not Mansel, had told Adèle of the theft. So I held my peace.

The next day we left for Wiltshire promising soon to return. Yet we did not, though our homes were but fifty miles apart; for with the coming of summer there was much to be done at Maintenance, and, though the hunting was over, we had our hands full.

---

===Chinese===
「这我也相信，」我说。「可他为什么不安？」

女士耸了耸肩。

「独身汉看每一记鞠躬都藏着丑闻。最无伤大雅的一段调情，回想起来也是套在约拿脖子上的磨盘。」

她这番解释叫我放了心，因为我确信她有眼光；可是，虽说我极想说给乔治听，我却不肯承认，把失窃一事告诉阿黛尔的是我，而不是曼塞尔。于是我守口如瓶。

次日我们返回威尔特郡，许诺不久再来。结果却没有再来，虽说两家相距不过五十英里；因为夏天一到，赡养庄里事务繁多，狩猎虽已收场，我们依然忙得不可开交。

---

===Original===
The Pleydells and Jonathan Mansel left for Carinthia in July.

Mansel was soon to come back, for he had business at home; and then, on the first of September, he and George Hanbury and I were to go out together by road.

And so it fell out﻿—though not as we had expected: for, though Mansel came leisurely to England, he took the road back to Carinthia, like a man possessed. And Hanbury and I with him.

===Chinese===
七月里，普莱德尔一家和乔纳森·曼塞尔动身去了克恩滕。

曼塞尔不久便要回国，因为家中有事务待理；随后，九月一日，他要同乔治·汉伯里和我一道驱车出行。

事情也果然如此——只是不似我们预想的那样：曼塞尔慢悠悠回到英国，回克恩滕时，却像着了魔一般上了路。汉伯里和我随他同行。

===Original===
On the thirtieth day of August we dined in Cleveland Row, to settle the hour of departure and other things.

Our plans were simple and soon laid.

We were to meet at Folkestone and cross by the morning boat; and, since it seemed idle to take two cars, yet send three servants by train, we arranged to keep two with us and to send the third to Salzburg in charge of our heavier stuff. As luck would have it, all three had done this journey before﻿—for Rowley, Hanbury’s old servant, had lately reentered his service﻿—and, since they were all efficient, any one of the three could be trusted to shift for himself: but, as Carson and Bell were accustomed to handling a car, but Rowley was not, the latter was chosen to take our baggage by train.

Not until the cloth had been drawn did Mansel tell us that he had some unfortunate news.

===Chinese===
八月三十日，我们在克利夫兰罗聚餐，商定动身的时辰及其他事项。

我们的计划简单，转眼便定当了。

我们约定在福克斯通（Folkestone）会齐，搭早班船渡海；既然自己带两辆车、却打发三个仆人坐火车，未免可笑，我们便安排留两个跟车，第三个押着较重的行装，坐火车去萨尔茨堡（Salzburg）。也是赶巧，三个人从前都走过这趟路——汉伯里的老仆罗利（Rowley）不久前才重回他手下——况且三人个个得力，随便哪一个都可以放心让他自行其是：但卡森和贝尔惯于摆弄汽车，罗利却不惯，便选定后者坐火车押运行李。

直到饭毕撤席，曼塞尔才告诉我们一个不幸的消息。

===Original===
“Boy Pleydell,” he said, “Adèle’s husband, has broken his leg. I heard this morning. Years ago, not twenty-five miles from the scene of his accident, he broke a couple of ribs; so it looks as though Carinthia was bad for his health. However, there’s nothing to be done. He’s under a Salzburg surgeon, and I’m taking out thirty novels to help him to pass the time.”

Here the door was opened, and Carson came in with a note. This was addressed to Mansel and marked “Immediate.”

“Who brought this?” said Mansel, taking it up.

“The porter found it on the steps, sir, one minute ago.”

Mansel asked us to excuse him and broke the seal.

===Chinese===
「『小子』普莱德尔，阿黛尔的丈夫，摔断了腿。我是今天早上听说的。多年前，离他出事的地方不到二十五英里，他断过两根肋骨；看来克恩滕这地方于他的健康不利。不过，事已如此，无可奈何。他由萨尔茨堡的一位外科医生照管着，我打算带三十本小说去，帮他消磨时光。」

正说着，门开了，卡森拿着一封信进来。信封上写着曼塞尔收，还标着「急件」。

「谁送来的？」曼塞尔拿起信问。

「一分钟前门房在台阶上拾到的，先生。」

曼塞尔请我们见谅，拆了封。

===Original===
After a little he gave me the letter to read.

> The stolen goods will be returned on the receipt by the Manager of the —﻿—﻿— Bank, Zurich, of your cheque for five hundred thousand pounds. This sum you can raise, if you please. No time should be wasted for the goods are perishable.
> August 30th.

The body of the letter was written in a clerkly hand, but the date had been rudely added, I suppose, that day.

I passed the letter to George and turned to Mansel.

===Chinese===
稍顷，他把信递给我读。

> 被窃之货物，一经苏黎世—— ——银行经理收讫尊驾五十万镑之支票，即行奉还。此款，如您乐意，筹得出来。切勿迁延，因货物易腐。
> 八月三十日。

信的正文是一手工整的抄写体，日期却是粗粗添上的，我想就是当天。

我把信递给乔治，转向曼塞尔。

===Original===
“ ‘Perishable’?” said I. “ ‘Perishable?’ What does he mean?”

“I can’t think,” said Mansel slowly, knitting his brows. “And why has he waited nine months?”

“It must be Rose Noble,” said Hanbury, looking up from the sheet: “for nobody else would know you could raise such a sum. Otherwise, I should say that the writer was out of his mind. I mean, half a million for some papers.﻿ ﻿…”

“I agree,” said Mansel. “It’s fantastic. I value them certainly: but I wouldn’t give more than a hundred to get them back. If as much. I can’t understand it,” he continued, taking the letter again; “for Rose Noble must know what they’re worth rather better than I.”

For a while we sat silent, for there was nothing to say: but I could not help wondering what was the nature of the papers which Rose Noble held and reflecting that, until we knew that, neither George nor I could make any useful remark.

===Chinese===
「『易腐』？」我说。「『易腐』？他是什么意思？」

「我想不出，」曼塞尔缓缓地说，蹙着眉。「还有，他为什么等了九个月？」

「这准是罗斯·诺布尔，」汉伯里从信纸上抬起眼来：「旁人不会知道你筹得出这样一笔款子。换了旁人，我要说写信的人疯了。我是说，几份文件就要五十万。……」

「我同意，」曼塞尔说。「这未免匪夷所思。我自然珍视它们：但要赎回来，一百镑我也不肯出。兴许还不到。我不明白，」他重新拿起信，接着说：「罗斯·诺布尔该知道它们值多少，比我知道得更清楚。」

我们默默坐了片刻，因为无话可说：可我忍不住琢磨，罗斯·诺布尔攥着的那些文件究竟是什么性质，又想，这一点不明之前，乔治和我谁都出不了有益的主意。

===Original===
Mansel was speaking in a quiet, even tone.

“The papers are the letters of a girl﻿—occasional letters and notes﻿—in all, I suppose ten or twelve. Their matter is so casual and ordinary that I feared that Rose Noble would wonder why I had kept them safe. They were in order of date, with her photograph. I feared he would think that she meant something to me. I mean, *that was the only explanation* of my keeping so carefully such artless documents.”

===Chinese===
曼塞尔开口了，语调平静而匀整。

「那些文件是一个姑娘的信——零零散散的信和短简——总共，我料想有十封十二封。内容那样随意寻常，我原本还担心，罗斯·诺布尔要奇怪我怎么把它们保存得妥妥帖帖。信按日期先后排着，还夹着她的相片。我怕他会认为，她于我有几分意思。我是说，*这是唯一的解释*——不然我何苦这样细心收着这些质朴无华的文字。」

===Original===
There was a long silence, and all that Adèle had said came to my mind with a rush. And I could have laughed for relief, but that I knew that Rose Noble was no fool.

At length﻿—

“I still see no daylight,” said Hanbury. “He offers you those letters back. When you ignore his offer, what will he do?”

Mansel shrugged his shoulders.

“He may send them to her husband,” he said. “I would very much rather he didn’t, but that’s as far as I go.”

===Chinese===
接着是长长的沉默，阿黛尔说过的话一齐涌上我心头。我险些笑出声来，如释重负——若不是我知道罗斯·诺布尔不是傻瓜。

终于——

「我还是看不见亮儿，」汉伯里说。「他提出把那些信还你。待你不理他的提议，他会怎么做？」

曼塞尔耸了耸肩。

「他也许把它们寄给她丈夫，」他说。「我巴不得他不这么做，可我也只能让到这一步。」

===Original===
Again I took the letter and read it through.

“ ‘The goods are perishable,’ ” I said. “That’s a curious way of saying ‘I’m going to send them to him.’ ”

“I agree,” said Hanbury. “And it’s not at all like Rose Noble. He always made himself clear.”

“Painfully clear,” said I, and could have bitten out my tongue.

But Mansel gave no sign of having heard what I said.

===Chinese===
我又把信拿过来，从头读了一遍。

「『货物易腐』，」我说。「这话说得可真别致，倒像是说『我要把它们寄给他』。」

「我同意，」汉伯里说。「这全然不像罗斯·诺布尔的作风。他向来把话说得明明白白。」

「明白得扎人，」我说，恨不能咬掉自己的舌头。

但曼塞尔对我这话毫无表示。

===Original===
Then a bell was rung, and, sitting in breathless silence, we heard a servant pass to the flat’s front door.

The next moment Carson entered, bearing a telegram.

Mansel ripped open the envelope, glanced at the sheet and clapped his hands to his face.

The three of us stared at him.

Presently﻿—

===Chinese===
接着铃响了，我们屏息静坐，听见一名仆人应声走向公寓正门。

转眼卡森进来，拿着一封电报。

曼塞尔撕开信封，扫了一眼电文，双手猛地掩上面孔。

我们三个盯着他。

少顷——

===Original===
“Tell the man to wait,” he said quietly. “He shall have an answer in five minutes’ time.”

Carson withdrew.

Mansel rose to his feet and handed the telegram to me:

> Return Adèle disappeared shall I call in police Pleydell.

===Chinese===
「叫送报的等一等，」他平静地说。「五分钟之内给他答复。」

卡森退了出去。

曼塞尔站起身，把电报递给我：

> 速复 阿黛尔失踪 可否召警 普莱德尔

===Original===
“Good God,” I cried, rising.

Hanbury snatched the form from my hand.

“You were quite right, Chandos,” said Mansel. “Rose Noble has a way of making himself painfully clear.”

I could only stare, and Mansel gave a short laugh.

“Let me do the same,” he said. “*The letters he took had been written to me by Adèle.*”

===Chinese===
「天哪，」我叫着，站起身来。

汉伯里一把从我手里抢过电单。

「你说得一点不错，钱多斯，」曼塞尔说。「罗斯·诺布尔自有法子，把话说得明白扎人。」

我只能干瞪眼，曼塞尔短促地笑了一声。

「我也照直说了吧，」他说。*他拿走的那些信，是阿黛尔写给我的。*

===Original===
“Oh, my God,” said Hanbury.

“And, when he says ‘stolen goods,’ he’s not referring to the letters, but to something more﻿—more valuable, *something which ‘disappeared’ a few hours ago*.”

Not until then did the scales fall from my eyes: but though I would have spoken, I could not utter a word.

I watched Mansel pick up the letter and read it through.

“ ‘Perishable goods,’ ” he said quietly, speaking as though he were alone. “Yes. I suppose you might call *Adèle in Rose Noble’s hands*﻿—‘perishable goods.’ ”

---

===Chinese===
「哦，老天，」汉伯里说。

「况且，他说『被窃的货物』，指的不是那些信，而是更——更贵重的东西，*一件几个钟头前『失踪』的东西*。」

直到那时，我心头的蒙翳才霍然褪尽：可我虽有话要说，却一个字也吐不出来。

我看着曼塞尔拿起那封信，从头读了一遍。

「『易腐的货物』，」他轻声说，像在自言自语。「是啊。*阿黛尔落在罗斯·诺布尔手里*——我想，也可以叫作『易腐的货物』了。」

---

===Original===
There was champagne on ice on a sideboard, and Mansel opened a bottle and poured the wine.

When we had drunk, he sat down and wrote his reply:

> On no account **Mansel.**

And when this had been dispatched, he picked up Rose Noble’s letter and lighted a cigarette.

===Chinese===
餐具柜上镇着一瓶香槟，曼塞尔开了瓶，斟上酒。

我们干过杯，他坐下写了回电：

> 万万不可 **曼塞尔。**

回电发出之后，他拿起罗斯·诺布尔那封信，点上一支烟。

===Original===
His agitation cannot have been over, but all sign of it was gone; and from this time on until the end, he was, as always, the coolest and most patient of us all. Few men, I think could have maintained such mastery of themselves; but Mansel’s self-control was absolute, and, though it was now to be proved as surely no man’s has ever been proved before, it never failed and seldom enough gave any sign of strain. Indeed, I often think that the flash of feeling he showed, when the telegram was brought in, was because when he read it, he knew that his secret was ours. Had Rose Noble’s letter followed instead of preceding the telegram, he never would have told us the nature of the papers which had lain in his safe, and I am sure that neither George Hanbury nor I would ever have suspected the truth. Yet I am glad we knew it and I think that, now it was done, Mansel was glad also; for, be a man never so reserved, there is a pitch of trouble which he is thankful to share.

After a little, Mansel folded the letter and held it up.

===Chinese===
他心里的激动不会已经平息，可激动的形迹已荡然无存；从这时起直到终局，他一如既往，是我们中间最冷静、最有耐性的一个。我想，能把自制保持到这个地步的人没有几个；曼塞尔的自制是绝对的，虽说它此后要经受的考验，确乎空前，它却从未失守，连吃力的样子也难得露出一丝。说实在的，我常常想，电报送进来时他那一闪而过的激动，是因为他一读电文，便知道他的秘密从此瞒不过我们了。倘若罗斯·诺布尔的信不是先于、而是后于电报到来，他绝不会告诉我们他保险柜里那些文件的性质，而且我确信，乔治·汉伯里和我谁也不会疑心到真相。然而我很庆幸我们知道了；我也想，事已至此，曼塞尔同样庆幸；因为一个人无论多么缄默，烦恼到了某个地步，总是感激有人分担的。

稍顷，曼塞尔把信折起，举到眼前。

===Original===
“I am not going to act,” said he, “upon the suggestion here made, because, for one thing, such a sum is ruinous, and, for another, I do not trust Rose Noble.”

I got to my feet.

“We’re all three in this,” I said. “That’s abundantly clear. If he’d drawn blank in this flat he’d have started on George or on me. But, whichever of us he’d attacked, his price would have been the same.”

“That’s beyond doubt,” said Hanbury. “He’s out to recover the fortune: and, not knowing how much it came to, he’s put it as high as he dares.”

“Exactly,” said I. “Very well. My share was two hundred thousand: in two days’ time you shall have three fourths of that back.”

===Chinese===
「这封信里的提议，」他说，「我不打算照办：一则，这样一笔款子足以叫人倾家荡产；二则，我不信罗斯·诺布尔。」

我站起身。

「这件事我们三个都在里头，」我说。「这是明摆着的。倘若他在这套公寓空手而回，他早对乔治或者我下手了。但不论他冲我们哪一个来，他的价钱都会是一样的。」

「这毫无疑问，」汉伯里说。「他一心要夺回那笔财产：又不知道它究竟有多少，便把价钱开到他敢开的最高。」

「正是，」我说。「很好。我名下的一份是二十万：两天之内，你便可收回其中的四分之三。」

===Original===
“Same here,” said Hanbury.

“I know that,” said Mansel. “Thank you. But it would break her heart. Sooner or later she would most surely find out, and then﻿—well, you can’t lay anyone under a debt like that. It’s not to be thought of. And, since, as I say, I do not trust Rose Noble, I think it will be convenient to count this document out.”

With that, he put the letter towards a candle’s flame, but after a moment, withdrew it and put it away in his case.

“So all that we know,” he continued, “is that Adèle has disappeared: and, since my cousin, her husband, is out of action and we three know Carinthia as the palm of our hand, we are naturally going to seek her with all our might. Of course we *suspect* abduction: I think anyone would. *But that is all.* Have I made myself clear?”

===Chinese===
「我也一样，」汉伯里说。

「这我知道，」曼塞尔说。「谢谢你们。可那会叫她心碎。或早或迟，她必定会发觉，到那时——唉，谁也不能让人家背上这样一笔债。那想都不必想。况且，如我所说，我不信罗斯·诺布尔；这份文件，我看就勾销了事最相宜。」

说着，他把信凑向烛火，可片刻之后又缩回手，把信收进了手提箱。

「所以，我们所知道的，」他接着说，「只是阿黛尔失踪了：她的丈夫、我那位表亲眼下动弹不得，我们三个又对克恩滕了如指掌，我们自然要倾尽全力去找她。我们当然*疑心*是绑架：我想谁都会疑心。*但仅止于此。*我把话说清楚了没有？」

===Original===
“Yes,” said I.

“Good,” said Mansel. “And now please don’t talk for a minute. I want to think.”

I was glad to sit still, with my head in my hands, for the turn of events had shocked me, and I felt as though I were dreaming some disagreeable dream.

The disclosure of Mansel’s secret, the unconscionable daring of Rose Noble, the horror of the plight of Adèle had dealt me three swingeing blows; but what had hit me still harder was the sudden appreciation that, thanks to our talk in the forest, *Adèle herself must now know that she was the very lady that Mansel loved.*

What Mansel would have said, had he known this, I dared not think: but I was quite certain that, when he found it out, as he most surely would, he would be most particular never to see her again.

===Chinese===
「清楚了，」我说。

「好，」曼塞尔说。「现在请你们一分钟别说话。我要想一想。」

我乐得抱头静坐，因为事态的转折震住了我，只觉得自己像在做一场不愉快的梦。

曼塞尔秘密的败露，罗斯·诺布尔丧心病狂的大胆，阿黛尔处境的可怖，给了我结结实实三记重击；可打得更重的，是我猛然醒悟：多亏林中那番谈话，*阿黛尔此刻必定已经明白，曼塞尔所爱的那位女士，正是她自己。*

曼塞尔若知道这一层会说什么，我不敢想：但我十分确定，等他发觉——他必定会发觉——他必定万分决绝，从此再不见她一面。

===Original===
This was no conjecture, for I knew the man.

Full measure he gave in all things, though it were to his own beggary; and that he would palter where a girl’s heart was concerned was unimaginable.

Adèle was his cousin’s wife, at once his liege lady and his familiar friend: that much I had seen with my eyes: there never was, I believe, so gentle a relation. That the one valued this was patent: it was, I suppose, the light of the other’s life. And now it was soon to founder, sunk by Mansel’s own hand, rather than let come into the shallows of embarrassment.

The thought that my tongue would be to blame for this most bitter upshot haunted me for days, although, as I shall show, I need have had no concern. Indeed, throughout our venture Mansel bore himself with such exalted gallantry that I have often thought since that, though he could not have known of the speech I had had with Adèle, yet he knew in his heart that she would know why she had been taken and that he was carrying her colours for the first and last time.

===Chinese===
这不是臆测，因为我了解这个人。

他凡事必给足十分，哪怕自己落到行乞的地步；至于在一颗姑娘的心上还要讨价还价，那不可想象。

阿黛尔是他表亲的妻子，既是他的主母，又是他的挚友：这一点我是亲眼见的：这样温煦的一段情谊，我相信再没有第二份。一方珍重这份情谊，那是明摆着的；而它，我料想，是另一方生命里的光。可如今它眼看就要沉没——宁可由曼塞尔亲手凿沉，也不肯让它搁上难堪的浅滩。

这最苦涩的结局要归咎于我这条舌头——这个念头缠了我好几天，虽然后文将表明，我本不必担忧。的确，在整个冒险期间，曼塞尔的举止自有一种高华的侠气，以致我后来常常想：他虽不可能知道我同阿黛尔的那番谈话，心里却明白，她知道自己为何被劫，也明白他这是头一遭、也是末一遭，高擎着她的旗帜出战。

===Original===
My mind being so exercised, I do not find it surprising that I cannot clearly remember all that was said that evening, but I know that Mansel determined to sail the next night, but not before then, because he must have a day in which to prepare for the battle to which we were now to go.

Rowley was to leave with our baggage by an earlier train and Hanbury was to change the arrangements for shipping the cars.

Mansel’s Rolls-Royce was furnished with secret lockers and trays, but ours had no such fittings, and, since we must now carry arms, he gave me a note to his coachbuilder and bade me seek him the next morning at eight o’clock. While he and his men were at work, fitting a hidden coffer, Carson and I together were to test and prepare the two cars, so that, with luck, if need be, they could run for a month on end without attention.

Bell was to cross to France by a morning boat, there to buy food and petrol against our coming and to promise ten thousand francs to the officials concerned if we were clear of the Customs in half an hour.

===Chinese===
心里这样翻腾，那一晚的话我记不全，也就不足为怪；但我记得，曼塞尔定于次日夜里渡海启程，不能再早，因为必须留出一天，筹备我们如今要投身的那场战斗。

罗利带着行李搭早一班火车先走，汉伯里去改两辆汽车的船运安排。

曼塞尔的劳斯莱斯（Rolls-Royce）配着暗格暗屉，我们这两辆却没有这类装置；既然如今要携带枪械，他写了张条子给他的车身匠，吩咐我次日早晨八点去寻他。车身匠督着工人装置暗柜的当口，卡森和我一起检验、整备那两辆车，务求运气不坏的话，一旦需要，它们可以连跑一个月不用照管。

贝尔搭早班船先渡法国，在那里替我们置办吃食和汽油，以备我们到达；再向有关的官员们许下一万法郎，只要我们半小时内出得海关。

===Original===
From the port we should drive to Salzburg as hard and as straight as we could and thence direct to St. Martin, for that was the name of the village which served the Pleydells’ farm.

That Adèle was in Austria seemed certain, for without her passport and against her will she could hardly be taken out: moreover the countryside lent itself to violence, for much of it was most solitary, and the lives of its inhabitants were too strait for them to intrude upon matters with which they had no concern. I suppose there were constables of sorts, though I never saw one, except, of course, in the towns: and they would have shrugged their shoulders upon any business less homely than a breach of the local peace. For this, indeed, we were thankful, for official notice of the matter was the last thing we desired: this for more than one reason, but most of all because, so far from aiding, it would have put our enterprise in deadly peril.

I have no doubt at all that, had the police been called in, Adèle would have paid with her life for their assistance. The fight was between us and Rose Noble, and the bare threat of an ally to whom, if he lost, Rose Noble would have to answer, would have been instantly silenced in the most dreadful and effective of ways. Another man might have balked at so detestable a crime, but Rose Noble was ruthless and would, I think, have slain ten pawns, had they stood in the way of his safety or revenge. I verily believe it was this terrible quality, if, indeed it can be so called, to which he owed his immunity, for, while “dead men tell no tales,” it has but to be known that a man keeps that for his motto and those who have to do with him will tell none either.

It was midnight before we parted, and three o’clock of the morning before I fell asleep: but six hours later the coachbuilder was urging his men, and Carson and I were at work. Except that we drove to St. James’s and back again, we laboured incessantly till four, but, when I reported to Mansel at five o’clock, the cars were as ready for the road as the wit of man could make them and both were at hand in his garage by Stable Yard. There they were packed and loaded by Carson alone, and at half past six they were standing in Cleveland Row.

===Chinese===
出港之后，我们要一径疾驰、取直路赶往萨尔茨堡，再从那里直奔圣马丁（St. Martin）——普莱德尔家那座农庄归这个村子照应。

阿黛尔人在奥地利，这一点似乎确定：没有护照、又违着她的意愿，很难把她带出国境；何况这乡野也便于行凶，大片地方人迹罕至，居民的生计又太紧巴，无暇过问与自己不相干的事。我想乡间总也有几个巡警之类，不过除了镇上，我一个也没有见过：而但凡事情比扰乱乡里安宁更稀奇些的，他们多半耸耸肩膀了事。这一层我们倒真感激，因为此事惊动官府，是我们最最不愿的：缘由不止一端，最要紧的是，官府一来非但无助，反倒会把我们这桩事业置于死地。

我毫不怀疑：倘若报了警，阿黛尔必得拿性命为他们的「相助」付账。这一仗是我们和罗斯·诺布尔之间的事；哪怕仅仅扬言要引入一位盟友——一旦事败，罗斯·诺布尔须向这位盟友交代——这么一句空口的威胁，也会立刻被人用最可怕也最有效的法子掐灭。换一个人，对这样可憎的罪行或许还要踌躇，罗斯·诺布尔却全无怜惜；我想，但凡挡了他生路或复仇之路的抵押小卒有十个，他能把十个全杀了。我真诚相信，正是这种可怕的品性——如果那也算得品性——保得他至今无恙：因为，虽说「死人不开口」，只需让人知道一个人拿这句话当座右铭，那么不得不同他打交道的人，也就一个都不会开口了。

我们散时已过午夜，我入睡已是凌晨三点：可六小时之后，车身匠已在催工，卡森和我也上了手。除了驱车去圣詹姆斯宫打个来回，我们马不停蹄干到四点；五点钟我向曼塞尔复命时，两辆车已尽人力之所能，收拾得万无一失，都停在他马厩院（Stable Yard）旁的车库里。在那里，行装由卡森一人收拾装车；六点半，两辆车已停在克利夫兰罗。

===Original===
Rowley and Bell were gone, so we were but four to travel as far as Dieppe: yet in a way we were five, for Tester, Mansel’s Sealyham, went with us, and, if he was but a dog, he was ever better company than many a man.

He was fine to look at, very strong and healthy, intelligent beyond belief. Given an order that he could appreciate, he would obey it to the death. He knew no fear, was very quick and cheerful, would countenance no one but those his master had commended and worshipped Mansel himself with the most lively devotion that I have ever seen.

At a quarter to seven we passed out of Cleveland Row.

We made no secret of our going, simply because it was a movement we could not hide: “all the same,” said Mansel, “it doesn’t very much matter if they do send Rose Noble a wire. He never expected that I should pay out of hand: but he means me to find it hopeless and then to put on the screw.”

We dined at Newhaven and saw the cars taken on board: then we turned into our cabins to take what rest we could, for, though Mansel had not said so, we all four knew very well that, until we were come to Carinthia, we should none of us sleep again.

---

===Chinese===
罗利和贝尔已经先行，所以到迪耶普（Dieppe）为止，我们只有四个人同行：但从某种意义上说，我们是五个，因为曼塞尔的西里汉姆梗犬特斯特（Tester）也随行；他虽不过是条狗，做伴却向来胜过许多人。

他模样俊朗，筋骨结实，健康非常，聪明得叫人难以置信。凡是他听得懂的命令，他必执行到死。他不知恐惧为何物，机敏快活，除了主人认可的人对谁都不假辞色，对曼塞尔本人更是崇奉备至，那份炽烈的忠爱，我生平仅见。

七点差一刻，我们驶出克利夫兰罗。

此行我们并不隐瞒，只因这动静根本瞒不住：「不过，」曼塞尔说，「他们真给罗斯·诺布尔拍电报，也无关紧要。他原本就没指望我当场付钱：他是要让我觉得此事无望，然后再拧紧螺丝。」

我们在纽黑文（Newhaven）用了晚饭，看着两辆车上船：随后各自回舱，能歇多少歇多少；因为曼塞尔虽没说破，我们四个心里都雪亮——不到克恩滕，谁也别想再合眼。

---

===Original===
The steward roused me by order half an hour before we were due, and I came on deck to find a clean morning and two or three lights marking the coast of France.

Mansel had told me to breakfast before I left the boat, so after a turn or two, I went below, there to find him and Hanbury making a wretched meal.

Whilst I was waiting to join them, he gave me a map.

“We must keep together,” he said: “but, as we can’t see ahead, this is in case we part. Don’t use it at all until then. I’ve marked the route in blue pencil, so that you can’t go wrong. I’d better take the lead. I shall go pretty fast, but please try to keep me in sight. If I lose you, I shall slow up, but I don’t want to have to do that. If you want to attract my attention, use your horn. I’ll take Carson to start with; but later we might make a change, and you or Hanbury drive for a while with me.”

===Chinese===
到港前半小时，服务员奉命叫醒了我；我走上甲板，迎面一个晴爽的早晨，两三处灯火标着法兰西的海岸。

曼塞尔嘱咐过我，下船前先用早饭，所以在甲板上转了一两圈，我便下舱去，只见他同汉伯里正对着早饭食不下咽。

我等着入座的当口，他递给我一张地图。

「我们必须结伴走，」他说：「可前面的事谁也料不定，这张地图以防走散。走散之前完全用不着它。路线我用蓝铅笔标好了，保你们走不错。最好我来领头。我会开得相当快，但请尽力别让我出了你们的视线。我若丢了你们，会放慢车速，可我不想常有这种事。你们要引起我注意，就按喇叭。起头我带卡森；往后不妨换一换，你或汉伯里过来同我开一段。」

===Original===
“First stop, Carinthia,” said George.

“If you please,” said Mansel. “I hope we shall be at St. Martin in twenty-four hours. We must water and feed, of course, and fill up the cars: but I wired to my cousin that we should be there by dawn, and I rather fancy, poor fellow, he’ll watch the clock.”

We had already decided that, though we might rest at Poganec after our run, we should leave our baggage at Villach, at an inn which we knew; for not only was this town more central, but to have to “report progress,” as we should if we stayed with the Pleydells, whenever we came or went would be intolerable. This may seem a harsh decision, as they were so deeply concerned, but we should be dealing, we knew, with life and death, and that we should be hampered by any sort of obligation was not to be thought of. We did not expect, however, to have much use for a base, but to be constantly moving in search or pursuit of Adèle: and this was why Mansel had been insistent that the cars should demand no attention, yet withstand incessant use.

As the boat entered the harbour, we came on deck and presently made out Bell, who was standing with three officials on the edge of the quay. So soon as he saw us, he pointed us out to his companions, one of whom boarded the steamer before she was fairly at rest. I met him with our papers, and, since they were what he had come for, he took them without a word.

===Chinese===
「第一站，克恩滕，」乔治说。

「说的是，」曼塞尔说。「我希望二十四小时内赶到圣马丁。当然，人要打尖，车要加水添油：可我已电告我那位表亲，我们天亮前到；我颇疑心，可怜的人，他会数着钟点等。」

有一件事我们早已商定：赶完这段路，尽可以在波加内克（Poganec）歇脚，行李却要留在菲拉赫（Villach）一家我们熟识的旅店；因为这城不但位置居中，而且倘若住进普莱德尔家，那么每来一回、每去一回都得「禀报进展」，那可叫人吃不消。他们同此事干系那样深，这决定看似薄情；但我们打交道的，自己心里有数，是生死之事，再受任何形式的拘束，便不可想象了。不过我们并不指望多大用得上一处基地，而是要不停地奔波，寻访阿黛尔、追踪阿黛尔：这也正是曼塞尔坚持的缘故——两辆车必须无需照管，又经得起连轴使用。

船进港时，我们上了甲板，一眼便认出贝尔：他同三名官员站在码头边沿。他一见我们，便把我们指给同伴；船还没完全停稳，其中一个已上了船。我拿着证件迎上去；那正是他此来要的东西，他一言不发地收下了。

===Original===
This was well enough, but the cars had to be unshipped, and, since the boat-train was waiting, the ordinary registered baggage must, as always, be taken off first. That this would be a long business seemed very probable, for there was but one crane manned, and, as luck would have it, there were many passengers.

As the man who had taken our papers regained the quay, the main gangways were run inboard, and I saw for the first time that Bell had a watch in his hand. A little way off was a lad in charge of a basket and a small stack of petrol cans.

“Full marks to Bell,” said Mansel. “They’re going to take the cars first.”

And so indeed they did﻿—such is the power of money.

Mansel’s Rolls was ashore before any of us, and, at a sign from Bell, the lad with the basket began to fill her tank. As the second car was landed, the man who had taken our papers came running out of some office with the documents stamped and signed, and, after a glance at our number-plates, handed me back the wallet and raised his hat. Then our baggage was hastily chalked, and, as Mansel started his engine, Bell put away his watch.

===Chinese===
这一关还算顺当，可汽车得卸下船；联运列车在等着，照例又得先卸普通托运的行李。这多半是桩旷日持久的活计，因为开动的吊车只有一台，偏偏又赶上乘客众多。

收走我们证件的那人回到码头时，主跳板收进了船舷，我这才头一回看见，贝尔手里捏着一块表。不远处一个小伙子守着一只提篮和一小摞汽油桶。

「贝尔满分，」曼塞尔说。「他们要先卸汽车了。」

果然先卸汽车——金钱的威力，一至于此。

曼塞尔的劳斯莱斯比我们谁都先上岸；贝尔一打手势，守提篮的小伙子便动手给车灌油。第二辆车落岸时，收走证件那人已从某个办公室里跑出来，证件盖好了章、签好了字；他朝我们的车牌看了一眼，把证件夹还我，举了举帽。随后我们的行李草草画上粉号；曼塞尔发动引擎时，贝尔收起了表。

===Original===
“We’re free to proceed, sir,” he said, touching his hat. “By your leave I’ll pay them the money and find you outside.”

“Well done indeed,” said Mansel. “How long have we been?”

“Just under a quarter of an hour, sir. I promised them five thousand if they did it in half an hour, and I said I’d double the money if they did it in half the time.”

With that, he disappeared, and Hanbury started our engine, as Mansel, with Carson and Tester, drove off the quay.

The lad charged to fill our tank was a clumsy workman, so I told him to stand aside and did it myself: and George descended and helped me by taking the caps from the cans.

===Chinese===
「我们可以走了，先生，」他碰了碰帽檐说。「请您恩准，我去把钱付给他们，到外头找您。」

「干得实在漂亮，」曼塞尔说。「我们用了多久？」

「一刻钟还不到，先生。我许了他们五千，只要半小时办妥；又说，时间若只用一半，钱就翻倍。」

说罢他便去了；汉伯里发动我们的引擎，曼塞尔带着卡森和特斯特驶下了码头。

派来灌油的小伙子手脚笨拙，我叫他站开，自己动手：乔治也下了车，帮我拧桶盖。

===Original===
“You drive first,” he said. “It won’t be light for some time, and your eyes are keener than mine.”

“Very well,” said I.

The landing of the registered baggage was now in full swing, and the quay was alive with porters, bustling to and fro in the lamplight and making less progress than noise: and, since the baggage itself was being swung over our heads, I was glad to screw its cap to the tank and to take my seat in the car.

Hanbury picked up the basket and followed me in.

The eastern sky was pale, but it was yet very dark.

===Chinese===
「你先开，」他说。「天一时半会儿亮不了，你的眼睛比我尖。」

「好吧，」我说。

这时卸托运行李正卸得火热，码头上满是要脚夫，在灯光里跑来跑去，动静大而出活少：行李又正从我们头顶上荡来荡去，我便赶紧拧上油箱盖，在车里坐定。

汉伯里提起提篮，跟着上了车。

东方的天际已经泛白，四下却还很黑。

===Original===
Now as we were moving slowly towards the street, I became aware of some paper upon which I seemed to have sat down. So soon as I had a hand free, I plucked this from under my legs, to find it a dirty envelope, bearing no superscription, but sealed.

“What’s that?” said George, peering.

“It must have fallen from the baggage,” I said. “The nets passed over the car. See what it is. If it’s a bill of lading, we’d better give it back.”

As Hanbury ripped open the envelope, Bell stepped out of the shadows on to the running board.

“Captain Mansel’s fifty yards on, sir; on the right of the street.”

===Chinese===
车缓缓往街上挪的时候，我觉出屁股底下像是坐着一张纸。一腾出手，我便把它从腿下抽出来看：一只脏兮兮的信封，不具名址，却封着口。

「那是什么？」乔治眯眼瞧着问。

「准是从行李上掉下来的，」我说。「装车时网子是从车顶罩过去的。看看是什么。要是提单，最好还给人家。」

汉伯里撕开信封的当口，贝尔从暗处跨上踏脚板。

「曼塞尔上尉在前面五十码，先生；街的右手。」

===Original===
“Very good,” said I, turning across the lines.

“It’s not a bill of lading,” said George sharply.

“What then?” said I, setting a foot on the brake.

By way of answer, he held it to the light of the lamp which illumined the instrument board.

===Chinese===
「很好，」我说着，转过了路轨。

「这不是提单，」乔治的嗓音一紧。

「那是什么？」我说，一只脚踩上刹车。

算作回答，他把它凑到仪表盘灯的光下。

===Original===
It was a half-sheet of notepaper on which were printed four words:

> The goods are perishable.

===Chinese===
那是半张信纸，上面印着四个词：

> 货物易腐。

## II We Take the Field / 二 出征

===Original===
To show Mansel a memento so ugly went against the grain: yet we dared not suppress it, so I drove to where he sat waiting, and Hanbury gave the paper into his hand.

He glanced at it, turned it about and then put it away.

“Where did you find it?” he said.

I told him on the seat of the car.

At once he turned to Bell and asked him to try to recall every person that he had seen in the last two hours: “for,” said he, “one of those men was a spy, and, though we can do nothing now, it might be very convenient if you would know him again.”

Then he asked if I was ready and let in his clutch.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
把这么一件丑恶的纪念物拿给曼塞尔看，实在有违本心：可我们又不敢将它隐下，于是我把车开到他等候的地方，由汉伯里把那张纸放到他手里。

他瞥了一眼，翻来覆去看了看，便把它收起。

「你在哪儿找到的？」他说。

我告诉他，是在车座上。

他立刻转向贝尔，请他尽力回想过去两个钟头里见过的每一个人：「因为，」他说，「那些人当中有一个是坐探，虽然我们眼下拿他没办法，可你若往后还能认出他来，说不定大有用处。」

然后他问我是否准备停当，接上了离合器。……

===Original===
The day was coming, but, so long as the night lasted, we had the road to ourselves. Taking advantage of this, Mansel went like the wind, and, since all I could see was his taillight and this poor pointer vanished with each dip and bend of the road, it was more by luck than by cunning that I managed to cling to his heels. Indeed, the first hour of our journey imposed a continuous strain, for he drove as though the way was familiar, but I did not know it at all, and, what with fear of leaving the road if I drove too fast, and of losing my guiding light if I slackened speed, I wished for the day with a fervour which I think would have opened the eyes of St. Paul himself.

Yet, in a way, this trial stood me in stead, for, when at last it was light and I could see the landscape and Mansel’s Rolls scudding before me like a gull, to follow behind seemed child’s play and, though he went faster than ever, I was able without any trouble to maintain the pace which he set.

The sun rose about six into a cloudless sky, and, since rain had lately fallen, the country was fresh to look on and smelled very sweet. Mansel raised next to no dust, for this had been laid by the rain and was not yet dry, and, as the chill of the dawn changed to the cool of the day, our passage became every moment more agreeable. There was, however, a look of heat in the heaven which there was no mistaking.

===Chinese===
天色将明，但只要夜幕未撤，这条路便归我们独享。曼塞尔趁此机会一路疾驰如风；我所能望见的只有他的尾灯，而这可怜的指针又随着道路每一次起伏转折而隐没，因此我竟能咬住他的车尾，靠的多半是运气而非巧劲。的确，旅程的头一个钟头令人始终不敢松懈，因为他开起来仿佛轻车熟路，我却对路径一无所知；又怕开得太快冲出路面，又怕一放慢便丢了引路的灯光，我只得热切地盼天快些亮——那份热切，我想连圣保罗（St. Paul）本人也要为之睁眼。

不过，这场磨炼倒也于我有益：待到天光终于大亮，望得见四野风物，也望得见曼塞尔那辆劳斯莱斯（Rolls）像海鸥一般掠在前头，跟在后面便成了儿戏；任他开得比先前更快，我也能毫不费力地保持他定下的步调。

六点前后，太阳升入无云的天空；新雨方过，乡野望去清新，气息也甜。曼塞尔几乎扬不起一点尘土——雨水已将它压住，尚未干透——而当初晓的微寒转为白昼的凉意，我们的行进便一刻比一刻惬意。只是天色里分明带着一股酝酿酷热的样子，错认不得。

===Original===
Our road was no longer so free as it had been by night, but I was surprised to encounter so little traffic, and, indeed, the clocks had struck seven before the pace at which we were travelling had to be sensibly reduced.

At a quarter to eight we met our first definite check in the shape of a level crossing, the gates of which were closed.

To make the best of the business, we halted for a quarter of an hour, and, while Tester was given his freedom, we ate and drank of the food which Bell had brought.

===Chinese===
路面已不似夜间那般清静，但往来车辆如此稀少，仍出乎我的意料；说实在的，直到时钟敲过七点，我们行进的速度才不得不切实放缓。

八点差一刻，我们遇上了第一处明确的阻碍：一道平交道口，栏门紧闭。

索性善处其事，我们停了一刻钟；趁特斯特撒欢儿的工夫，我们把贝尔带来的吃食用了些。

===Original===
Mansel was plainly pleased with the pace at which we had come, but insisted that now was the time for Hanbury to take my place: then he turned to Bell and asked if he had recalled the people he had seen at Dieppe.

“I’ve done my best, sir,” said Bell: “but I fear there’s not very many I’d know again.”

“That remains to be seen,” said Mansel. “But please try to bear them in mind, and picture to yourself the time you spent on the quay. I expect you walked up and down. Try to remember if anyone was doing the same.”

“Very good, sir,” said Bell obediently.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔显然满意我们跑出的速度，却坚持此刻该轮到汉伯里来替换我；随后他转向贝尔，问他可曾把在迪耶普所见的人回想起来。

「我尽了力，先生，」贝尔说，「只怕没有几个能再认出来的。」

「那还得走着瞧，」曼塞尔说。「不过请务必把他们记在心头，把你在码头上消磨的那段时间在心里过一过。我料想你曾来回踱步。想想有没有谁也在那样踱来踱去。」

「是，先生，」贝尔顺从地说。

===Original===
Five minutes later I was sitting with Mansel, and we were again under way.

We had now come to roads that we had travelled before, but, though the features of the way seemed faintly familiar, I could not have taken it safely without the aid of the map nor have threaded a single town without direction. Mansel, however, drove with the utmost confidence, never hesitating where the road forked or peering at any signpost, but whipping through town after town as though it were the place of his birth.

When I remarked upon this, he said it was nothing at all and that I knew the way to Salzburg as well as did he, but that he was using his knowledge, while I was not.

“And that,” said he, “is why I’m badgering Bell. Bell is observant, and I’ll lay a hundred to one that he observed the fellow that left that note. I don’t think I did, for when he saw me coming, he probably made himself scarce. And you were behind the car. But Bell and he were both waiting for the boat to come in. Well, there’s the making of a clue. It’s slight enough in all conscience, but﻿—well, I don’t know where Adèle is, and Austria’s quite a big place.” After a little, he continued thoughtfully. “Memory’s a storeroom, that’s all: the storeroom of things you observe. If you’re observant, so much the richer your store. But you must be a good storeman﻿—able to produce the requisite, when the moment comes. Some time ago I observed the way to Salzburg: and now I’m producing it. Bell undoubtedly observed the man who carried that note: let’s hope he’ll be able to produce him﻿—when the moment comes.”

===Chinese===
五分钟后，我便坐在了曼塞尔身边，我们又上路了。

我们此刻走上了从前走过的路，但尽管沿途景物隐约眼熟，若没有地图相助，我仍不能稳妥行车，若无人指引，更休想穿行哪怕一座城镇。曼塞尔却开得信心十足：遇岔路从不犹豫，也不盯看路牌，一座接一座城镇飞掠而过，仿佛处处都是他的桑梓之地。

我说起这一点，他说这根本不算什么：去萨尔茨堡的路，我与他一样熟，只不过他在运用自己的所知，而我却没有。

「正因为如此，」他说，「我才揪着贝尔不放。贝尔眼尖，我敢拿一百对一打赌，留下那张字条的家伙他一定看在眼里了。我想我倒没有，因为他见我来，多半闪避开了。而你当时在车后。可贝尔和他都在等船进港。瞧，这就是一条线索的坯子。说良心话，它实在单薄——不过，唉，我不知道阿黛尔在哪儿，而奥地利实在是个大地方。」过了一会儿，他又若有所思地接下去。「记忆是间库房，仅此而已：存放你所观察之物的库房。你越善观察，存货便越丰。可你也得做个好司库——到了时刻，要能取出所需之物。前些时候我观察了去萨尔茨堡的路：如今我把它取出来用。贝尔无疑观察过带那张字条的人：但愿到时候他也能把那人取出来——在那时刻来临之际。」

===Original===
We said no more at the time, and, being, I suppose, more tired than I had suspected, I fell asleep.

When I awoke, it was noon, and the day was immensely hot.

Mansel was driving in the same effortless way, looking very well content with his lot and seeming to be perfectly fresh.

When he saw that I was awake, he told me that we should make Strasbourg in two hours’ time. We had stopped for fuel whilst I slept, but that was all.

===Chinese===
当时我们没有再多谈；而我，想必比自以为的更疲惫，竟睡着了。

一觉醒来已是正午，天气热得厉害。

曼塞尔仍以那副毫不费劲的姿态驾驭着，一副对自身境遇十分知足的模样，而且看上去精神焕发。

见我醒来，他告诉我，再过两个钟头便可抵达斯特拉斯堡。我睡着时我们停车加过油，不过仅此而已。

===Original===
Sure enough, we came to Strasbourg at two o’clock, and at half-past two we were lunching on German soil.

Then I changed places with George, who was white as a miller with dust, for the sun had done its work, and, since there was next to no wind, the cars would have had to travel a mile apart if the second was to escape the clouds which its fellow raised.

The afternoon was made dreadful by the heat and glare of the sun, which oppressed us mercilessly and made the air so sultry that our pace brought us no relief: I never found shade so grateful in all my life, nor yet so scarce, and how Mansel, who had had no respite, bore the burden of that day so lightly I never shall understand.

Twice we were checked by the gates of level crossings and once we stopped for fuel, but these delays were too brief to be refreshing, and I, for one, was thankful when we came to rest at sundown by the side of a little stream. Into this we were happy to plunge our heads and shoulders, and, whilst Mansel and I ate and drank, George and the servants cleaned the windscreens and sponged down and sluiced the cars﻿—by no means a fruitless exercise, for we were sick of the dust, and the sight of the glistening coachwork did us all good.

===Chinese===
果不其然，两点钟我们到了斯特拉斯堡；两点半，我们已在德国的土地上午餐。

随后我与乔治换了班，他被尘土扑得满面皆白，活像个磨坊师傅——太阳已尽了它的本分，而由于几乎没有风，后车若要躲开前车扬起的尘阵，两车本该相隔一英里行驶。

午后为烈日的灼热与炫光所苦：它无情地压逼我们，把空气焖得又湿又闷，连车速也带不来半点宽解。我平生从未觉得绿荫如此可感戴，也从未见它如此难觅；而曼塞尔始终未得片刻喘息，竟把那一日的重负承得那样轻省，我永远无法明白。

我们两次被平交道口的栏门拦停，又一次停车加油，但这些耽搁都短得谈不上解乏。日落时分，我们在一条小溪旁歇下车来，单就我而言，已是感激不尽。我们欣然把头和肩膀扎进溪水；趁曼塞尔与我吃喝的当儿，乔治和仆人们擦净挡风玻璃，把车身揩洗冲刷了一遍——这绝非徒劳之举，因为尘土早让我们腻透，而车身重焕光彩的景象，令我们人人畅怀。

===Original===
Night came upon us soon after eight o’clock, but a fine moon was westering, and two and a half hours later we entered Austria.

At Salzburg we drove straight to the station, where Rowley lay, stretched on our luggage, fast asleep. It took but a moment to rouse him and lift the trunks into the cars, and I do not think this digression cost us five minutes of time.

So soon as we were clear of the city, a halt was made, and Mansel called the servants to listen to what he would say.

“You all know what has happened,” he said. “Mrs. Pleydell has disappeared. Everything points to abduction﻿—by no ordinary gang. As one of the family, I should in any event have done what I could: but, with Captain Pleydell out of action, it’s up to me to take charge. Very well. Here’s my first order. Discuss this business with no one. We’re certainly going to friends, but we’re going to enemies too, *and the latter are already in touch*. I’ve had two letters already warning me not to proceed.

===Chinese===
八点刚过，夜便罩了下来，但一轮好月正向西沉，两个半钟头之后，我们进入了奥地利。

到萨尔茨堡，我们径直开往车站：罗利正摊开四肢躺在我们的行李上，睡得香甜。唤醒他、把箱子搬上车不过片刻工夫，我想这一段岔出去的路统共没费我们五分钟。

一出城，我们便停了车，曼塞尔把仆人们唤来听他讲话。

「你们都知道出了什么事，」他说。「普莱德尔夫人失踪了。种种迹象都指向劫持——而且绝非寻常匪帮。作为这个家庭的一员，无论如何我总要尽力而为：可普莱德尔上尉如今动弹不得，主持大局便责无旁贷，落在我身上。很好。这是我的第一道命令：此事不得与任何人谈论。我们此去固然是见朋友，却也是见敌人，*而且后者已经搭上了线*。我已收到两封信，警告我不得前行。」

===Original===
“We’re going to drop Rowley at Villach, where he will go to bed. Tomorrow he will take four bedrooms and unpack all our things. And he may as well order supper, for we shall surely sleep there tomorrow night.

“The rest of us go to Poganec, and, whilst we are there, Carson and Bell will stay with and sleep in the cars.”

Then he told Carson to drive and called for Tester, and a moment later we were moving again.

===Chinese===
「我们要把罗利放在菲拉赫，他先上床歇着。明天他去订四间卧室，把我们所有的行装开箱安顿好。他不妨再吩咐下晚饭，因为明晚我们必定宿在那里。」

「其余的人去波加内克（Poganec）；我们在那儿期间，卡森和贝尔守着车，睡也睡在车里。」

然后他吩咐卡森驾车，又唤了特斯特，片刻之后我们重新上路。

===Original===
All of us knew the way now, so there was no thought of leading or being led, and though I remember little﻿—for Hanbury was driving and I slept most of the way﻿—I know that we stopped at Villach, to find the inn shut, that the landlord looked out of the window, with an old-fashioned cap on his head and that, when he saw who it was, he began to cry to his household that at last his luck had turned, for the best guests he ever had had were come again.

We were gone, however, before he had opened the door, leaving Rowley in the midst of the luggage, with his hand to his hat.

We were now in most handsome country, very mountainous and closely wooded, with streams and pastures lying in every dale: the air was most soft and rich, and plainly suited the engines, for the cars sailed over the hills like giants refreshed.

We swept through St. Martin, to hear the church clock strike one, and ten minutes later sighted a long low house, lying in the lap of a meadow, with rising woods behind.

This was Poganec: and I well remember remarking how fantastic a picture it made, for the moonlight was all about it and all its windows were open and lights burning in every room.

===Chinese===
如今人人都认得路，也就无所谓谁领谁随了；虽然我记得的不多——一路是汉伯里驾车，我大半程都在睡——但我记得我们在菲拉赫停了停，见旅店已经闭门，店主头上戴一顶老式便帽，从窗里往外张望，待看清来的是谁，便开始朝家里人大呼小叫，说他的运气到底转过来了，因为他这辈子接待过的最好的客人又来了。

然而，不等他开门，我们已绝尘而去，把罗利撂在行李堆中，一手扶帽，行着礼。

我们此刻置身一片极美的乡野，重峦叠嶂，密林覆被，每道山谷里都卧着溪流与草场：空气温软丰腴，显然也合发动机的脾胃，因为两部车翻越山冈，竟如重振过精神的巨人。

我们掠过圣马丁（St. Martin），听教堂的钟敲了一点；十分钟后，望见一幢低矮的长屋，卧在一片草场的怀抱里，背后是渐次升起的树林。

这便是波加内克了：我清楚地记得自己当时惊叹，这景象是何等奇幻的一幅图画——月光遍洒于它四周，所有窗户都敞着，间间屋里灯烛荧然。

---

===Original===
It was, I suppose, ten minutes before Mansel came to a window upon the first floor.

“Will you two come up?” he said.

The front door was open, and Hanbury and I passed in.

Major Pleydell met us at the top of the stairs and brought us into a room at the back of the house.

Daphne Pleydell rose to greet us, and her brother propped with pillows, called to us from the bed. Beside him sat Mansel.

===Chinese===
大约过了十分钟，曼塞尔才来到二楼的一扇窗前。

「你们两位上来吧？」他说。

前门敞着，汉伯里与我走了进去。

普莱德尔少校在楼梯顶上迎住我们，把我们领进宅子后部的一间屋子。

达夫妮·普莱德尔起身相迎；她弟弟垫着靠枕，在床上向我们招呼。他身旁坐着曼塞尔。

===Original===
As I went to take his hand﻿—

“Jonah can drive, can’t he?” he said with a smile.

Before I could answer﻿—for I had some sort of condolence upon my tongue﻿—he began to speak of the cars, reciting their qualities with judgment and remembering runs he had taken against the clock and, so, making smooth an encounter which I had been dreading more than I care to admit.

“I’ve often followed Jonah,” he concluded, “but I’ve never had to chase him when he had his whip out and, to be perfectly honest, I hope I never shall.” He laughed lightly. “And now sit down, you two﻿—anywhere except on my leg﻿—and Berry will bring you some beer. I think you’ve earned it.”

===Chinese===
当我上前去握他的手——

「约拿很会开车，不是么？」他含笑道。

不等我回答——我舌尖上原备着几句慰唁——他便谈起汽车来，颇有见地地历数它们的优点，回忆他从前掐着表跑过的几趟路，就这样，把一场我羞于承认有多畏惧的会面，熨帖得平平顺顺。

「我常跟在约拿后面跑，」他末了说，「可他从没让我追得这么狠——他连鞭子都甩出来了。老实说，我盼着永远不必再追。」他轻轻一笑。「好了，你们两位坐——哪儿都坐得，独我这条腿坐不得——贝里会给你们端啤酒来。我想你们是挣来的。」

===Original===
I tried to reply, but the words stuck in my throat.

I could not cope with such bravery. The man was jesting: his eyes were steady and his speech was firm, but the thick, dark hair I remembered was white as snow.

As Major Pleydell came forward, with a glass in his hand﻿—

“And now to business,” he said.

===Chinese===
我想要应答，话却哽在喉头。

这样的勇气，我招架不住。这个人是在打趣：他目光安稳，言语坚实，可我记得的那头浓密黑发，已白得像雪。

普莱德尔少校手里拿着一只杯子，走上前来——

「现在谈正事，」他说。

===Original===
“Today is Thursday. At seven on Monday morning Adèle went out for a ride. She rode in the direction of Sava, a village ten miles away. Her mare came in on her own at a quarter past eight. Everyone turned out at once, but found nothing. The mare was clean and unhurt. Inquiries were made at all farms in the direction of Sava. They proved fruitless. Nobody saw Adèle on Monday morning. The day was hot, and she was wearing breeches and boots, a white silk shirt and gloves. She had no hat on.

“Early on Tuesday morning Fitch found one of her gloves. It was lying by the side of the road almost exactly eleven miles from here, but not in the direction of Sava.

“Well, there you are. I’m afraid I’ve no more to say.”

===Chinese===
「今天是星期四。星期一早上七点，阿黛尔骑马出门，朝萨瓦（Sava）方向去了——那是个十英里外的村子。八点一刻，她的母马独自跑了回来。众人即刻出动，却一无所获。母马身上干净，没有伤。朝萨瓦方向的所有农家都问遍了，毫无结果。谁也没在星期一早上见过阿黛尔。那天天气很热，她穿马裤马靴，白绸衬衫，戴着手套，头上没戴帽子。

「星期二一大早，菲奇拾到她的一只手套。手套躺在路边，离这儿几乎不多不少十一英里，却不在萨瓦的方向。

「喏，情形就是这样。恐怕我再没有可说的了。」

===Original===
There was a little silence, and presently Mansel spoke.

“You’ve had no demand for money?”

His cousin looked away.

“None,” he said quietly. “I’ve got the money ready: it’s all I’ve been able to do.” He stretched out a trembling hand. “There’s five thousand pounds worth of notes in that chest of drawers. They’ve only to come and ask. But they﻿—they don’t do that.”

===Chinese===
短短一阵沉默过后，曼塞尔开了口。

「你们没有收到索钱的勒索？」

他那位堂兄把目光移开了。

「没有，」他轻声说。「钱我已备好了：这是我能做到的全部。」他伸出一只发颤的手。「那口五斗柜里放着五千镑票子的钞票。他们只消来开口。可他们——他们不来。」

===Original===
There was something so dark about the way he said this as took us all three by surprise: and this, I suppose, he was expecting, for he let out the ghost of a laugh.

“I know,” he said. “I didn’t get it at first. You know, I made quite certain that they were out for money. *It never occurred to me that Adèle had been taken for herself.*”

Mansel started to his feet.

“I don’t believe it,” he cried.

===Chinese===
他说这话的神情里有种说不出的阴郁，把我们三个都惊住了；我猜想他正料着这一手，因为他发出了一声幽魂似的轻笑。

「我知道，」他说。「起初我也不明白。你们晓得，我原拿定了他们是为钱而来。*我压根没有想到，掳走阿黛尔，为的是她本人。*」

曼塞尔霍地站起身。

「我不信，」他喊道。

===Original===
“I know,” said the other calmly. “Neither did I. But you’ll come round in the end and face the facts﻿—when the days go by and you don’t find her, but no demand is made; when the weeks go by and I’m crawling round with a stick and people are forgetting what she looked like﻿—”

“Oh, Boy, Boy,” wailed Daphne.

“﻿—but no demand is made; when﻿—”

“Never,” cried Mansel. “Never.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，」对方平静地说。「我原先也不信。可你终归会想通，面对事实——等日子一天天过去，你找不到她，勒索却无一字来；等一星期一星期过去，我拄着手杖四处爬，人们都在淡忘她的模样——」

「哦，小子，小子，」达夫妮哀声道。

「——可勒索无一字来；等——」

「绝不会，」曼塞尔喊道。「绝不会。」

===Original===
“Then, why don’t they ask?” said the other, with the sweat running down his face.

“They will,” said Mansel.

The other shook his head.

“What about the police?” he said suddenly. “Why don’t you want them called in?”

===Chinese===
「那么，他们为什么不开口？」对方说，汗珠顺着他的脸往下淌。

「他们会的，」曼塞尔说。

对方摇了摇头。

「警察呢？」他突然说。「你为什么不愿让他们插手？」

===Original===
“Because I want my hands free. If battle and murder will help, I’m out to do both: but I can’t do either, if I’ve got to apply for a warrant before I can force a door. We’re six men armed: we know how to work together; and we’re not afraid of lying out in the rain. Call in the police and you put us out of court.”

“That’s right,” said his cousin, nodding. “The police wouldn’t do any good.” He lay back and closed his eyes. “But, as they’re not out for money, neither will you.”

“I know they’re out for money,” said Mansel.

“Face the facts,” said his cousin. “Seventy hours since they took her, and no demand. Face the facts, Jonah: and, when you’ve had a good look at them, come back and try to tell me not to turn my face to the wall.”

===Chinese===
「因为我需要一双放开的手。若是战斗与杀戮帮得上忙，这两样我都决意去干：可若每撞一扇门都得先去申请一张拘票，那我便一样也干不成。我们是六个带武器的人：我们知道如何协同行事；也不怕露天宿在雨里。把警察叫来，我们便全无用武之地了。」

「说得是，」他的堂兄点头道。「警察帮不上忙。」他向后躺下，阖上眼。「可他们既不为钱而来，你们也一样无能为力。」

「我知道他们就是为钱而来，」曼塞尔说。

「面对事实吧，」他的堂兄说。「她被掳走至今七十个钟头，勒索无一字。面对事实吧，约拿：等你看清了它们的全貌，再回来劝我打消把脸冲着墙等死的念头。」

===Original===
There was a dreadful silence, broken only by Daphne’s sobs.

At last her brother called her and asked for some drug.

While she was pleading with him, we others stole out of the room.

I was confounded by this perversity of Fortune and I dared not look at Mansel, for here, at the outset of our venture, he was faced with a choice of two evils, neither of which, it seemed, he could possibly accept.

===Chinese===
一阵可怕的静默，只有达夫妮的呜咽将它打破。

末了，她弟弟唤她过去，讨一点药。

趁她劝慰他的工夫，我们其余的人悄悄溜出了房间。

命运这般乖张，叫我茫然失措；我不敢看曼塞尔，因为我们这番事业才启程，他便面临两害相权的选择，而无论哪一害，看来他都万万不能接受。

===Original===
To conceal the truth from his cousin was to withhold water from a dying man: yet how could he ever allow that he, *and not her husband*, had been offered the lady back? Explain it as he would, the fact must arouse misgivings in the steadiest mind: to a man in his cousin’s condition, it would be plain poison.

Major Pleydell was speaking.

“If they don’t make a demand, he’ll lose his mind. He hasn’t slept since she went. His hair was grey on Tuesday, and yesterday it was white. If they’d only hold her to ransom, he wouldn’t care. If they asked a million, he wouldn’t care a damn. We might not be able to pay it, but he’d know she was safe and sound. But when the hours go by and there’s no demand﻿—”

===Chinese===
对他的堂兄隐瞒实情，无异于扣住清水不给垂死之人：可他又怎能承认，对方提出奉还夫人的对象是他，*而不是她的丈夫*？任他怎样解释，这一事实都必在最沉稳的心里搅起疑云：对他堂兄眼下那种心境的人，这分明是毒药。

普莱德尔少校又说话了。

「他们若再不提勒索，他就要疯的。自她走后他没合过眼。星期二他的头发还是灰的，昨天已经白了。只要他们肯拿她勒赎，他倒不在乎。就算要一百万，他也毫不在乎。我们兴许付不出这笔钱，可他会知道她安然无恙。可眼见钟点一个个过去，勒索却杳无音信——」

===Original===
“Where have you looked?” said Mansel.

The other stared at him.

“Looked?” he said.

“D’you mean to say,” cried Mansel, “that you’ve been waiting for it to come by post? Why, man alive, that’s the last agency they’ll use! *Haven’t you even looked in the box in the gate?*”

===Chinese===
「你们往哪里找过？」曼塞尔说。

对方直瞪着他。

「找？」他说。

「你该不会是说，」曼塞尔嚷道，「你们一直坐等它从邮局寄来？哎呀，老兄，那是他们最不肯用的法子！*大门里的信箱，你们难道连看都没看过？*」

===Original===
The front door was open and we pelted out of the hall.

Mansel cried to Hanbury and me to bring some tools and a torch and, in spite of his limp﻿—for he was lame of a wound he had had in the War﻿—ran well ahead of his cousin down the drive.

It was seldom that he took some action which I could not understand: but now I was bewildered, for I knew as well as did he that the box in the gate would be as bare as my hand. Still there was no time to think, and, Hanbury being gone with the tools, I plucked a torch out of a pocket and ran in his wake.

The box was a doll’s-house business, cut out of the gatepost itself, with a slit for letters before and a little iron door behind. No doubt it was meant to be used in days gone by, for a man on horseback could reach the slit from the road, but the door had not been undone for many a year, although there was nothing to show this upon the other side.

===Chinese===
前门还敞着，我们一窝蜂冲出了门厅。

曼塞尔喊汉伯里和我带上工具和一支手电，顾不得自己的跛行——他一条腿落了大战时的枪伤——沿车道把他堂兄远远甩在了身后。

他难得做出令我无从索解的举动：可这一回我懵住了，因为我和他一样清楚，门上的信箱必定空空如也。然而不容细想，汉伯里已带着工具去了，我便从口袋里摸出一支手电，跟在他身后跑。

那信箱是桩娃娃屋式的玩意儿，就着门柱本身凿成，正面开一道投信的窄缝，背后扇一扇小铁门。无疑它是给往昔年月预备的，骑马的人自路上便够得着那道缝；小铁门则多年不曾开过，尽管从另一面看不出这层。

===Original===
I held the torch, while Mansel played with the chisel and presently forced the lock.

As he wrenched the little door open, Major Pleydell thrust in his hand.

When he drew out a folded paper, I could hardly believe my eyes.

===Chinese===
我举着手电，曼塞尔摆弄凿子，不多时便撬开了锁。

他猛地扳开小铁门，普莱德尔少校一把将手探了进去。

待他抽出一张折起的纸，我几乎不敢相信自己的眼睛。

===Original===
> The stolen goods will be returned on the receipt by the Manager of the —﻿—﻿— Bank, Zurich, of your cheque for five hundred thousand pounds. This sum you can raise, if you please. No time should be wasted, for the goods are perishable.
> August 30th.

===Chinese===
> 被盗货物将于苏黎世—— ——银行经理收到贵方五十万镑支票之际奉还。此款贵方如愿，自可筹措。切勿迁延时日，因货物易腐。
> 八月三十日。

===Original===
Before my wits were in order, Major Pleydell was well up the drive, shouting for Daphne and crying aloud his news.

“It’s come!” he bellowed. “It’s come! It’s been here since Monday. The demand.﻿ ﻿…”

As we walked after him﻿—

“Learn of me,” said Mansel. “*Never burn anything.*”

===Chinese===
我神魂未定，普莱德尔少校已奔上车道老远，高声喊着达夫妮，把消息嚷了出来。

「来了！」他吼道。「来了！打星期一起它就搁在这儿。勒索信。……」

我们跟在他身后走——

「跟我学吧，」曼塞尔说。「*任何东西，永远不要烧掉。*」

===Original===
When we came to the house, he called a footman to serve us with food and drink and himself went up to the chamber where Captain Pleydell lay.

Ten minutes later he returned, to say that the latter was asleep.

===Chinese===
回到宅子里，他唤一名男仆伺候我们饮食，自己上楼去了普莱德尔上尉躺着的那间寝室。

十分钟后他回来说，后者已经睡下了。

---

===Original===
A brief council was held the next morning at a quarter to twelve, and the moment I entered the bedroom I saw with half an eye that the patient was a new man. He spoke with eagerness, and the grey look was out of his face. His sister sat beside him, with shining eyes.

There was no argument, and everything went our way.

All were agreed that the sum demanded was fantastic and that the letter be ignored: and, when Mansel said that we should leave after lunch and should not return, Daphne began to protest, but the sick man inclined his head.

“I thought that was coming,” he said. “You were ever a dark horse, Jonah, and dark horses train alone. But tell me where I can find you and send me word when you can.”

===Chinese===
第二天上午十一点一刻，开了一个简短的会议；我一进寝室便一眼看出，病人已判若两人。他说话急切，脸上的灰败褪尽了。他妹妹坐在旁边，双眼发亮。

没有争执，事事都依了我们的主张。

众口一词：索要的金额荒诞不经，那封信理应置之不理；曼塞尔说我们午饭后便走、不再回来，达夫妮刚要抗议，病人却点了头。

「我料到会有这一出，」他说。「你从来是匹埋得深的黑马，约拿，而黑马总是单独训练的。不过告诉我，到哪里寻得着你，一有可能便捎信给我。」

===Original===
“That I will do without fail.”

It was then decided that the Pleydells should take no action, whatever befell, without communicating with us, that they should accept no message as coming from us unless one of us had brought it, but should detain the messenger; and that we should recognize no one as coming from them but Major Pleydell or one of the servants we knew.

And, so far as I can remember, that was all.

Mansel had already ridden most of the way to Sava and had driven with Major Pleydell to the spot where the glove had been found: he had also seen and talked with all the servants employed about the farm; “and, since,” he said, rising, “I believe them all to be honest, though painfully unobservant, this is where we come in.”

===Chinese===
「这事我必办到，绝无差池。」

随即议定：普莱德尔一家人无论出何变故，不先与我们通气，不得有任何举动；任何口信，除非由我们中一人亲自带来，一概不得当作我们所致，且须扣下传信之人；而来自他们的消息，除普莱德尔少校或我们认识的仆人外，我们一概不认。

就我记忆所及，议程便到此为止。

曼塞尔已经骑马走过去萨瓦的大半路程，又同普莱德尔少校驱车到过拾获手套的地点；农庄上雇的仆人，他也一一见过谈过；「而既然，」他起身说道，「我相信他们个个诚实，只是观察力迟钝得叫人痛心，往下就该我们登场了。」

===Original===
Then he asked that Adèle’s dressing-case should be sent for and packed with such things as she would be glad to have. This we were to take with us wherever we went.

Daphne left to arrange this, and we bade Captain Pleydell “Goodbye.”

For a moment he held my hand.

“Adèle’s very lucky,” he said, “to have such good friends.”

===Chinese===
接着他吩咐把阿黛尔的梳妆箱取来，装上些她会乐于使用的东西。无论我们走到哪里，这箱子都要随身带着。

达夫妮去安排此事，我们向普莱德尔上尉道了「再见」。

他握住我的手，握了片刻。

「阿黛尔真有福气，」他说，「有这样的好朋友。」

===Original===
“Oh,” said I, “the boot’s on the other leg.”

“Sleep well,” said Hanbury.

Two minutes later the engines of the cars were running, and Hanbury and I were about to take our seats, when Mansel called us into a parlour and shut the door.

“It is inconceivable,” he said, “that Adèle was taken by chance. Her movements had been watched for some time. Let me go further. They watched her ride out on Monday and met her six miles away.

===Chinese===
「哦，」我说，「这话该倒过来说。」

「好生安睡，」汉伯里说。

两分钟后，两部车的引擎都已转起来，汉伯里与我正要各就各位，曼塞尔却把我们唤进一间小客厅，掩上了门。

「难以想象，」他说，「阿黛尔是碰巧被掳的。她的行踪已被盯了些时日。让我更进一步说。星期一，他们看着她骑马出门，在六英里外迎住了她。」

===Original===
“Now no one was seen near Poganec, with or without a car: it follows that their observation post was distant, yet close to a road, so that once they had seen her ride out and the way she went, they could instantly move to meet her﻿—six miles away. Very well. Now turn to the window and lift up your eyes.”

We did so, to see the breadth of a valley, as fresh and green as you please, and, beyond, a press of high hills, rising up very sudden and wooded cap-à-pie. These lay, I afterwards found, four miles away. High up in their midst rose a fountain that fell by leaps and rushes down to the valley below, a considerable head of water, for the trees could not hide it, and, from where we stood, I could see its unbroken length.

“Can you see the bridge?” said Mansel. “A fifth of the way down the fall.”

===Chinese===
「而波加内克左近，无人见过任何人影，带车不带车都一样：由此可见，他们的瞭望点离得远，却又紧傍着一条大路，如此，一见她骑马出门、认明去向，他们便能立刻出动迎截——在六英里外。很好。现在转向窗户，抬起眼来。」

我们依言望去，只见一整幅山谷的宽度，新鲜碧绿，无可挑剔；再往外，一群高山拔地而起，从头到脚覆满林木——我后来得知，这些山离此四英里。群山正中高处涌起一道水柱，翻腾跳跃，泻向下方的山谷，水势颇为可观，因为林木也遮它不住；从我们立足之处，我能望见它一落到底、不见间断的全身。

「看得见那座桥吗？」曼塞尔说。「在瀑身下方五分之一处。」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Hanbury, and after a moment or two I made it out.

“Good,” said Mansel. “Now their observation post was somewhere about that bridge. A post in the woods would be useless, for the trees would get in the way; but that torrent commands Poganec; and a man sitting there with a glass could see anyone come and go. Then, again, that bridge serves a road to Sava﻿—the only road thereabouts.”

I took a step to the window, but Mansel stopped me at once.

“I hope and believe,” he said, “that they’re watching Poganec now. I mean, our movements must interest them no end. So don’t give them food for thought by looking straight into their eyes.

===Chinese===
「看得见，」汉伯里说；过了一两分钟，我也把它认了出来。

「好，」曼塞尔说。「他们的瞭望点，就在那座桥左近某处。设在林中的瞭望点没有用处，树木要碍事；而那道奔流正俯瞰着波加内克；有人坐在那里，拿一副望远镜，谁来谁往都尽收眼底。再者，那座桥连着一条去萨瓦的路——那一带唯一的路。」

我朝窗前跨了一步，曼塞尔立刻止住我。

「我希望并且相信，」他说，「他们此刻正盯着波加内克。我是说，我们的动静必定让他们关切到极点。所以别直勾勾望进他们眼里，白白送他们琢磨的资料。」

===Original===
“And now come and look at this map.

“Here’s Poganec, and there’s the bridge﻿—due South. We’re all going there at once. But Chandos and I are going to come up from the West, while Hanbury, Carson and Bell will drive from St. Martin to Sava and come from the East. We may find; we may draw blank; we may meet our friends by the way.” He turned to Hanbury. “Your way will be much the longer, so, if, when you come to the bridge, Chandos and I are not there, drive on round to St. Martin and thence to Villach. If you find anyone suspicious, detain him, but not by force: if he won’t be detained, follow him: drop Bell at the first crossroads, to put us wise: but, whatever you do, don’t lose him, for he’ll show us the way to Adèle.”

With that he put up the map, and two minutes later the cars were clear of the drive and were making towards St. Martin at a leisurely speed. Not until we parted were we to let them go.

===Chinese===
「现在来看这张地图。

「这里是波加内克，那边是桥——正南。我们全体即刻过去。但我和钱多斯从西边上，汉伯里、卡森和贝尔从圣马丁驱车去萨瓦，从东边上。我们或许有所获；或许扑个空；或许半路撞见我们的朋友。」他转向汉伯里。「你那条路要长得多，所以，若你到了桥边，我和钱多斯不在，便继续绕行到圣马丁，再从那里去菲拉赫。若遇上可疑的人，扣下他，但别动强：他不肯就范，便盯住他：到第一个岔路口放下贝尔，给我们报信：但无论做什么，别丢了此人，因为他会给我们指出通往阿黛尔的路。」

说罢他收起地图；两分钟后，两部车驶出车道，朝圣马丁从容行去。不到分道扬镳之处，我们不许它们放开脚步。

===Original===
“There’s a road on the left,” said Mansel, “somewhere just here.”

With his words the turning appeared, and, as we swung round, Hanbury flashed past our tail-lamp in a pother of dust.

Our road ran into the valley and lay in full view of the bridge, and, since anyone who was watching must now suspect our move, we went like lightning till we came to the foot of the hills and a pretty, white-walled hamlet where four roads met.

A woman, busy at a runnel, gave us good day.

===Chinese===
「左边有一条路，」曼塞尔说，「差不多就在这一带。」

话音未落，那转弯便出现了；我们车身一转，汉伯里已在一团尘雾中从我们的尾灯旁一闪而过。

我们的路通入山谷，全程袒露在桥的视界之内；既然任何在望的人此刻必已生疑，我们便闪电般疾驰，直到山脚下、四路交汇处一座白墙赏目的村庄。

一个妇人正在小溪边忙活，向我们道了日安。

===Original===
“I’m looking for some friends,” said Mansel. “Have you seen any car go by?”

“I only came in from the fields, sir, a quarter of an hour ago. But no car has passed since then.”

Mansel thanked her and immediately turned to the left.

Almost at once the road rose into the woods, doubling upon itself, like any serpent, and so beset with foliage as to afford no view of anything beyond a ribbon of sky.

===Chinese===
「我在寻几位朋友，」曼塞尔说。「你可曾见有汽车驶过？」

「先生，我一刻钟前才从地里回来。打那以后，没有车经过。」

曼塞尔谢过她，随即向左转去。

几乎立刻，路便升入林间，自我盘绕，活似一条长蛇，枝叶四合，密不透光，除了带子似的一线天，什么也望不见。

===Original===
For a while there was no sound at all, except the brush of our tires: but, after a little, we could hear the roar of water some distance away. This grew gradually louder, until it quite stopped our ears, so that we knew less than ever what each bend of the road might bring forth.

It was, I suppose, an ideal site for an ambush, for with every turn you entered an inner bailey of the wood, and, though upon such a score we had nothing to fear, I remember thinking that, where two men were at variance, the odds were on him who came first to such a place. Be that as it may, our ears being stopped, there was nothing to be done but watch: and this I know I did with a quick pulse, for the scene was set for a surprise as I have never known it.

It was soon evident that we were approaching the bridge, for the noise of the fall was thunderous and the pleasant smell of wet earth was unmistakable. Indeed, an instant later we saw the bridge not sixty paces away, and at half that distance a car in the midst of the road, with a man bent double beside it, trying to pull off a wheel.

He was so much engaged that we had stopped alongside before he knew we were there, and, when on a sudden he was aware of our presence, he gaped at us and the Rolls as at an apparition.

===Chinese===
有一阵子，除了轮胎的轻擦，万籁俱寂；但过了片刻，我们听得远处水声轰隆。它渐渐响起来，直到把两耳完全灌满，于是每一个路弯后头会转出什么，我们比先前愈发无从知晓。

我想，这是设伏的绝佳地势，因为每转过一个弯，你便深入树林又一重的内苑；尽管在这一点上我们无所畏惧，我记得自己当时想：两人相争，先抵此种地势者占尽先机。也罢，两耳既塞，除了留心看别无他法；而我知道，自己是脉息急促地看着的，因为这一幕俨然已为一场惊变布好了景，我平生从未见过这般阵仗。

很快便分明，我们在逼近那座桥：瀑声如雷，湿土的芬芳沁人，错认不得。果然，转瞬间我们便望见桥就在六十步外，而在一半的距离上，一辆车横在路心，一个男子在车旁伛偻着身子，正要卸一只车轮。

他干得那样专心，直到我们停在近旁，他才知道有人来了；待他猛然察觉我们的存在，便张着嘴瞪住我们和劳斯莱斯，像见了显形的鬼魂。

===Original===
Now this was not the way of a spy; and, indeed, it was easy to see that the fellow had nothing to do with those we sought. His cloth apart﻿—for he wore a clergyman’s habit﻿—one look at his face was enough. The man was a genial simpleton, in whom there was no guile. And I think Mansel would have gone by, for his hand went out to the brake, if the other’s delight to see us had been less manifest.

“You’re English,” he cried, twittering.

“Yes,” said Mansel. “And we were to meet a man here. By that bridge. But we’re late for our appointment, and I’m afraid he may have gone.”

“No one was there,” said the other, “ten minutes ago. And I’ve been there more than an hour. But he might have come since.”

===Chinese===
这可不是坐探的做派；而且，一看便知这家伙与我们寻的人毫无瓜葛。且莫论他那身衣裳——他穿的是教士服——只消朝他脸上望一眼便够了。此人是位和气到家的傻子，胸中不见半点心机。我想，若不是此人见到我们的欢喜表露得那样明白，曼塞尔本会径直驶过，因为他的手已经朝制动闸伸了过去。

「你们是英国人！」他尖声尖气地嚷道。

「是的，」曼塞尔说。「我们本约了人在此地碰头。就在那座桥边。只是我们赴约迟了，恐怕他已经走了。」

「十分钟前那儿没有人，」对方说。「我在那边待了一个多钟头。不过此后他也许来过。」

===Original===
With that, before we could stop him, he started to run to the bridge. We overtook him halfway, for, fool though he looked, it seemed prudent to be there first: but he only sought to step on to our running-board and, fouling the tool-chest, fell heavily into the road.

And there you have Hannibal Rouse, clerk in holy orders. He was, I think, the embodiment of that imaginary curate who has for years been the target of an unkind age. The man was futile. He was most garrulous, seldom said anything worth saying and laughed at everything he said: he was prodigal of energy, seldom did anything worth doing and bungled everything he did. I have never known anyone whose company was so distracting; and the patience with which Mansel endured it was more than human. Yet out of the fool came wisdom, and, but for this pelting idiot, I do not believe we should ever have traced Adèle.

I helped the man to his feet, and, since, in view of his report, there was nothing to be gained by proceeding, Mansel berthed the Rolls by the bridge and walked with us back to his car.

Rouse may be fairly judged by the fact that he was seeking to change a wheel without first raising his car by means of a jack: and, when, perceiving that the wheel was sound, we asked him why he wished to change it, he insisted that its tire was punctured and seemed dumbfounded to find it as tight as a drum. It presently emerged that another of his tires was flat and that he had confused the two. He was not at all abashed by these errors, but attributed them boldly to his being “no engineer.”

===Chinese===
话音未落，不等我们拦阻，他便撒腿向那座桥跑去。我们在半途赶上了他——尽管他看上去是个傻子，先到一步似乎更为稳妥：谁知他一心只想踏上我们的踏脚板，绊在工具箱上，重重跌进了路面。

诸位，这就是汉尼拔·劳斯（Hannibal Rouse），一位领受圣职的教士。我想，他是那位虚构副牧师的原身——多年来，那样一个人物一直是一个不厚道的时代取笑的靶子。此人一事无成。他饶舌之极，说值得一说的话的时候少，而每说一句都要笑上一场：他耗起劲来挥霍无度，做值得做的事的时候少，而每做一件都要砸锅。我从不知道谁的陪伴如此乱人心神；曼塞尔忍受他的那份耐心，超乎凡人。然而智慧偏从这个傻子身上生了出来，若没有这个十足的蠢货，我不信我们日后能寻到阿黛尔的踪迹。

我把这人扶起来；既然照他的话，前行已无可图，曼塞尔便把劳斯莱斯泊在桥边，同我们一起走回他的车那儿。

要公允评断劳斯其人，有一事足矣：他换车轮，竟不先用千斤顶把车顶起来。待我们看出那只轮子完好无损，问他为何要换，他咬定轮胎瘪了，及至发现它绷得像面鼓，还显得目瞪口呆。不多时水落石出：瘪的是他另一只轮胎，他把两只弄混了。对这些差错他毫不汗颜，反倒大大方方，归咎于自己「不是个工程师」。

===Original===
He then told us that he was touring and cared not where he went, but proposed to stay at Villach and prove the country round. This was ill news enough; but when, after staring upon Mansel, he presently addressed him by name and then, in an ecstasy of triumph, went on to remember White Ladies and how he had attended some fête there before the War, I know that I groaned in spirit and wished the man at the devil. But Mansel was perfectly civil, though something cold.

We had changed his wheel, in spite of his assistance, and were upon the point of leaving, when Hanbury arrived. This necessarily delayed our going and gave Rouse time to remember that he had a camera with him with which he must photograph us all. We protested that we could not wait, but, while we were still protesting, the thing was done. I confess that it did not delay us, for he took his picture as we reentered the cars, laughing the while like a maniac and promising to show us a proof.

“The man’s a scourge,” said I, as we sped back the way we had come. “He’ll make Villach untenable.”

===Chinese===
接着他告诉我们，他正在漫游，去哪儿全不介意，但打算在菲拉赫落脚，把四乡踏勘一番。这消息已够糟糕；可当他盯住曼塞尔瞧了一阵，忽然叫出他的名字，接着又在一阵得胜的狂喜里，记起了白女士庄（White Ladies），记起战前自己如何在那儿赶过一场游园会，我知道自己在心里呻吟了一声，只盼这人滚去见鬼。但曼塞尔礼数十足，只是有些冷。

我们顶着他的帮忙，把他的车轮换好，正要动身，汉伯里到了。这免不了又延宕了行程，也给了劳斯工夫，想起他随身带着一架照相机，非把我们全体拍下不可。我们声言再等不得了，可话音未落，事已办成。我承认那倒不曾耽误我们，因为他是趁我们重新上车时拍的照，一面笑得像个疯子，一面许诺给我们看样片。

「这人是个祸害，」我们循来路疾驰而回时，我说。「他会闹得菲拉赫没法住人。」

===Original===
Mansel shrugged his shoulders.

“I think we must suffer him,” he said. “He may be of use.”

“ ‘Of use?’ ” said I.

“Of use,” said Mansel.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔耸了耸肩。

「我想我们只好受着他，」他说。「或许有用。」

「『有用？』」我说。

「有用，」曼塞尔说。

===Original===
With that, he drove very fast to where Adèle’s glove had been found, and set us all to seeking some mark of a tire; “for here,” he said, “I am certain that she changed from one car to another and, while she did so, contrived to drop her glove, for they would have watched her too closely to let her throw it out as she went.”

In proof of this, he showed us where oil had been dripping on the edge of the grass by the road, as is sometimes the way of a car which is standing still.

“The relay was waiting,” said he, “half on and half off the grass, close up to the hedge. The other ran up alongside, and the transfer was made. And now we’ll all work in a line, searching the ground as they do on a dairy farm.”

We did so for more than an hour, but found nothing.

===Chinese===
说罢，他把车开得飞快，直奔拾获阿黛尔手套的地点，叫我们全体搜寻轮胎的印痕；「因为在这儿，」他说，「我确信她从一辆车换到了另一辆车；换车的当儿，她设法丢下了手套——他们盯她盯得太紧，不容她趁行车把手套丢出车外。」

为证明这一点，他指给我们看路边草沿上机油滴沥的痕迹——停驻不动的汽车，有时便有这副做派。

「接应的车在等候，」他说，「半在草上、半在草外，紧贴着树篱。另一辆车靠到旁边，人便这么换了过去。现在我们排成一线，像奶牛场上那样，一路搜过去。」

我们这样搜了一个多钟头，却一无所获。

===Original===
Then we took to the cars and drove very slowly west, now travelling together, now parting and presently meeting again, until by evening we had come to a great hog’s back, some fifty miles from the spot where the glove had lain.
