There we rested and watched the sun go down, and then Mansel led us to Villach as fast as he could.

There, more to my disgust than surprise, we found that Rouse was to lodge in the very same inn. Indeed, he announced his presence by springing out like a child from behind the parlour door, so that even Mansel was short with him, while I could have taken the fool and wrung his neck.

I will not dwell upon his follies, the tale of which was enough to make the angels weep, but merely record that for the next three days he continually invited violence, taking the most curious interest in all we did, pressing his company upon us whenever we were at hand and openly trying to follow us when we went forth.

This we did every morning at break of day, as best we could to search the country towards the west, for, to that quarter, we made sure, Adèle had been carried off. Mansel, who alone could speak German, visited the villages in turn, stopping at wayside inns and engaging in conversation men and women whose business kept them in sight of the roads. George or Carson or I went always with him. The others repaired to the hog’s back, from which four men with glasses could command a very great view: if ever a car was sighted it was carefully watched, and two would leave in pursuit, so soon as the line it was taking could be fairly presumed. But questionings and scrutinies alike bore us no fruit, for Mansel learned nothing of value, and the occupants of the cars, which were few, gave us no cause to doubt their honesty. Indeed, at the end of three days, we seemed to be no nearer Adèle than when we left Cleveland Row. In that time, however, we received a savage monition that, though we had no idea where Rose Noble was, he had his hand upon us and could, so to speak, twist our tail whenever he pleased.

===Chinese===
随后我们上车，极缓地向西而行，时而同行，时而分张，旋即又会合，到傍晚，来到一道高大的猪背岭（hog’s back）——离那只手套躺过的地方约五十英里。我们在那里歇息，看着太阳落山，然后曼塞尔领着我们，能多快便多快地赶回菲拉赫。

到了那里，我们发现劳斯竟要投宿在同一家旅店——与其说惊讶，毋宁说厌恶。他还真像个孩子似的，从客厅门后蹦出来宣告自己到场，连曼塞尔都对他简慢起来，而我简直想揪住这蠢货，拧断他的脖子。

他的种种蠢行，桩桩件件足以令天使落泪，我不去细表，只记一桩：此后三天，他无时无刻不在招人动粗——我们一在跟前，他便凑上来作陪；我们出门，他便公然试图尾随。

我们每天破晓出门，尽力向西搜索乡野，因为我们断定，阿黛尔正是朝那个方向被掳去的。唯独曼塞尔会讲德语，便挨村走访，在路旁旅店歇脚，同那些营生令他们守在大路视野之内的男男女女攀谈。乔治、卡森或我总有一人随行。其余的人上猪背岭去——从那里，四个人持着望远镜，可以掌控极开阔的视野：一旦望见汽车，便细细盯住，待它的去向可大致判定，便有两人出发追赶。然而问询与监视同样未结出果子：曼塞尔没打听到任何有价值的消息，而那些车——本就寥寥——车上的人也没让我们有半点可疑。说真的，三天下来，我们离阿黛尔，似乎并不比离开克利夫兰罗（Cleveland Row）那会儿更近。不过在这段日子里，我们收到一记凶狠的警告：虽然我们全然不知罗斯·诺布尔身在何处，他的手却已经按在我们身上，而且可以说，他高兴几时拧我们的尾巴，便能几时拧。

## III In Touch / 三 保持联络

===Original===
When we came in at nightfall, at the end of our second patrol, Rouse was still abroad in his car. For this relief we were thankful, for the evening before he had sat with us during our supper and, hungry though we were, had gone far to spoil the meal. Our respite, however, was short, for we had scarcely sat down before we heard him arrive, and a moment later he thrust his head into the room.

“Guess who I’ve seen,” he said archly.

No one vouchsafed any answer; but Tester spoke for us all, by leaving his cushion and passing beneath the bed.

“Mr. Wilberforce,” said Rouse triumphantly.

The name meant nothing to me, and a glance at Mansel and Hanbury showed that their case was the same.

At length﻿—

===Chinese===
夜幕降临时分，我们结束第二次巡逻归来，劳斯还驾车在外头。为此我们谢天谢地，因为前一天晚上，他坐在我们身边看我们用晚饭，我们虽然饥肠辘辘，那顿饭还是几乎被他败了个干净。可是这口喘息并不长久，因为我们才刚坐定，便听见他回来了，转眼间他的头已经探进房来。

「猜猜我见到了谁，」他狡黠地说。

谁也没有赏他一句回答；倒是特斯特代我们全体发了言——离开垫子，钻到床底下去了。

「威尔伯福斯先生（Wilberforce），」劳斯得意洋洋地说。

这名字对我毫无意义；朝曼塞尔和汉伯里瞥一眼，便知他们俩也不例外。

末了——

===Original===
“Who’s Wilberforce?” said Mansel wearily.

Rouse’s grin faded, and his eyes grew round with surprise. Then he came into the room.

“He﻿—he said he knew you,” he stammered. “He said he knew you quite well. I met him by the side of the road. He asked me the way to Salzburg, and then we got talking and, when I said you were at Villach, he asked how you were.”

“What was he like?” said Hanbury.

Rouse described some man I had never seen.

“When was this?” said Mansel.

===Chinese===
「威尔伯福斯是谁？」曼塞尔疲惫地说。

劳斯的嬉笑敛了下去，双眼惊得滚圆。随后他走进了房间。

「他——他说他认得你们，」他结结巴巴地说。「他说他跟你们相当熟。我是在路边遇上他的。他向我打听去萨尔茨堡的路，随后我们攀谈起来，等我说起你们在菲拉赫，他就问你们可好。」

「他生得什么模样？」汉伯里说。

劳斯描述了一个我从未见过的人。

「这是什么时候的事？」曼塞尔说。

===Original===
Rouse said about six o’clock.

“I think you must know him,” he added. “He said that he lived near Bournemouth and he asked all about you and how you were getting on. I said you were out a great deal and﻿—There now, I’ve left them in the car.”

As he turned to the door﻿—

“Left what in the car?” said I.

“The flowers, of course, stupid,” said Rouse. I could have choked, but Mansel and Hanbury began to shake with laughter. “I tell you he gave me some flowers. Carson will you be so good? The box in the car.”

===Chinese===
劳斯说，大约六点钟。

「我想你们一定认得他，」他补充道。「他说他住在伯恩茅斯一带，他把你们的事问了个遍，问你们过得怎么样。我说你们常常外出，还有——哎呀不好，我把它们落在车里了。」

他转身向门去——

「什么落在车里了？」我说。

「当然是花儿呀，傻瓜，」劳斯说。我简直要气噎，曼塞尔和汉伯里却抖开了笑。「我告诉你，他给了我一些花。卡森，劳你的驾？车里的那只盒子。」

===Original===
With a resigned look, Carson left the room.

“The man’s mad,” murmured Hanbury.

“No, he isn’t,” said Rouse excitedly. “He said you were a great gardener.”

“He said I was?” said Mansel.

“Oh, yes,” said Rouse. “I’ve no doubt about it at all. We were talking of horticulture and he said﻿—”

“You must have got the name wrong,” said Mansel. “I know nothing of gardening.”

===Chinese===
卡森一脸听天由命，出了房间。

「这人疯了，」汉伯里低声道。

「不，他没疯，」劳斯激动地说。「他说您是一位了不起的园艺家。」

「他说我是？」曼塞尔道。

「哦，是啊，」劳斯说。「这一点我毫不怀疑。我们正谈着园艺学，他就说——」

「你准是把名字听岔了，」曼塞尔说。「园艺的事我一窍不通。」

===Original===
“You must mean fishing,” said Hanbury.

“No, it must have been gardening,” said Rouse. “Why else should he give me the flowers?”

Here Carson appeared in the doorway, with a shallow, white cardboard box, fastened with string.

“There you are,” said Rouse, handing the box to Mansel, as though there were no more to be said. “He asked me to give them to you.”

“I’m sure you’re mistaken,” said Mansel, taking a knife. “I’m certainly fond of flowers, but﻿—”

“He cut those himself,” said Rouse, “and he said he could think of no one who would value them more than you. James Wilberforce, his name was, and, when I asked﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你准是想说钓鱼，」汉伯里说。

「不，准是园艺没错，」劳斯说。「不然他干吗给我花？」

正在这时，卡森出现在门口，捧着一只浅浅的白色纸盒，用细绳系着。

「喏，给您，」劳斯说着，把盒子递给曼塞尔，仿佛话已再无可说。「是他托我把这些交给您的。」

「我看你准是弄错了，」曼塞尔说着，取过一把刀。「我固然爱花，可是——」

「那些是他亲手剪下的，」劳斯说，「他还说，他想不出还有谁会比您更珍惜它们。詹姆斯·威尔伯福斯，他叫这个名字，等我问起——」

===Original===
“My God!” said Mansel, and Hanbury and I cried out.

The box was full of beautiful, soft, brown hair.

In an instant the room was in an uproar.

As I leapt to my feet, my head struck the electric light and put it out; but I saw George throw himself forward, as Rouse recoiled against the wall. There was a lamp by the bed, and I sought like a madman for the switch, while Tester was barking and George and Rouse were shouting and Mansel was calling George to order in a steady, metallic voice.

As the light went up﻿—

===Chinese===
「我的老天！」曼塞尔道，汉伯里与我失声惊呼。

盒子里盛满了美丽的、柔软的褐色头发。

转眼之间，满屋大乱。

我一跃而起，脑袋撞在电灯上，把它撞灭了；但我看见乔治向前猛扑，劳斯则退缩到墙根。床边有一盏灯，我疯了一般摸索它的开关，这边特斯特狂吠不止，那边乔治和劳斯大呼小叫，曼塞尔则用一种平稳而冷硬的声音，喝令乔治检点。

灯亮起来——

===Original===
“It’s beyond a joke,” said Rouse, painfully getting to his feet. “You might have hurt me very much. Supposing﻿—”

“How did you come by that box?” said Mansel.

“I tell you,” said Rouse, “he told me to give it to you. I understood they were flowers and that you﻿—”

“You say he was going to Salzburg?”

“I think so,” said Rouse, wincing. “He﻿—”

===Chinese===
「这可开不得玩笑，」劳斯忍着痛爬起来说。「你们差点儿把我伤得不轻。要是——」

「那只盒子，你是怎么到手的？」曼塞尔说。

「我跟你说了，」劳斯道，「是他叫我交给您的。我只当它们是花，还当您——」

「你说他要去萨尔茨堡？」

「我想是吧，」劳斯疼得皱眉道。「他——」

===Original===
“Please describe him again.”

Rouse gave the particulars, staring.

“And now describe his car.”

When he had done so, Mansel stepped to the door.

“I’m sorry for what’s happened,” he said. “The fault was not yours. And now please go. We’ve plans to make and rather a lot to discuss.”

===Chinese===
「请把他的模样再描述一遍。」

劳斯瞪着眼，把种种细节一一说了。

「现在再说说他的车。」

等他说完，曼塞尔举步向门口。

「方才的事我很抱歉，」他说。「过错不在你。现在请回吧。我们要定计划，还有不少事要商量。」

===Original===
Without a word, Rouse turned and limped from the room.

For a little, none of us spoke.

Then﻿—

“Do you believe him?” said Hanbury.

“Why not?” said Mansel. “If the enemy knows we’re here, you can bet he hasn’t missed Rouse. And there you are. I said Rouse might be of use, and I was right. He’s just been of use to Rose Noble. Perhaps tomorrow he’ll be of use to us.”

===Chinese===
劳斯一言不发，转身一瘸一拐地出了房间。

有好一会儿，我们谁也没作声。

接着——

「你信他的话吗？」汉伯里说。

「为什么不信？」曼塞尔说。「敌人既知道我们在这儿，你尽管打赌，他不会放过劳斯。这不就对了。我说过劳斯或许有用，我说对了。他方才刚好为罗斯·诺布尔效了一回劳。说不定明天，就该为我们效劳了。」

---


===Original===
My room and Mansel’s shared a small balcony, and that night Tester waked me by putting his nose on my arm. I was up in an instant, for the dog was plainly uneasy and I feared that something was wrong. As I passed barefoot to the window, I saw that the light was burning in Mansel’s room.﻿ ﻿…

Mansel was on his knees by the side of the bed, with one arm across his eyes, like a weeping child, and the other hand full of the curls which lay in a little heap in the midst of the counterpane.

For a long time he never moved, but at last he lifted his head and, when the dog came running, he picked him up in his arms.

I stole back to bed.

===Chinese===
我的房间和曼塞尔的房间共着一座小阳台。那天夜里，特斯特把鼻子拱上我的手臂，把我弄醒了。我即刻起身，因为这狗分明心神不定，我怕出了什么岔子。我赤着脚走向窗前时，看见曼塞尔房里灯还亮着。……

曼塞尔跪在床边，一条手臂横遮着眼睛，像个哭泣的孩子，另一只手里攥着一把发卷——那一小堆头发，还摊在床单正中央。

很久很久，他一动也不动，末了终于抬起头来；狗奔过去时，他把它抱进了怀里。

我悄悄溜回床上。

---


===Original===
The next day was Sunday.

Mansel started for Salzburg at seven o’clock, taking Carson with him, and the rest of us left for the hog’s back within the hour.

I ran into Rouse on the doorstep, as I was leaving the inn.

===Chinese===
第二天是星期天。

七点钟，曼塞尔带着卡森动身去萨尔茨堡；不出一个钟头，我们其余的人也上路去了猪背岭。

我走出旅店时，在门阶上撞见了劳斯。

===Original===
“I must beg your pardon,” he said, “for the way I spoke last night. It was very wrong of me. And I feel you were most justly provoked.” He laughed inanely. “I was very disappointed myself. But I do hope Captain Mansel won’t take it up with Mr. Wilberforce. After all, we’re all liable to make mistakes, and I’m sure he had no intention of playing a joke.”

At first I could make no reply.

At length﻿—

“That’s all right,” I said. “I’m afraid we were rather﻿—rather hasty. Supper’s a bad time, you know.”

With that, I rushed off, before he could clasp my hand.

===Chinese===
「我必须请求您的宽恕，」他说，「为我昨夜说话的态度。那实在是我的大错。而且我觉得，你们的动怒实在再正当不过。」他傻笑了一声。「我自己也失望得很。可我实在希望，曼塞尔上尉不要为这事去找威尔伯福斯先生计较。毕竟，人人都难免出错，我敢担保，他绝没有开玩笑的意思。」

起初我竟答不上话来。

末了——

「没事了，」我说。「恐怕我们当时有些——有些急躁。晚饭时分总是不妙的，你晓得。」

说罢我便一溜烟跑了，不给他抓住我手的机会。

===Original===
When I told Hanbury﻿—

“The man’s half-witted,” he said. “And I’m ashamed of myself for bringing him down. I don’t wonder Rose Noble hangs his hat on him. I think we should soon find Adèle, if *he* had a comic idiot round his neck.”

“Yet Mansel has hopes of him,” said I.

“As a hatstand,” said George. “That’s all.”

===Chinese===
我把这事讲给汉伯里听时——

「这人是个半傻子，」他说。「我竟把这么一位招到身边来，自己也觉汗颜。难怪罗斯·诺布尔要把帽子挂在他身上。我想，倘若*他*脖子上也吊着这么个滑稽的傻子，我们早该寻到阿黛尔了。」

「可曼塞尔还对他存着指望呢，」我说。

「当个帽架罢了，」乔治说。「仅此而已。」

===Original===
Not until ten that night did Mansel return to the inn.

We had come in at sundown, and Rouse had badgered us nearly out of our lives, for, remembering our offences of the night before, we felt constrained to be civil, and the fact that the day was Sunday seemed to entitle his cloth to consideration. Before retiring, he asked if he might read us the Gospel. We could hardly refuse, but I fear it did us no good and only enlarged the sympathy we felt for his flock.

Very soon after, we heard the sigh of the Rolls, and a moment later Mansel entered the room.

“Gone to bed?” he said, looking round.

===Chinese===
直到夜里十点，曼塞尔才回到旅店。

我们是日落归来的，劳斯把我们纠缠得几乎没了命：因为记着前一晚的冒犯，我们觉得非客气不可，而这一天又是星期天，似乎更叫他的教士身份有资格受些体恤。临睡之前，他问可否给我们读一段福音书。我们难以回绝，只是恐怕这于我们毫无益处，反倒把我们对他那群教民的同情又放大了一圈。

此后不久，我们听见劳斯莱斯的一声低吟，转眼间曼塞尔进了屋。

「都睡下了？」他环顾四周说。

===Original===
“Ten minutes ago,” said I, “by the grace of God.”

“Then you’re friends again,” said Mansel.

I told him how Rouse had spoken on the steps of the inn and what we had suffered since sundown at the hands of the fool.

“Good,” said he. “Any news?”

===Chinese===
「十分钟前，」我说，「托上帝的福。」

「那你们又跟他和好了，」曼塞尔说。

我告诉他，劳斯在旅店台阶上如何致了辞，日落以来，我们又在这蠢货手里吃了些什么苦头。

「好，」他说。「有消息吗？」

===Original===
“Devil a bit,” said I. “And you?”

“Salzburg’s no earthly,” says he. “Indeed, I was so sure of that that I’ve been at Poganec all day.” We opened our eyes. “But don’t tell Hannibal R.”

“He never dreamed you’d gone to Salzburg. If he had﻿—”

“Oh, yes, he did,” said Mansel, sitting down in a chair. “But he knew I should waste my time. I tell you, he’s pretty hot stuff, is Hannibal R. And you must admit he’s put up a wonderful show. Talk about sheep’s clothing.”

Hanbury was looking at me in a helpless way, and I fancy he found slight comfort in my expression, for he soon returned to Mansel, and I did the same.

===Chinese===
「半个字儿也没有，」我说。「你呢？」

「萨尔茨堡全然无用，」他说。「说真的，我对这一点把握到这般地步——一整天我都待在波加内克来着。」我们睁大了眼睛。「可别告诉汉尼拔·劳（Hannibal R.）。」

「他做梦也想不到你去过萨尔茨堡。要是想到了——」

「哦，不，他想到了，」曼塞尔说着，在一把椅子里坐下。「可他知道我只会白费工夫。我告诉你，汉尼拔·劳这家伙相当有料。而且你们必须承认，他这出戏演得漂亮极了。这才叫披着羊皮呢。」

汉伯里一筹莫展地望着我，我想他没能从我脸上寻到多少安慰，因为不多时他便又把心思转回曼塞尔身上，我也一样。

===Original===
“Last night,” said Mansel, “you asked me if I believed him. The true answer was ‘No’; and the whole truth, ‘I never did.’

“And now listen.

“The moment I saw him, I felt sure that he was our man. He was where I had expected to find him, and he couldn’t escape, because he had a flat tire. Yet in the first five minutes he played the fool so beautifully that *against my better judgment* I found my suspicions failing and I had to fight with myself to keep them alive. And then he made a mistake. It was a very slight one, and I don’t think he thought I saw. He did a thing that no clergyman that wears a round collar would ever do. He put up two hands, as though to straighten a tie.”

He paused there, to pour himself some ale: and, when he had drunk, he lighted a cigarette.

===Chinese===
「昨夜，」曼塞尔说，「你问过我信不信他。如实的回答是『不信』；而全部的真相，是『我从来就没信过』。

「现在听着。

「一望见他，我便确信，我们要找的正是此人。他在我料定能找到他的地方，而且脱不了身，因为他有一只轮胎瘪了。可是开场五分钟里，他把傻子演得那样精采，以致*违背了我清醒的判断*，我的疑心竟渐渐撑不住，我只得跟自己较劲，才把它勉强留住。随后他出了个破绽。极轻微的一个，我想他也没料到我看见了。他做了一件事，一件戴圆硬领的教士绝不会做的事：他抬起双手，仿佛要正一正领带。」

说到这里他停了停，给自己斟了些麦酒；喝过之后，又点上一支烟。

---


===Original===
“Well,” he continued, “the obvious thing to do was to play his game﻿—satisfy him that we didn’t suspect him at all. And that’s why I didn’t tell you. Your undisguised contempt for him has been simply invaluable. I should think he’s been revelling in it﻿—I know I have. By the way, I’m telling you now, because you won’t see him again﻿—at least, not upon the same terms.

“Now, first, what was his job? His job was to keep in touch. Rose Noble holds a fine hand, but he knows us too well to sleep sound when we’re out of his ken. So Rouse was to keep in touch﻿—an extremely difficult, job. We were six to one, you see, and ready to find a spy under every hat. So what does he do but step in *under our guard*? It was done in the War, you know: but only a born artist can bring it off. And Rouse is an old master.

“And now for his value to us. It may become imperative that a spy should return to his chief. Very well. If Rouse thought we suspected him, he’d never return, until he had shaken us off. Never. But, *now that he knows we don’t*, he’ll go when he thinks he’d better and never take the trouble to look behind. *And I think he’ll think he’d better before very long, for he’ll have some news for Rose Noble which will not wait.*

“And now, if you please, we shall all go to bed at once, for I’ve no idea when or where we shall sleep again.”

===Chinese===
「后来呢，」他接着说，「显而易见的对策，便是照他的戏码演下去——让他笃信我们对他毫无疑心。这便是我一直不告诉你们的缘故。你们对他那份不加掩饰的轻蔑，简直是无价之宝。我料想他一直泡在其中受用——我知道我自己受用得很。顺便说一句，我现在才告诉你们，是因为你们不会再见到他了——至少，不会以同样的身份再见。

「先说第一件：他的差事是什么？他的差事，就是保持联络。罗斯·诺布尔手里握着一副好牌，可他太了解我们，只要我们出了他的视野，他便睡不安稳。于是便由劳斯来保持联络——一桩难乎其难的差事。你要明白，我们是六个对他一个，而且随时打算从每顶帽子底下揪出个坐探来。可他偏偏就从*我们眼皮底下*登了堂、入了室。这种事，大战期间也有过：但唯有天生的艺术家才能奏功。而劳斯，是一位老大师。

「再说他对我们的价值。也许会有那么一刻，坐探非回他主子那里去不可。很好。倘若劳斯以为我们疑心他，他便绝不会回去，除非先把我们甩脱。绝不。可是*如今他既知道我们并不疑心*，他一觉得该走就走，再不肯费神朝后望一眼。*而且我想，要不了多久他就会觉得该走了，因为他手头有一条带给罗斯·诺布尔的消息，等也等不得。*

「好了，诸位请吧，我们这就通通上床，因为再往后何时何地还能睡觉，我心里一点数也没有。」

===Original===
I had slept very ill for three hours when I heard a car making like fury towards the inn. At once I sat up, for, in view of what Mansel had said, I fully expected that it was bound for our door; but it swept up the street, like a squall, and presently turning some corner, passed out of earshot.

A moment later, however, I heard it coming again.

Again it went by the inn, but stopped, with its brakes screaming, a score of paces away. Then the reverse gear was engaged, and the car shot back with a snarl, to come to rest under our windows, before the inn.

The next instant someone was pounding upon the front door.

===Chinese===
我睡得极糟，才睡了三个钟头，便听见一辆汽车疯也似的朝旅店驶来。我立刻坐起身，因为想到曼塞尔说过的话，我满心以为它是冲我们门口来的；可它像一阵狂风，卷起长街，不多时转过哪个街角，出了听得见的范围。

然而片刻之后，我听见它又回来了。

它再次驶过旅店，却在二十步开外刹住，刹车一路尖啸。接着倒挡挂上，那车咆哮着倒射回来，停在旅店门前、我们的窗下。

转眼间，已有人擂响前门。

===Original===
As I rushed to the window﻿—

“What is it, Berry?” said Mansel, from the balcony.

Major Pleydell stepped back on the pavement and threw up his head.

“*Jonah*,” he cried, “*she’s sent word. She’s got a message through.*”

“I’ll come down,” said Mansel.

===Chinese===
我冲到窗前——

「什么事，贝里？」曼塞尔在阳台上说。

普莱德尔少校退到人行道上，昂起了头。

「*约拿*，」他喊道，「*她递出话来了。她把信传出来了。*」

「我这就下来，」曼塞尔说。

===Original===
But George and I were before him and had the front door open, while he was still on the stairs.

For a moment the cousins spoke together: then Mansel turned to us.

“The man’s at Poganec,” he said. “We must go there at once.”

For all our haste, Mansel was ready before us, and I heard him call Carson and tell him to follow with Rowley and that Bell was to pack our light luggage and load the second Rolls.

Two minutes later we left in Major Pleydell’s car: Mansel was driving, while the chauffeur sat by his side.

===Chinese===
可乔治和我抢在他前头，趁他还在楼梯上，已把前门敞开。

表兄弟二人低语了片刻，随后曼塞尔转向我们。

「那人就在波加内克，」他说。「我们必须即刻赶去。」

尽管我们动作飞快，曼塞尔仍比我们先备办妥当；我听见他唤来卡森，吩咐他带着罗利随后跟来，又说由贝尔收拾我们的轻装行李，装上第二部劳斯莱斯。

两分钟后，我们乘普莱德尔少校的车出发了：曼塞尔驾车，司机坐在他身旁。

===Original===
We did not go to Poganec, but, instead, twenty miles to Crayern﻿—the first station after Villach, if you are travelling east.

Arrived there, Mansel descended, and, wondering what was to happen, we followed him out of the car.

To our surprise, he bade Major Pleydell “Goodbye.”

“And thanks very much,” he said. “You did it most awfully well.”

“I wish it had been true,” said his cousin.

===Chinese===
我们没有去波加内克，却往二十英里外的克拉耶恩（Crayern）去了——倘若你向东旅行，那是菲拉赫之后的第一站。

到了那里，曼塞尔下了车；我们不知将要发生什么，也跟着下了车。

令我们吃惊的是，他竟向普莱德尔少校道起「再见」来。

「还有，多谢之至，」他说。「你办得漂亮极了。」

「但愿那一切都是真的，」他表兄说。

===Original===
“So do I,” said Mansel. “So long.”

“Good night, you two,” said the other. George and I cried “Good night.”

Then the car slipped into the darkness and Mansel turned to us.

“You must forgive me,” he said. “I’ll never blind you again. And now let’s walk to the station, and talk as we go along.

“Rouse’s room’s above mine. I’m sure the car must have waked him, and I’m sure he heard what was said. *She’s got a message through.* And that’s the ‘news for Rose Noble which will not wait.’

===Chinese===
「我也但愿，」曼塞尔说。「回见。」

「你们两个，晚安，」另一位说。乔治与我应声「晚安」。

随后那辆车滑入黑暗，曼塞尔转向我们。

「你们得原谅我，」他说。「我往后再也不蒙你们了。现在我们往车站走吧，一路走一路说。

「劳斯的房间就在我房间楼上。我敢肯定，那辆车必定惊醒了他，我也敢肯定，方才的话他都听见了。*她把信传出来了。*这便是那条『带给罗斯·诺布尔、等也等不得的消息』。

===Original===
“Very well. What will Rouse do? He’ll clear out at once. Well, that’s all right, but I don’t think his car will start. He’ll try to locate the trouble, and so will Bell﻿—they’re probably sweating blood now﻿—but they’ll only waste their time. And so Brother Rouse will have to take to the train. I think it more than likely that Bell will drive him to the station, for an Innsbruck train leaves Villach at a quarter to three. The same train leaves Crayern at a quarter past two, that is to say, in exactly ten minutes’ time. So, if we’re quick and that’s the station ahead, we four shall travel together as far as Rouse wants to go.

“Now it’s all very well to make plans for somebody else. I think Rouse will start right away and I think he will take this train. But Rowley will be on the platform to see if he does. If he doesn’t, look out for a flash from Rowley’s torch. If, as the train moves out, we see a flash, we leave the train at the next station: and there we shall find Carson waiting to bring us home. If we don’t get out, he’ll follow the train along, calling at every station until he finds one of us waiting to pick him up.

“Well, there you are. I’ve set my trap and I’ve tried to look ahead. I’d have given a very great deal to have your counsel, but I dared not tell you that Rouse was Rose Noble’s man.”

===Chinese===
「很好。劳斯会怎么做？他会立刻开溜。那没关系，只是他的车，我看发不动。他会去排查故障，贝尔也会——这会儿他们多半正急得血汗直流——可他们只会白费工夫。于是劳斯弟兄只好改乘火车。我想，十之八九是贝尔开车送他去车站，因为一趟开往因斯布鲁克的火车，三点差一刻离开菲拉赫。同一班车两点过一刻离开克拉耶恩——也就是说，恰恰在十分钟之后。所以，只要我们腿脚快、前头那座建筑就是车站，我们四个便可结伴同行，劳斯要去多远，便同到多远。

「替别人作盘算，原是说来容易。我想劳斯立刻就会动身，也想着就是这一班车。但罗利会守在站台上，看他上没上车。他若没上——留神罗利手电的一道闪光。火车出站时我们若见到闪光，便在下一站下车：卡森会在那里等着，接我们回家。我们若不下车，他便沿着铁路一路跟着火车，逢站必停，直到发现我们中有一人候着，把他捎上。

「喏，局面就是这样。陷阱我设下了，前路也尽力料到了。原该不惜重价换你们两位的参谋，只是我不敢让你们知道，劳斯是罗斯·诺布尔的人。」

===Original===
“Thank God you didn’t,” said Hanbury. “I couldn’t have played the hand to save my life.”

“I covet his nerve,” said I.

“You may well do that,” said Mansel. “Look at that﻿—those flowers. Of course it made you suspect him, but he had you back in your place by eight the next day.”

By now we had come to the station, and ten minutes later we had our tickets for Innsbruck and were aboard the train.

===Chinese===
「谢天谢地你没说，」汉伯里道。「要靠我把这手牌打下去，拼了命也打不来。」

「我倒眼红他的胆量，」我说。

「你尽可以眼红，」曼塞尔说。「瞧瞧那个——那些花儿。那固然叫你们起了疑心，可到了第二天八点，他已经把你们个个安回了原位。」

这时我们已到车站，十分钟后，去因斯布鲁克的车票到了手，人也上了车。

===Original===
As luck would have it, there was a sleeping car; and, as this was nearly empty, we were able to buy a compartment which held three beds. Comfort apart, this would save us no end of trouble, for now we could lock ourselves in and put out the light. To insure against interference, we told the attendant to make our beds at once and on no account to disturb us till Innsbruck was reached.

He was a talkative fellow and proud of what English he had, and I was soon in a fever lest we should come to Villach before the beds were made: indeed, as the minutes went by, I could hardly sit still, but at last he had set all to his liking and wished us good night.

Two minutes later the train began to slow down.

===Chinese===
侥天之幸，车上挂着一节卧铺车；而且几乎是空的，我们便包下了一个带三张铺位的包厢。舒适且不论，这还替我们省却无穷的麻烦，因为这样一来便可闩门熄灯。为确保不受打扰，我们吩咐列车员立刻铺床，而且在抵达因斯布鲁克之前，无论如何不得来搅扰我们。

他是个健谈的家伙，又为自己会的那几句英文沾沾自喜；我很快便心急如焚，生怕床还没铺好就到了菲拉赫：说真的，随着一分钟一分钟过去，我几乎坐不安席，好在末了他终于把一切安顿得称心如意，向我们道了晚安。

两分钟后，火车开始减速。

===Original===
“Draw the blinds,” said Mansel, “because of the station lamps.”

When we had done so, he took his stand by the window and Hanbury put out the light.

As the train drew into the station, we stood as still as death, and to this day I remember every sound.

From the resonance of all noise, I judged the station empty, except for those on duty and a handful of freight. There was no haste or clamour, and, if passengers came or went, they gave no sign. After a little, a gong clanged five or six times, and a moment later somebody slammed a door. Then the guard, I suppose, wound a horn, the engine whistled in answer and the train began to move.

===Chinese===
「把窗帘放下来，」曼塞尔说，「防着站台的灯。」

我们照办之后，他守定在窗边，汉伯里熄了灯。

火车进站时，我们站得如死一般静，直到今天，那里的每一声响我都记得。

从种种声响的回音判断，车站是空的，只有值班的人和寥寥几车货物。没有匆促，没有喧哗；即便有乘客上下，也不露半点声息。过了一小会儿，一面锣铛铛敲了五六下，随即有人砰地摔上一扇门。接着，我猜是列车员，吹响一声号角，机车应了一声汽笛，火车便动了。

===Original===
As Mansel was lifting the blind﻿—

“I must be alone,” said Rouse.

I think we all started.

“Orright, orright,” said the attendant.

The voices came from the corridor outside our door.

Mansel stood like a statue, with the blind half raised in his hand.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔正掀起窗帘——

「我得单要一间，」劳斯说。

我想我们全都惊跳了一下。

「好咧，好咧，」列车员说。

声音来自我们门外的大过道。

曼塞尔如雕像般立着，窗帘半掀在手里。

===Original===
“Well, get a move on,” said Rouse. “I’m pretty tired. *What time do we get to Lass?*”

Then the two passed on down the passage, and we could hear no more.

It was safe enough now to move and to switch on the light: yet we did neither, but continued to stand breathless, each of us busy with his thoughts.

For those of Hanbury and Mansel I cannot answer, but I know that I was thinking neither of what was before us nor of the secret which Mansel had so brilliantly won. I was thinking how the tone of a man’s voice may show the colour of his heart: for the voice was Rouse’s voice, but the tone was that of a harsh and evil man.

===Chinese===
「行了，麻利点儿，」劳斯说。「我累得很。*咱们几点到拉斯（Lass）？*」

随后两人顺着过道走远，我们再也听不见了。

这时便是动一动、开开灯，也尽可放心了：可我们一样也没做，只是继续屏息站着，各人忙着各人的心事。

汉伯里与曼塞尔想的是什么，我无从代答；我只知道自己想的，既非摆在眼前的行程，也非曼塞尔如此漂亮赢到手的秘密。我想的是，一个人的嗓音，竟能照出他心肠的颜色：因为那嗓音是劳斯的嗓音，那腔调，却属于一个狠毒凶恶的人。

---


===Original===
At length Mansel let the blind go and asked me to switch on the light.

A short study of the map suggested that we should reach Lass not later than six o’clock. The place lay fifty miles from Innsbruck and was either a fair-sized village or a very small town. The country about was highly mountainous.

It was then arranged that each should watch for one hour, while the others slept. I was to take the first watch, and Mansel the last; “although,” said he, “I think that may be a short one, for, before we get to Lass we must settle one or two things. Still, I shall begin to get pensive at five o’clock, and so, if you two don’t mind, I’ll sleep till then.”

Then the light was put out, and he and George lay down, whilst I sat still by the window, to watch the landscape go by, very fair and peaceful and, under the rule of the moonlight, like an enchanted land.

===Chinese===
末了，曼塞尔松手放下窗帘，请我开了灯。

把地图略加研读，可知我们应在六点之前抵达拉斯。那地方离因斯布鲁克五十英里，要么是座颇具规模的村子，要么是座顶小的城镇。四外乡野尽是丛山。

随即议定，每人轮值一个钟头，其余的人睡觉。我值头一班，曼塞尔值末班；「不过，」他说，「那一班怕是长不了，因为到拉斯之前，我们还有一两件事要定。虽说如此，到五点钟我就要开始凝神沉思了，所以你们两位若不介意，我要一觉睡到那时。」

而后灯灭了，他和乔治躺下，我仍静坐窗边，看风景一路向后退去——那样秀美，那样安宁，而且在月光的统辖之下，宛如一片着了魔的疆土。

===Original===
When Mansel waked us, I found to my surprise that it was half past six, but it seemed that at some small station we had waited nearly an hour, because of some disorder a few miles ahead.

“Which is all to the good,” said Mansel, “for now, when we get to Lass, there will be more people about and we ought to be able to follow without being seen.”

Then he arranged that, until Rouse was off the platform, we should not leave the train, and that, once outside the station, we should immediately disperse: we were then to ignore one another and each in his own way contrive to keep Rouse in view. I was not to follow for more than a quarter of a mile, but was then to return to the station and wait for Carson to come.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔唤醒我们时，我吃惊地发现已是六点半；据说在一个小站，因为前方几英里处的某种故障，我们滞留了将近一个钟头。

「这反倒全是好事，」曼塞尔说，「因为这样一来，我们到拉斯时，四处的人会多些，我们跟踪起来，便该不至于被人瞧见。」

接着他又做了布置：劳斯不下站台，我们便不离开火车；一出车站，立即分散：此后彼此只当不识，各凭各的路数，设法把劳斯收在视野之内。我跟踪不得超过四分之一英里，便须折回车站，等卡森到来。

===Original===
Rough as were these proposals, I believe they would have wholly succeeded, had not Fortune served us a very ill turn: for Rouse left the train as casually as though he travelled to Lass every day of his life and made his way out of the station without, so far as I saw, once looking behind.

He paused outside for a moment, to light a cigarette, and then started down the short road which served the station alone. The road was planted with lime trees six paces apart, and, as he walked down the middle, we had but to keep to the path to be out of his view. Mansel and I took the left path, and Hanbury the right.

All of a sudden, Mansel who was leading, stopped dead.

I instantly looked at Rouse, still sauntering carelessly on, with his head in the air, *as yet completely unconscious of the two Rolls stealing towards him, perhaps fifty paces away*.

===Chinese===
这些安排虽然粗疏，我相信本可全盘奏功，若非命运狠狠坑了我们一道：因为劳斯下车时那副懒散自在，仿佛一辈子天天都去拉斯似的，一路走出车站，就我所见，连一次头也没回。

他在外头停了片刻，点上一支烟，便顺着那条专供车站使唤的短路走下去。路边每隔六步植着一棵椴树；他既然走在路当间，我们只消守着树旁的小径，便不致落进他的视野。曼塞尔与我走左边的径，汉伯里走右边的。

突然间，领头的曼塞尔猛地钉在了原地。

我立刻去看劳斯：他仍旧信步闲庭，昂首而行，*对那两部正朝他悄悄掩近的劳斯莱斯——约莫五十步开外——还浑然不觉*。

===Original===
In a flash I knew what had happened.

The delay on the line had put a spoke in our wheel, and Carson, with Bell behind him, had caught up the train.

Even as I looked, my gentleman saw the two cars.

For an instant he faltered. Then he glanced over his shoulder and walked straight on.

===Chinese===
一瞬间我便明白出了什么事。

铁路上的误点，在我们轮子里插了一根辐条：卡森载着贝尔，竟已把火车追上了。

就在我望着的当儿，我那位先生瞧见了那两部车。

他踌躇了一瞬。随后朝肩后扫了一眼，径直走了下去。

===Original===
As they approached, he spread out ridiculous arms, and, when Carson stopped, he stepped abreast of the car and engaged him in talk, laughing and stamping and stooping and slapping his thighs, as though he found the encounter a matter of infinite jest, and completely ignoring the approach of a small motor bus. This had met our train, in the hope, I suppose, of custom for some hotel, and, the hope proving vain, was at last going empty away.

Of the little road the Rolls took more than its share, and, since Rouse with his antics occupied all that was left, the bus could not have gone by without running him down. This it very nearly did, for its driver did not seem to believe that the man would not budge: but, in spite of all manner of warnings, Rouse held his ground to the last, so that the driver was forced to clap on his brakes and actually lock his wheels to let the other get clear. Some argument followed, and this seemed natural enough; but, before it was over, Rouse took his seat by the driver and under the eyes of us all was rapidly driven away.

Maybe we were fools, but the thing was cleverly done.

===Chinese===
车驶近时，他摊开两条可笑的胳膊；卡森停车后，他便迈到与车齐平的地方，同他说起话来，又是笑，又是跺脚，又是俯身，又是拍大腿，仿佛觉得这场相遇是无穷无尽的笑料，同时对一辆小型公共汽车的驶近全然置之不理。那车原是来接我们这班车的，我猜是哪家旅店指望揽些生意，指望落了空，这会儿正空着往回开。

这条小路，劳斯莱斯占去的已不止自己该占的份；劳斯又用他那套活宝把戏占了剩下的全部，巴士要想过去，不从他身上碾过便无路可走。它也真差一点这么办了，因为司机似乎不信这人真会纹丝不动：但任凭千般百样的示警，劳斯硬是死守到末了，逼得司机猛踩刹车，当真把车轮都抱死了，才让这位爷脱身。跟着是一番口角，倒也合乎情理；可口角未了，劳斯已在司机身旁落座，当着我们众人的眼睛，飞快地被载走了。

也许我们是一群傻子，但这一手，干得实在漂亮。

===Original===
The cars were facing the station and were far too long to be at all quickly turned: the town lay five hundred yards distant, and no other car was in sight.

Too late Rowley leapt from his seat and flung up the road, in a vain endeavour to mount the bus from behind: too late Carson spurted for the station, to turn in the yard: only, as the bus disappeared, a figure flashed out of the lime trees and into its dust. This was Hanbury: and, since Rouse could not have known that he was behind, I think we all had some hope that he might be able by his speed to pull the unfortunate business out of the fire.

I do not mean to submit that we had cause for complaint. If Adèle was not lying at Lass, she was in the neighbourhood: our quest, from being desperate, had become full of promise; and we had passed, so to speak, at one stride into the running. Yet, but for the train’s delay, we might well have been led clean up to the prison gates and, taking Rose Noble unawares, have had his prisoner out *before he knew we were there*. And there was the rub. To have lost Rouse was tiresome: to have shown him that we were at Lass, and that in full force, was nothing less than a disaster. There is an old saying that a miss is as good as a mile; but, unless we could overtake Rouse *without his knowledge*, we were like to lose half the ground we had been at such pains to win.

===Chinese===
两部车头朝着车站，车身又太长，压根无法迅速掉头：城在五百码之外，视野里再无别的汽车。

罗利迟了，他从座位上跃起，沿路狂奔，妄想从后面扒上巴士，终归徒劳：卡森也迟了，他朝车站冲去，想进站场掉头：只有巴士消失的当口，一条人影从椴树间闪出，一头扎进它拖起的尘土里。那是汉伯里：而劳斯既不可能知道他缀在身后，我想我们人人都存着几分指望，盼他能凭一双快腿，把这桩倒霉事从火里捞出来。

我并不是要说，我们有什么可抱怨的。阿黛尔若不躺在拉斯，便在左近一带：我们这桩追寻，本已近乎绝望，如今变得满有指望；而且可以说，我们一步便跨进了局内。然而，若非火车误点，我们本可能被一路引到牢门跟前，打罗斯·诺布尔一个措手不及，*趁他还不知我们到来之时*，便把他的囚徒抢了出来。难就难在这里。丢了劳斯，固然恼人；可让他看见我们到了拉斯，还是倾巢而来，那简直就是一场灾难。老话说得好，毫厘之失与千里之差，同为落空；除非我们能*在他毫不知情之下*赶上劳斯，否则我们千辛万苦赢来的一半阵地，怕是要得而复失。

===Original===
Such thoughts and the like slid into and out of my mind, as the cars went about in the yard and, waiting a favourable moment, I boarded the second and took my seat by Bell.

Only pausing to take up Rowley, we tore after Mansel and Carson, now well under way.

Had the town lain further away, we must have come up with the bus, but the road this had taken was crooked and after a quarter of a mile ran into the streets. These were as narrow and faithless as any I know, for almost at once Carson was in a blind alley, and, though I saw his mistake in time to save Bell, the way we took brought us into a miniature market and left us there to get out as best we could.

===Chinese===
诸如此类的念头，在我心里出出进进；两部车在站场里掉头转弯的当儿，我觑得一个合宜的时机，上了第二部车，在贝尔身旁坐定。

只停车捎上罗利，我们便朝早早上路的曼塞尔和卡森疾追而去。

倘若城离得更远些，我们必定赶上了那辆巴士；可它走的那条路曲折多弯，行了四分之一英里便一头扎进街巷。那些街巷之窄之刁，不输我平生所知的任何一处：几乎转眼之间，卡森便进了一条死巷；我虽及时看出他的错处，让贝尔避开了，我们自己走的那条道，却又把我们带进一处巴掌大的市场，撂在里头，任凭我们自己设法脱身。

===Original===
At once I jumped out of the car and, calling to Bell to follow, began to run back. A passage presenting itself, I took this at once, in the hope of striking that quarter which we had failed to find, but, though it led into a street, I had to turn right or left, whereas, to my way of thinking, I needed to hold straight on. I ran to the right and turned up the first street I saw, but this curled round in a hoop, and, as soon as I had the chance, I turned again.

It follows that within five minutes I knew neither where I was nor how I had come, and, since I could speak no German, I had no means of obtaining any direction. Bell I must have outrun, for he was not to be seen.

Now why I called Bell to follow, instead of Rowley, I never can tell: it was against all reason, for Bell could handle the Rolls, but Rowley could not, and we had left the car so blocking the jaws of the market that nothing could come or go. It was, indeed, so unnatural a mistake to have made that I have often wondered whether Providence itself did not put his name into my mouth, for, had I called Rowley instead, we should have lost a chance which would not have come again.

===Chinese===
我立刻跳下车，唤贝尔跟上，往回跑去。恰有一条夹道现出来，我当即钻了进去，指望一头撞进我们没能寻见的那一片；可它虽通到一条街上，我却只能右转或左转，而依我的盘算，我需要笔直向前。我朝右跑，拐上望见的第一条街，谁知它兜了一个圆圈，一等有了机会，我便又转了向。

于是不出五分钟，我既不知身在何处，也不知这一路是怎么走来的；我又不会讲德语，连问个方向也无法可想。贝尔想必早叫我甩在了后头，因为已不见他的影子。

至于我为什么唤的是贝尔而不是罗利，我永远说不上来：这全然不合情理，因为贝尔驾驭得了劳斯莱斯，罗利却不能，而我们又把车撂在那里，堵住了市场的两口，什么东西也进不来出不去。这错处实在犯得悖乎常情，以致我时常疑心，莫不是天意亲自把他的名字送进了我嘴里；因为倘若我唤的是罗利，我们便要失去一个再也不会重来的机会。

===Original===
I had walked for another five minutes, without result, and was standing at a corner, where a very ancient fountain was playing behind a grill, wondering which way to take and thinking how childish it was to be lost in a town the size of St. James’s Park, when a soft, green fig fell suddenly down at my feet. I at once looked up and around, to see Hanbury’s face at a window and Bell’s behind.

As our eyes met, George beckoned to me to come up, but, before I could give any sign, they had disappeared.

The window at which I had seen them was that of a handsome oriel, serving a fine, old house, which must once, I think, have lodged persons of high degree, for a coat of arms had been cut above the doorway and each of the oriel’s corbels was charged with some device. The floor below was now used as a bookseller’s shop, and little but the doorway remained to show what it had been.

I immediately crossed the street and entered the shop, when an old man at once came forward and, using very fair English, inquired of what service he could be. Before I could answer, Bell came from behind a bookcase and said that I was a friend, whereupon I was ushered upstairs without a word.

===Chinese===
我又走了五分钟，一无所得，正立在一个拐角上——一眼十分古老的泉水，在铁栅后面喷涌——盘算该走哪条路，暗想在一座不过圣詹姆斯公园（St. James's Park）大小的城里迷了路，何其孩子气，忽然，一只软软的青无花果蓦地落在我脚边。我立刻抬头四望，只见一扇窗里有汉伯里的脸，后面还衬着贝尔的。

我们目光相接，乔治招手唤我上去；可不等我作出任何表示，两张脸已经不见了。

我望见他们的那扇窗，是一扇漂亮的凸窗，隶属一座精美的老宅；我想这宅子从前必定宿过高贵的人物，因为门洞上方凿着一面纹章，凸窗的每一块托石上，也都镌着图记。楼下如今开作一间书铺，除了那道门口，几乎再没什么能显出它当初的身份。

我立刻穿过街，走进铺子；一位老人随即迎上前来，操着相当好的英文，问我有何可效劳。不等我回答，贝尔从书架后转了出来，说我是朋友，于是我一声不响地被引上了楼。

===Original===
A moment later we entered a handsome room, more than half of which was loaded with books, while the hither or oriel end was curtained off and made an agreeable parlour, full of light.

George was sitting on a table, gazing out of the window from which he had beckoned me up: this was commanding a close, at the corner of which played the fountain by which I had stood.

“Bill,” said George, “where’s Mansel?”

“God knows,” said I. “Where’s Rouse?”

===Chinese===
片刻之后，我们走进一间漂亮的屋子，大半间都堆满了书；靠凸窗的这一头用帘子隔出，成了一方宜人的客厅，满室生辉。

乔治坐在一张桌子上，正从那扇招我上来的窗里向外凝望：窗子俯临着一处围院（close），院角上喷涌的泉水，正是我方才立足其旁的那一眼。

「比尔（Bill），」乔治说，「曼塞尔在哪儿？」

「天知道，」我说。「劳斯呢？」

===Original===
“Down in that close,” said George. “He went into one of those houses, but I’ve no idea which.”

“How on earth did you do it?” said I, clapping him on the back.

“I don’t know,” said George. “I couldn’t stand up when I got here, but just fell down in the shop. The old fellow was quite upset. By the grace of God he speaks English, and, when I could breathe, I pointed to *Alison’s Europe* and asked him how much it was. He said ‘Two pounds﻿—English money.’ So I said I’d give him five if he’d let me sit up in this window as long as I pleased. He threw the figs in, but don’t touch them﻿—they’re the only munitions I’ve got. And not everyone stands by that fountain: I had to throw three at Bell. And now what’s to be done? I know how to hurry, but I look to you for the brains.”

Standing well back from the window, I stared at the close.

===Chinese===
「就在下面那处围院里，」乔治说。「他进了那些宅子中的一家，只是哪一家，我全无头绪。」

「你到底是怎么做到的？」我说着，拍了拍他的背。

「我不知道，」乔治说。「赶到这儿的时候，我站都站不住，一头栽倒在铺子里。老头儿慌得不轻。托上帝的福，他会英文；等我喘过气来，我指着一部*《阿利森的欧洲史》（Alison's Europe）*，问他要多少钱。他说『两镑——英镑。』我便说，他若许我爱在这扇窗前坐多久便坐多久，我就给他五镑。无花果是他白饶的，不过你别碰——那是我仅有的军火。再说，也不是人人都肯站到那泉边去：我朝贝尔扔了三个，他才站定。现在怎么办？赶路我在行，动脑子可得指望你。」

我离窗远远站定，端详起那处围院。

===Original===
How many houses there were I could not tell, for they were irregularly built and several were overhung: but the close was shallow﻿—a bare fifty paces in depth, with a heavily timbered mansion blocking the farther end. The place was plainly most aged and would have been a feature of any city I know, but Lass was so old and curious and had been so little touched that the close was no more than in keeping with the rest of the town.

“I think one thing is certain,” said I, “that Adèle is not in this town: it’s much too busy for Rose Noble. Rouse has gone to ground in some house of call﻿—some lodging or other they’ve taken down in that close. And, unless there’s a back way out, I don’t think he’ll leave before dark. If I am right and the house is a meeting-place, there’s no reason why he should, for he can send word to Rose Noble by someone we’ve never seen.”

“That’s encouraging,” said Hanbury. “Not that it seems a very popular walk: only five people have used it since I’ve been here. But it’s early yet and﻿—well, there’s a man coming now. He doesn’t look very likely, but then they never do.”

“I don’t see what we can do,” said I, “until we know which is the house. You can’t run after a man just because he comes out of that close. It’s suspicious, of course, but we’re only six, all told, and, until we’ve got more to go on﻿—”

“Sir,” said Bell, “that’s the man. *He was on the quay at Dieppe.*”

===Chinese===
那里头究竟有多少座宅子，我说不上来，因为它们盖得错落不齐，有几幢上头还压着别家的屋体：但这围院并不深——纵深不过区区五十步，末端堵着一幢木架粗重的巨宅。这地方分明古老已极，放在我所知道的任何一座城里，都够得上一处名胜；只是拉斯本身实在太古老、太奇崛，又太未经触动了，以致这围院与全城其余部分相比，不过是各如其分罢了。

「我想有一点是确定的，」我说，「阿黛尔不在这座城里：对罗斯·诺布尔来说，这里太过热闹。劳斯已在某个落脚的宅子里遁了形——就是他们在那围院里赁下的某处住处。而且，除非另有后门出路，我想他天黑之前不会离开。倘若我猜得对、那宅子是个聚头的地方，他也没有离开的道理，因为他尽可以差一个我们从没见过的人，给罗斯·诺布尔传话。」

「这可真叫人振奋，」汉伯里说。「倒不是说那条道看来多有人走：我到这儿以来，统共只有五个人打那儿过。可时候还早，再说——唔，正好来了一个人。他瞧着不大像，不过话说回来，这类人从来就不像。」

「我看不出我们还能有什么办法，」我说，「除非先弄清是哪一家。总不能单凭一个人从那围院里出来，就拔腿去追。这当然可疑，可我们拢共不过六个人，在拿到更多凭据之前——」

「先生，」贝尔说，「就是这个人。*他当年在迪耶普的码头上待过。*」

## IV The Castle of Gath / 四 加斯城堡

===Original===

There was no time to make any plan.

The man was walking fast, and, as I have shown, Lass was no better than a maze.

With one consent, the three of us rushed to the door, and, while Hanbury was speaking the bookseller, Bell and I stepped directly into the street.

===Chinese===

来不及定什么计划。

那人脚步匆匆，而且如我前文所说，拉斯（Lass）同迷宫没有两样。

我们三个不约而同抢向门口；趁汉伯里同书商交涉，贝尔和我径直闪进了街心。

===Original===

Mercifully a cart was standing not six feet away, and as we darted behind it, I saw that we had before us the very deuce of a task. The man was, of course, on his guard and knew us by sight: the hour was early, and, though the shops were open﻿—because, I suppose, it was Market day﻿—there were not twenty people in view: yet, had the street been crowded, our dress was fatally distinctive and had only to be seen to betray us and our design.

In a flash I had my coat off and had flung it into the cart. I pitched my hat after it, rolled my sleeves to the elbow and, seizing a sack of refuse, which was standing open-mouthed on the kerb, swung this on to my shoulder and started, bent double, down the street. I know the disguise was feeble, but I dared wait no more; and that was as well, for, before I had taken five steps, the man glanced over his shoulder and turned to the left.

We had now a stroke of good fortune, without which I cannot believe that we should have got very far, for it gave us a breathing-space and let me fall back a little and Hanbury and Bell come up.

===Chinese===

谢天谢地，一辆货车正停在六英尺开外；我们躲到车后时，我看清了摆在面前的差事有多棘手。那人当然存着戒心，而且同我们照过面；时辰还早，虽说铺面都开着——我猜想，因为那天是集市日——放眼望去，看不满二十个人：然而，纵使街上人潮拥挤，我们这身衣服也特征得致命，只消被人瞧见，便足以出卖我们和我们的图谋。

转眼间我已脱下外衣甩进货车，帽子跟着掷了进去，袖子挽到臂肘，又抓起路缘上一只张着口的垃圾袋，抡上肩头，便伛着腰沿街走去。我知道这伪装蹩脚，可再等不得了；这也正好，因为不出五步，那人已回头望了一眼，向左拐去。

这时我们走了一步好运；没有它，我不信我们能走出多远，因为它给了我们一口喘息，容我稍稍退后，也让汉伯里和贝尔赶了上来。

===Original===

I had hardly come to the corner, when I saw the fellow enter a tobacconist’s shop.

At once I turned to see Hanbury close behind. He was wearing a black overcoat, green with age, and the bookseller’s hat, which came down over his ears. In his hands was a newspaper, open as though to be read.

“Watch the tobacconist’s,” I said, and dashed between two houses to tear off my collar and tie.

As I returned a man passed, wheeling a bicycle. His form seemed familiar, and, when I looked at him again, I saw that it was Bell. He had no coat, hat or collar; his trousers were clipped at the ankle and seemed to be falling down; his face and hands were filthy beyond compare. He glanced down the street on his left, and, there, I suppose, seeing Hanbury, picked up his cue and turned.

===Chinese===

我刚到街角，便见那家伙走进一家烟店。

我立刻回头，见汉伯里紧随在后。他穿一件黑大衣，旧得发绿，头戴书商的帽子，帽檐直压到耳朵上。手里一份报纸，摊开着，像正要读。

「盯住烟店，」我说，随即闪进两座房屋之间，扯下硬领和领带。

我回来时，一个推着自行车的男人打身旁经过。身形眼熟，再定睛一看，原来是贝尔。他不穿上衣，没有帽子，也没有硬领；裤脚在脚踝上夹着，眼看就要坠落；脸上手上脏得无可比拟。他朝左边的街上瞟了一眼——我猜想是在那里望见了汉伯里——得了提示，便转了向。

===Original===

As I came again to the corner, our man came out of his shop. For a moment he looked about, him, but seeing, I imagine, nothing at all suspicious, presently turned on his heel and went his way.

He was a tall, loose-limbed fellow, with a pasty face: he walked as though he liked the look of himself and wore his hat cocked on one side: and, since I presently learned that he was called “Casemate,” from now on I will speak of him by name.

I was slouching along behind him, still shouldering my sack, very conscious of my respectability and full of admiration for Bell, when I saw a crowd gathered ahead, where the street bellied into a place in front of a church. This seemed to interest Casemate, for all his haste, for I saw him peering to see what the matter might be; but, when he was nearer, he suddenly turned away and passed by on the opposite pavement, with his eyes on the ground.

===Chinese===

我再回到街角时，我们那位已从烟店出来。他四下张望了片刻，想来没望见半点可疑，便转身自去了。

他是个高个子、手脚松垮的家伙，一张白得发面团似的脸：走起路来仿佛很欣赏自己的模样，帽子歪扣在一边：既然我不久便得知他唤作「凯斯梅特」（Casemate），往后我就以名呼他。

我在他背后蔫头蔫脑地跟着，肩上还扛着袋子，一面惦着自己的体面，一面佩服贝尔佩服得五体投地；这时我望见前方聚起一堆人——街面在那里鼓成教堂前的一方场子。这情景似乎引起了凯斯梅特的兴趣——尽管他行色匆匆——因为我见他探头探脑，要看出了什么事；可待他走近了些，却突然掉开脸，从对面的人行道擦过去，眼睛盯着地上。

===Original===

I was wondering at his behaviour, when the crowd began to move and then fall away Then I saw a familiar bonnet and Mansel, with Rowley beside him, driving a Rolls. Behind came Carson.

Now such a meeting was more than I could have hoped for, for I had been racking my brain for some way in which to find Mansel and tell him what was afoot. Yet, though we were so happily met, I dared make no sign, for Casemate had his chin on his shoulder, and I could not have caught Mansel’s eye without catching his.

Going as slowly as I dared, with the tail of my eye I watched the car draw abreast; but the crowd was about her wings, and Mansel never looked up. I could have stamped for vexation, for Hanbury was shuffling upon the far side of the street, and Bell was already by. Then Carson cried out a warning and I heard a clatter and crash. Bell had wheeled his bicycle into the second car.

===Chinese===

我正诧异他的举动，人群挪动起来，随即四下散开。于是我望见一顶眼熟的软帽——是曼塞尔，罗利坐在他身旁，驾着一辆劳斯莱斯。后面跟着卡森。

这样的相逢，本是我求之不得的，因为我正绞尽脑汁，想寻个法子找到曼塞尔，告诉他眼下的动静。然而，虽则遇得这样凑巧，我却不敢做半点暗号，因为凯斯梅特的下巴总搁在肩上回望，我若要捕捉曼塞尔的目光，便逃不过他的目光。

我提着胆子能走多慢走多慢，拿眼角的余光看那车赶了上来；可人群围在车的两翼，曼塞尔始终没有抬头。我急得几乎跺脚，因为汉伯里还在街那头蹭着步子，贝尔已经错过。这时卡森喊出一声警讯，我听见哗啦一声撞响。贝尔把他的自行车拐进了第二辆车。

===Original===

As he picked himself up, I heard Mansel asking for news.

“It’s all right, sir,” said Carson: “a cyclist﻿—” Bell gave him a malignant look﻿—“a cyclist walked into the car. And, excuse me, sir, but you’re rather down on the right. *I think you must have broken a spring.*”

That was as much as I heard and all that I wanted to know, for, if, when upon the road, one of us wished to say something which was not for everyone’s ears, he had but to state that the car before or behind him had broken a spring; such a statement would naturally call for investigation, and, while this was being conducted, the communication could be easily made.

===Chinese===

趁他自己爬起来的当口，我听见曼塞尔问怎么回事。

「不要紧，先生，」卡森说：「一个骑车的——」贝尔恶狠狠瞪了他一眼——「一个骑车的撞上了车。对了先生，恕我直言，车身右边有点塌。*我想准是断了一根弹簧。*」

我听到这里便够，也正是我想知道的一切；因为在路上，我们中若有人要说一句不便入众人之耳的话，只消宣称他前头或后头的车断了一根弹簧；这话自然要引出一番查验，查验进行之际，那句话便可从容说罢。

===Original===

Now all this was well enough, but, when I returned to Casemate, I saw that he had stopped and was standing under an archway, with his eyes on the two cars and a hand to his mouth.

For a moment my brain faltered. I dared not dally, and the last thing I wanted to do was to overtake the man. He had but to dwell upon my clothes to find them strange, and to walk with his eyes upon my back would be the way of a fool. Then I saw a garage before me and, without so much as blinking, I plodded in.

An engine was running, and two mechanics were busy about its head. As I came in, one lighted a cigarette and pitched the match still flaming, on to the floor. There must have been petrol there, for flames leapt up. As the fellow stood staring, I shot my sack of refuse on to the fire, and a moment later the three of us stamped it out.

Both seemed very grateful and clapped me upon the back. This goodwill was just what I wanted, and after an age of dumbshow, the taller of the two took off his dirty overalls and helped me to put them on. I gave him far more than they were worth and I think this satisfied them that I was in fear of the police: and, when I plunged my hands into a tray of old oil, wiped them upon my sleeves and smeared these across my face, I am sure they no longer doubted that a warrant was out for my arrest. Still, with my sack of refuse, I had done them a very good turn, and, though they looked none too easy, they shook me by the hand when I went and gave me a cigarette.

===Chinese===

这一切本来都还顺当，可我回到凯斯梅特身后时，却见他已停步，立在一道拱洞底下，眼睛盯着那两辆车，一只手拢在嘴边。

一时间我的脑子打了愣。我不敢耽搁，而最不愿做的，便是赶上那人。他只消多看我两眼衣服，便会觉出蹊跷；再说，缀在他身后、让他眼睛盯住我的脊梁，那是蠢人的路数。恰好眼前有一家汽车行，我连眼皮也没眨，便一头蹭了进去。

一台发动机正转着，两个技工在机头旁忙活。我进门的当口，其中一个点着香烟，把还燃着的火柴随手丢在地上。地上想必泼了汽油，火苗腾地蹿起。那家伙僵在那里瞪着，我把那袋垃圾一甩，正罩在火上，片刻之后，我们三个已把它踩灭。

两个都显得感激不尽，拍着我的背。这份善意正是我要的；比划了半天哑语，高个的那个脱下自己油污的工装，帮我穿上。我付给他的钱远超那身衣裳的价值，我想这便让他们认定，我正怕着警察：待我把双手插进一盘陈油，往袖子上抹，又把油往脸上一涂，我敢说他们再不怀疑，缉拿我的拘票已经开出了。不过，靠那袋垃圾，我确实帮了他们一个大忙；所以虽说他们神色不算自在，临走时还是同我握了手，还给了我一支烟。

===Original===

At first I could not see Casemate. Then I saw him striding along fifty yards off. He was just passing Bell, who was pumping up one of his tires, and Hanbury was shuffling along twenty paces behind. Mansel and the two cars were gone.

I was now much easier in my mind and followed with confidence, for my greasy suit had given me a new lease of life, and the thought that Mansel was soon to be in the background did my heart good.

Casemate went down to a river and over an arched, stone bridge. This was in the midst of the town, and here for the first time I was able to see about me and to mark the lie of the land.

Lass lay in a trough of the mountains, the wooded slopes of which pressed close upon every side: the approaches to the town were hidden, and even the railway line was not to be seen. I have known villages so bound, but never another town, and the dignity of spires and gables so landlocked was very pleasing. Yet, as I have said, I was sure that Adèle was not here, and, when Casemate soon took a way which ran out of the town, to curl out of sight between two beetling woods, I was not at all surprised.

===Chinese===

起初我望不见凯斯梅特。随后见他大步流星走在五十码外，正打贝尔身边经过——贝尔正给一只轮胎打气——汉伯里蹭着步子跟在二十步之后。曼塞尔和那两辆车已经不见了。

这下我心里松快多了，放心大胆地跟了上去；这一身油腻的行头给了我第二条性命，而想到曼塞尔不久便会接应在后，我心里更是熨帖。

凯斯梅特下坡到一条河边，过了一座石砌的拱桥。这里已在镇子中心，我头一回能够四下打量，认清地势。

拉斯卧在群山的槽谷里，林木覆盖的山坡四面紧逼：进镇的路都藏在暗处，连铁路也望不见。被这样围死的村子我见识过，被这样围死的镇子，却再没见过第二个，而尖顶与山墙深锁内陆的那份端庄，着实悦目。然而，如我所说，我确信阿黛尔不在这里；所以待凯斯梅特不久拐上一条出镇的路、在两壁森然逼人的密林间蜷曲着隐没时，我一点也不惊讶。

===Original===

That here, however, we three must make ourselves scarce was obvious enough, for, if the streets had been idle, except for a labouring wagon the road to come was bare. And, while I was thinking how we should bring this about, the thing was done.

Bell mounted his bicycle, slowly overtook Casemate and passed out of sight: Hanbury turned up a passage which seemed to lead to the woods: and I was left. As plainly as though they had spoken, they meant me to bring up the rear.

The manoeuvre was sound and perfectly carried out: I was filled with admiration for Hanbury and Bell: but the burden of Casemate’s suspicion was now thrown full upon me, and I was by no means certain that I could carry the weight. I had no cover: mechanics are seldom seen tramping a country road: no man or beast seemed to be going our way.

I continued to walk on slowly, watching Casemate approach the bend and racking my brain. As he turned, he hung on his heel, and after a long look behind him, passed out of view.

My fears now came to a head, and I dared not go on. I was sure the fellow was waiting for me to come up. Yet I dared not stop without reason, nor, without reason, disappear. Then I saw a café beside me, with a table outside its door.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

不过，我们三个在这里必须销声匿迹，这是明摆着的：如果说镇里的街面还算冷清，往前的路上，除了一辆干活的货车，便光秃秃了。我正盘算如何抽身，事情已经办妥了。

贝尔跨上自行车，慢慢赶过凯斯梅特，消失在前方：汉伯里拐进一条似乎通向林子的小巷：剩下我。他们的意思明白得如同当面说破——要我殿后。

这一着部署稳妥，执行得干净利落：我对汉伯里和贝尔满心佩服：可凯斯梅特那份疑心，如今整个压到了我一人肩上，而我全无把握扛得起。我没有掩护：乡间土路上，难得见着技工徒步赶路：看样子，没有人或牲口与我们同路。

我继续慢慢前行，看着凯斯梅特走近那道弯，心里七上八下地盘算。他转过弯去时，脚步悬了一悬，朝身后望了许久，才隐没不见。

这时我的恐惧到了顶点，不敢再往前走。我断定那家伙正等着我赶上去。可我又不能无缘无故停步，也不能无缘无故消失。恰好身旁有一家咖啡馆，门外支着一张桌子。……

===Original===

As they brought me wine and cigarettes, I saw Casemate’s head appear round the bend of the road.

How long he watched me I do not know, for I dared not look again at the bend until I had drunk my wine: but, when at last I threw it a careless glance, he was out of sight.

At once I left the café and took to the road.

Ten minutes went by before I saw Casemate again, for the way was a natural pass and humoured every whim of the mountains, rising and falling and twisting as these decreed. Indeed, I was afraid I had lost him and was on the point of breaking into a run, when I rounded a corner to see him ten paces away. I drew back at once out of sight, but the encounter shocked me, for it showed how unfitted I was for such an exercise.

But worse was to come.

===Chinese===

伙计给我端上酒和香烟的当口，我看见凯斯梅特的脑袋从路的拐弯处探了出来。

他盯了我多久，我不知道，因为酒喝完之前，我不敢再看那道弯：可待我终于漫不经心地瞥它一眼时，他已没了踪影。

我当即离开咖啡馆，上了路。

过了十分钟我才再见到凯斯梅特，因为这条路是一道天然的关隘，处处顺着群山的脾气，山叫它起它就起，叫它伏它就伏，叫它拧它就拧。说实话，我已怕跟丢了他，正要撒腿奔跑，一转过弯，却见他就在十步开外。我立刻缩回弯后，躲出他的视线，可这一遭遇叫我胆寒，因为它表明，这种营生我是多么不胜任。

可更糟的还在后头。

===Original===

I had gone, I suppose, a mile in fear and trembling, and the road had begun to rise in a steady climb, when I peered round a bend, to see Casemate *retracing his steps*.

Now I had just covered a fairly long, straight reach of road, and, before I could have retreated, he would have had me in view. This was plainly the reason why he had gone about, for, if he was being followed, here was the very place to discover his man.

Frantically I looked round for cover, but there was none at all. On one hand an open meadow fell down abruptly to the level the road had left: on the other a high bank of earth rose into a crumbling cornice that overhung the way. Only an oak leaned out of the earthy wall and was thrusting a branch like a rooftree over the road.

I am a tall man, but heavy, and to this day I cannot tell how I managed to leap so high: but, if I am heavy, I was desperate, and, as I have found before, desperation is a remarkable goad.

Be that as it may, in an instant I had hold of the bough and a moment later was astride it, looking down upon the ground. I then fell forward and cautiously raised my feet, till I was lying along it and, though there was little foliage, pretty well hid. The branch was massive and rigid as any rock.

===Chinese===

我战战兢兢走了约莫一英里，路开始稳步爬升，这时我朝一道弯外窥去，却见凯斯梅特*正沿原路折返*。

我方才走完的，是一段相当长的直路，不等我退回去，他早把我收在眼底了。他之所以掉头，缘由显然在此：因为倘若有人缀着他，这里正是拿住那人的绝佳之处。

我发疯似的四下找掩护，却什么也没有。一边是开阔的草场，陡然跌落到公路抛下的那级平地：另一边，一道高高的土壁升起，顶上结着一道酥裂的檐口，悬在路的上方。只有一棵橡树从土壁里斜探出身子，把一根树枝像房梁似的横伸到路上。

我个子高，分量重，到今天也说不清自己是怎么跳得那么高的：可我纵然沉重，当时是豁出去了，而且我从前就领教过，豁出去是一根了不得的马刺。

无论如何，转眼间我已抓住那根树枝，再一转眼已骑坐在上面，俯望着地面。随后我向前伏倒，小心翼翼地收起双脚，直到整个人顺着枝干趴平；虽说枝上叶子稀少，却也藏得八九不离十。那枝子粗壮，坚硬如石。

===Original===

Casemate rounded the corner and passed below where I hung. Finding the road deserted, he stopped in his tracks and, after a long look, turned on his heel and began to walk back up the hill, whistling some air as he went. He paused for a moment beneath me, to take out a cigarette, and, as he did so, we both heard the sound of the horn of an oncoming car.

Now this meant nothing to me, for the horn was that of some car which was going to Lass, but Casemate stiffened like a pointer and stood with his ears pricked and a match in one of his hands and its box in the other. Indeed, so concerned was his demeanour, that I expected him every instant to take to his heels, though why he should fear some car which could hardly be one of ours I could not think.

The horn was not sounded again, but soon we heard a car coming at a high enough speed.

Casemate immediately whipped to the side of the road, and, taking his stand by the bend, peered anxiously round. The next moment he was out in the fairway, spreading and waving his arms as a signal to stop.

===Chinese===

凯斯梅特转过弯来，打我悬身处下方走过。见路上空无一人，他原地站住，久久望了一眼，便掉转身往坡上走去，一路吹着什么调子。他在我正下方停了片刻，掏香烟出来，正在这时，我们两个都听见了迎面驶来的汽车的喇叭声。

这声音于我毫无意味，因为那是某辆正往拉斯去的车；凯斯梅特却像条猎犬般僵住了，站在那里，双耳竖起，一只手捏着火柴，另一只手攥着火柴盒。说真的，他那副如临大敌的神气，叫我时时刻刻等着他撒腿就逃，虽说那辆车多半不会是我们一方的车，他有什么可怕的，我想不明白。

喇叭没有再响，但不一刻，我们便听见一辆车开得飞快地来了。

凯斯梅特立刻闪到路边，在弯道旁站定，焦急地朝外张望。下一刻他已蹿到路中央，张开双臂挥舞，打信号叫车停下。

===Original===

I heard the brakes clapped on, but the car was round the corner before it had come to rest.

Casemate came running back, and somebody put out a head.

“What is it?” said Rose Noble.

---

===Chinese===

我听见刹车猛地拉死，可车还没停稳，就已拐过了弯。

凯斯梅特跑回车边，有人探出一颗头来。

「什么事？」罗斯·诺布尔说。

---

===Original===

I was so much dumbfounded that I nearly fell down from the bough, and then I saw that the luck I had found so unkind a moment ago was playing clean into our hands.

“Somebody’s talking,” said Casemate. “She’s got word through to mother, and Big Willie’s at Lass.”

There was a moment’s silence.

Then﻿—

“How d’you know?” said Rose Noble.

===Chinese===

我惊得目瞪口呆，险些从树枝上跌下去；随即我明白，方才还觉得那样刻薄的运气，此刻正干干净净地落进我们掌心。

「有人走漏了风声，」凯斯梅特说。「她把话传到了老妈那里，大个子威利（Big Willie）到了拉斯。」

片刻的沉默。

然后——

「你怎么知道的？」罗斯·诺布尔说。

===Original===

“Jute,” said Casemate. “Last night they came for Big Willie and told him that she had made touch. Jute heard them say so. Big Willie was off like a cracker, and, when he was good and gone, Jute slipped up to the station and took the train.”

“*And led him to Lass,*” said Rose Noble. “Go on.”

“B-but﻿—”

“*Go on.*”

“He’s there,” said Casemate doggedly. “I’ve seen him. Stuck up in the streets, with a Punch-and-Judy crowd round both of his cars.”

===Chinese===

「是朱特（Jute），」凯斯梅特说。「昨夜他们来找大个子威利，告诉他说她已经搭上了线。朱特听见他们这么讲。大个子威利像窜天的爆竹一样射了出去，等他走得干干净净，朱特便溜到车站，上了火车。」

「*还一路把他引到了拉斯，*」罗斯·诺布尔说。「接着讲。」

「可——可——」

「*接着讲。*」

「他在那儿，」凯斯梅特倔头倔脑地说。「我亲眼看见的。堵在街上动弹不得，两部车周围还围着一圈看潘趣与朱迪木偶戏似的人。」

===Original===

“Where’s Jute?”

“Lying low in the lodge.”

“Thought he’d done enough harm, I suppose?”

“He didn’t bring him,” said Casemate. “Big Willie’s﻿—”

“Of course he brought him,” roared Rose Noble, bursting out of the car. “You snake-faced idiot, what do you take me for? ‘Got word through to mother.’ This isn’t Marlborough Street.” He slammed the door with a fury that shook the car. “By —﻿—﻿—,” he added, using a dreadful oath, “wait till I get at Jute. I told him they’d try to bounce him and I made him swear never to move when Big Willie was out of sight. *Never.* Why did he come by train?”

===Chinese===

「朱特呢？」

「在门房里猫着。」

「想必觉得自己闯的祸已经够多了吧？」

「他没把他带来，」凯斯梅特说。「大个子威利——」

「他当然把他带来了，」罗斯·诺布尔吼道，从车里一跃而出。「你这蛇脸的蠢货，你把我当成什么人？『把话传到了老妈那里。』这里可不是马尔伯勒街。」他砰地摔上车门，那股怒气震得车身直晃。「凭—— ——起誓，」他找补上一句可怕的咒骂，「等我逮着朱特。我告诉过他，对方会想法子唬他动窝，我还要他赌过咒：大个子威利一离开视线，就绝不动弹。*绝不。*那他为什么坐火车来？」

===Original===

“Car wouldn’t start,” said Casemate.

“And *that* never showed him?” raged Rose Noble.

Casemate essayed no answer, but only stared upon the ground, plainly resenting a trouncing which Jute had won, yet sullenly conscious that, by adopting Jute’s reading, he had put himself out of court.

Rose Noble ripped off his hat and mopped his face.

“Who was with Big Willie?” he demanded.

“Two of the servants,” said Casemate.

===Chinese===

「车发动不起来，」凯斯梅特说。

「*这*还没叫他看出苗头？」罗斯·诺布尔怒喝。

凯斯梅特没敢作答，只死死盯着地面；他分明怀恨，这顿痛骂本是朱特挣下的；却又闷闷地自知，既然顺着朱特那套讲法，自己早没了置喙的余地。

罗斯·诺布尔一把扯下帽子，抹了把脸。

「谁跟着大个子威利？」他追问。

「两个仆人，」凯斯梅特说。

===Original===

For a moment Rose Noble stood still, with his head in the air. Then he lowered his eyes and looked at Casemate.

I suppose there was that in his gaze which shocked the other, for he shrank back against the wall, protesting the truth of his words.

“Quite right,” purred Rose Noble, “quite right. You only saw two of the servants. *I passed the third on the road five minutes ago.*”

A moment later the car was being turned round.

I think this short colloquy must show how fine and swift a brain our principal enemy had. No matter how clogged, he had the truth free in an instant, and, though, when he passed him, he had not recognized Bell﻿—and for that he can hardly be blamed, for he had seen him but once﻿—the moment he learned that one servant was not with Mansel, he knew why the dirty cyclist was taking his subordinate’s road. That he made no mention at all of Hanbury or me, was, I confess, a considerable blow to my pride, but I fear he credited us with little cunning, but only a blunt pugnacity when it came to a fight.

===Chinese===

罗斯·诺布尔昂着头，静立了片刻。然后他垂下眼来，看着凯斯梅特。

我猜想他目光里有什么东西震住了对方，因为凯斯梅特缩到土壁跟前，连声申辩自己句句属实。

「一点不错，」罗斯·诺布尔打呼噜似的柔声说，「一点不错。你只看见了两个仆人。*第三个，五分钟前我在路上刚打过照面。*」

片刻之后，车正在掉头。

我想，这一番短短的对答，足以显出我们的头号仇敌有着怎样精密而迅捷的头脑。无论事情怎样纷乱，他一瞬之间便叫真相脱缚而出；虽说他错身而过时没有认出贝尔——这一点很难怪他，他统共只见过贝尔一面——可他一听说有个仆人不在曼塞尔身边，立刻便明白，那个邋遢的骑车人为什么走上了他手下的路。至于他对汉伯里和我竟只字不提，我承认，这对我的自尊是相当一击；只是我怕，他压根没把我们算作有心机的人，只当我们动起手来有一股蛮劲罢了。

===Original===

Now whether this stung into action my mother wit I cannot say, but I know that, as he and Casemate entered the car, I saw at my feet a chance which never would come again.

The car was closed, and its canopy was fixed: the road was narrow, and, in his endeavours to turn, the driver was bringing his charge directly below where I hung. For me to alight on its roof was the easiest thing in the world, and, once I was there, lying flat, although there was no luggage-rail, I could grip the canopy’s edge above the driver, for a shield, which is called a “sun-visor,” was there to conceal my hands.

In a flash I was sitting sideways upon the bough, and, as the car came back for the last time, I stepped gingerly on to its roof, trusting to my rubber-soled shoes to make no noise. I was flat in an instant, or the branch would have knocked me down, and a moment later I had fast hold of the rim which was to keep me steady and save me from sliding off.

As the car thrust forward﻿—

“Bunch,” said Rose Noble to the driver, “if you see that cyclist, run him down.”

“You bet,” said Bunch, and put the car at the hill with the rush of a bull.

===Chinese===

这情景是否刺得我天生的机灵醒了过来，我说不上；我只知道，趁他和凯斯梅特上车的当口，我看见眼前——就在我脚下——一个再不会来的机会。

车身是封闭的，车篷是固定的：路又窄，司机掉头的当口，正把他掌管的这部车带到我悬身的正下方。落上车顶，于我是天下第一等容易事；而一旦趴在上面，虽说车上没有行李栏杆，我却能攥住司机头顶那道车篷的边沿，因为那里立着一块唤作『遮阳板』（sun-visor）的挡板，正好遮住我的双手。

说时迟，那时快，我在枝上侧身坐起；趁车末一趟兜回来的一刻，我轻手轻脚踏上它的车顶，全指望胶底鞋不出声响。转眼间我已趴平——不然那树枝早把我扫了下来——再一刻，我已牢牢攥住那道边沿，靠它稳住身子，不至于滑落。

车往前一蹿——

「邦奇（Bunch），」罗斯·诺布尔对司机说，「你要看见那个骑车人，就轧死他。」

「您瞧好吧，」邦奇说，随即像公牛冲锋一般，驱车朝山上猛冲。

===Original===

I was not much alarmed for Bell’s safety, for I was quite certain that he had observed Rose Noble and, if he was yet upon the road, had only to hear the car coming to disappear. Indeed, I had my work cut out to think for myself, for the road was none too even and I was mortally afraid of losing my place.

Bunch was a good driver and swooped at his corners in a spectacular way, but, whenever he swung the car round, I had to fight like a madman to stay where I was, and if the fowls of the air have a sense of humour my progress must have afforded them infinite mirth. Except for one sharp descent, our road continued to rise or to keep the height it had won; and so far as I can remember we met no other vehicle. Two or three peasants passed, and the sight of them staring at me sent my heart to my mouth; but no one of those in the car seemed to notice their gaze, or, if they did, to find it curious.

At last we came to crossroads, where we turned to the right, and very soon after, we left the road for a drive which led into a wood so artlessly and was so shrouded with moss and the litter the trees had made that a man might have passed it by, as promising nothing at all. Yet in less than two minutes of time the wood was behind us, and we were heading for a castle which seemed to me to command all the kingdoms of the earth.

We were now upon the spur of a mountain, and, I judged, some three thousand feet up. The drive ran straight down the spur, the sides of which were plainly precipitous, for, after a little, the turf which flanked the drive fell suddenly out of sight. The whole of the end of the spur was masked by the castle’s façade, and it was easy to see that the building had been set on the spur as the nail upon a man’s finger, that is to say, moated on three of its sides by God knows what depths and accessible only on the fourth.

===Chinese===

我并不怎么为贝尔的安危担心，因为我十分确定，他已经看清了罗斯·诺布尔；倘若他还在路上，只消听见车来，便会躲开。说实话，我自顾不暇，因为路面高低不平，我怕极了丢掉自己的位置。

邦奇是把开车的好手，过弯掠得漂亮之极；可每当车身一甩，我都得像疯子一样搏斗，才能钉在原处；倘使天上的飞禽也有幽默感，我这副行进相必定供它们笑了个够。除了一段陡降，我们的路一直在爬升，或是守着已夺得的高度；就我记忆所及，我们没遇上别的车辆。路上过了两三个农人，他们盯着我瞧的模样，叫我的心都提到了嗓子眼；可车里似乎谁也没留意他们的目光，纵然留意了，也没觉得有什么可怪。

终于我们到了岔路口，向右转去；不多时，我们便撇开大路，拐上一条车道。那车道通进一片林子，通得那样毫无心机，又被苔藓和树木落下的枯屑遮蔽得严实，谁路过都会只当它一无所有，径直走过去。然而不到两分钟，林子已抛在身后，我们正向一座城堡驶去——在我眼里，它仿佛君临着普天下的万国。

我们此刻已在一条山嘴上，我估摸，海拔约三千英尺。车道笔直地沿山嘴而下，山嘴两侧分明是断崖，因为不多时，夹着车道的草皮便突然跌出了视野。山嘴的整个末端被城堡的正面罩尽，一看便知，这建筑安在山嘴上，正如指甲安在人的手指上——也就是说，三面被天知道多深的深渊围作护城河，唯有第四面可以通行。

===Original===

The walls were high and massive, rose into battlements and looked as good as new; and the place had an air of being old rather than ancient and more of an aerie than a keep. It was clearly inhabited, for there were blinds in its windows and smoke was rising from some chimney I could not see. Indeed, I could see nothing beyond the great façade, except a crocketed spire which stood up on the left. A stout, round tower rose at each end of the wall: these were corniced and had red, conical roofs: in the midst of the wall was a gateway, the great doors of which were shut.

I was now trembling with excitement, for the castle, of course, was the goal to which we had been trying to come, but what carried me away was the knowledge that I was about to be landed within its walls. Once I was in, and my presence unsuspected, who could tell what might not come about?

A yard or so from the gateway Bunch brought the car to rest. As he did so, his passengers alighted and passed at once to a wicket, cut out of the left-hand door. Then a bell clanged, and almost at once I heard steps. There was a grill in the wicket, and somebody drew its shutter to look between its bars. Whoever it was must have seen me, had not Rose Noble’s head been in the way.

“All right?” I heard him say.

“OK,” said the other, and opened the little door. Rose Noble and Casemate passed in.

===Chinese===

城墙高峻厚重，向上收成雉堞，看上去完好如新；这地方有一股「旧」而非「古老」的气息，与其说是堡垒，不如说更似鹰巢。它分明有人居住，因为窗上挂着百叶，一处我看不见的烟囱正冒着烟。确实，除了那面高大的正墙，我什么也望不见，只有左侧立起一座带卷叶饰的尖塔。墙的两端各起一座浑圆的粗壮碉楼：楼上带着檐口，覆着红色的锥形顶：墙的正中是一座门洞，两扇大门紧闭。

这时我激动得发抖，因为这座城堡，不用说，正是我们一直想到达的目标；可真正叫我神魂颠倒的，是我知道自己就要被送进它的墙里。一旦进去，而踪迹不为人觉，还有什么事情不会发生，谁说得准呢？

离门洞一码开外，邦奇把车停稳。就在这时，车上的人下来，径直走向左扇大门上开出的一扇小门。铃铛一响，几乎立刻有脚步声传来。小门上开有格栅，有人拉开格栅的小窗，从铁条间往外张望。若不是罗斯·诺布尔的脑袋正挡着，那不管是谁，必定看见我了。

「没事吧？」我听见他说。

「OK，」对方应道，开了那扇小门。罗斯·诺布尔和凯斯梅特走了进去。

===Original===

Now, as soon as the car had stopped, I had moved as far as I could to the right of the canopy, so as to make the most of what cover I had: whether I moved again was depending on where the man stood who admitted the car. With one of the great doors set wide the car could pass in, and, if, as I fully expected, the left-hand door was opened, the porter was sure to hold it and I should be out of his sight.

Now so much fell out very well. Only one door was opened, and that was the left: I saw the hands of the porter pulling it back: *but the door had been made in two halves, and, while the lower swung open, the upper stayed fast*.

The car was about to pass in﻿—*with three inches of headroom to spare*, and, if I stayed on her roof, I should be swept off or crushed﻿—probably crushed.

Had I not been on the edge, I must have been badly hurt, for Bunch thrust forward, as though he knew I was there.

I got to my knees and jumped as best I could.

Happily I lit upon turf, but this sloped down from the drive, and I tumbled, like a clown in the sawdust, before I could bring myself up. As I did so, I heard the door shut and the bolts shot home.

---

===Chinese===

车一停稳，我便朝车篷右侧挪到不能再挪，好把我那点掩护用尽：至于再挪不挪，全看放车进门的人站在哪里。两扇大门有一扇敞开，车便开得进去；倘若——这也全在意料之中——开的是左扇，那门房必定要扶着门，我也就出了他的视线。

这一切竟然都顺遂。只开了一扇门，正是左扇：我看见门房的双手正把它往回拉：*可那扇门造作上下两半，下半扇荡开了，上半扇纹丝不动*。

车正要开进去——*顶上只余三英寸的空当*；我若还赖在车顶上，不是被扫下来，就是被压扁——多半是被压扁。

幸亏我本就伏在边上，否则必定伤得不轻，因为邦奇往前一撞，倒像他知道我就在那里似的。

我跪将起来，拼尽全力往下一跳。

幸而落点是草地，可草地朝车道下方倾斜，我未及收住身子，便像锯末堆里的丑角一样滚作一团。也正在这时，我听见门阖上了，门闩落进了槽里。

---

===Original===

To report to Mansel was plainly the first thing to do: and, since anyone at a window could have shot me down, I got to my feet and started to run for the wood. Except that I was shaken, I felt none the worse for my fall; and here I think I was lucky, for I had leapt blindly and had not taken off clean.

Halfway to the wood I rested behind a tree and looked about me.

The prospect was magnificent indeed: all around were mountains and forests, rising and falling as far as the eye could see, and, the sky being very blue and the sunshine brilliant, the castle looked fabulous, and a man that came suddenly upon it might well have been forgiven for rubbing his eyes.

How Rose Noble had come to be so installed I could not think, but, had he spent his life searching, he could not have found a prison one half so suitable. The place was solitary and most secure: no cries could be heard, nor any signals seen: the one approach was hidden, and such as found it could not conceal their coming, for there were not six trees on the spur and the wood lay two hundred yards from the castle wall.

This reflection made me take to my heels, for, if I could withdraw unseen, that would be a point in our favour, and we had none to spare. Indeed, when I thought of those walls my spirits sank, and remembering how near I had come to passing in, I could have struck myself. What was worse, I was sure that, had he been placed as I, Mansel would somehow have done it and not have let slip a chance which surely was gone for good.

===Chinese===

头一件事，显然是向曼塞尔复命：而且随便哪扇窗后的人都可能一枪撂倒我，我便爬起来，朝林子奔去。除了吓得不轻，这一跤并没有伤着我；这一层我想是运气，因为我跳得盲目，起跳也蹭得不干净。

跑到半途，我在一棵树后歇脚，四下打量。

景色实在壮丽：四面皆山皆林，起伏绵延到目力尽头；天色湛蓝，阳光灿亮，城堡望上去恍如仙境，谁若同它劈面相逢，惊得直揉眼睛，也情有可原。

罗斯·诺布尔怎么会落脚得这样妥帖，我想不出；可他就是寻上一辈子，也寻不到一半这样合适的牢狱。此地孤绝而万无一失：呼救传不出去，信号望不见：唯一的那条进路藏在暗处，寻到它的人却藏不住自己的来路，因为山嘴上统共没有六棵树，而林子离城堡的墙有二百码。

这样一想，我撒腿便走；因为倘若我能不被察觉地退走，便是我方挣得的一分，而我们一分也折损不起。说真的，一想到那些墙，我的心气便沉下去；想到自己离进门只差一线，我恨不能揍自己一顿。更糟的是，我确信，换了曼塞尔处在我这境地，他总有法子办成，绝不会放跑这样一个机会——而这机会，怕是真的一去不返了。

===Original===

So I came to the wood, and, after watching for some time for any sort of sign that I had been seen, made my way down the drive as fast as I could.

Twenty minutes later I reached the crossroads.

There to my great surprise, Rowley rose out of a ditch and said that Mansel was waiting a stone’s throw away. Then he told me which way to take, and a moment later I saw the two cars below me and Mansel and George poring over a paper book, whilst Carson was feeding Tester and Bell was washing his face in a little rill.

They seemed very glad to see me and most eager to hear my news.

I told my tale.

When I had finished, Mansel put a hand on my arm.

===Chinese===

我就这样到了林边，守了半晌，看有没有任何被人瞧见的迹象，随后顺着车道，能多快便多快地奔了下去。

二十分钟后，我到了岔路口。

在那里，罗利从一道沟里站起身来，叫我大吃一惊；他说曼塞尔就在投石之遥外等候。他指明我该走的方向，片刻之后，我便望见下方那两辆车，曼塞尔和乔治正埋头共读一册纸面小书，卡森在喂特斯特，贝尔在一条小溪里洗着脸。

他们见了我都很高兴，急不可待地要听我的消息。

我把经过讲了一遍。

讲完，曼塞尔把一只手搭在我臂上。

===Original===

“William,” he said, “she will thank you. I haven’t got the words. But please don’t count it bad luck that you were shut out. If you had gone in, you’d have made a fatal mistake. Alone, unarmed and with no idea of the building, you wouldn’t have stood an earthly: and, what would have been far worse, *we shouldn’t have known where you were*.”

“But﻿—”

“There is no ‘but’ here,” said Mansel. “You’ve played and you’ve won your game: but, if you’d gone on, you’d have thrown the rubber away. Do please remember that battles have gone wrong, wars have been lost and the history of the world has been changed, because valour has outrun discretion and *men have lost touch*.”

It was easy enough to see the force of his words, and I have often thought since that the ways of Providence are strange indeed, for, if Casemate had not turned back, I should not have hung in the oak, and, if the castle door had not been cut asunder, I am sure that I should not have lived to tell this tale.

===Chinese===

「威廉，」他说，「她会感谢你的。我寻不出话来。但请不要把被关在门外算作坏运气。你若是进去了，那才是犯了致命的大错。孤身一人，手无寸铁，对那建筑又一无所知，你是全无活路的：而更糟得多的是，*我们就不知道你在哪里了*。」

「可是——」

「这里没有『可是』，」曼塞尔说。「这一局你打了，而且打赢了：可你若是再打下去，便要把整盘输掉。请务必记住：多少战役出了岔子，多少战争归于败亡，多少世界史因之改写，都因为勇猛跑到了审慎前头，*因为人失了联络*。」

他这话的分量，不难掂出；我后来也常想，上天的道路实在奇妙：倘若凯斯梅特不曾折返，我便不会悬在那棵橡树上；倘若城堡的门不曾分作两半，我确信自己活不到今天来讲这个故事。

===Original===

Then Mansel told me that Bell had seen Rose Noble go by and had followed him down; that George and Bell had both seen me on the roof of the car and, while George had pushed on to the crossroads, Bell had raced back to meet Mansel and bring up the cars.

“And now,” said he, “to breakfast. I picked up some food at Lass. And you’ll be glad of a wash. And as soon as ever we’ve done, I want you to show us around.”

Whilst we were eating, we decided that Mansel and George and I should go out on foot, that one servant should watch the crossroads and the others stay with the cars until we returned. If one of the enemy passed he was not to be stopped or followed, but only marked, “for,” said Mansel, “Mr. Chandos has done the trick, and we don’t want to start a new hare till we know where we are. Of course, if Rose Noble comes by, you will shoot him at sight: but, unless I’m much mistaken, he won’t come out any more.”

With that, he told Carson and Bell to serve out a pistol apiece, and, when this was done, he and George and I set out for the wood.

===Chinese===

接着曼塞尔告诉我：贝尔看见罗斯·诺布尔驶过，便一路跟了下来；乔治和贝尔都望见我在车顶上，乔治径直赶往岔路口，贝尔则飞奔回去接曼塞尔，把车带了上来。

「好了，」他说，「吃早饭吧。我在拉斯带了些吃的。你也乐得洗一洗。一吃完，我要请你给我们领路。」

吃饭时我们议定：曼塞尔、乔治和我徒步出去，一名仆人看守岔路口，其余的守着车，等我们回来。若敌方有人经过，不得拦截，不得尾随，只记下面目；「因为，」曼塞尔说，「这一手钱多斯先生已经办成了，在我们摸清自己的方位之前，不想另惊起一只新兔子。当然，若是罗斯·诺布尔打这里过，你们一见面便开枪：不过除非我大错特错，他是再也不会出来的了。」

说罢，他吩咐卡森和贝尔各发一支手枪；发罢，他便携了乔治和我，动身往林子去。

===Original===

As we went I asked what was the book which I had found them reading when I came in.

George pulled it out of his pocket and held it up.

“Souvenir,” he said. “When I gave the bookseller his money and borrowed his plumes, he pressed this into my hand. He had a speech ready, I fear, but, beyond that he was the author, I have no idea what he said. Whilst I was waiting at the crossroads I looked to see what it was. It’s a guide in English to Lass and the neighbourhood. The grammar’s unequal, but, if I could write half as good a guide in German, I should be more than pleased. And it’s got a good plan of Lass and a couple of maps.”

===Chinese===

一路走着，我问起我到时他们埋头在读的是什么书。

乔治把它从口袋里抽出来，举了举。

「纪念品，」他说。「我付给书商钱、借他这身行头的时候，他把这个塞进了我手里。恐怕他还有一篇演说预备着，可除了他是作者这一点，他讲了什么我全无所知。在岔路口等候的工夫，我看了看它是什么。这是一本英文的拉斯及其四乡指南。文法深一脚浅一脚，可我若能用德文写出一半这么好的指南，就要喜出望外了。书里还附有一幅不错的拉斯平面图和两幅地图。」

===Original===

We said no more at that time, for we were approaching the wood.

This we afterwards found was nearly a mile across, by some half a mile in depth. The drive which led to the spur went to the heart of the thicket and there turned sharp to the left: from there it ran slightly up hill to the edge of the wood and then directly down to the castle gate. It follows that, from anyone walking, the spur and the castle were hidden till he came to the edge of the wood, but then burst upon him in an instant, as though some curtain had suddenly been let fall.

Before we breasted the rise, for caution’s sake we had stepped in among the trees, and, when I had parted the branches and Mansel and George had come up, I heard the one catch his breath, while the other stood staring like a zany upon the remarkable scene.

Presently we moved to a knoll and lay down behind its swell.

The spur was empty, and the castle walls bare as I had left them: only the smoke I had noticed gave any sign of life.

“Where’s that guide?” said Mansel suddenly. “See if it mentions this place.”

===Chinese===

这时我们没有再多说，因为林子已在望。

这片林子，我们后来量得，宽近一英里，纵深约半英里。通向山嘴的那条车道直入林子的心脏，在那里猛地向左一折：从那里微微上坡，升到林缘，再径直降到城堡门前。由此，对步行的人来说，山嘴和城堡一直藏到林缘方才现形，而一现形便劈面扑来，仿佛一道大幕骤然落下。

还没登上坡顶，为谨慎起见，我们已闪身入树；我拨开枝条，曼塞尔和乔治赶上前来，只听得一个倒抽一口气，另一个像个呆子似的钉在那里，直瞪着这奇景。

少顷，我们挪到一座小丘后，伏在丘脊背面。

山嘴空空的，城堡的墙还同我离开时一样光秃：只有我留意过的那缕烟，透出一点生命的迹象。

「指南呢？」曼塞尔忽然说。「看看它提没提到这个地方。」

===Original===

Hanbury had the book in an instant and was studying one of its maps.

“Gath,” he said, after a moment. “This should be the Castle of Gath. And now for the text.”

Hastily he scanned the pages.

Then he bent the book open and gave it into Mansel’s hand.

And, since I cannot better the bookseller’s description of the place, I will set it down word for word.

===Chinese===

汉伯里转眼把书拿到手，正研究其中一幅地图。

「加斯（Gath），」他过了一会儿说。「该就是加斯城堡（the Castle of Gath）。好，来看正文。」

他急急翻检书页。

随后他把书翻摊开来，递到曼塞尔手里。

而且，既然我无法把书商对这地方的描述改写得更好，我便一字不易地把它录在这里。

===Original===

> Few peoples know of this castles, because great care was taken from at first that it must be most private and for a long time it was not mark on the state maps.
> Gath was builted in the end of the fifteenth century by the great King Maximilian, Holy Roman Emperor, “The Last of the Knights,” as he is named by his proud loving subjects, so as to be a tranquil castles where he can retire to rest sometimes from the cares of reign. It was always kept in readiness to receive him and a small suite.
> The visitor who will go the seven miles according to the plan of the page 7 will be handsomely rewarded, for the site is unique and the building is posed on the brink of a mountain and seems to be a veritable “castles in the air.” It is easy to believe that the great King was happy to stay here during his short respites and in the company of a few trusteds retainers to find peace and refreshment, not to be said inspirations for future glory.
> But if one has found it so fine to see from afar, it will be a pity not to visit the interior. This may be done on Wednesdays and Saturdays between two and four o’clock by permission of the present owner, Count —﻿—﻿— of —﻿—﻿—.
> The visiting of the apartments is made under the custody of a caretakers who fulfils intelligence with curtesy.
> We now enter by a deep archway into a fine courtyard, with a waterbason in its midst. A spring rises in the bason and flows in a cascade through the courtyard and under a smaller archway. Let us follow it. Lo, we are in a pretty terrace, where flowers blow, and running all of the length of the South of the Castles, not counting the round tower at each end. Here we seem to be standing on the edge of some earth, for the green cliff falls down directly and the cascade curves over it like a bow. This is the water supplies of the Castles.

===Chinese===

> 很少的人民知道这座城堡们，因为从最初起就费了大大的心，务使它必须最为私密，而且在很长的时间里，它不曾被标在国家地图上。
> 加斯是被建造于十五世纪的末尾，由伟大的国王马克西米利安——神圣罗马皇帝，被他的骄傲而亲爱的臣民们命名为「最后的骑士」——所建，为的是作为一座安宁的城堡们，他可以有时从治国的操劳中退隐到那里休息。
> 愿意依照第七页的平面图走这七英里的游客，将受到大大的报答，因为这地点是独一无二的，而这建筑物被摆在一座山的边缘上，看起来是一座真正的「空中城堡们」。很容易相信，伟大的国王在他的短促的歇息里乐于居留此地，与少数几位受信任的侍从们做伴，寻得安静与爽神，更不必说获得未来光荣的灵感。
> 但是一个人既然发现它从远处看是这样美，不去拜访一下内部便可惜了。此事可在星期三与星期六、两点至四点之间做到，须得现今主人的许可，即—— ——地方的—— ——伯爵。
> 各房间的拜访是在一位管理员们的看守之下做成，他以礼貌满足智慧。
> 我们现在由一道深深的拱洞进入一座美好的庭院，它的中央有一个水盆。一道泉水在盆中升起，成一道小瀑布流过庭院，又从一道较小的拱洞底下流去。让我们跟随它。看哪，我们来到在一座美丽的台地，那里花朵吹放，它沿着城堡们的南侧展开全体的长度，每一端的一座圆塔不计算在内。在这里我们像是站立在某些大地的边上，因为绿色的峭壁直直地落下去，小瀑布像一张弓似的在它上面弯过去。这是城堡们的水的供给。

===Original===

> We now retrace our steps to the courtyard and ascend the Grand Staircase on the West. The first room is an antichamber, as the furniture suggests, for everything has been left in the State Apartments as when the great King died.
> The following was the reception room of the King. The tapestries are notable.
> From this we go into a gallery of stone. Here, no doubt, were guards when the King was there, for the gallery is at the corner of the courtyard and the King’s apartments are about to begin. This gallery leads into the southwest tower, which is not shown and has been modernized, and another staircase goes down from it to the terrace where the flowers blow.
> Now we come to the south front and the King’s apartments. These are untoucheds.
> The first is another antichamber.
> Then we enter the royal dining-room. The tapestries are very fine. The visitor will observe that the room is not large because the King has not entertained a guest here.

===Chinese===

> 我们现在折回我们的脚步到庭院，登上西面的大阶梯。第一间房间是一间前厅，家具们如此地提示，因为国务各室里的一切都被留下了伟大的国王去世时的原样。
> 随后的曾是国王的接待室。挂毯是值得注目的。
> 从这里我们走进一条石头回廊。当国王在的时候，无疑这里有卫士们，因为回廊是在庭院的角上，而国王的房间们正要开始。这条回廊通入西南塔，该塔不予展示且已被现代化，另有一道阶梯从它下去，到那花朵吹放的台地。
> 现在我们来到南正面与国王的房间们。这些是未加触动们。
> 第一间是又一同的前厅。
> 然后我们进入王家的餐厅。挂毯非常精美。游客将会观察到这房间并不大，因为国王不曾在这里款待过一位客人。

===Original===

> We now pass into the King’s Bedchamber. This is very stately. The crimson hangings of the magnificent bed and the superb furniture and tapestries, the richery of the polished woodcarvings all reflects the departed majesty of “The Last of the Knights.”
> The King’s Closet comes next.
> From this we enter the Queen’s Bedchamber, which is like the King’s, but, of course, less magnificence. The hangings are purple.
> Then comes another day-chamber for the uses of the Queen, and we then enter an antichamber before passing into a stony gallery similar in all respects to the first.
> The southeast tower is not shown.

===Chinese===

> 我们现在步入了国王寝宫。这是非常庄严的。华丽大床的绯红帷幔、华贵的家具与挂毯、磨光木雕们的富丽，全都反映着「最后的骑士」已去的威严。
> 接下来是国王密室。
