> 从这里我们进入王后寝宫，它与国王的相像，但当然较少壮丽。帷幔是紫色的。
> 随后来了又一同王后使用的日间房间，我们于是再进入一间前厅，然后走进入一条多石的回廊，它与第一条在一切方面都相似。
> 东南塔不予展示。

===Original===

> Now we come to the oratory. There is a door and stairs going down from the oratory into the chapel. There are many shrines in the mountains, but not like this. It is still here always, in the midst of the storm. The glass in the panes is unquestionable and the finery of the woodcarving is beyond praise.
> That is all that is shown.
> The other part of the Castles have been modernized.
> It is regretful that, since above written, the owner of Gath has lately died and there is a lawsuit for the possession of the Castles. Because of which the interior is not now shown.

===Chinese===

> 现在我们来到祈祷室。有一道门和阶梯从祈祷室下去，进入礼拜堂。山中有许多的圣所，但没有像这一座的。它至今始终在这里，处于风暴的正中。窗格中的玻璃是无可置疑的，木雕的华丽则超出赞美之外。
> 那便是所示的一切。
> 城堡们的其他部分已被现代化了。
> 令人惋惜的是，自上述文字写成以来，加斯的主人新近去世了，为城堡们的占有发生了一件诉讼。因为这件事，内部现在不再展示。

===Original===

This admirable description we read all three together, Mansel holding the book and waiting, until we had finished, to turn a page.

When it was done, he looked up into the sky.

Presently he sighed.

“One has much to be thankful for,” he said. And then, “Let’s hope they let her walk on ‘the pretty terrace, where flowers blow.’ ”

Hanbury and I said nothing, for, indeed, there was nothing to be said.

Presently Mansel spoke again.

===Chinese===

这篇令人赞叹的描述，我们三人一起读完；曼塞尔捧着书，等我们两个都读完了，才翻过一页。

读罢，他仰头望天。

少顷，他叹了口气。

「人有许多该感恩的事，」他说。随即又道：「但愿他们容她在『花朵吹放的美丽露台』上走动。」

汉伯里和我一言不发，因为实在无可措辞。

少顷，曼塞尔又开了口。

===Original===

“One thing stands out﻿—any chance of a snap division, which we may have thought we had, can be written right off. If they thought we were still at Villach, it would be different: when your enemy’s that far away, you’re apt to be careless about keeping the door he can’t enter and watching the wall he can’t scale. But to rush a position like this when it’s properly manned is quite impossible. Very well. There’s only one thing to do, and that is to go on playacting.

“If Chandos was not seen on the spur﻿—and I think it unlikely that he was﻿—Rose Noble has a right to infer that when we came to the crossroads we didn’t know which way to go. Well, he hasn’t much use for inferences, and so I propose to prove to his satisfaction that this inference is a fact. We’re going to forget about Gath, and we’re going to scour the country for miles around. We shall take up our quarters at Lass, come to the crossroads at dawn and spend the whole of the day visiting villages and farms and combing the countryside. While we are doing this we shall take care to study Gath *from every side*. That’ll be easy enough. And tomorrow, to round the picture, we shall discover the castle and drive up and ring the bell. I don’t imagine Rose Noble will answer the door, but I’m sure that he’ll be within earshot and I’d like him to hear what I say.

“And, if all goes well, two or three days later we’ll call again. But this time we’ll come by night, and I think we’ll let the bell go.”

===Chinese===

「有一条明摆着——任何出其不意、速战速决的机会，哪怕我们曾自以为握有，如今都可以一笔勾销。倘若他们以为我们还在菲拉赫，那另当别论：敌人离得那样远，他进不来的那扇门、爬不上的那道墙，你难免疏于看守。可要强攻一座守备严整的阵地，全无可能。很好。只有一件事可做：继续演戏。」

「倘若钱多斯没有在山嘴上被瞧见——我想被瞧见的可能微乎其微——罗斯·诺布尔便有理由推断：我们来到岔路口时，并不知道该往哪边走。好吧，他不大把推断当一回事，所以我打算向他证明得叫他满意：这条推断乃是一个事实。我们要把加斯忘掉，把方圆多少英里的乡野都篦一遍。我们驻进拉斯，破晓便到岔路口来，成天价走访村庄农舍，把四乡梳个遍。与此同时，我们要留心*从每一个方向*研究加斯。这容易得很。明天，为把这幅图画补圆，我们要『发现』这座城堡，驱车上山，按响门铃。我不指望罗斯·诺布尔会亲自开门，但我敢肯定他就在听得见的距离之内，我正想让他听见我说的话。」

「倘若一切顺遂，两三天后我们再登一次门。只是这一回，我们夜里来——而且我想，铃就不必劳它了。」

===Original===

For a little we lay there silent, digesting his words.

Then﻿—

“At the moment,” said I, “they’ve lost us. Doesn’t it seem a pity to let them find us again?”

“Yes,” said Mansel, “it does. But no man can have it both ways. So long as he’s out of touch, Rose Noble won’t close an eye: and so long as his eyes are open, we shall never get into Gath. So far as I can see, one sentry is more than enough to watch those walls. Very well. Would you omit that precaution, when the last time you’d seen the enemy he was two miles away?”

Hanbury fingered his chin.

“How did Rose Noble get there?” he said. “I mean, it’s a private house.”

===Chinese===

我们伏在那里静了片刻，消化他的话。

然后——

「眼下，」我说，「他们把我们丢了。再让他们找回来，岂不可惜？」

「是的，」曼塞尔说，「确实可惜。可谁也不能两全。只要同我们失着联络，罗斯·诺布尔便合不上眼：而只要他睁着眼，我们就永远进不了加斯。依我看，看守那些墙，一个哨兵绰绰有余。很好。上回你看见敌人，他不过两英里远——这样的防备，你省得掉么？」

汉伯里摸着下巴。

「罗斯·诺布尔是怎么进去的？」他说。「我是说，那是私人宅邸。」

===Original===

Mansel shrugged his shoulders.

“I imagine he’s bought the caretakers. It’s often been done in London﻿—in the good old days. And there it was dangerous: but here there’s next to no risk. No neighbours to raise their eyebrows: seven miles from the nearest town: ownership in dispute, and no visitors allowed. Once he had found the place, it was too easy.”

“I agree,” said Hanbury. “And the greatest of these is ‘ownership in dispute.’ Supposing the disputants heard that the Castle of Gath was taking in paying guests.”

“What then?” said Mansel.

===Chinese===

曼塞尔耸了耸肩。

「我猜他把看守人收买了。这一套在伦敦是家常便饭——在那美好的旧时光里。在那儿干还要冒险：这儿却几乎没有风险。没有会挑眉毛的左邻右舍：离最近的镇子七英里：产权争执未决，谢绝来访。地方一被他找着，剩下的事就太容易了。」

「我同意，」汉伯里说。「而这几桩里头，最大的还是『产权争执未决』。倘若争执的两造听说，加斯城堡竟收起付钱的房客来了。」

「那又如何？」曼塞尔说。

===Original===

“Well, wouldn’t they take some action?”

“Of course they would,” said Mansel. “But what action would they take?”

“I don’t know,” said Hanbury. “But﻿—”

“Neither do I,” said Mansel, “and there’s the rub. If they’d let me take the action, well and good. But they’d never do that. If I pitched it in pretty strong, they’d probably come along with a couple of local police and a lawyer’s clerk.﻿ ﻿… And don’t forget﻿—whatever action was taken, Rose Noble would know whom to thank. He’d know that I was behind it, and I’m frankly afraid to think what his answer would be. You see, ‘the goods are perishable.’ Because of that, *we must never let him see that the game is up. It’s got to be up, before he sees that it’s up﻿ ﻿… before he has time to hit back*.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

「喏，他们能不采取点行动么？」

「他们当然会，」曼塞尔说。「可他们会采取什么行动呢？」

「我不知道，」汉伯里说。「可是——」

「我也不知道，」曼塞尔说，「难就难在这里。若是由我来采取行动，那自然好。可他们绝不会答应。倘若我把话说得相当重，他们多半会带着两名本地警察和一名律师的文书找上门来。……还有，别忘了——无论采取什么行动，罗斯·诺布尔都会知道该谢谁。他会知道幕后是我，而他的回敬会是什么，我坦白说不敢想。你们知道，『货物易腐』。正因为如此，*我们绝不能让他看出戏已收场。这出戏必须先收了场，赶在他看出收场之前……赶在他来得及还手之前。*……」

===Original===

Half an hour later we were at the crossroads.

It was now eleven o’clock, and within a quarter of an hour our plans were laid.

For five miles about we were to prove the country, making no attempt at concealment and courting the observation of anyone watching from Gath: in this way, whilst appearing to search, we should gain such a knowledge of the district as might any moment be of the greatest use. In the course of our movements we were to visit two points from each of which it seemed likely that a man could look full upon Gath: these particular visits were, of course, to be surreptitiously made, and we hoped that they would help us to discover whether an endeavour to climb to the castle by the cliffs could possibly succeed.

Mansel and I would take one car, and Hanbury and Bell the other: each patrol was to go its own way, returning to the crossroads at sundown, en route for Lass.

Carson and Rowley, meanwhile, were to lie close in a wood and take their rest, “for tonight,” said Mansel, “you two must drive to Poganec and take Captain Pleydell our news.”

===Chinese===

半小时后，我们到了岔路口。

此时十一点钟，一刻钟之内，我们的计划便定当了。

我们要踏勘方圆五英里的乡野，毫不掩饰行藏，还要专候从加斯瞭望的人来看：这样一来，我们表面在搜索，实则把这一带的地形摸个通透，随时可能派上大用场。在行动中，我们还要造访两处地点，从那里似乎都能正面望尽加斯：这两处造访当然要偷偷进行，我们指望借此弄清，从悬崖攀上城堡的尝试究竟有没有成功的可能。

曼塞尔和我带一辆车，汉伯里和贝尔带另一辆：两队巡逻各行其路，日落时回到岔路口，取道去拉斯。

卡森和罗利则暂且伏在林中歇息；「因为今夜，」曼塞尔说，「你们两个必须驱车去波加内克，把我们的消息带给普莱德尔上尉。」

===Original===

“How much shall you tell him?” said I, as we took to the car.

“Only where he can find me and that we know where she is.”

Yet when the time came to send it, the note was longer than that.

It was half past three when Mansel and I began to ascend a mountain which we thought must command the castle and be commanded in turn. Its sides and peak were wooded, so we had little to fear. The Rolls we had left with Tester by the side of the way, and each of us carried a binocular of a considerable power.

At last we came to the summit, and almost at once we saw Gath, not directly opposed, but lying a little to the left, so that we could observe the whole of its south and east sides.

===Chinese===

「你要告诉他多少？」我们上车时我问。

「只告诉他到哪里寻得着我，还有——我们知道她在哪里。」

可到了发信的时候，那封信比这长。

三点半，曼塞尔和我开始登一座山；我们估摸，此山必能俯瞰城堡，也必受城堡回望。山腰和山顶皆有林木，所以我们没什么可怕的。劳斯莱斯连同特斯特留在路旁，我们两个各带一架倍数相当高的双筒望远镜。

终于我们登上山顶，几乎立刻便望见了加斯：不在正对面，而是偏左一些，于是它的南面和东面尽收我们眼底。

===Original===

I could dwell upon its emplacement, but I should waste my words, for I have not the pen to hail a miracle, and that is what we saw.

Enough that the face of the cliff was “produced” in the castle walls, and that Gath was the crown upon a crag that no man could ever scale.

So much the naked eye showed us.

It was the binoculars that showed us Adèle.

She was standing on the little terrace, with her hands on the balustrade. Whether she was watched I cannot say, but she was alone. Below her was the leaping cascade, and, behind, an archway, framing a door that was shut. She was standing very still; her head was up, and her eyes lifted to the peak upon which we stood.

===Chinese===

我尽可以细写它的地势，但那只是浪费笔墨，因为我没有那支能唤出奇迹的笔，而我们望见的，正是一个奇迹。

只消说一句便够：悬崖的立面被『延长』进了城墙，而加斯，便是戴在一座无人能够攀援的危岩头上的冠冕。

肉眼给我们看来的，就是这些。

是望远镜给我们看到了阿黛尔。

她正立在那座小小的台地上，双手搭着栏杆。有没有人监视她，我说不上，但她只身一人。她的脚下是奔跳的瀑布，身后一道拱洞，框着一扇关闭的门。她立得纹丝不动；头昂着，眼睛抬向我们立足的峰顶。

===Original===

Mansel tore off his wristwatch, ripped its case open and thrust the watch into my hand.

“Flash it in the sun,” he said, clapping the glasses to his eyes. “If she sees it, she’ll understand.”

The silver was bright as a mirror, and, while Mansel watched her, I twitched the case to and fro.

At last﻿—

“She’s seen it,” he said. “Now flash it six times, and cover it up with your hand between each flash.”

I did so.

===Chinese===

曼塞尔扯下腕表，撬开表壳，把表塞进我手里。

「对着太阳晃它，」他说着，把望远镜扣上眼睛。「她若看见，自会明白。」

银面亮得像一面镜子；曼塞尔望住她，我便把表壳一下一下来回轻晃。

终于——

「她看见了，」他说。「现在晃六下，每晃一下，用手遮一遮。」

我依言而行。

===Original===

“Now flash it once more,” said Mansel. “And then cover up.”

I did as he said.

After what seemed a long time﻿—

“Flash it again,” said he.

I did so: and, after a little, he told me to pocket the watch.

At last he put down his glasses and wiped the sweat from his face.

===Chinese===

「再晃一下，」曼塞尔说。「然后盖住。」

我照他说的做了。

仿佛过了许久——

「再晃一下，」他说。

我又晃了一下；过了片刻，他叫我收起表来。

终于他放下望远镜，抹去脸上的汗。

===Original===

“She saw the signals,” he said. “She raised her hand. When you had acknowledged her gesture, she turned away. She’s﻿—very wise.”

“Has she gone?”

He nodded.

“She went up the staircase into the left-hand tower. And, after a moment, she showed some white at that window, halfway up. When you acknowledged that, she took it away. She’s very wise﻿—Adèle.”

That was a true saying: only the quickest wit and the steadiest brain would ever have done so well. I think her heart must have leapt, but she took no needless risk. One wave of her hand, and then﻿—straight to her quarters to show us where she was lodged. And that was all. She had no more to tell us, and the game was a dangerous one. The sooner it was done, the better for all concerned.

And here for the first time I saw how well and truly she and Mansel were matched. They were made of the same fine substance, a little higher than their fellows, and could speak a common language which others did not know. And, as Mansel and I lay there, on the edge of the great gulf fixed between us and Gath, I remember wondering whether, could she have done so, Adèle would have kept such letters as she had received from him.

===Chinese===

「她看见信号了，」他说。「她举起一只手。你回应过她的手势，她便转过了身。她——非常聪明。」

「她走了么？」

他点了点头。

「她顺阶梯上进了左边的塔。过了一会儿，她在半腰那扇窗口露出一点白。你一回应，她便把白收走了。她非常聪明——阿黛尔。」

这话一点不假：只有最敏捷的心智、最沉着的头脑，才能做得这样漂亮。我想她的心必定跳了，可她不冒一丝无谓的风险。挥手一示意，随后——径直回自己的住处，告诉我们她被安置在哪里。如此而已。她再没有别的可告诉我们，而这出戏，本是性命交关的一出。越早收场，对所有相关的人越好。

也是在这里，我头一回看清，她和曼塞尔是何等天造地设的一对。他们由同一种精良的质料做成，比他们的同类略高出一头，能说一种旁人不懂的共同语言。当曼塞尔和我伏在那里、伏在我们与加斯之间那道被隔定的大渊的边上，我记得自己曾想：倘若她能够，阿黛尔会不会也把他写给她的那些信，一样地收着呢。

===Original===

At sundown we joined the others at the crossroads, and within the hour we sat down to supper at Lass.

When they had eaten, Carson and Rowley left for Poganec, taking a note with them.

This was brief, for Mansel was a man of few words.

===Chinese===

日落时分，我们与另外两队在岔路口会合；不出一个钟头，我们已在拉斯坐下用晚饭。

饭后，卡森和罗利带着一封信动身去波加内克。

信很简短，因为曼塞尔本是话少的人。

===Original===

> I know where Adèle is confined and saw her today from a distance. She seemed to be well. I am glad to say that I managed to attract her attention, and she now knows that help is at hand. I have reason to think that she is comfortably lodged. If need be, I can be found at the Three Kings Hotel, Lass. Please destroy this note and communicate its contents to no one but Daphne and Berry alone.

===Chinese===

> 我知道阿黛尔被囚之处，今日曾自远处望见她。她似已安好。我欣慰地说，我设法引得了她的注意，她现已知道救援在即。我有理由相信，她被安置得舒适。如有需要，可在拉斯的三王旅馆（Three Kings Hotel）寻得着我。请将此信销毁，其内容除达夫妮与贝里二人之外，不得告知任何人。

## V Mansel Takes Off the Gloves / 五 曼塞尔摊牌

===Original===
At ten o’clock the next morning Mansel, Carson and I visited the Castle of Gath.

From first to last this visit had been closely rehearsed, so that even a spy in the wood would not, I think, have suspected that we were playacting.

Indeed, the play began some miles away, for we first seemed to notice the drive, as we were returning to the crossroads after a thirty-mile run. After due hesitation we determined to see whither it led.﻿ ﻿…

At the sight of the Castle we stopped, as anyone would have done, and were plainly uncertain whether or no to proceed: but, after a little discussion, I drove the car down the spur and drew up before the gateway in a perfectly natural way. Then Carson opened a door, and Mansel got out.

When he had rung, he stood waiting, with a hand on the great stone jamb, while Carson, with his hands behind him stood leaning against a wing, and I pulled out tobacco and started to fill a pipe.

I shall never forget those moments or how hard it was to keep cool. Eyes were upon us, watching each breath we drew: it was likely that we were covered and certain that we were at the mercy of those we were seeking to dupe. What was about to happen, no one could tell. For once Rose Noble was dealing, and, whatever the cards he dealt us, with those we should have to play.

===Chinese===
第二天上午十点，曼塞尔、卡森和我造访了加斯城堡。

这次造访从头至尾都经过周密排演，因此我想，纵然林中藏着坐探，也决计看不出我们是在做戏。

说实在的，这出戏在几英里外便开演了：我们先驱车跑了三十英里，在返回十字路口的途中，才仿佛初次留意到那条私家车道。经过一番像模像样的踌躇，我们决定去看看它究竟通向何处。……

一望见城堡，我们便停了车——换了谁也会这样——并且显而易见地拿不准去还是不去：可是略作商议之后，我便把车驶下山嘴，以再自然不过的姿态在门前停稳。随后卡森打开车门，曼塞尔下了车。

他拉过门铃之后，便一只手搭在巨大的石门框上站着等候；卡森背着手，斜倚着车身一侧；我则掏出烟丝，动手装填烟斗。

我永远忘不了那几分钟，也忘不了要保持镇定有多么艰难。多少双眼睛正盯着我们，注视我们的每一次呼吸：很可能已有枪口罩住了我们，而可以断定的是，我们全凭那些我们想要蒙骗的人发落。接下来会发生什么，谁也说不上来。这一回发牌的是罗斯·诺布尔，而且无论他发给我们什么牌，我们都只能拿这手牌打下去。

===Original===
The day was very fine, and a gentle breeze was blowing across the spur: except for the whisper of the engine, there was no sound: and I remember thinking how gay the greenwood looked in the brave sunshine and how black the clean-cut shadow which the battlements threw upon the turf.

After a little, Mansel rang again.

For an age we waited: then I heard a step on the pavement and the click of a lock. Then two bolts were drawn, the wicket swung open, and a woman put out her head.

“Good day,” said Mansel, using German. “What’s the name of this place?”

“The Castle of Gath, sir.”

“Who lives here?”

===Chinese===
那天天气极好，一阵和风掠过山嘴：除了发动机的低语，四下没有一点声息：我记得自己当时还想，明媚的阳光下，绿林显得多么欢快，而雉堞投在草地上的那道轮廓分明的影子，又是多么黢黑。

略过片刻，曼塞尔又拉了一次铃。

我们等了足足半晌：随后我听见石径上响起脚步声，还有锁舌一响。接着两道门闩抽开，小门荡开，一个妇人探出头来。

「您好，」曼塞尔用德语说。「这地方叫什么名字？」

「加斯城堡，先生。」

「谁住在这儿？」

===Original===
“No one, sir. My husband and I are the caretakers.”

“D’you know if it’s for sale?”

The woman shook her head.

“It’s not for sale, sir.”

“Which is the castle hereabouts which is for sale?”

“I have no idea, sir.”

===Chinese===
「没有谁，先生。我和我丈夫是看管人。」

「您知道它出卖不出卖？」

妇人摇了摇头。

「它不出卖，先生。」

「这一带待售的是哪座城堡？」

「我毫无所知，先生。」

===Original===
“Well, there’s one somewhere,” said Mansel, “not very far from here. It belongs to an English lady, who’s out of her mind. She drives about in a car without any hat, and she has male nurses with her wherever she goes. Surely you’ve heard her spoken of?”

“No, sir,” said the woman, evenly.

“A big, closed car, painted grey. Sometimes the nurses use it when she is ill.”

The woman shook her head.

“You surprise me,” said Mansel. “It’s common talk down in Lass.”

The woman shrugged her shoulders.

===Chinese===
「唔，反正有那么一座，」曼塞尔说，「离这儿不太远。主人是位英国太太，神志失常。她乘车四处走，头上连顶帽子也不戴，走到哪儿都有男看护跟着。您总该听人说起过她吧？」

「没有，先生，」妇人不紧不慢地说。

「一辆灰色的大型厢式汽车。她犯病的时候，看护们有时也用那辆车。」

妇人摇了摇头。

「这可让我意外了，」曼塞尔说。「拉斯那下面都传遍了。」

妇人耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“In Lass, perhaps, but we are so isolated here.”

“I wonder if your husband could help me.”

“I fear he is out, sir. But I do not think he would know.”

“Which way has he gone?” said Mansel.

The woman mentioned a hamlet four miles away.

“Perhaps I shall meet him,” said Mansel. “But ask him about this grey car when he comes in.”

===Chinese===
「拉斯兴许如此，可我们这儿实在太偏僻了。」

「不知您丈夫能不能帮我这个忙。」

「恐怕他出去了，先生。不过我想他也不会知道。」

「他往哪边去了？」曼塞尔说。

妇人说了个四英里外的村子。

「说不定我能碰上他，」曼塞尔说。「不过等他回来，请您问问这辆灰汽车的事。」

===Original===
“I will, sir.”

“And the castle, or house, somewhere round about here. And, if he thinks he can help me, send him to The Three Kings at Lass.”

“I will, sir.”

Mansel gave her money and returned to the car.

“Nothing doing,” he said shortly. “I was afraid it was useless. Too much off the beaten track.”

As he spoke, the wicket was shut, and the bolts were shot.

===Chinese===
「我会问的，先生。」

「还有这一带什么地方的城堡，或者宅子。要是他觉得帮得上忙，请他到拉斯的三王旅馆（The Three Kings）来找我。」

「我会的，先生。」

曼塞尔赏了她一点钱，回到车上。

「毫无所得，」他简短地说。「我就怕是白费劲。这地方太远离人走的道了。」

他说话间，小门关上了，门闩也插上了。

===Original===
“Let’s have a look at the map,” said Mansel.

I gave him the sheet.

As I did so, the sharp clack of wood striking wood came from the door. We all looked round﻿—naturally enough. *Someone had drawn the shutter which masked the grill.* No doubt they would have done so in silence; but the shutter, I suppose, resisted, and then gave way with a rush.

Mansel laughed.

“Seeing us off the premises,” he said. He returned to the map. “There’s the village she spoke of: we might as well go that way.” He began to fold up the map and turned to the door. “By the way,” he said, using German, “was your husband on foot?”

As he spoke, he stepped to the grill, pocketing the map as he went.

===Chinese===
「咱们看看地图，」曼塞尔说。

我把那一页图递给他。

正递图时，门口传来木头磕碰木头的脆响。我们全都回头望去——这再自然不过。*有人拉上了那块遮住铁栅的护窗板。*毫无疑问，他们本想悄没声地办妥这件事；可我猜那护窗板先是不肯就范，随即又猛地一下滑了就位。

曼塞尔笑了。

「这是在送我们出门呢，」他说。他重新看地图。「她说的村子在这儿：咱们不妨打那儿走。」他动手折起地图，转身朝向门口。「顺便问一句，」他又用起德语来，「您丈夫是步行的吗？」

说话间，他朝铁栅踱去，一面走一面把地图揣进口袋。

===Original===
No answer was given.

With his hand in his pocket, Mansel peered through the bars.

“I say,” he said. “My good lady.﻿ ﻿…”

No one replied.

“Come on,” said I. “She’s scared.”

Mansel took his hand from his pocket and turned away.

===Chinese===
没有人应声。

曼塞尔一只手插在口袋里，眯眼贴着铁条往里张望。

「喂，」他说。「我的好太太。……」

无人回答。

「走吧，」我说。「她吓着了。」

曼塞尔把手从口袋里抽出来，转身走开。

===Original===
“That’s the worst of these people,” he said. “No sense, no observation and an inherent fear that you’re trying to do them down.”

With that, he got into the car.

Carson followed, and I drove slowly away. Not until we were five miles off did Mansel open his mouth.

“Rose Noble was there all the time. On the woman’s left. He was standing with his back to the doors, with his arms folded and a pistol in his right hand. I could see him in the glass of a lantern that hangs from the archway roof: its sides were tilted, and it couldn’t have been better placed.

“It was he that drew the shutter and stood looking out: that’s an assumption, of course; but I’m sure it’s correct. I went back in the hope that he’d stay there﻿—to laugh in my face. But he very properly didn’t. He resisted a great temptation and thereby saved his life. He wouldn’t have expected a bullet, and I don’t think I could have missed. When he saw me coming, he stooped. If he’d moved, I should have heard him: so he stood where he was and stooped. And that was why the woman never came back: he was in the way, and she couldn’t get to the grill.”

===Chinese===
「这种人最要命的就是这个，」他说。「没头脑，没眼力，还天生怕你要坑害他们。」

说完，他上了车。

卡森跟着上了车，我缓缓把车开走。直到驶出五英里之外，曼塞尔才开口。

「罗斯·诺布尔从头到尾都在那儿。就在那妇人左手边。他背对着大门站着，双臂合抱，右手握着一支手枪。我是从拱道顶上悬着的一盏提灯的玻璃里看见他的：那灯的四面斜斜相对，位置再好不过。

「拉护窗板、又站在后面往外张望的正是他：这当然是推测；可我敢肯定没错。我走回去，就是指望他还待在那儿——好当面笑话我。可他很有分寸，没有这么干。他抵制住了一个极大的诱惑，也就此保住了性命。他料不到会有一颗子弹，而我不认为我会打偏。他看见我走过去，便弯下了腰。他若是挪动，我必定听得见：所以他站在原地，弯下腰去。那妇人之所以再没露面，也是这个缘故：他挡在那儿，她没法走到铁栅跟前来。」

===Original===
Then he turned to Carson and asked him how much he had seen.

“The walls are forty feet high, sir﻿—that is, from the gaps to the ground.”

“ ‘Embrasures’ they’re called,” said Mansel. “Yes?”

“I’m sure they’re not more, sir: they may be a foot or so less. The first windows are fourteen feet up, but they’re very heavily barred: so are the ones above. There’s no downpipe at all and no ledges that you could hold.”

“Then we must have a ladder?”

“Three, sir. Each twelve feet long. The first hooks on to the bars of a window fourteen feet up: the second on to the bars of a window above: and the third to the top of the wall.”

===Chinese===
随后他转向卡森，问他又看出了多少。

「墙高四十英尺，先生——就是从那些缺口量到地面。」

「那叫『垛口』，」曼塞尔说。「还有呢？」

「我敢肯定高不过四十英尺，先生：兴许还矮一英尺上下。头排窗户离地十四英尺，但都装着极粗的铁条：上头的窗也一样。墙上压根没有水落管，也没有一处可供攀抓的凸沿。」

「那就得用梯子了？」

「三架，先生。每架十二英尺长。第一架钩在十四英尺高的窗栏上：第二架钩在再上一层窗户的铁条上：第三架钩上墙头。」

===Original===
“Very good,” said Mansel. “Are the windows above each other?”

“No, sir. Clear by about a foot. But you go up the left of the first and the right of the one above. There’s a gap﻿—embrasure directly between the two.”

“I see,” said Mansel. “Wrought-iron, one-pole ladders, made by a village smith: ends and rungs covered with rubber tubing, so that they make no noise.”

“I hadn’t thought of that, sir.”

“You would have,” said Mansel. “Once they’re in place, it’ll be like going upstairs.”

“A rough night would help, sir.”

===Chinese===
「很好，」曼塞尔说。「上下两层窗户正对着吗？」

「不对，先生。错开一英尺光景。不过您从第一扇的左边上去，再从上面那扇的右边上去。两扇窗正中间有一处缺口——垛口。」

「明白了，」曼塞尔说。「锻铁打的单杆梯，交给村里的铁匠做：梯头和踏档都套上胶皮管，这样便不会出声。」

「这我倒没想到，先生。」

「你早晚会的，」曼塞尔说。「梯子一架好，就跟上楼梯差不多。」

「要是碰上个风雨夜就更好了，先生。」

===Original===
“We must hope for one,” said Mansel, “in two days’ time. And now, Chandos, let me drive. We must find a forge: and it’s got to be forty miles off.”

The tale we told the smith is of no consequence: the ladders were simple to make, and Mansel’s directions were clear: we were to find the work done by the evening of the following day.

Then Mansel found a rope factory and purchased a quantity of rope, after which we drove to some town, whose name I forget, where we bought what else we had need of to aid our assault. All this gear we presently hid in a dell﻿—a pretty, private place, high up in the fold of a mountain, some ten miles by road from Gath. There we could work upon it during the next two days, and thence take it direct to the Castle when the moment came.

Of foul weather we had not much hope, for the sky was clean, and Mansel’s barometer set fair: “however,” said he, “I’m not going to wait any more. At two on Friday morning we’re going over the top: even if we don’t get Adèle, we shall see the inside of that rattrap, and that’ll be devilish useful next time we come.”

How much we were nowadays watched I do not know. A spy can go out, but he is plainly useless unless he can later come in; and, in view of the Casemate business﻿—to say nothing of that of Jute﻿—I fancy Rose Noble was shy of sending his subordinates further than the edge of the wood. Had he but known, he might have spared his concern: the last thing we wished was to be led to the Castle, and Mansel had given orders that, if we saw anyone watching, we were, if we possibly could, to turn a blind eye.

===Chinese===
「咱们就指望两天之后来上这么一场吧，」曼塞尔说，「现在，钱多斯，让我来开车。我们得找一家铁匠铺：而且非在四十英里外不可。」

我们对铁匠编的说辞无关紧要：梯子打制起来不难，曼塞尔的吩咐也交代得一清二楚：第二天傍晚，我们就能取到活计。

随后曼塞尔寻到一家制绳厂，购下一批绳索；之后我们又驱车去了一座城镇——名字我忘了——在那儿置办了助攻所需的其他物件。这一应家伙什，我们随即藏进一处小谷——一个秀气而隐蔽的所在，高踞山坳之中，离加斯约有十英里车程。接下来两天我们可以在那儿摆弄它们，时机一到，再从那里直运城堡。

对于坏天气我们没抱多大指望，因为天空明净无云，曼塞尔的晴雨表也指着晴：「不过，」他说，「我不打算再等了。星期五凌晨两点，我们就翻墙冲锋：就算救不到阿黛尔，我们也能看看那座捕鼠笼的内部，下回来时大有用处。」

如今我们受监视到什么程度，我不得而知。坐探出得去，可要是回不来，那也分明无用；况且想到凯斯梅特那桩事——更不必说朱特那一桩——我料想罗斯·诺布尔也不放心把部下派出林缘以外。他若知道内情，本可不必操这份心：我们最不愿意的就是被人引到城堡去；曼塞尔早已下过命令，倘若看见有人盯梢，只要办得到，就只当没看见。

===Original===
Now not to look for a spy is easy enough: yet, because, perhaps, we did not want him, Fate must needs deliver one into our hands.

This was the way of it.

Both cars left Lass the next morning at eight o’clock. We were bound for the dell, where there was work to be done. Hitherto, on reaching the crossroads, the cars had gone different ways: but today both took the road out of which ran the drive which served Gath, so that, if someone was watching, he should be able for once to account for us all. Mansel was leading, and I was sitting with Hanbury, who was driving the second car.

No doubt our ways were known; but, be that as it may, when Mansel had swept past the drive, a man rose out of the bushes, stepped to the edge of the road and stood watching the car out of sight.

To ignore him was out of the question: we were less than a hundred yards off. If he ran, we were bound to give chase; and we were three to one. We might contrive to lose him, but you cannot run through a wood without declaring your line, and, unless he had a fair start, such a failure would be instantly suspected, if not by the spy, by Rose Noble, the moment he made his report.

“Take him aboard,” said George. “It’s the only way.”

===Chinese===
要说不去留意坐探，本来再容易不过：可是，也许正因为我们不想要他，命运偏偏非要送一个到我们手里。

事情是这样发生的。

第二天早上八点，两部车一齐驶离拉斯。我们要去那处小谷，那里有活计要做。以往到了十字路口，两部车总分道而行：今天却双双拐上那条有岔道通往加斯的大路，这样一来，倘若有人盯梢，他总算能把我们全伙的去向交代个明白。曼塞尔的车在前，我和汉伯里坐在第二部车里，由他驾驶。

毫无疑问，我们的行踪是为人所知的；但不管怎样，曼塞尔的车一掠过那条岔道，便有一个汉子从灌木丛里立起，走到路边，目送着车影消失。

对他视而不见是办不到的：我们离他不到一百码。他若拔腿跑，我们就非追不可；何况我们是他三个人对一个。我们或许可以想法子把他甩脱，可人一钻进树林就必定暴露自己的路线；除非让他先跑出一大截，否则这样的失手立刻就会惹人疑心——坐探未必看得出来，可等他一报告，罗斯·诺布尔立时便知。

「把他弄上车，」乔治说。「只有这个办法。」

===Original===
That this was so became increasingly clear, for we made no manner of sound, and the man was absorbed in his view of Mansel’s car. Indeed, I had no time to think and barely enough to act. The man had no time to do either.

As we passed, I took him by the neck, and Bell leaned out behind me and dragged his legs into the car.

Not until then did I see that it was Jute.

He did not attempt to struggle; but I held him as I had seized him, till Bell had strapped together his ankles and wrists. Then we took a pistol from his pocket and put him on the floor of the car. And so we had meant to leave him, but such was his criticism of our conduct that after a little we gagged him with a handful of cotton waste.

===Chinese===
此话不假，而且愈发显明：我们未出半点声响，那汉子又正专心致志地望着曼塞尔的车。说实话，我连想的工夫都没有，动手的工夫也刚刚够。那汉子则连想和动的工夫一概没有。

车子擦身而过时，我一把扼住他的脖颈，贝尔在我身后探出身来，把他的双腿拖进了车厢。

直到那时我才看清，此人竟是朱特。

他没有试图挣扎；但我照起初扼住的架式按着他，直到贝尔把他的脚踝和手腕捆在了一处。随后我们从他口袋里缴下一支手枪，把他搁在车厢底板上。我们本打算就这么由他去，可他对我们行事的那一番批评实在难听，不多时我们便拿一把棉纱头堵上了他的嘴。

---


===Original===
“Understand this,” said Mansel. “It’s entirely your fault that you’re here. Chandos would have ignored you, but you didn’t give him a chance. You served your turn very well, but I finished with you at Lass. A man of your parts should have known that and have taken the greatest care to keep out of my way.”

Jute made no answer, and presently Mansel went on.

“I have no time for a prisoner, for prisoners must be watered and fed. So I’m going to do one of two things. Which I do will depend upon you. Either I return you to Rose Noble, or else I hang you by the neck.”

“Murder?” said Jute, and laughed.

“Murder,” said Mansel, beginning to fill a pipe.

===Chinese===
「你要明白，」曼塞尔说，「你落到这步田地，全是你自己的错。钱多斯本可以只当没看见你，可你没给他这个机会。你那一趟差事当得很好，但在拉斯我就跟你两讫了。像你这样有两下子的人，本该明白这一点，也本该格外小心，别撞进我的道上来。」

朱特没有作声，过了一会儿，曼塞尔又说下去。

「我没工夫看管俘虏，因为俘虏总得给水给饭。所以我要在两件事里做一件。做哪一件，全看你自己。要么我把你交还罗斯·诺布尔，要么我就吊死你。」

「谋杀吗？」朱特说，还笑了。

「谋杀，」曼塞尔说着，动手装起烟斗来。

===Original===
I glanced round the dell.

The spot was peaceful: a gurgling brook, a little lawn and the shade of spreading trees made it seem fit for a shepherd’s piping-match. Jute and all of us looked curiously out of place.

Perhaps, because of this, I had a strange feeling that I should presently awake and find that I had been dreaming, and to this day, recalling the happenings of that sunshiny morning, I seem to be remembering some vision rather than a downright business of life and death.

Mansel was speaking.

“Now, if I return you to Rose Noble, I shall take you up to the Castle and watch you go in. That is, if it’s dark. If it’s during the day, I shall watch from the wood. You see, I don’t want to be seen.”

===Chinese===
我环顾了一下这处小谷。

这地方一片安宁：一弯淙淙的小溪，一小片草茵，加上舒展的树荫，倒像是个该让牧人赛笛的去处。朱特和我们这一伙人站在里面，都显得说不出的格格不入。

也许正因如此，我生出一种奇异的感觉，仿佛转眼就要醒转，发现方才不过一场梦；直到今天，一回想起那个阳光明媚的上午发生的事，我仍觉得记起的与其说是一件性命交关的正经事，不如说是一场幻境。

曼塞尔正在说话。

「这么着吧，我若是把你交还罗斯·诺布尔，就会把你带上城堡，看着你进去。那是说，得天黑才行。若是白天，我就从林子里望。你要知道，我不想让人瞧见。」

===Original===
Jute’s face was a study.

“Now, in view of what Rose Noble said when you reported, after you had ‘led me to Lass’ ”﻿—I saw Jute start﻿—“I imagine your next meeting will be even less cordial﻿—*unless you return precisely when you are expected, and say nothing of having met me*. I mean, he might easily argue that you had ‘led me to’ Gath.”

His eyes upon Mansel’s face, Jute was plainly thinking extremely hard.

Mansel continued slowly, pressing his tobacco home.

“I can’t return you today, because I’ve too much to do. In fact﻿—”

===Chinese===
朱特那张脸可真值得玩味。

「再说了，想想你回去禀报、说是『把我引到了拉斯』之后，罗斯·诺布尔说过些什么话」——我看见朱特浑身一震——「我想你们下回见面，只怕更说不上亲热——*除非你掐着他们等你的钟点分毫不差地回去，并且只字不提撞见了我*。我是说，他满可以一口咬定，是你『把我引到了』加斯。」

朱特两眼盯着曼塞尔的脸，显然在飞快地动脑筋。

曼塞尔慢悠悠地说下去，一面把烟丝按实。

「今天我不能送你回去，因为我手头的事太多。其实——」

===Original===
“See here,” said Jute. “You can make it tomorrow night. Do that, and I haven’t seen you since you went by in the car.”

“Don’t try to bluff,” said Mansel. “It’s only wasting my time. You haven’t a card. You had quite a good one about five seconds ago: but I’ve just drawn that. You see, I wanted to know whether Rose Noble would worry if you didn’t come in tonight.”

I watched the blood come into the other’s face.

Mansel continued in the same even tone.

“I tell you this to show you that it’s no good playing with me. Bear that in mind. And now to business.”

I cannot describe the coldness with which Mansel spoke: there was no insolence in his speech, only an iron contempt, which must, I think, have entered into the other’s soul.

===Chinese===
「听我说，」朱特道。「你可以挪到明天夜里。就这么办，从你们坐车过去之后，我就没见过你们。」

「别虚张声势了，」曼塞尔说。「那不过是白费我的工夫。你手里一张牌也没有。五秒钟之前你还有一张相当好的：可我刚把它抽走了。你要知道，我想弄清楚的是，你今夜若不回去，罗斯·诺布尔会不会着急。」

我看着血色涌上对方那张脸。

曼塞尔还是用同样平缓的声调说下去。

「我把这个告诉你，是要让你明白，跟我耍花样没有用处。把这一点记牢。现在谈正事。」

曼塞尔说话时那股冷劲，我无法描摹：他的话里没有半点倨傲，只有一种铁一般的轻蔑，我想，那必定直透进对方的灵魂里去了。

===Original===
“I’m going to ask you some questions, and I’ll allow you one lie: if you tell two lies, I shall hang you from a branch of that oak.”

“And you talk about bluff,” sneered Jute.

“It’s not bluff,” said Mansel. “I’ve got the gloves right off. Two lies, and you’re for the high jump, as sure as I’m sitting still. And now we’ll begin. Assume you’re on the ramparts above the gateway. How would you go from there to where Mrs. Pleydell lies?”

Jute gave a short laugh.

“I thought,” he said, “you’d finished with me at Lass.”

“So I had,” said Mansel, bringing a match to his pipe.

===Chinese===
「我要问你几个问题，容你说一句谎：你要是扯了两句谎，我就把你吊上那棵橡树的枝子。」

「亏你还说别人虚张声势，」朱特讥笑道。

「这不是虚张声势，」曼塞尔说。「我这副手套可是摘了个干净。两句谎话，你就等着上绞架，就像我此刻稳稳坐着一样确凿。现在咱们开始。假设你站在大门上方的城台上。从那儿到普莱德尔太太躺的地方，你怎么走？」

朱特短促地笑了一声。

「我还当，」他说，「你在拉斯就跟我两讫了。」

「是两讫了，」曼塞尔说着，把一根火柴凑到烟斗上。

===Original===
“Looks like it, doesn’t it?”

“I don’t care what it looks like,” said Mansel. “But I think you may as well know that I’m pressed for time: and, if you elect to be hanged, I shall have less still, because it’ll take two hours to dig you a grave.”

“Rake it out,” said Jute sharply. “I know your shape. You can wear a gun on your back side, but it’ll never fit. You’re out of your depth, Mansel: and, if you take my advice, you’ll kick for the shore. Your job’s to pay and be damned. We’ve got your girl, and﻿—”

“Carson,” said Mansel, “get a rope on that bough. Timber hitch on the wood, slip knot the other end.”

For a moment the servants spoke together. Then Bell was on Rowley’s shoulders and up in the tree, and Carson was down in a gully, with a knife in his hand. The next minute he reappeared, with a coil of rope on his arm.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「瞧这光景，倒像是两讫了，不是吗？」

「像不像我不管，」曼塞尔说。「不过我想你不妨知道，我时间紧得很：你要是选择挨吊，我的时间就更紧了，因为还得花两个钟头给你挖个坑。」

「收起这一套吧，」朱特尖利地说。「你的底细我清楚。你背后别一杆枪唬人尽可以，到底不合身。你水已经没顶了，曼塞尔：听我一句劝，趁早往岸边蹬水吧。你们的本分是掏钱，掏完了见鬼去。你的女人在我们手里，还有——」

「卡森，」曼塞尔说，「拿条绳子上那根枝子。贴木头那头打个木材结，另一头打个活结。」

仆人们低声商议了片刻。随即贝尔踩上罗利的肩头攀进树里，卡森提着刀下到一道沟里。转眼他又现身出来，臂上挽着一圈绳索。……

===Original===
I knew that Mansel was bluffing, for he was not a hard man. He would have killed Rose Noble, for he was the head of the corner, and, with his death, the conspiracy would have gone. He would have killed anyone whose death would materially help him to reach Adèle. But a spy that, when taken, refused to open his mouth, was as safe in Mansel’s hands as a priest on his altar steps. I knew he would never hang Jute, though God knows he had just cause. Mansel was bluffing: and I was greatly afraid that the fellow would call his bluff.

Mansel returned to Jute.

“As I said, I’ll give you one lie. A refusal counts as a lie. Assume you’re on the ramparts above the gateway. How would you go from there to where Mrs. Pleydell lies?”

After a long silence﻿—

“Which way am I facing?” said Jute sullenly.

“You are facing the wood.”

===Chinese===
我知道曼塞尔是在唬人，因为他不是个狠心的人。他会杀罗斯·诺布尔，因为罪魁祸首正是此人，他一死，整个阴谋便会随之瓦解。凡是一死便能实实在在帮他够到阿黛尔的人，他都下得去手。可是坐探一经被擒便闭口不言的，落在曼塞尔手里，就跟站在祭坛台阶上的教士一样安全。我知道他绝不会绞死朱特，虽然天知道他理由十足。曼塞尔是在唬人：而我非常害怕这家伙偏要拆穿他。

曼塞尔回到朱特跟前。

「照我方才说的，容你一句谎。拒绝回答也算一句谎。假设你站在大门上方的城台上。从那儿到普莱德尔太太躺的地方，你怎么走？」

沉默了良久——

「我面朝哪边？」朱特阴沉着脸说。

「面朝树林。」

===Original===
Jute shut his eyes.

“I turn to the right,” he said, “and go as far as the tower. Then I turn again and walk along by the wall. At the end of that I come to another tower. There’s a door there.”

“In the tower?”

“Yes.”

“Go on.”

“You go through that and down steps till you come to a hall. Cross this to the door in front. That leads you into a room out of which runs a flight of stairs.”

===Chinese===
朱特阖上眼睛。

「我向右转，」他说，「一直走到那座塔楼跟前。然后我再拐个弯，贴着墙走。走到头，又是一座塔楼。那儿有一道门。」

「在塔楼里？」

「是。」

「说下去。」

「穿过那道门，下台阶，一直下到一座厅堂。横穿过厅堂，到正面的那道门。那道门通进一个房间，房间里有道楼梯。」

===Original===
“Yes.”

“Go down them, and they’ll bring you into her room.”

“Or the chapel?” said Mansel quietly.

Jute started violently. Then he glared at Mansel, with a working face.

“That’s one lie,” said Mansel.

Jute let himself go.

===Chinese===
「嗯。」

「顺着楼梯下去，就进她的房间了。」

「要不然，就是小教堂？」曼塞尔轻声说。

朱特猛地一颤。随后他死死瞪着曼塞尔，脸上的肌肉直抽动。

「这是一句谎，」曼塞尔说。

朱特索性撒开了。

===Original===
Out of a foam of imprecation odd sentences thrust, like timbers plunging in a flood.

“I’ll see you, you one-legged —﻿—﻿—﻿ ﻿… Put it over, you movie king﻿ ﻿… When next I meet you, I’ll cut my name on your back﻿ ﻿… We’ve got the goods, and, by —﻿—﻿—, we’ll make you sweat﻿ ﻿… ‘Cut flowers’ won’t be in it﻿ ﻿… I’ll make you covet the day you played me up.”

Mansel put his pipe in his pocket and rose to his feet.

“You refuse to answer?”

A beastly light slid into Jute’s bloodshot eyes.

“My answer’s here,” he said, glancing down at his coat. “You were to have had it tonight, but, if you’re not too ‘pressed for time,’ perhaps you’ll look at it now.”

===Chinese===
咒骂的泡沫里戳出一句句怪话，像洪流里翻滚下泻的木料。

「我要找你算账，你这独腿的—— ——……装模作样吧，你这电影大王……下回再见着你，我要在你背上刻下我的名字……货在我们手里，凭—— ——起誓，我们要叫你出汗……『剪花』那一手根本排不上号……我要叫你恨不得把跟我作对的那一天吞回肚里。」

曼塞尔把烟斗收进口袋，站起身来。

「你拒绝回答？」

一道恶兽般的凶光溜进朱特布满血丝的眼睛。

「我的回话在这儿，」他说着，朝自己的上衣瞟了一眼。「本该今晚才轮到你瞧的，不过，您要是没那样『时间紧』，现在就不妨看看。」

===Original===
To this day I cannot tell what possessed the man.

I suppose he could not forgive Mansel for beating him at his own game: the thought that all the antics of Hannibal Rouse had been gravely accepted at exactly their proper worth, the memory of the trap into which he had so readily rushed and the bitter reception which he had met at Gath had, I think, inspired a hatred which knew no law. And now to be again confounded, outwitted and scornfully reduced had sent the blood to his head.

“What do you mean?” said Mansel.

“Try my inside pocket,” said Jute.

At a nod from Mansel I stepped to the fellow’s side and took a bulging envelope out of his coat.

“That’s my answer,” said Jute, “to all your backchat today. And, between you and me, Big Willie, I guess it’s pretty complete.”

===Chinese===
直到今天我也说不清，这人是着了什么魔。

我想，他是不能原谅曼塞尔在他自己的行当里赢了他：一想到汉尼拔·劳斯那些滑稽把戏，竟被人郑重其事地恰恰按其实际分量照单全收；一想起自己那么爽快就一头扎进的圈套，还有他在加斯挨的那顿苦待——我想，这些已在他心里激起一股无法无天的仇恨。而如今又一次被人挫败、算计、又教人轻蔑地收拾得服服帖帖，血便一齐冲上了他的脑袋。

「你这是什么意思？」曼塞尔说。

「摸摸我衣服内袋，」朱特说。

经曼塞尔一点头，我便走到这家伙身边，从他上衣里掏出一只鼓鼓囊囊的信封。

「这就是我的回话，」朱特说，「回敬你今天所有这些花腔。咱们私下里说句实话，大个子威利，我看这回话是相当周全了。」

===Original===
Mansel ripped open the paper and took out a white silk blouse.﻿ ﻿…

I thought he would never move.

After a long time, very slowly he lifted his head.

“Call the servants,” he said, “and put the gag in his mouth.”

He spoke so low that I scarcely heard what he said: but, with his words, I knew that Jute’s hour was come.

For a moment the glade seemed misty, and my knees loose. Then my head cleared.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔撕开封纸，取出一件白绸女衬衫。……

我以为他再也不会动弹了。

过了许久，他才极缓慢地抬起头来。

「把仆人们叫来，」他说，「把布团塞进他嘴里。」

他说得极低，我几乎没有听清；可话一出口，我便知道，朱特的时辰到了。

有一瞬间，林间空地仿佛蒙上了雾，我的双膝也发了软。随后我的头脑清明过来。

===Original===
I saw Jute’s eyes follow Hanbury, as he stepped to the oak: then his gaze flashed to me, as I picked up the cotton waste. When I approached, he recoiled.

Sharply he looked at Mansel, and caught his breath.

“You﻿—you’d never dare,” he said hoarsely.

At last we had the gag in his mouth﻿ ﻿…

“This man,” said Mansel, “is engaged in one of the vilest crimes. In his lust for money he is not content to play even that filthy game according to its filthy rules.” He held up the blouse. “*He is trying to win by taking Mrs. Pleydell’s clothes from her back and advertising that outrage to make me throw in my hand.* I do not think that a man who does that is fit to live.”

His eyes bulging out of his head, Jute fell upon his knees.

===Chinese===
我看见朱特的目光跟着走向橡树的汉伯里移动：随后他眼锋一闪，又落到拾起棉纱头的我身上。我一走近，他便往后缩。

他猛地看向曼塞尔，倒抽一口气。

「你——你可不敢，」他声音嘶哑地说。

末了，我们终于把布团塞进了他嘴里……

「这个人，」曼塞尔说，「干的是天下最卑污的罪行中的一桩。他贪财成性，连那桩肮脏勾当按其肮脏规矩来玩，他都不满足。」他举起那件女衬衫。「*他妄想靠扒走普莱德尔太太身上的衣裳、再把这桩暴行四处张扬，逼我拱手认输，来赢下这一局。*我认为，干出这种事的人，不配活在世上。」

朱特双眼凸出眼眶，扑通跪倒在地。

===Original===
Mansel turned to Hanbury and me.

“You will return to the cars and wait there until I send.”

We did as he said.

Two hours later, Rowley brought us back to the dell.

Mansel was sitting smoking, with a distant look in his eyes: as he worked, jacketing a crowbar, Carson was whistling to himself: Bell was wiping a spade with a handful of grass.

The contented mien of the servants, if nothing else, showed that the world was the cleaner.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔转向汉伯里和我。

「你们回车那儿去，在那儿等我传话。」

我们照他的话办了。

两个钟头之后，罗利把我们领回了小谷。

曼塞尔坐着抽烟，眼中一副恍惚出神的神情：卡森一面干活、给一根撬棍加着护套，一面自顾自吹着口哨：贝尔正用一把青草擦拭铁锹。

哪怕别的且不论，单看仆人们那副心满意足的气色，便知道这世界又干净了几分。

---


===Original===
By half past one the next morning all of us, except Bell, were standing upon the roof of the Castle of Gath.

The night was starlit, but the moon had set.

Each of us carried a knife as well as a pistol and wore a coil of rope, like a sash. Bell was at the foot of the ladders, with a signal-cord in his hand, and Tester was back in the wood, guarding the cars.

The castle was built foursquare, as college buildings, about a great courtyard. Its roof was flat and paved and so made a spacious rampart, which the battlements fenced upon one hand and a massive balustrade upon the other.

Indeed, standing there in the starlight, we seemed to be upon some ectype of the walls of Babylon, upon which, if I rightly remember, six chariots could be driven abreast.

Peering between the balusters, I could see the ripple of the water of which the bookseller had written and could hear it fall out of the basin on its way to the terrace and the cliff.

===Chinese===
次日凌晨一点半，我们全体——除了贝尔——都已站在加斯城堡的屋顶上。

夜里星光满天，月亮却已经落了。

我们人人带刀带枪，腰间像斜挎绶带似的盘着一圈绳索。贝尔守在梯子脚下，手里握着信号绳，特斯特则回到林子里看守汽车。

这座城堡四四方方，像学院的楼舍那样环抱着一座大庭院。屋顶是平的，铺着石板，于是成了一片开阔的城台，一侧以雉堞为栏，另一侧是一道浑重的石栏杆。

站在星光下，我们仿佛置身于巴比伦城墙的某种摹本之上——如果我没记错，那城墙上可以六辆战车并驱齐行。

从栏杆柱间俯望，我看得见书商笔下写过的那道水纹，也听得见水流出水盆、奔向台地和悬崖的声响。

===Original===
Now the basin was our first objective: and, since a rope provides the most silent path, we let one fall to the courtyard, and Carson and I went down.

We gained the basin and passed to the channel it fed. This was ten inches deep, and its floor was as smooth as glass. I followed the channel along till I came to the arch. This was shut by a gate, beneath which the water flowed. The gate was of iron and exactly fitted the arch: a man might have lain in the channel and crawled underneath, but, no doubt to foil such cunning, the channel was barred with a grating, through which the water fussed. I tested the bars and found them firm as rock.

I made my way back to Carson, and together we sought the rope down which we had come. Upon this Carson pulled twice, when a hundredweight of fine rope was lowered into our arms. We carried this to the grating and laid it down. To its end were attached three floats weighted with lead. With these in my hand, I thrust my arm under the grating as far as I could. Then I released them, and Carson paid out the rope. Now if there was another grating, this would arrest the floats: but, if there was not, the floats would leap with the water and carry the rope down the cliff. With a hand on the sliding rope, I waited for the check. But none came: only a sudden pull told us that the floats had leapt. There were eight hundred feet of cord, and, when we had paid them all out, we returned for more. Six hundred feet more we lowered, and that was as much as we had. The end we made fast to the grating below *the water line*. Then we went back to the rope down which we had come, and at a signal the others pulled us up.

===Chinese===
那水盆正是我们的第一个目标：而绳子是最无声的路径，于是我们放下一根到庭院里，卡森和我顺绳而下。

我们到了水盆边，随即转入它泻出的水道。水道深十英寸，底面光滑如镜。我沿水道一路走到拱洞跟前。拱洞被一道闸门封住，水从门下流过。闸门是铁的，与拱洞严丝合缝：人本可以躺进水道、从门底爬过去，但无疑为防这类伎俩，水道里横了一道铁栅，水正从栅缝里喧腾着挤过。我试了试栅条，坚固如岩。

我摸回卡森身边，两人一同寻到下来的那根绳。卡森在绳上拉了两下，上面便放下一英担细绳，正落进我们怀里。我们把绳搬到铁栅前放下。绳端系着三只带铅坠的浮子。我把浮子攥在手里，胳膊尽量往栅底下探。随后我松开手，卡森一面往下滑放绳子。倘若底下还有一道栅，浮子便会被拦住：倘若没有，浮子就会随水一跃，带着绳子坠下崖去。我一只手搭在那根滑动的绳上，等着那一顿。可始终没有：只觉绳子猛然一牵，我们便知道浮子跳下去了。绳长八百英尺，放完了，我们又回去取。又放下六百英尺，家底也就尽于此了。我们把绳头拴牢在*水面以下*的铁栅上。然后回到下来的那根绳旁，上面一发信号，便把我们拽了上去。

===Original===
Whatever the night might bring forth, we had taken at least one trick; for, though this time we might fail to release Adèle, we had now a way up to the terrace which the enemy would not dream of and a desperate man might take.

We were now beneath the shadow of the tower to the right of the gateway as you came from the wood: the southwest tower, from which Adèle had signalled, lay the length of the castle away.

So far as we knew, there were two ways into that tower from the open air﻿—one by the roof and another by the steps from the terrace upon which we had seen Adèle. The windows which lighted the tower were not to be reached, and the conical roof of the tower gave us no hope. And, since the way by the roof was plainly the first to try, we began to steal over the pavement, one by one.

Mansel went first: the rest of us followed, at one-minute intervals. Only Rowley stood fast, with a signal cord in each hand.

I had gone most of the way, when I felt a hand on my arm.

===Chinese===
这一夜无论结出什么果子，我们至少已经赢了一墩；因为这一回纵然救不出阿黛尔，我们也已经摸出一条登上台地的路——敌人做梦也想不到、而亡命之徒恰好能走的路。

这时我们置身于大门右侧塔楼的阴影之下——那是从树林过来时的右手边：阿黛尔发过信号的那座西南塔楼，则远隔着整整一座城堡的距离。

据我们所知，从露天进那座塔楼有两条路——一条经屋顶，另一条经我们望见阿黛尔的那片台地上的台阶。塔楼的窗户高不可及，锥形的塔顶也让我们无隙可乘。既然经屋顶这条路显然该先试，我们便开始一个接一个地潜行过石板屋顶。

曼塞尔打头：其余人每隔一分钟跟上一个。只有罗利原地不动，两手各握着一根信号绳。

我已走过大半程，忽然觉得有只手搭上我的手臂。

===Original===
At once I stopped, and Hanbury, who was before me, spoke in my ear.

“There’s an alarm-cord, knee-high, two paces from where you stand. Tell Carson and then come on.”

He left me to wait for Carson and disappeared.

I was desperately afraid of fouling the cord, so I went on my stomach until the danger was past. I afterwards found that the others had done the same.

Mansel and Hanbury were waiting, when I came to the tower. And, as we had expected, there was a door.﻿ ﻿…

The door was of wood, very massive and studded with iron. It was shut and locked, or bolted, upon the other side. Whether we could have forced it, I do not know: but Jack Sheppard himself could not have had it open without making noise enough to awaken the dead.

===Chinese===
我立刻停住，走在我前面的汉伯里凑到我耳边说话。

「有一道警铃绳，齐膝高，离你站的地方两步远。告诉卡森，然后跟上。」

他把我留下等卡森，自己消失在前面。

我生怕碰着那道绳，于是趴下身子匍匐前行，直到过了险处。后来我才知道，别人也都是这么爬的。

我到塔楼跟前时，曼塞尔和汉伯里正等着。而且不出我们所料，那儿有一道门。……

那门是木头的，厚重非常，钉满铁钉。门关着，并且从另一面上着锁，或是闩上了。我们能否撞开它，我说不好：可即便是杰克·谢泼德（Jack Sheppard）本人，也休想打开它而不闹出足以吵醒死人的动静。

===Original===
And there, of course, was our principal handicap.

The door of Adèle’s apartment was sure to be locked; but that we were ready to force, no matter what noise we made: until, however, we were standing without her door, we dared make no manner of sound, for, the instant the alarm was raised, Rose Noble was certain to fly to his prisoner’s side and, if he was there before us, to put her life in the balance against our further advance.

There was nothing to be done but to try the terrace steps.

In silence we passed to the battlements, and from there looked down upon the terrace and the sliding ribbon of water that cut it in two. It was quieter here than in the courtyard, where the four walls gave back sound; the steady rustle of the cascade was only just to be heard.

In a moment a rope was dangling, and Mansel and George went down.

That the steps would offer an entrance we had great hope, for the bookseller’s guide had said nothing of any door, but only that the steps led out of the “gallery of stone.”

===Chinese===
这当然就是我们最大的难处。

阿黛尔居室的门必定也锁着；可那道门我们准备强撬，闹出多大动静都不管：然而在站到她门外之前，我们不敢弄出任何声响，因为警报一响，罗斯·诺布尔必定飞奔到他囚徒身旁，他若抢在我们前头赶到，就要拿她的性命来抵我们再进逼的每一步。

除了试台地那边的台阶，别无他法。

我们悄没声地移到雉堞边，从那里俯瞰台地，以及那条把它拦腰切成两段、滑溜溜的水带。这里比庭院里安静，庭院四壁还会回声；瀑布那阵绵绵的沙沙声，也只是隐约可闻。

转眼一条绳已经垂下，曼塞尔和乔治顺绳而下。

我们满心希望那台阶能给我们一个入口，因为书商那本指南压根没提什么门，只说台阶是打『石头回廊』里出来的。

===Original===
Carson and I stood like statues, he holding the signal cord and I with the rope in my hand.

Two gentle pulls from below told us to take the strain, and a moment later Hanbury was by our side.

He put his lips to my ear.

“There’s a door at the top of the steps﻿—locked. Mansel’s reconnoitring the terrace and then coming up.”

I confess that my spirits sank.

To enter by some window seemed now the only way: and we had already decided that, if we were put to such a shift, we must essay some window that looked upon the terrace below. What windows looked into the courtyard we neither knew nor cared, for anyone at work in the courtyard would be working in a four-walled trap and could be observed and commanded from any side.

===Chinese===
卡森和我像雕像似的站着，他握着信号绳，我手里攥着那条绳。

下面轻轻两牵，是叫我们吃住劲，转眼汉伯里已回到我们身边。

他把嘴唇凑到我耳边。

「台阶顶上有一道门——锁着。曼塞尔正在侦察台地，随后就上来。」

我承认，我的心一沉。

从某扇窗子进去，如今看来是唯一的路了：而且我们早已议定，真被逼到这个地步，就得挑一扇俯临下方台地的窗下手。至于哪些窗朝向庭院，我们既不知道也不关心，因为在庭院里动手的人，无异于困在四面是墙的陷阱里，任人从任何一侧窥伺、制压。

===Original===
Now the windows that looked upon the terrace were those of the royal rooms. *They were not barred*, because, I suppose, with guards upon the roof and in the galleries, no one could have come upon the King.

Again, *that the royal apartments would be occupied was most unlikely*. The caretakers might have been bribed, but they would certainly stipulate that the state rooms were not to be used. The last thing we wanted to do was to force an entrance into an occupied room.

Finally, *the windows of the royal apartments had the world to themselves*, for the towers which flanked the terrace were presenting two empty walls. Of no other side of the castle could the same thing be said.

Now which of the seven windows might be the best to essay we could not tell, but reason suggested that the one which was nearest Adèle should be the first to be tried. This we supposed to be serving the antechamber which admitted directly into the “gallery of stone.”

It was our belief that if we could reach the gallery we should have the control we sought, for, so far as we could determine, no one could enter the tower without passing through the gallery, unless he came down from the roof by the door which we had found shut. This belief we found to be just: the gallery was the key to the tower, and whoever held the gallery held Adèle. But one thing we did not suspect, namely, that there was a way into the gallery of which the bookseller’s guide said nothing at all.

===Chinese===
而朝向台地的窗，正是王室诸厅的窗。*那些窗不装铁条*，我想是因为屋顶和回廊上都有卫兵，谁也到不了国王跟前。

再者，*王室套房有人住着的可能性微乎其微*。看管人也许受了贿，但他们必定言明正殿厅房不得动用。我们最不愿干的事，就是撬门闯进一间住着人的房间。

最后，*王室套房的窗子独享整个天地*，因为夹峙台地的两座塔楼向它亮出的都是空墙。城堡其他任何一面，都谈不上有这等好处。

七扇窗中哪一扇最好下手，我们说不好，但道理提示，离阿黛尔最近的那扇该先试。我们推想，那扇窗该属于一间前厅，前厅便直通『石头回廊』。

我们相信，只要能进到回廊，便握住了我们要的控制权，因为据我们判断，谁进塔楼都绕不开回廊，除非从屋顶经我们发现锁着的那道门下来。这个信念后来证明不误：回廊是塔楼的钥匙，谁占住回廊，谁就攥住了阿黛尔。唯有一桩我们不曾疑心，那就是进回廊另有一条路，书商那本指南对此只字未提。

===Original===
A quarter of an hour went by before Mansel gave us the signal to hoist him up.

As he alighted, I perceived that he was drenched to the skin.

At once he drew us together and spoke very low.

“At the head of each flight of steps there’s a massive door: both doors are fast. The door at the mouth of the archway is shut and locked. I managed to pass beneath it, by lying down in the channel and working my way along. In the side of the archway I found a flight of steps﻿—a very steep spiral staircase, that comes to a sudden end. I’m certain it serves a trapdoor. If the bookseller’s guide is sound, that trapdoor should be in the King’s Closet. That would be natural enough.

“We can’t go that way tonight, because the trapdoor is fast. At least, I imagine it is, because I can’t move the slab I found at the top of the stairs: but, if we can enter the Closet by some other way tonight, we can unbar the trapdoor, and then, when we come by the terrace, we shall have our way in. Don’t think I’ve no hope of tonight, because I have: but, *if we fail this time, we shall certainly fail the next﻿—unless we can turn this failure into a stepping-stone*.”

===Chinese===
过了一刻钟，曼塞尔才发信号叫我们把他吊上去。

他一落地，我便看出他浑身湿透了。

他立刻把我们聚拢到一处，声音压得极低。

「每段台阶顶上都有一道厚重的门：两道门都关得死死的。拱洞口上的那道门关着，还落了锁。我躺进水道里，一路蹭着钻过了门底。在拱洞侧壁上，我发现一道石级——一盘极陡的旋梯，到顶戛然而止。我敢肯定它通着一扇活板门。书商那本指南若是可靠，那扇活板门应当就在国王密室里。这也在情理之中。

「今夜我们不能走那条路，因为活板门闩死了。至少我料想如此，因为楼梯顶上那块石板我推不动：可要是今夜我们能从别的路子进密室，就能把活板门的闩退了，往后打台地过来，我们便自有门路。别以为我对今夜不抱指望，我是抱的：只是，*这一回若失手，下一回也必定失手——除非我们能把这回的失败垫成一块踏脚石*。」

===Original===
With that he told Carson to let him have his shoes, because they were dry, and then to stand fast where he was, with the signal cord in his hand. George and I were to follow the way he went.

We moved as before, one by one, and, when again I found him, he was standing above the archway through which the water flowed.

I knew that below us was a window of three long lights.

An instant later Mansel was descending the wall.

This time we used two ropes, one fastened about him and the other down which he slid. When he jerked the one about him, we were to make this fast: and, if he should pull it five times, George was to go for Carson and they were to let me down.

Wet to the skin, clinging like a fly to the wall, with only one hand to help him, without a glimmer of light, Mansel worked upon that window for half an hour. I was kneeling directly above him, but I never heard a sound. Indeed, I could not believe that he was at work, but supposed that he had seen someone within the room and was content to watch them from where he hung. Yet all the time he was drawing the wrought-iron latch﻿—a feat which, had he not done it, I would have put beyond the power of man.

===Chinese===
说罢他让卡森把鞋换给他，因为卡森的鞋是干的，然后叫卡森原地守住，手里握好信号绳。乔治和我则循他走的路跟下去。

我们照旧一个一个地挪动，等我再次找到他时，他正站在水流穿过的那道拱洞上方。

我知道我们脚下是一扇三联长窗。

转眼间曼塞尔已经缘墙而下。

这回我们用了两条绳，一条系在他身上，另一条供他滑降。他一抖身上那条，我们就把它固定住：那条绳若是被拉上五下，乔治就去叫卡森，他们俩再把我放下去。

曼塞尔浑身湿透，像苍蝇似的贴在墙上，只有一只手得力，没有一丝光亮，就在那扇窗上足足干了半个钟头。我就跪在他正上方，却始终没听见一点声响。说实话，我简直不信他在干活，只当他看见屋里有人，甘心悬在原处守望。可他自始至终都在拨那枚锻铁窗闩——这门功夫，若非他亲手做成，我定要断言非人力所能及。

===Original===
At last the ropes trembled and then swung slack in my hands. An instant later he signalled that he was within the room.

When I came down, he swung me in like a baby and asked for my torch.

The room was stately and full of the smell of age. The floor was of polished oak: the walls were panelled head high and tapestried above. The furniture was rich and massive, but very stiff, and had been ranged in order against the walls. A heavy carpet, much smaller than the room, lay in the midst of the floor, but all the furniture stood clear upon the oak. The two doors were conspicuous, for their frames rose above the panelling and each of them was fitted with a box lock of polished steel.

With one consent we turned to the door upon our left.

Very slowly, with infinite care, Mansel drew the spring latch. At once the door yielded, and Mansel set it wide. Not until he had wedged it with a morsel of rubber did we pass on.

So we entered the King’s Bedchamber.

===Chinese===
末了，绳子一颤，随即在我手里荡然松垂。转眼他发来信号，人已进了房间。

我降下去时，他像抱娃娃似的把我悠了进去，然后要我的手电。

这房间气派堂皇，满是一股年代的气味。地板是打磨的橡木：墙面齐头高处镶着木护板，护板之上挂满壁毯。家具华贵而厚重，却极为板正，一概依序靠墙列着。一块厚地毯比房间小得多，铺在地板正中，所有家具都站在地毯之外的橡木地板上。两道门十分显眼，门框高出于护板之上，各自装着一把抛光钢的匣式锁。

我们不约而同地转向左手那道门。

曼塞尔极慢、极小心地拨开弹簧锁舌。门应手而开，曼塞尔把它推到最宽。直到他用一小块橡胶把门楔住，我们才往前走。

就这样，我们进了国王寝宫。

===Original===
Not even the unjust light of the torch could deny the majesty of that room.

I have never beheld a chamber so fit to lodge a king, and all the standards of greatness which the fairytales I read as a child had set in my heart were in a twinkling supplanted by what I saw.

The ceiling was of black oak, picked out with gold. The walls were panelled head-high: between the panels stood pilasters, picked out with gold. The carving of the panels and pilasters was very deep. Above the panelling hung tapestries, very rich in colour, presenting hunting scenes. The bedstead was four-posted: each of the posts was carved into the life-size semblance of a man-at-arms: coverlet, canopy and curtains were of what seemed to be a crimson faced cloth, very fine to look at and clearly of a great weight: all the stuffs and hangings with which the furniture was done were of the same dignity. The floor was of polished oak.

We stole across to the door in the farther wall.

This admitted us to the royal dining-room.

This chamber resembled the Closet and was of much the same size. A handsome table stood in the midst of the floor.

===Chinese===
即便是手电那不肯秉公行事的光，也抹杀不了那房间的威仪。

我从未见过哪间屋子这样配得上住一位国王；儿时读过的童话在我心里立下的种种伟大的标准，被眼前所见霎时间尽数推翻。

天花板是黑橡木的，描着金。墙面齐头高镶着护板：护板之间立着壁柱，也描着金。护板和壁柱的雕工极深。护板上方悬着壁毯，色彩浓丽，绘的是行猎图景。床是四柱大床：每根床柱都雕成真人大小的武士之形：床罩、顶盖和帐幔用的仿佛都是绯红的绒面呢料，外观极美，分量显然也极沉：家具上所有的织物与垂幔，一律是同样的贵重气派。地板是打磨的橡木。

我们悄悄穿过房间，走到对面墙边那道门前。

它把我们带进王室的餐厅。

这间屋子与密室相仿，大小也相去不远。地板正中立着一张气派的桌子。

===Original===
We wasted no time but passed on.

A moment later we stood in the antechamber.

This was small, but notable. The walls were not panelled, but covered with tapestry. Two massive, high-backed chairs were all the furniture.

The door we now found before us was not at all like those through which we had come. It admitted of course to the gallery which led to the tower: and it was like a church door, that is to say, iron-studded, and Gothic in shape. A wrought-iron lock of great size was fixed upon the inside, and above this a simple latch which had only to be lifted to be freed.

If hope could but open gates, I think this door would have crumbled before our eyes: but hope cannot open gates, and﻿—the door was fast.

Mansel stood very still.

===Chinese===
我们不敢耽搁，径直往前。

转眼我们已站在前厅里。

前厅不大，却不同凡响。墙上没有护板，满覆壁毯。家具只有两把厚重的高背椅。

我们此刻面前这道门，与一路穿过来的那些毫无相似之处。它自然是通向去塔楼的那条回廊：模样像一道教堂大门，也就是说，铁钉密布，形制是哥特式的。内侧装着一柄硕大的锻铁锁，锁上方是一枚简单的门闩，只须一抬便脱。

倘若希望开得了门，我想这道门早就在我们眼前碎裂了：可希望开不了门，于是——门纹丝不动地闩着。

曼塞尔一动不动地站着。

===Original===
At length he gave a short sigh and touched me upon the arm. A moment later we were moving the way we had come.

When we were again in the Closet, he put his mouth to my ear.

“Take a message to Hanbury. He and Carson will bring every tool we have to the southwest tower: then they will let fall a rope to the window of the antechamber, moving it to and fro until we take hold: when we do that they will come and take up the ropes which are hanging outside this room.”

When I returned, Mansel was down on his knees by a hole in the floor. On the carpet, now drawn to one side, lay a square of polished wood. The recess which he had disclosed was floored by a grey, stone slab: this had a ring in its midst and was rudely locked into place by a pair of hasps and staples, the pins of which Mansel had withdrawn.

Gently I lifted the slab, to disclose the winding stair which led to the archway below.

===Chinese===
末了他短短叹了口气，碰了碰我的手臂。转眼我们已循原路往回走。

回到密室后，他把嘴凑到我耳边。

「给汉伯里传句话。他和卡森把我们全部的家伙都搬到西南塔楼去：然后放一条绳到前厅窗前，来回晃动，直到我们抓住为止：我们一抓住，他们就来收走悬在这间屋子外面的绳索。」

我回来时，曼塞尔正跪在地板上一处洞口旁。一块方形抛光木板搁在地毯上，已推到一旁。他揭开的那个凹坑底上盖着一方灰色石板：石板正中有一只铁环，靠一对搭扣和扣环粗率地锁定，搭扣的销钉已被曼塞尔拔了去。

我轻轻掀起石板，露出通往下方拱洞的旋梯。

===Original===
“Can you hold it?” breathed Mansel.

I nodded.

At once he replaced the square of polished wood: then he laid the carpet, face downward, between my legs.

I lowered the slab.

Using the carpet as a sledge, we drew the slab into the Bedchamber and up to the King’s bed. There we transferred it to my coat, and, lifting the crimson valence, thrust it beneath the bedstead and out of sight.

To restore the carpet to its place, close the window and take the wedge from the door took but a moment of time.

===Chinese===
「托得住吗？」曼塞尔低声道。

我点了点头。

他立刻把那块方形抛光木板放回原处：然后把地毯面朝下铺在我两腿之间。

我放下石板。

我们把地毯当雪橇使，将石板拖进寝宫，一直拖到国王的床前。在那儿我们把石板挪到我的外套上，掀起绯红的床沿垂幔，把它塞进床架底下，看不见为止。

把地毯归回原位、关好窗子、取下门上的楔子，不过片刻工夫。

===Original===
Then Mansel gave me a cloth and bade me polish the woodwork where we had stepped, especially about the casement which we had used.

When I had done so, he overlooked all with the torch.

Then we stole out of the room and closed the door.

Five minutes later we stood again in the antechamber and when we had opened a casement and found the rope, there was nothing to show, much less to suggest that we had seen the inside of any other room.

“And now the tools,” said Mansel, “and then Hanbury. Carson to stay where he is. We shall make the devil’s own noise, and he is to shoot at sight.” I climbed up and gave the message as fast as I could.

===Chinese===
随后曼塞尔递给我一块布，叫我把我们踩过的木地板擦亮，尤其是我们用过的那扇窗子周围。

我擦完，他打着手电把各处检视了一遍。

然后我们溜出房间，掩上了门。

五分钟后我们重又站到前厅里，等我们推开一扇窗、寻到绳索之后，这里再无一物能表明——更不用说暗示——我们进过任何别的房间。

「现在要家伙，」曼塞尔说，「然后接汉伯里下来。卡森原地不动。咱们要闹出魔鬼一样的动静，叫他见人就开始。」我攀上去，尽快把话传到。

===Original===
The time was now half past three: the stars were no more to be seen, and rain had begun to fall. This to our liking, for now any footprints we had made above or below would be wiped out: but the wet was against our foothold, and, as I came back, I slipped on the windowsill.

We had received the tools and were awaiting Hanbury, when some door within the castle was sharply closed.

It was some door behind us﻿—not very far away.

Called upon to say which, I would have named the door of the Closet. The clash of a heavy, spring lock was unmistakable.

Mansel put out the light, and we stood as still as death, straining our ears for footsteps and hearing none.

A rustle without the window told us that Hanbury had begun to descend the rope.

===Chinese===
这时已是三点半：星星不见了，雨也落了下来。这正合我们的意，因为我们在上上下下留下的任何足迹都将被抹去：可湿滑却不利于立足，我回来时便在窗台上滑了一下。

我们接了工具，正等汉伯里，忽听城堡里某一道门砰然关上。

是我们身后的某一道门——离得不很远。

若一定要我说出是哪一道，我会说是密室的门。沉重弹簧锁的一记脆响，错不了。

曼塞尔灭了灯，我们死一般僵立着，竖起耳朵捕捉脚步声，却一无所闻。

窗外一阵窸窣，告诉我们汉伯里已开始顺绳而下。

===Original===
We could not stop him, for we had no signal-cord, and together we stood to the window to take him in without sound.

As we did so, came the creak of a floorboard, faint yet distinct. I would have said that it came from the Dining-room.

It was now as dark as pitch. I could not even see Mansel, two feet from where I stood.

Something swayed at the window.﻿ ﻿…

Then we had George in our arms and Mansel was unfastening the rope which was holding him up.

Again I heard a board creak﻿—somewhere at hand.

===Chinese===
我们拦不住他，因为没有信号绳，只好双双守在窗口，打算悄没声地把他接进来。

正接着，传来一声地板的吱呀，轻微却清晰。要我说，声音出自餐厅。

此时黑得像沥青。连离我两英尺的曼塞尔，我都看不见了。

窗口有什么东西晃了一晃。……

随后乔治已落在我们怀里，曼塞尔正解着吊住他的那条绳。

我又听见一块地板吱呀作响——就在近旁什么地方。

===Original===
With the greatest care we lowered George to his feet. As we did so, the dais upon which we were standing tilted suddenly forward, and, with nothing to save us, the three of us crashed to the ground. As we fell, the massive step resumed its proper position with a deafening clap.

There had been nothing to show that the step was not fixed, and, indeed, it was so solid that I do not think it would have moved under ordinary use: but the weight of three men happening to fall upon its edge had, I suppose, been too much for its counterpoise.

For a moment we lay as we had fallen, straining our ears: then Mansel got to his feet and lighted the torch.

“You two all right?” he said.

We told him “yes.”

“Then quick,” says he, “for, by thunder, we’ve rung the bell.”

===Chinese===
我们万分小心地把乔治放落脚来。就在这时，我们脚下那方平台猛地向前倾去，无物可攀无物可扶，我们三人轰然摔在地上。倒下之际，那块厚重的踏级震耳欲聋地一响，弹回了原位。

先前没有任何迹象表明这级台阶不是死的，何况它那样厚重，我想寻常踩踏绝不会使它移动：只是三个人的分量碰巧一齐压上它的边沿，我想，它的配重终于招架不住了。

有一瞬间，我们保持着摔倒的姿势躺着，竖耳细听：随后曼塞尔爬起来，点亮手电。

「你们俩没事吧？」他说。

我们告诉他「没事」。

「那就快，」他说，「因为，凭雷霆起誓，咱们已经把钟敲响了。」

===Original===
With that, he set Hanbury to watch the Dining-room door and hold the torch, while I took the stoutest chisel and laid its edge where the lock met the wood of the door.

“You strike,” said Mansel, taking it out of my hand.

I picked up and swung a hammer with all my might.﻿ ﻿…

I cannot attempt to tell the noise we made.

Perhaps our reluctance to make any noise at all and the infinite care we had taken to smother all sound magnified for us this sudden breach of silence: certainly the hour, the emptiness of the apartments and the style and proportions of the building made so many sounding-boards. Be that as it may, had the castle been full of troops and these been suddenly summoned by trumpet and tuck of drum I do not think the uproar could have won to our ears.

It took us, I suppose, two minutes to reduce that lock.

===Chinese===
说罢，他派汉伯里盯住餐厅的门、举着手电，我则拿起最粗壮的一柄凿子，把刃口抵在锁具与门木相交的地方。

「你来抡锤，」曼塞尔说着，把凿子从我手里拿了过去。

我抓起铁锤，抡圆了砸下去。……

我们闹出的那阵动静，我没法形容。

也许是我们先前的不愿出声、以及为闷灭一切声响所下的无穷小心，把这一记骤然撕破寂静的巨响在我们耳中放大了：但时辰、套房的空旷和这建筑的风格与比例，确实都成了一面面共鸣板。不管怎样，纵使城堡里驻满了兵，号角战鼓齐鸣骤然召集他们，我想那片喧嚣也未必赛得过我们这一声。

把那把锁解决掉，前前后后，我想约莫花了两分钟。

===Original===
As Mansel wrenched it away, an iron fillet, into which its tongues were protruding, came also. This fillet was a false jamb, that ran the whole length of the door and was laid upon stone. Now that it was displaced, we could see that the door was bolted, top and bottom, upon the opposite side. The bolts were shot into the stone, but, the fillet gone, we could reach them and, with the slightest manipulation, could draw them clear of their sockets and open the door.

I put up a hand, but Mansel caught my arm. *Someone was pounding upon the other side of the door.*

Together we stared at the oak.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔把锁硬拽下来时，锁舌插着的那根铁嵌条也一并脱出。这嵌条是一道假门框，顺着门的全长贴在石头上。它既已挪位，我们便看见门在对面上着上下两道闩。门闩都插进石头里，可嵌条一去，我们便够得着它们，稍一拨弄，就能把它们从间眼里退出来，把门打开。

我举起一只手，曼塞尔却一把抓住我的手臂。*有人正在门的另一面捶打。*

我们一齐盯着那扇橡木门。

===Original===
Then came Adèle’s clear voice.

“Is that you Jonah?”

“Yes, dear,” said Mansel. “One moment, and﻿—”

“*No, no*,” cried Adèle. “*Stop. Stay where you are. Rose Noble’s here by my side. And he says if you open that door you’ll lose your match.*”

===Chinese===
随后传来阿黛尔清亮的嗓音。

「是你吗，约拿？」

「是我，亲爱的，」曼塞尔说。「稍等一下，还有——」

「*不，不*，」阿黛尔喊道。「*站住。留在原地别动。罗斯·诺布尔就在我身边。他说你要是开了那扇门，就会失去你的佳偶。*」

## VI The Love of a Lady / 六 一位淑女的爱

===Original===

Never, I suppose were hopes lifted so high one moment and dashed so low the next.

Indeed, I was so much confounded by the sudden overthrow of our fortune that I stood staring at the door, as a clown at a strange fish, and when I turned to Hanbury, who had come across to our side, he was wearing the blank expression of a player of chess who, having but two moves to make to win his match, suddenly perceives that his opponent has but one.

And this shows how fine was the stuff of which Mansel was made, for, though our dismay was nothing to the bitterness which must have been his, beyond raising his eyebrows he gave no manner of sign that he was put out, and, what was far more, he had a plan in an instant to save the game.

===Chinese===

我想，人的希望从不曾这样：这一刻被抬得那样高，下一刻又被摔得那样低。

说实在的，时运这突如其来的一跤，摔得我晕头转向，我直挺挺瞪着那扇门，像丑角瞪着一条怪鱼；待我转向走过我们这边的汉伯里，他脸上是一片空白，活像一位棋手，再有两步便要赢下全局，却猛然看出对手只需一步。

这便见出曼塞尔的质料有多精良：我们的沮丧，比起他心头必定翻搅的苦涩来，算不得什么，可他除了扬一场眉毛，没有露出半点动气的形迹；更要紧的是，转眼之间，他已有一步救回全局的棋。

===Original===

Before he could answer Adèle, we heard the clatter of footsteps and, then, Rose Noble’s deep voice speaking Casemate and Bunch by name.

Under cover of this distraction, Mansel caught us each by an arm.

“Time to be gone,” he whispered. “We’ve shot our bolt. Leave everything and clear out. *I’m going to stay.* Food and dry clothes to me by the waterfall cord. And paper and pencil. So long.”

Before we could speak, he had returned to the door.

===Chinese===

他还没来得及回答阿黛尔，我们便听见一阵杂沓的脚步声，随后是罗斯·诺布尔低沉的嗓音，点名似的唤着凯斯梅特和邦奇。

趁着这阵纷乱，曼塞尔一手一个，抓住我们的胳膊。

「该走了，」他耳语道。「我们的箭射完了。丢下一切，撤出去。*我留下。*用瀑布绳给我送吃的和干衣服。再要纸和铅笔。回见。」

我们还没来得及开口，他已回到门边。

===Original===

“My dear Adèle,” he said, “I’m delighted to hear your voice.”

“Same here,” said Adèle, cheerfully.

Rose Noble laughed.

“ ‘Journeys end in lovers meeting,’ ” he said.

===Chinese===

「亲爱的阿黛尔，」他说，「听见你的声音，真叫我高兴。」

「我也是，」阿黛尔快活地说。

罗斯·诺布尔笑了。

「『旅程的尽头，是情人的相会。』」他说。

===Original===

“How’s Boy?” said Adèle.

“None too bad,” said Mansel. “A bit hot and bothered you know, till we’d run you to earth: but that was natural. His leg’s just splendid.”

“How much does he know?” said Rose Noble.

That was as much as I heard, for Hanbury was at the window, and I had something to say before he was gone.

===Chinese===

「『小子』怎么样了？」阿黛尔说。

「不算糟，」曼塞尔说。「你知道，有点心急火燎，直到我们把你的下落查了个水落石出：可那也是人之常情。他的腿好得很。」

「他知道多少？」罗斯·诺布尔说。

我听到的就这么多，因为汉伯里已在窗边，趁他还没走，我有话要说。

===Original===

“I stay with Mansel,” I said. “Give me your pistol and torch.”

“I can’t leave you here,” said George.

“Quick,” said I. “We’ll argue another time.”

With that, I put my hands in his pockets and helped myself. There was a bunch of wedges we had not used. These I took, and his torch. He gave me his pistol unwillingly enough. Then he shook his head and went up the rope.

===Chinese===

「我陪曼塞尔留下，」我说。「把手枪和手电给我。」

「我不能把你留在这儿，」乔治说。

「快，」我说。「要抬杠，改日再抬。」

说着，我把手伸进他的口袋，自行取用。有一把我们没用过的楔子。这些我拿上了，还有他的手电。手枪他给我给得很不情愿。然后他摇了摇头，攀上绳去。

===Original===

High words were flying as I swung myself into the rain. Rose Noble was clearly angry, and I heard Adèle’s scornful laugh.﻿ ﻿…

As soon as my feet touched the terrace, I made my way to the water and scrambled under the door. I had a vile wet passage, but I managed to save both pistols and Hanbury’s torch. The archway was dank and draughty, so I lost no time in finding the little staircase that served the trapdoor and, when I had found it, in climbing as high as I could. Here I was out of the wind and might have been snug; but my clothes were wringing wet and that made the stone seem cold.

I was quite sure that this was the spot at which Mansel proposed to be hid﻿—for that, of course, was his plan. He intended Rose Noble to think that he had withdrawn, and, presently taking the monster off his guard, to strike again.

===Chinese===

我荡身进入雨里时，激烈的言辞正飞来飞去。罗斯·诺布尔分明动怒了，我还听见阿黛尔轻蔑的笑。……

双脚一沾台地，我便朝水边摸去，从门底下爬了过去。那段水路湿得可憎，好在两支手枪和汉伯里的手电都保住了。拱洞底下又潮又透风，我便赶紧去找那条通往活板门的小楼梯，找到后又尽力往高里爬。这里避风，本可以待得舒坦；可我的衣裳拧得出水，那石头便显得冷了。

我十分确定，曼塞尔打算藏身的地方正是这里——因为那自然就是他的计划。他要让罗斯·诺布尔以为他已经撤走，然后瞅准这怪物不备，再度出手。

===Original===

And here I may say that I found his plan very good. To release Adèle might be beyond our power, but our luck would be out indeed, if we could not kill Rose Noble before two days were gone by, and his death, as I have said, would break the enemy’s back.

What Mansel would say, when he found me, I could not tell. He did not like disobedience: but for one man to stay alone in such an enemy’s camp seemed to me out of reason, and I hoped very much that he would share my view.

I had not long to wait.

===Chinese===

在这里我可以说，我觉得他的计划非常好。救出阿黛尔或许非我们力所能及，可两天之内若还不能结果罗斯·诺布尔，我们的运气就真是糟透了；而如我所说，他一死，敌人的脊梁便断了。

曼塞尔发现我时会说些什么，我说不上来。他不喜欢别人抗命：可是叫一个人独守这样的敌营，在我看来不近情理，我非常希望他同我见解一致。

我没等多久。

===Original===

A sudden quickening of the rustle the water made told me that Mansel was passing beneath the door, and an instant later I heard his foot on the stair.

“Mansel,” I breathed. “Chandos speaking.”

“Ah, William,” said he: and that was all.

He sat down on the step below mine and put his head in his hands.

===Chinese===

水声的节拍忽然一紧，我知道曼塞尔正从门下经过；转眼间，我听见他的脚踏上了楼梯。

「曼塞尔，」我低声道。「是我，钱多斯。」

「啊，威廉，」他说：仅此而已。

他在我下面一级台阶上坐下，把头埋进双手。

===Original===

After a little he spoke.

“I didn’t dare stay. Rose Noble was bound to exploit a chance like that. You see, without an audience there’s no point in doing her harm: but with me, so to speak, looking on.﻿ ﻿… He struck her, Chandos. And, because she wouldn’t cry out, he struck her again.﻿ ﻿… And so it seemed best to go.﻿ ﻿… Oh, my God,” he cried suddenly, “there are times when I’d take your offer and buy her out.”

“It’s always open,” I said.

“I know,” he said, “I know.”

===Chinese===

过了一小会儿，他开口了。

「我不敢留下。那样的机会，罗斯·诺布尔势必会拿来用足。你要知道，没有看客，伤她便无益处：可偏偏有我在场，可以说是，旁观。……他打了她，钱多斯。因为她不肯喊出声，他又打了一下。……所以走掉似乎最妥。……哦，我的上帝，」他忽然喊道，「有些时刻，我真想接受你的提议，把她赎出去。」

「这个提议随时有效，」我说。

「我知道，」他说，「我知道。」

===Original===

For a long time we sat in silence. Then he gave a short sigh.

“Drink some of this,” he whispered, and put a flask into my hand.

It was brandy and did us both good, for our state of mind and of body was wretched enough.

Presently he spoke again.

===Chinese===

我们默默坐了许久。然后他短短叹了一口气。

「喝一口这个，」他悄声道，把一只扁酒瓶塞进我手里。

是白兰地，对我们两个都有好处，因为我们的身心都已凄惨得很。

不久他又开口了。

===Original===

“No,” he said. “No. What’s the good of buying a broken heart? We’ve got to beat Rose Noble: there’s no other way. But it’s a dreadful business to have to go so slow.”

That was a true saying.

Indeed, the next sixteen hours were the worst I have ever spent. I can never remember them without a shudder, and to our restless senses they seemed more like sixteen days. Without cause we dared not emerge: no cause presented itself. The rain fell down without ceasing, and, though we took it in turns to watch from the archway, no one appeared in the courtyard, or, so far as I know, visited the terrace upon the opposite side. Command the terrace we could not, unless we lay down in the channel below the door: and that was dangerous, for the noise which the water made embarrassed the ear, and we might well have been noticed before we had time to withdraw. There was, however, the keyhole of the great door itself, and, whilst we were listening there, I do not think one could have passed without our hearing his steps.

We saw the dawn come in and the day draw to its close: we heard the drip of the rain and the sigh of the wind: and that was all.

===Chinese===

「不，」他说。「不。买回一颗碎了的心，有什么好？我们必须打垮罗斯·诺布尔：没有别的路。可是不得不这样慢慢来，真是件可怕的事。」

这话说得不错。

的确，随后的十六个钟头是我平生熬过的最坏的时光。如今想起，我没有一次不打寒噤；在我们烦躁不安的感官里，它们更像是十六天。没有缘由，我们不敢露头：缘由也始终没有送上门来。雨下个不停，我们虽轮流从拱洞守望，围院里不见人影，据我所知，对面的台地也无人踏足。台地我们控制不了，除非躺进门下的水道里：那又危险，因为水声闹得耳朵失灵，我们很可能来不及退走便被发觉。好在大门自己还有个锁眼，我们守在那里听时，我想谁也别想打门前经过而不叫我们听见脚步。

我们看着黎明进来，看着白日收尾：听着雨的滴沥和风的叹息：仅此而已。

===Original===

One thing only we decided, for you cannot make bricks without straw, and we had no data with which to make any plan. We determined that, when night had fallen and we dared pull up the rope, we would enter the King’s Closet and eat and rest in such comfort as that room could give. The risk of discovery was small, and, if we could not take shelter which would allow us to sleep, we should, we knew, be unfitted to strike when the moment came. I do not mean that when the day was over we had no resistance left, for we were both very strong and had suffered adversity far more exacting than this; but we knew that to rob Rose Noble the eye must be clear, the hand unearthly swift and all the senses at the very top of their pitch or, as the saying is, a man might as well go home.

At last the daylight faded, and the rain ceased.

When it was quite dark, we laid hold of the rope in the channel and took the strain. To our delight it was loaded, so, bidding me stay where I was and haul it in, Mansel crawled under the door and made for the balustrade beneath which the waterfall leapt.

At last I felt a check, and, very soon after, a package done up in oiled silk was thrust under the door.

===Chinese===

我们只定下一件事，因为巧妇难为无米之炊，我们手里没有任何足以定计划的底细。我们拿定主意：夜幕落定、我们敢于收绳之后，便进国王密室，在那间屋子给得起的舒适里吃喝休息。被发觉的风险很小；而若不能寻个容我们睡觉的遮蔽，我们知道，时机一到我们便无力出手。我并不是说这一天熬完，我们就没有一丝气力了，因为我们两个都很强壮，也熬过比这苛刻得多的逆境；但我们知道，要从罗斯·诺布尔手里抢人，眼睛必须清明，手必须快得出奇，官能必须调到极限——否则，俗话说的，还不如回家去。

天光终于暗了，雨也停了。

天全黑之后，我们攥住水道里的绳，吃上了劲。令我们欣喜的是绳上坠了东西，于是曼塞尔吩咐我守在原地往里收绳，自己从门下爬了出去，朝瀑布跃落处那道栏杆摸去。

我终于觉得绳上一沉，紧跟着，一个油绸包裹从门底下塞了进来。

===Original===

Two minutes later we were in the King’s Closet, with good, oak boards beneath us and wedges under the doors.

As well as changes of clothing, Hanbury had sent us a blanket sewn into the shape of a bag: this was just what we needed and, with the square of carpet, promised a good night’s rest.

But first there was work to be done.

There was food enough for five, and, what was better still, a bottle of excellent brandy by way of drink.

===Chinese===

两分钟后我们已在国王密室里，脚下是上好的橡木地板，门下塞着楔子。

除换洗衣裳外，汉伯里还给我们送来一条缝成袋状的毯子：这正是我们需要的，配上那方块地毯，有望睡个好觉。

但先还有活儿要干。

吃食够五个人用的，更好的是，还有一瓶上好的白兰地作饮料。

===Original===

We changed and made our meal by the shaded light of a torch, for to do such things in the dark without making a sound required more time and care than we were prepared to expend: and, when we had done, Mansel wrote out the first message which we were to send to George:

> George.
> Send up Adèle’s dressing-case.

I wished to go out this time, but Mansel would not consent. Whilst, therefore, I let down the message, he changed again into his dripping clothes, and, as soon as I took the strain, he returned to the balustrade.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

我们换了衣裳，就着手电遮掩的光用饭，因为要在黑暗里不出一点声响地做这些事，所需的工夫和仔细，超出我们预备花费的：用过之后，曼塞尔写出了我们要发给乔治的头一封信：

> 乔治。
> 把阿黛尔的化妆箱送上来。

这一回我想出去的是我，可曼塞尔不肯。于是趁我放下信去，他又换回那身滴水的衣裳；我这边一吃上劲，他便回到栏杆那边去了。……

===Original===

Hanbury’s answer was clear.

As soon as he had changed, Mansel wrote out his reply.

> Good. Visit the beechwoods twice daily at ten and three. Don’t wait any longer tonight. Send Carson and another to Poganec with the enclosed note instead.

===Chinese===

汉伯里的回音清清楚楚。

曼塞尔一换好衣裳，便写出了他的复信。

> 好。每日两次、十点钟与三点钟巡视山毛榉林。今夜不必再等。改派卡森另带一人，携附函去波加内克。

===Original===

The note was addressed to Captain Pleydell and was very short.

> I have spoken with Adèle. She is very cheerful and seems to be in excellent health.

These dispatches I sent alone, and, when they were gone, we finally closed the trapdoor and getting into our blanket, lay down on the carpet to sleep.

===Chinese===

附函是写给普莱德尔上尉的，很短。

> 我已同阿黛尔说过话。她情绪很好，身体似极佳。

这几件信函由我独自送出；送完之后，我们最终合上活板门，钻进毯子，在地毯上躺下睡觉。

===Original===

Whether Mansel rested I know not, but I slept like the dead. The Closet was full of pale light when I awoke, and Mansel was at the window, with his eyes on the east.

---

===Chinese===

曼塞尔休息没休息，我不知道；我却睡得像死人。醒来时密室里满是一色淡光，曼塞尔已在窗边，眼睛望着东方。

---

===Original===

It was ten o’clock in the morning, the weather was very fine, and I had my ear to the keyhole of the archway door, when I heard a footstep upon the terrace beyond.

As I turned to summon Mansel, I felt his hand on my arm.

“Casemate is with her,” he said.

The next instant he was down in the channel and was peering under the door.

===Chinese===

早晨十点钟，天气很好；我正把耳朵贴在拱洞大门的锁眼上，忽听外面台地上响起脚步声。

我正要回头唤曼塞尔，已觉出他的手落在我臂上。

「凯斯梅特跟她在一起，」他说。

转眼间他已下到水道里，从门底下张望。

===Original===

Slowly Adèle and her warder passed into and out of my view. Her hair had been rudely shorn to the shape of her head, and about her shoulders she wore a rag of a shawl.

Then I heard Casemate’s voice.

“﻿—﻿—﻿— this sunshine,” he said. “Don’t you want a hat on your curls?”

“No,” said Adèle.

===Chinese===

阿黛尔和看守她的人缓缓走过我的视野，又缓缓走出。她的头发已被粗暴地剪得贴着头皮，肩上裹着一条破烂的旧披巾。

接着我听见凯斯梅特的声音。

「—— ——的大太阳，」他说。「不想给这头卷毛戴顶帽子么？」

「不戴，」阿黛尔说。

===Original===

“Well, I guess I do,” said Casemate. “You carry on, an’ move. You’re not here to wave to Charlie: you’re here for exercise. An’ Rose is red-hot this morning, so I shouldn’t tread on his toes.”

With that, he left her.

“Pull up the rope,” said Mansel; and, before I could think, he was gone.

I was taken so much by surprise that, though I did his bidding, I could hardly believe that our attempt at rescue was fairly begun. Casemate was seeking his hat: that, before he returned, we should be able to let down Adèle to safety seemed to me a chance in a thousand, while, if we were caught in the act, her life would be in great peril and our present enterprise wrecked. I found it unlike Mansel to take such a risk and was wondering whether for once his zeal had outrun his discretion, when Adèle’s face appeared in the channel almost between my legs.

===Chinese===

「哼，我看我是要戴的，」凯斯梅特说。「你只管走你的，动起来。你在这儿不是冲查理（Charlie）挥手的：你在这儿是来活动身子的。罗斯今儿早上火气正旺，最好别踩他的脚指头。」

说完，他撇下她走了。

「把绳收上来，」曼塞尔说；我还没来得及想，他已不在了。

我惊得厉害；虽照他的吩咐做了，却几乎不敢相信我们的营救当真已经开始。凯斯梅特找帽子去了：趁他回来之前把阿黛尔放下去送到安全处，在我看来是千分之一的侥幸；而若被当场拿住，她的性命便危殆万分，我们眼下的全盘图谋也将倾覆。这不像曼塞尔的行事；我正纳闷是否这一回他的热忱压过了他的审慎，阿黛尔的脸已在水道里露出来，几乎就在我两腿之间。

===Original===

In a flash I had her under the arms, and had drawn her clear of the door and lifted her up. Mansel followed at once, and, whilst I worked like a madman to pull up the rope, he spoke to Adèle.

“If we can do it in time, we shall let you down the face of the cliff. The moment you’re down, unfasten the rope and run for your life to the beechwoods that lie northeast.” He pointed the direction with his arm. “Over there. George or Carson will be there with one of the Rolls. Tell them to drive you﻿—”

He stopped short there, and, after listening intently, caught my wrist.

“Let go the rope,” he breathed.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

电光石火间我已抄住她的腋下，把她从门口拖出来，举了上去。曼塞尔跟着就到；趁我疯了似的收绳，他对阿黛尔说话。

「要是来得及，我们就把你从崖面放下去。你一落地，就解开绳，拼着命往东北面的山毛榉林跑。」他用手臂指了方向。「在那边。乔治或卡森会带一辆劳斯莱斯在那儿。叫他们把你载——」

他猛地收住话头，凝神听了听，一把攥住我的手腕。

「撒手，」他屏息道。……

===Original===

For a moment I could hear nothing. Then came Rose Noble’s voice.

“Hat be damned. I said ‘See that she moves.’ By —﻿—﻿—, that’s clean enough talking. D’you think﻿—”

And there, I suppose, he saw that Adèle was gone.﻿ ﻿…

For a moment there was dead silence. Then came a rush of steps to the balustrade.

===Chinese===

有一瞬我什么也听不见。随后传来罗斯·诺布尔的声音。

「帽子见鬼去。我说的是『瞧着她动弹』。凭—— ——，这话够干净明白了。你以为——」

我猜想，说到这里，他看见阿黛尔不见了。……

有片刻是死一般的寂静。随后是一阵奔向栏杆的脚步。

===Original===

Adèle and Mansel and I stood still as death, while the rope fled steadily back the way it had come. That Rose Noble must now observe it seemed almost certain: *whether he would see that it was moving we could not tell*.

A sudden roar from the terrace made my heart stand still.

“By —﻿—﻿—, she’s gone,” screeched Rose Noble. “Look at that —﻿—﻿— cord.”

“She’s never had time,” cried Casemate. “I only﻿—”

===Chinese===

阿黛尔、曼塞尔和我站着，僵如死尸，那条绳则稳稳地顺原路溜了回去。罗斯·诺布尔此刻多半会注意到它：*至于他会不会看出它正在动，我们无从知道*。

台地上一声怒吼，吼得我的心停了摆。

「凭—— ——，她跑了，」罗斯·诺布尔尖叫道。「瞧那条—— ——绳。」

「她哪来得及，」凯斯梅特喊道。「我才——」

===Original===

“*Time?*” howled Rose Noble. “*Time?* It’s only your white-livered sort that wants time to break out of hell. Bunch! Punter!” he yelled. “Turn out the —﻿—﻿— car. Go and help them, you —﻿—﻿—. And if we don’t take her at the foot of this blasted rock I’ll twist your block off your body with my bare hands.”

Before the threat was issued, Casemate was gone. I could hear him shouting for Bunch like a man possessed.

The rope was now back in the channel and lying as snug and as taut as though it had never been touched.
