Mansel was speaking low.

===Chinese===

「*时间？*」罗斯·诺布尔嚎道。「*时间？*」只有你们这种白肝的孬种，越狱才需要时间。邦奇！庞特！」他大吼。「把那—— ——车开出来。去帮他们的忙，你们这—— ——。要是不能在这块天杀的石头脚底下把她拿下，我就赤手空拳把你们的脑袋从脖子上拧下来。」

威胁的话还没有发完，凯斯梅特已经没影了。我能听见他唤邦奇的声音，像鬼附了体。

绳已回到水道里，服帖而紧绷地躺着，仿佛从未被人碰过。

曼塞尔在低声说话。

===Original===

“Sit down on the step, dear, and let me pull off your boots.”

Adèle obeyed him at once. She was, of course, drenched to the skin.

“Now go up these stairs: they’ll bring you into a room. Your dressing-case is there. Change your clothes as quickly as ever you can. The instant you’re through, come back.”

Here came voices from the courtyard, and almost at once I saw figures about the car. This was in the midst of the archway that ushered the castle gate and directly opposed to that beneath which we stood. The car was standing, as we were, in deep shadow, but the courtyard was full of light. It follows that I could not distinguish whose the figures might be but, after a little, I heard Rose Noble’s voice. I suppose the car was unready, for her engine would not start for all the frenzy with which someone was swinging the shaft. Another﻿—Casemate, I think﻿—was clumsily dashing spirit into the tank.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

「台阶上坐下，亲爱的，让我脱掉你的靴子。」

阿黛尔立刻依从了。她自然浑身湿透。

「现在上这些楼梯：会把你带进一间屋子。你的化妆箱在那里。尽快换掉衣裳。一换完，立刻回来。」

这时围院里传来人声，我几乎立刻看见车边的人影。车停在拱洞当中——那道拱洞通向城堡大门，正对着我们脚下这一道。车同我们一样待在深影里，围院却满是亮光。所以我分辨不出那些人影是谁，但过了一会儿，我听出了罗斯·诺布尔的声音。我想车还没备好，因为任凭有人发疯似的摇着摇柄，引擎就是不肯发动。另一个——我想是凯斯梅特——正笨手笨脚往油箱里灌酒精。……

===Original===

At last with a stammer and then with a sudden roar the engine came to life: and that put an end to my observation, for the noise was deafening and a dense smoke from the exhaust screened any movement that was made.

Suddenly the gate was opened, and the car shot out.

Mansel’s hand touched my shoulder.

“Can you see who’s shutting the gate?”

===Chinese===

引擎终于结结巴巴地、随即轰的一声活了过来：我的观察也就到此为止，因为那噪声震耳欲聋，排气管喷出的浓烟遮住了一切动静。

大门忽然开了，汽车射了出去。

曼塞尔的手碰了碰我的肩。

「看得见是谁在关大门么？」

===Original===

“I think it’s the woman,” said I. “Yes, I can see her skirt.”

“Good,” said Mansel. “And now I think we’ll follow. But I hope the others won’t try to get in their way. Could you tell at all how many got into the car?”

I shook my head.

“Rose Noble?”

===Chinese===

「我想是那个女人，」我说。「对，我看得见她的裙子。」

「好，」曼塞尔说。「现在我想我们也跟上去。但我希望其余的人别去拦他们的道。你到底看得出有几个人上了车么？」

我摇了摇头。

「罗斯·诺布尔呢？」

===Original===

“I assume so,” said I. “I certainly heard his voice. Besides, you heard what he said.”

“I don’t count what he says,” said Mansel. “If he saw that the rope was moving, I’ve played my cards wrong.”

“What else could you have done?”

“Followed Casemate and met him,” said Mansel.

===Chinese===

「我猜是他，」我说。「我确实听到了他的声音。再说，他刚才说的话你也听见了。」

「他说的话我不作数，」曼塞尔说。「他要是看见了绳在动，那是我牌打错了。」

「不然你还能怎么办呢？」

「跟着凯斯梅特，迎上去会他，」曼塞尔说。

===Original===

“But you couldn’t have known﻿—”

Mansel smiled.

“You never do﻿—at cards,” he said.

Adèle’s voice came from the stairway.

===Chinese===

「可你怎么可能料到——」

曼塞尔微微一笑。

「你是从来料不到的——打牌的时候，」他说。

楼梯上传来阿黛尔的声音。

===Original===

“I’m ready, Jonah,” she said.

A moment later we were in the King’s Closet.

With the utmost caution Mansel opened the door: then he signed to Adèle to follow. I caught up her dressing-case and brought up the rear.

The King’s Bedchamber was empty.

===Chinese===

「我好了，约拿，」她说。

转眼间我们已在国王密室里。

曼塞尔极其小心地开了门：然后示意阿黛尔跟上。我提起她的化妆箱，殿在后头。

国王寝宫空无一人。

===Original===

Like thieves in the night, we stole across its floor and into the Dining-room.

A moment later we entered the antechamber.

The door was as we had left it; but the hammers and chisels were gone, and the window was shut.

With a little manipulation I had the upper bolt free and wheedled it clear of the jamb. The lower, however, resisted, and Mansel, who was down on his knees, was able neither to turn it nor thrust it back.

===Chinese===

我们像夜里的窃贼，蹑足溜过寝宫的地板，进了餐厅。

随后我们进了前厅。

门还是我们离开时的样子；但锤子和凿子不见了，窗也关上了。

稍一摆弄，我便弄松了上面的插销，把它从门框里诓了出来。可下面那根纹丝不动；跪在地上的曼塞尔既转不动它，也推不回它。

===Original===

At length he rose to his feet.

“Are the ropes there, Chandos?”

I stepped to the window, but the ropes were gone.

When I told him, he frowned.

===Chinese===

末了他站起身来。

「绳在那里吗，钱多斯？」

我走到窗边，绳已不知去向。

我告诉他时，他皱起了眉。

===Original===

Then he wedged the door, so that it could not be opened from the opposite side, and rose to his feet.

“Come,” he said, turning. “We must try the other end of the suite.”

So we came back to the Closet and, passing through the Queen’s rooms, entered the other antechamber which the bookseller’s guide had shown us that we might expect.

There was now before us a door like that we had left, which gave, no doubt, into the other “gallery of stone.”

===Chinese===

于是他把门楔住，叫它从对面开不了，然后站起身。

「来，」他转身说道。「只好试试套房的另一头。」

我们回到密室，穿过王后的几间屋子，进了另一间前厅——书商那本指南让我们料到会有的那一间。

眼前又是一扇同我们刚才那扇一样的门，无疑通向另一条「石头回廊」。

===Original===

And this door also was fast.

I confess that here the sweat ran over my face.

To be within hail of freedom, to have the prison to ourselves, to have come so far, only to be prevented, as rabbits left in a hutch, was almost more than I could bear.

Mansel took a wedge from his pocket and thrust it under the door. Then he led us back to the Closet and down the winding stair.

===Chinese===

这扇门也锁得死死的。

我承认，到这里我脸上的汗淌了下来。

呼喊一声便能及到自由，牢狱尽归我们，走了这么远，却像留在笼里的兔子一般被拦住——这几乎超出我受得住的限度。

曼塞尔从口袋里摸出一枚楔子，塞到门下。然后他领我们回密室，下了那道螺旋楼梯。

===Original===

“Wait here,” he said. “It’s no good our all getting wet. I’m going through the door at the head of the terrace steps. That should be open, all right. And then, if I can, I’ll draw that blasted bolt.”

With that, he was gone.

I clapped my ear to the keyhole, and Adèle’s hand stole into mine. I remember thinking that she was seeking comfort, but now I know that she would have comforted me. I was trembling so much that I could not keep my head steady against the wood.

Then I heard the sound which I dreaded, and trembled no more.

===Chinese===

「在这儿等着，」他说。「犯不上我们全湿透。我从台地台阶顶上那道门过去。那道门应该是开的，没错。然后，要是我办得到，我把那根该死的插销拉开。」

说罢，他去了。

我把耳朵贴上锁眼，阿黛尔的手悄悄溜进我的手里。我记得当时以为她在寻求安慰，如今我才知道，她是想安慰我。我抖得厉害，头贴在木头上都贴不稳。

随后我听见了那叫我害怕的声音，便不再发抖。

===Original===

*I heard Mansel go by with a rush, to try the opposite door.*

He was back in an instant.

As I helped him out of the channel﻿—

“I should have kept a chisel,” he said. “But, chisel or no, we must try that bolt again.”

===Chinese===

*我听见曼塞尔一阵风似的奔过去，去试对面那扇门。*

转眼他又回来了。

我扶他从水道里出来时——

「我本该留下一把凿子，」他说。「可有凿子也罢，没凿子也罢，那根插销还得再试一回。」

===Original===

Adèle laid a hand on his arm.

“You’ll die of cold, dear,” she said. “Have you dry things?”

“We mustn’t wait, my lady. I﻿—”

“I won’t stir from here,” said Adèle, “until you’ve changed.”

===Chinese===

阿黛尔把一只手搭上他的手臂。

「你会冻死的，亲爱的，」她说。「你有干衣裳吗？」

「不能等了，我的女士。我——」

「你不换衣裳，我就不走，」阿黛尔说。

===Original===

Mansel smiled and was gone.

There were dry clothes in the Closet, for Hanbury had had the foresight to send us two changes apiece.

Adèle had me by the coat.

“Oh, why did you stay?” she breathed. “If Rose Noble gets him, he’ll break him by eighths of an inch. I never knew what hate was, till I heard that terrible man. And he’s got it in for Jonah from bottom to top. I’m nothing﻿—a lever at most﻿ ﻿… one of the levers of the rack. It’s only the money that can save him. Remember that. Dying men can’t sign cheques. But if ever the cheque is signed.﻿ ﻿… How much is he asking?”

===Chinese===

曼塞尔笑了笑，去了。

密室里有干衣裳，因为汉伯里有先见之明，给我们每人送了两套。

阿黛尔攥住了我的外衣。

「哦，你为什么要留下？」她悄声道。「罗斯·诺布尔要是逮住他，会把他一寸一寸地拆散。直到听见那个可怕的人，我才知道恨是什么滋味。他对约拿，从头到脚都是仇。我什么也不是——至多是根杠杆……拉肢架上的一根杠杆。只有钱能救他。记住这一点。要死的人开不了支票。可那张支票一旦签出。……他要多少？」

===Original===

“Five hundred thousand,” said I.

I felt her fingers tighten upon my coat.

“He’d take a hundred thousand﻿—*with Jonah thrown in*. I’ve heard him say so﻿—not once, but fifty times. I tell you﻿—”

“Chandos,” said Mansel from the stairway, “pull in that rope. Cut a length of a hundred yards. If we can’t shift that bolt﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

「五十万，」我说。

我觉出她的手指在我外衣上收紧了。

「十万他就肯收——*条件是把约拿饶进去*。我听他这么说过——不是一回，是五十回。我告诉你——」

「钱多斯，」楼梯上的曼塞尔说道，「把那绳收进来。割下一百码长的一段。那根插销要是挪不动……」

===Original===

What else he said I never knew, for the moment I handled the cord, *I knew that it had been severed a few feet away*.

I think I must have exclaimed, for Mansel called sharply to know what the matter might be.

“It’s cut already,” said I, and, with that, I pulled in what was left and proved my words.

With his back to Adèle, Mansel looked me full in the eyes.

===Chinese===

他还说了什么，我再没有知道，因为我的手一摸到绳，*就知道它已在几英尺外被割断了*。

想必我叫出了声，因为曼塞尔厉声问我出了什么事。

「已经割断了，」我说着，把剩下的收进来，证实了我的话。

曼塞尔背对着阿黛尔，直直望进我的眼睛。

===Original===

“I was right﻿—just now,” he said quietly. “I ought to have led out Clubs.” Then he turned to Adèle. “Come, my lady,” he said, “and we’ll have another whack at that door.”

“Tell me, Jonah,” said Adèle.

“My dear,” said Mansel, “Rose Noble’s a careful man. He locks the door before the horse is stolen, but he takes the precaution of locking it afterwards, too. Never mind. We’ll get out somehow.”

When we entered the Closet, he picked up the bottle of brandy and poured some into a glass.

===Chinese===

「我方才——是对的，」他静静地说。「我该先出梅花的。」然后他转向阿黛尔。「来，我的女士，」他说，「我们去把那扇门再敲一记。」

「告诉我，约拿，」阿黛尔说。

「亲爱的，」曼塞尔说，「罗斯·诺布尔是个仔细人。马没被盗之前他锁门，马盗走之后，他也照锁不误。别灰心。我们总能出去。」

进了密室，他拿起白兰地酒瓶，往一只杯子里倒了些。

===Original===

“The stirrup cup,” he said, smiling, and gave the glass to Adèle.

Then he and I drank quickly.

A moment later we were crossing the Bedchamber’s floor.

As we entered the Dining-room﻿—

===Chinese===

「上马酒，」他微笑着说，把杯子递给阿黛尔。

随后他和我急急喝了两口。

转眼我们正穿过寝宫的地板。

我们进餐厅时——

===Original===

“That’ll do,” said Rose Noble.

There was nothing to be said or done.

The fellow was sitting in a chair at the head of the table, with his elbows upon the board and a pistol in either hand.

---

===Chinese===

「行了，」罗斯·诺布尔说。

无话可说，无事可做。

那家伙坐在桌首的椅子里，两肘支在桌面上，两只手各握一支手枪。

---

===Original===

Never before or since have I felt so far out of my depth.

A moment before, we had had the suite to ourselves: the entrance doors had been wedged and as good as barred: no sound of any sort had come to our vigilant ears. Yet our movements had been closely observed, our intentions had been accurately gauged, and our enemy had been able to snare us with the effortless ease of a nurse outwitting a child.

“Keep your hands up,” said Rose Noble, “and come and sit down﻿—you two twopenny squirts to right and left, and the goods facing me. Move.”

Slowly we did as he said.

===Chinese===

从前没有，后来也没有，我曾这样六神无主过。

片刻之前，套房还尽归我们：入口的门楔得严实，与上了闩无异：任何声响都不曾进过我们警戒的耳朵。然而我们的一举一动都被密切注视着，我们的意图被估量得分毫不差，我们的敌手不费吹灰之力便扣住了我们，像一个保姆哄过一个孩子。

「手举着别放，」罗斯·诺布尔说，「过来坐下——你们两个铜板大的小崽子，一右一左，货物对面朝我坐。走。」

我们慢慢照他说的做了。

===Original===

“Now put your palms on the table.” Again we obeyed.

“And if anyone wants to die, they’ve only to move a hand.”

Desperately I tried to marshal my wits, for if ever a clear head was needed to save the game, it was needed now; but my brain was ever wayward, and I remember thinking how strange a picture we made and how much astonished King Maximilian would have been, could he have viewed such a company gracing his private board.

Before me Mansel sat easily, leaning back in his chair. His hair was wet and rumpled, and he wore no collar or tie. His light, tweed coat, turned up about his neck, became him admirably and a quiet smile was lighting his handsome face. On my left Adèle sat upright: her colour was high and, because of her short, crisp hair, she looked like some old picture of a beautiful boy. She was wearing a fawn-coloured dress, and white silk was edging her delicate wrists and throat. Laid upon the smooth, dark oak, her lovely hands were unforgettable. And on my right, deep in the King’s great chair, sat Rose Noble. One pistol lay before him: the other was in his right hand. His great bulk was loose as ever, and his huge face grey and flabby as when I had seen him first. He stayed so still that he might have been some gross idol, carved out of stone. Their lids, as usual, were almost hiding his eyes, and a faint smile was hanging upon the cruellest mouth that I have ever seen.

===Chinese===

「现在把手掌放到桌上。」我们又照办了。

「谁要是想死，只消动一动手。」

我拼命想收拢我的心智，因为若说何曾需要清明的头脑来救局，此刻最是需要；可我的脑子偏偏不听使唤；我记得当时在想，我们这幅图画何其古怪，马克西米利安国王若能看见这样一班人马装点他的私宴，又该何等惊讶。

我面前，曼塞尔从容地坐着，身子后仰。他的头发湿着，乱着，没有硬领，也没有领带。浅色的斜纹呢外套竖起领子拢在颈边，衬得他再好看不过，一抹安静的微笑正照亮他俊美的脸。我左边，阿黛尔端坐：脸色红润，因为那头剪短了的脆发，她像一幅旧画里俊美的少年。她穿一件浅黄褐色的裙子，白丝绸镶着纤细的手腕和喉咙。那双可爱的手搁在乌黑光洁的橡木上，令人难忘。我右边，深深陷在国王的大椅里，坐着罗斯·诺布尔。一支手枪摆在他面前：另一支握在右手里。他那庞大的身躯照旧松垮着，那张大脸灰白虚胖，同我初见他时一样。他纹丝不动，仿佛一尊石雕的粗蠢神像。那对眼睑照例几乎掩住了他的眼睛，一抹淡淡的笑，挂在人的嘴上——那是我见过的最残忍的一张嘴。

===Original===

For a long time he held his peace, but at length he gave a smooth laugh.

“ ‘Stone walls do not a prison make,’ ” he said softly. “An’ I guess you three could heckle the guy that wrote that.”

“My favourite maxim,” said Mansel pleasantly. He looked across the table at me. “William,” he said, “we must do better next time. There’s a door behind our host﻿—in the panelling. There’s probably one in each room. They open into a passage which﻿—”

“Quite so,” drawled Rose Noble. “Quite so. I call it ‘The Listening Post.’ ” His eyelids flickered, and the blood came into my face. “And now, perhaps, you’ll let the geography stew and listen to me. I wasn’t at Oxford College, but I guess the notes I’ve sent you were plain enough.”

===Chinese===

好半天他没有作声，末了发出一声圆滑的笑。

「『石墙造不成监狱，』」他柔声说。「我猜你们三位，尽可以去诘问写这话的家伙。」

「我最心爱的格言，」曼塞尔愉快地说。他隔着桌子望我。「威廉，」他说，「下一回我们得做得好些。我们这位东家背后有扇门——就在护壁板里。每间屋里恐怕都有一扇。它们通向一条走廊，那走廊——」

「正是，」罗斯·诺布尔慢条斯理地说。「正是。我管它叫『窃听哨』（The Listening Post）。」他的眼睑颤了颤，血涌上了我的脸。「现在呢，也许你们可以把地理搁在锅里炖着，来听听我的。我没上过牛津，可我写给你们的条子，我想够明白了。」

===Original===

“As plain as my replies,” said Mansel.

“I didn’t hear them,” said Rose Noble. “But, now we’re so snug, maybe you’ll say them again.”

“The first thing,” said Mansel at once, “is to clear the air. This lady may be your prisoner, but I am not. One doesn’t imprison one’s broker if one happens to want some funds. I mean, that’s elementary.”

“Maybe it is,” said Rose Noble. “One don’t spoil his right hand either. But I’ve known a guy that kicked when he had two lights, as mild as his mother’s milk when he had but one.”

===Chinese===

「同我的回条一样明白，」曼塞尔说。

「回条我没听见，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「不过如今我们这样舒舒服服，你们不妨再说一遍。」

「头一件事，」曼塞尔立刻说，「是把空气澄清。这位女士也许是你的囚徒，我却不是。人不会因为手头要用几个钱，就把自己的经纪人关起来。我是说，这是初等的道理。」

「也许是吧，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「人也不作践自己的右手。可我认识一个家伙，两只眼都完好的时候又踢又闹，只剩一只的时候，温顺得像他娘的奶。」

===Original===

“I daresay you have,” said Mansel. “It takes some people that way.”

Rose Noble moistened his lips.

“I’m not out to break you,” he said. “It’s the goods that’ll get the rough.”

“Are getting the rough,” said Mansel. “And there again I advise you to watch your step. ‘Perishable goods’ have a market: but ‘damaged goods’ make a very different price.”

===Chinese===

「想必你认识，」曼塞尔说。「有些人就是这个样子。」

罗斯·诺布尔润了润嘴唇。

「我不是冲着收拾你来的，」他说。「吃糙活的，是货物。」

「是正在吃着糙活，」曼塞尔说。「这里我再劝你放稳脚步。『易腐的货物』有市场：『受损的货物』可就是另一种价钱了。」

===Original===

“They’re not damaged﻿—yet,” said Rose Noble. “And I’m still waiting for a bid.”

Mansel raised his eyebrows.

“A few months ago,” he said quietly, “you stole some papers of mine. Give me them back, and I’ll pay you a hundred pounds and hold my tongue.”

I was aghast at his boldness and fully expected a truly dreadful outburst by way of reply. But none came: and after a little silence I breathed again.

===Chinese===

「还没有受损——眼下还没有，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「而我还在等出价。」

曼塞尔扬了扬眉。

「几个月前，」他平静地说，「你偷了我几份文件。还给我，我付你一百镑，并且守口如瓶。」

他的大胆吓得我发怔，满以为回敬我们的将是一场当真可怕的雷霆。可雷霆没有来：沉默了一小会儿之后，我又喘上了气。

===Original===

Rose Noble lifted his lids and looked at Adèle.

I cannot describe the awfulness of his gaze. Hatred, malice and all uncharitableness burned in those terrible orbs. Themselves monstrous, their message was like unto them, and before its beastly menace my blood ran cold.

“You hear?” he said grimly. “You’re pretty enough to fool round, but, when it’s a question of paying, your gentleman-friend gets off.”

Adèle flushed under his tongue.

===Chinese===

罗斯·诺布尔掀起眼睑，望着阿黛尔。

他的目光有多可怕，我无法描摹。仇恨、恶毒和一切不仁不恕，都在那双可怕的眼球里燃烧。那双眼本身已是怪物，传递的信息也同它们一样；在那兽性的胁迫面前，我的血凉了。

「听见了么？」他冷冷地说。「逗弄逗弄，你够标致的；可一旦谈到付钱，你那位绅士朋友就脱身了。」

他的话让阿黛尔红了脸。

===Original===

“It’s never been a question of paying,” she said.

“Big words,” said Rose Noble. “But they won’t pull you out of this mess. Your health and your name’s on the counter: and if you fancy either, you’d better trouble Big Willie to open his purse.”

“My name?” said Adèle, frowning.

“Your name,” said Rose Noble softly. “You see, I’m not selling to your husband. I’m selling to the man next door.”

===Chinese===

「这从来不是付钱的问题，」她说。

「好大的话，」罗斯·诺布尔说。「可它们拉你不出这摊烂泥。你的健康和你的名声都摆上了柜台：这两样你若稀罕哪一样，最好劳驾大个子威利解一解钱袋。」

「我的名声？」阿黛尔皱着眉说。

「你的名声，」罗斯·诺布尔轻声说。「你要知道，我卖的对象不是你丈夫。我卖给隔壁那个男人。」

===Original===

Adèle’s colour came and went. She looked round swiftly.

Then﻿—

“You mean﻿—”

“That I have not asked your husband to buy you back. That I have ignored his existence from first to last. That he’s all sure and grateful that Big Willie’s hoeing his row. *That Big Willie daren’t undeceive him﻿ ﻿… daren’t so much as breathe my name﻿—for fear of its putting ideas into his innocent head.*”

===Chinese===

阿黛尔的脸色红了又白。她飞快地四下看了看。

随即——

「你是说——」

「我是说：我没有开口要你丈夫把你赎回去。我是说：我从头到尾不理会他的存在。我是说：他一心笃定、满怀感激，只当大个子威利在替他锄他田里的垄。*我是说：大个子威利不敢点破他……连我的名字都不敢提一句——生怕往他那颗天真的脑袋里塞进念头。*」

===Original===

The brutal accuracy of this saying was to me like a buffet which makes the head sing again: and my brain seemed suddenly pygmy beside that of this terrible man.

Adèle had gone very pale.

“You mistake us,” she said coldly. “Our understanding﻿—”

“So I guess,” said Rose Noble, as though she had not opened her mouth, “your pretty name is as much for sale as your health. Of course, if it isn’t bought in, your husband can have it back. But it’s not every fool, by —﻿—﻿—, that’ll pick up a rotten rose.”

===Chinese===

这句话的残忍的准确，于我如同一记打得耳鸣眼花的耳光：在这可怕的人的头脑旁边，我的头脑忽然显得侏儒一般。

阿黛尔的脸已十分苍白。

「你误会我们了，」她冷冷地说。「我们的默契——」

「我也这么想，」罗斯·诺布尔说，仿佛她不曾开过口，「你那漂亮名声，同你的健康一样在出卖。当然，若没人买进，你丈夫还可以收回它。可凭—— ——，不是随便哪个蠢货都肯拾一朵烂玫瑰。」

===Original===

“I agree,” said Mansel. “In fact, all you say would be very much to the point, if I hadn’t told Captain Pleydell that I was in love with his wife.”

Very slowly the blood flowed into Adèle’s sweet face. She did not look at Mansel, and her eyes which were resting on Rose Noble, never moved. But, after a little, I saw that their focus had changed and that, though she was looking before her, she did not see, because she was lost in thought.

Rose Noble gave a thick laugh.

“I see,” he said smoothly. “And, of course, he couldn’t kick you﻿—because of his leg. Well, well﻿ ﻿… And if you think that chokes me, you’re nursing the dirty end. I guess I’ve a sleeve full of trumps﻿—but if you don’t want to see them you know the way.”

===Chinese===

「我同意，」曼塞尔说。「实际上，倘若我没有告诉过普莱德尔上尉我爱上了他的妻子，你说的这一切都会非常切题。」

血很慢很慢地涌回阿黛尔那张甜美的脸。她没有看曼塞尔，落在罗斯·诺布尔身上的眼睛也一动不动。但过了一会儿，我看出那双眼睛的焦点变了：她虽然望着前方，却什么也没看见，因为她已沉入思索。

罗斯·诺布尔发出一声浊笑。

「我明白了，」他圆滑地说。「当然，他踢不了你——因为他那条腿。好，好……你们若以为这就能噎住我，那你们是把脏的一头抱在怀里了。我猜我袖子里还有一把王牌——可你们要是不想看，路是认得的。」

===Original===

“I’m afraid you’ll have to play them,” said Mansel. “You see, we don’t mind paying, but it wouldn’t amuse the lady to know she’d been bought.”

“That’s right,” said Adèle.

Rose Noble sat back in his chair.

“You mean she don’t care to be mortgaged outside your arms?”

===Chinese===

「恐怕你非打出来不可了，」曼塞尔说。「你要知道，付钱我们并不介意；可让这位女士知道自己被买了去，她不会觉得有趣。」

「正是，」阿黛尔说。

罗斯·诺布尔朝椅背上一靠。

「你的意思是，她不愿在你双臂之外被抵押？」

===Original===

“I didn’t say that,” said Mansel.

“I’ll take it as read. I don’t move around on your dunghills, but I guess a woman’s a woman whether she’s warming her maid or selling fish. This man can bend her fingers, and that one can go to hell. ‘With love from little Willie’ don’t happen to suit her book. Maybe it don’t suit yours. And so I’ll help you out.” He leaned suddenly forward. “Be here in this room yourself a week from today, with two hundred and fifty thousand in Bank of England notes, and I’ll let the three of you go.”

“Nothing doing,” said Adèle swiftly.

“I am not prepared,” said Mansel, “to continue to talk this over while Mrs. Pleydell sits there.”

===Chinese===

「我没有这样说，」曼塞尔说。

「我就当是这样讲了。我不在你们的粪堆上转悠，可我看女人就是女人，不管她是让女仆暖衣的太太，还是卖鱼的婆子。这个男人能扳弯她的指头，那一个尽可以下地狱。『小威利谨赠爱心』偏偏不合她的账。也许也不合你的账。所以呢，我来给你们解围。」他突然俯身向前。「打今天起一个礼拜，你亲自带上二十五万镑英格兰银行钞票，到这间屋里来，我放你们三个走。」

「办不到，」阿黛尔立刻说。

「普莱德尔太太坐在那里，」曼塞尔说，「我便不打算再这样谈下去。」

===Original===

Rose Noble’s eyes narrowed.

“If you don’t like the rules,” he drawled, “you —﻿—﻿— well needn’t play. I’ve picked my words so far, but run me up and I’m not going to cramp my tongue. If you’re bunched round my table, God didn’t put you there. You horned right into this parlour, and, if you don’t like the eats, I guess you can swallow them whole.”

“I repeat my request,” said Mansel. “I decline to﻿—”

“I don’t fancy that verb,” snapped Rose Noble, using a savage oath. “You may feel ugly, but I’ve the right end of the gun. And now try and get me, you —﻿—﻿—. I’m running this —﻿—﻿— party from the soup to the pineapple’s bush. If you wanted a Bible reading, you’ve come the wrong day of the week. We’re talking business this morning. It mayn’t smell as sweet as lipsalve, but, if I find your linen dirty, I reckon you made it foul.”

===Chinese===

罗斯·诺布尔眯起了眼。

「你们要是嫌规矩不对胃口，」他懒洋洋地说，「那—— ——尽可不玩。到现在为止我都拣着字眼说话，可要是把我惹起来，我就不拿捏我的舌头了。你们围在我的桌子边，可不是上帝安放的。是你们自己一头拱进这间客厅的；饭菜不合口，我看你们可以囫囵吞下去。」

「我重申我的请求，」曼塞尔说。「我拒绝——」

「我不喜欢那个动词，」罗斯·诺布尔厉声道，夹出一句狠毒的咒骂。「你们心里发狠尽管发狠，可枪的顺手一头在我手里。现在来试试逮我啊，你们这—— ——。这场—— ——宴会，从头道汤到菠萝棵子，都是我在张罗。你们要听讲经文，来的日子不对。今天上午我们谈买卖。它闻着兴许不如唇膏香，可我要是发现你们的衬衣脏了，我估摸是你们自己弄脏的。」

===Original===

“I repeat my request,” said Mansel steadily.

Rose Noble sucked in his breath. Then he opened his blazing eyes.

“Then finish,” he said, “you —﻿—﻿—. You say you’ve told her husband you want his wife. I’ll give you some more to tell him, next time you meet. So far she’s lived alone in her private suite. Bedroom and bathroom adjoining, as tight as you please. Now we’ll cut out the bathroom, sonny, and she’ll share﻿—”

“I decline to﻿—”

===Chinese===

「我重申我的请求，」曼塞尔沉稳地说。

罗斯·诺布尔倒吸一口气。然后睁开了他那双冒火的眼睛。

「那就说完吧，」他说，「你这—— ——。你说你已告诉她的丈夫，你想要他的妻子。我再给你添几句话，下次见面带给他。到如今，她一直独个儿住在她的私人套房里。卧室带浴室，紧挨着，再妥帖不过。现在我们把浴室裁掉，小家伙，她来合住——」

「我拒绝——」

===Original===

With a roar Rose Noble flung forward, and Mansel’s bare hands shot out. The left struck aside the pistol, as Rose Noble fired: the right hit the beast on his mouth and knocked him into his chair. But for this, he must have gone down before the weight of the blow, but the chair was massive and, though it rocked for a moment, it held him up.

The sudden support saved him.

As I snatched at the second pistol, he swept it off the board, but the movement disordered his aim, and his second bullet went wide.

“Into the bedroom,” yelled Mansel, hurling an oaken footstool, with all his might.

===Chinese===

罗斯·诺布尔咆哮着猛扑过来，曼塞尔赤裸的双手闪电般击出。左手在罗斯·诺布尔开枪之际把枪格开：右手正中那张兽嘴，把他砸回椅中。若非有它，他必定承受不住这一击的分量栽倒在地；可那椅子厚重，虽晃了一晃，还是把他承住了。

这突如其来的支撑救了他。

我去夺第二支枪时，他把它扫离了桌面，但这一动作搅了他的准头，第二颗子弹打飞了。

「进卧室，」曼塞尔吼道，拼尽全力掷出一只橡木脚凳。

===Original===

As I hustled Adèle through the doorway, I heard a screech of pain and a third report. Then Mansel whipped into the room and I slammed the door.

“Quick! Wedge the doors,” breathed Mansel.

Now that we suspected their presence, the doors in the panelling were easy to find, and, since they opened inwards, before thirty seconds had passed the Closet and Bedchamber had been secured.

As I drove the last wedge, Mansel leaned his back to the wall and covered his eyes.

===Chinese===

我推着阿黛尔挤过门洞时，听见一声痛嚎和第三声枪响。随后曼塞尔旋进屋来，我砰地关上了门。

「快！楔门，」曼塞尔急促地低声道。

既已疑心它们的存在，护壁板上的门就好找了；又因门朝里开，不出三十秒，密室和寝宫都已楔牢。

我钉下最后一枚楔子时，曼塞尔背靠着墙，掩住了眼睛。

===Original===

“That chair,” he said brokenly. “That chair. It’s enough to break a man’s heart. I’d no time to hit him square, but I never so much as dreamed that that damned chair wouldn’t go. If it had﻿ ﻿…”

Adèle put her arms round his neck, drew down his head to hers and kissed his lips.

“My darling,” she said quietly, “no man could have done so well.”

“By God, that’s true,” I cried.

===Chinese===

「那把椅子，」他语声断续。「那把椅子。这能把人的心都气碎。我来不及正正地打他，可我连做梦也没想过，那把该死的椅子竟然不倒。它要是倒了……」

阿黛尔搂住他的脖子，把他的头拉向自己，吻了他的唇。

「亲爱的，」她平静地说，「换谁都做不到这么好。」

「老天在上，这话不假，」我喊道。

===Original===

“Oh, Adèle,” said Mansel simply, “I do love you so much.”

Then his arms went about her, and she hid her face in his coat.

---

===Chinese===

「哦，阿黛尔，」曼塞尔恳切地说，「我真是这么爱你。」

于是他的手臂环住了她，她把脸埋进他的外衣。

---

===Original===

Sitting on the floor of the Closet, I summed up our circumstances as best I could.

For the moment, Adèle was safe. What remained of the food which we had drawn up the cliff would last us with care for two days. We had three pistols, each holding seven rounds, and we had the run of two rooms and a way down into the archway﻿—for what it was worth. That we could hold this position I had no doubt: indeed, it seemed most unlikely that Rose Noble would make an attack, for, unless we three could break out or Hanbury and the servants could break in, we must in three or four days fall into his hands.

For us to break out would be extremely hard: we had long ago perceived that the two “galleries of stone” were the keys to the royal suite, and that these were joined by a passage meant that we were surrounded by a guardroom which two men could hold against twenty, the inside of which we had not so much as seen.

With Hanbury and the servants we were no longer in touch. Our line of communication had been cut, and, even though George should decide to attempt our relief, I could not see that four men could bring this about. Six had been none too many two nights ago, and for four to repeat an assault which had only succeeded because it was a surprise seemed to me a hopeless adventure.

===Chinese===

我坐在密室的地板上，尽我所能盘点了我们的处境。

眼下，阿黛尔是安全的。我们吊上崖来的剩余食物，省着吃够撑两天。我们有三支手枪，每支七发子弹，有两间屋子任我们出入，还有一条下到拱洞底下的路——尽管未必派得上用场。这一阵地守得住，我毫不怀疑：说实在的，罗斯·诺布尔多半不会来攻，因为除非我们三个冲得出去，或汉伯里和仆人们攻得进来，三四天之内我们必落进他手里。

我们要冲出去，难乎其难：两条「石头回廊」是王室套房的钥匙，这一点我们早就看清了；而两廊之间又有走廊相连，便等于我们被一间卫兵室团团围住——两个人就能在里面挡住二十个——而那里面，我们连看都没看过一眼。

我们同汉伯里和仆人们已断了联系。交通线已被切断，纵然乔治决心设法救我们，我也看不出四个人能成什么事。两夜之前，六个人尚且不算多；那一回突袭之所以得手，全仗出其不意，如今叫四个人故技重演，在我看来是无望的冒险。

===Original===

I found it hard not to believe that Fortune had taken her stand on the enemy’s side. Had we known of the doors in the panelling, not once but five times over should we have won our match: without that precious knowledge, two nights ago we had all but rescued Adèle: if Rose Noble had not met Casemate in search of his hat, Adèle by now might well have been thirty miles off: but for the weight of the chair, Rose Noble must have been dead ten minutes ago. This last was a bitter thought. Looking back, I perceived how Mansel had made the brute angry in order to make him move and had actually lured the master into the way of a fool, how he had ignored gross insult and let Adèle suffer in silence to gain his end, and how only the fear of depriving Adèle of his service had made him break off a battle which he might well have won.

Then I fell to considering Mansel and Adèle and their love﻿—for now I was sure that she loved him as he did her﻿—and what ever would be the outcome of an affair at once so passionate and so much out of joint.

God knows I did not blame them: I was rather exulting to see two such great hearts at one. It was the future that troubled me, the reckoning that would have to be paid.

And whilst I was in the midst of this reflection, the two came into the Closet, with shining eyes.

===Chinese===

我很难不认为，命运女神已把她的营盘扎到了敌人一边。倘若我们知道护壁板里有门，这场较量不说一次、足足五次都赢下来了：没有那条宝贵的知识，两夜之前我们也几乎救出了阿黛尔：倘若罗斯·诺布尔没有撞见找帽子的凯斯梅特，阿黛尔此刻多半已在三十英里外：若不是那把椅子厚重，罗斯·诺布尔十分钟前就死了。最后这一想最是苦涩。回想之下，我才明白曼塞尔怎样故意撩起那畜生的怒火、好叫他动弹，当真把这高手诱进了蠢人的路数；他怎样把不堪的侮辱置若罔闻，让阿黛尔默默受苦，以求达到目的；又怎样只是怕阿黛尔失去他的效力，才中断了一场他很可能打赢的搏斗。

随后我转而思量曼塞尔和阿黛尔，思量他们的爱情——如今我确信她爱他，正如他爱她——思量这样一段既炽热又全然错位的情事，到头来会有什么结局。

天知道，我毫不怪他们：眼见两颗这样伟大的心合而为一，我毋宁是欢欣的。让我不安的是将来，是那终须偿付的清算。

我正想着这些，那两位进了密室，眼睛闪闪发亮。

===Original===

I got to my feet, and Adèle gave me her hand.

I kissed it naturally.

“William,” she said, “I think I have the finest lover in the world.”

“You have, indeed,” said I, gravely.

===Chinese===

我起身，阿黛尔把手伸给我。

我自然而然地吻了它。

「威廉，」她说，「我想我拥有世上最出色的恋人。」

「的确如此，」我郑重地说。

===Original===

“William the Faithful,” said Mansel and laughed like a boy. He stepped to the trap. “And now,” said he, “I’m going to spy out the land. Don’t talk too loud, you two, and stay in the doorway, please, so that you can watch both rooms.”

“Very good,” said I, and went to my post at once.

When Mansel was gone, Adèle came to my side.

“Sit down, William,” she said. “I want you to know how I feel.”

===Chinese===

「忠诚的威廉（William the Faithful），」曼塞尔说，笑得像个孩子。他走到活板门边。「现在，」他说，「我去侦察地势。你们俩别大声说话，请守在门口，好看住两间屋子。」

「很好，」我说，随即上了我的岗位。

曼塞尔下去后，阿黛尔走到我身边。

「坐下，威廉，」她说。「我要你知道我的心情。」

===Original===

We sat down on the floor, like two children, and Adèle leaned her head against the wall and slid her slight arm through mine.

“I’m in love with Jonah,” she said. “I think I’ve loved him for years, but I never knew. And then, when I first saw Rose Noble and Jute pulled me off the mare, I remembered our talk in the forest and I knew what they wanted me for. They meant to hurt Jonah through me. That meant that Jonah loved me: and then, all of a sudden, I knew that I loved him. I was terribly worried at first, because it seemed so awful to be in love with anyone other than Boy. And then I came to see that, so long as nobody knew, no harm would be done. And I made up my mind that no one should ever know﻿—not even Jonah himself﻿ ﻿… Well, you saw me break down. You saw me kiss him and put my arms round his neck. I suppose I shouldn’t have done it, but I’m so thankful I did. I want you to know that, William. And Jonah’s so happy, too. We’ve had it all out and we both of us feel the same. You see, we’re locked up here, in a sort of No Man’s Land that lies between life and death. If we die﻿—well, that’s the end. If we live, then we go back. But we can’t tell which it will be, and, so long as we’re in that land, we’re going to love each other with all our heart.”

“But, oh, Adèle,” said I, “what about going back?”

A very soft light came into her wonderful eyes.

===Chinese===

我们在地板上坐下，像两个孩子；阿黛尔把头靠在墙上，一条纤细的胳膊滑进我的臂弯。

「我爱上了约拿，」她说。「我想我已经爱了他好些年，只是自己从不知道。后来，我头一回见着罗斯·诺布尔、朱特把我从马上拽下来时，我想起了我们在森林里的谈话，明白他们要我去做什么。他们是要借我来伤害约拿。那便意味着约拿爱我：于是忽然之间，我知道自己也爱他。起初我惶恐极了，因为除了『小子』竟爱上了别人，这显得太可怕了。后来我渐渐明白，只要没有人知道，便没有伤害造成。于是我打定主意，谁也不让知道——连约拿本人也不让……唉，你看见我失态了。你看见我吻他，搂住他的脖子。我本不该那样做的，可我多么感激自己做了。我要你知道这一点，威廉。约拿也那么快活。我们把话都说尽了，两个人的心思一样。你看，我们被锁在这里，锁在一片生死之间的无人之境里。若是死了——那就是终局。若是活着，我们便回去。可谁也说不准是哪一样；而只要还在那片土地上一天，我们就要全心全意地相爱。」

「可是，哦，阿黛尔，」我说，「回去以后怎么办呢？」

一缕极温柔的光，进了她那双奇妙的眼睛。

===Original===

“You can’t take away memory,” she said. “That we shall have forever, like a star that’s always in the sky. I’m not seeking to justify myself. I know I’m another man’s wife. But I don’t care, William. Nature and Fortune have driven us into this Eden, and I don’t think we should be human if we didn’t help each other to pick the flowers.”

“I don’t think you would,” said I heartily.

And that is the point of view I hold today. I daresay it cannot be defended. I can only say that Jonathan Mansel and Adèle were two of a kind that I never saw before and have never seen since. They were wise: “they were lovely and pleasant in their lives”: they were nonpareil: and, looking back, I find it most natural that, suddenly faced with life in the midst of death, two natures so alike and so peerless should have comforted each other.

Adèle looked at me swiftly.

===Chinese===

「记忆是夺不走的，」她说。「那我们将永远拥有，像一颗永远挂在天上的星。我不是要为自己辩白。我知道我是别人的妻子。可我不在乎，威廉。造化和命运把我们赶进了这座伊甸园，我想，我们若不彼此帮着摘一摘园里的花，便算不得人了。」

「我想你们不会不摘的，」我由衷地说。

直到今天，我持的还是这个看法。我承认这看法无从辩护。我只能说，乔纳森·曼塞尔和阿黛尔，是我前所未见、后无所见的一对人。他们有智慧：「他们在生之日，可爱而可悦」：举世无双：而回想起来，我觉得最自然不过——两个如此相似、如此无双的天性，骤然在死亡之中面对生命，自当彼此慰藉。

阿黛尔飞快地看了我一眼。

===Original===

“Do you mean that you understand? I don’t think you can do that?”

“I can understand enough,” said I, “to be very glad you’re so happy.”

“Thank you, William,” she said. “And please don’t feel left out. Jonah was only saying a moment ago that you were the only living being he didn’t mind seeing our love.”

“History repeats itself,” said I, indicating the rooms. “The equerry doesn’t feel left by his king and his queen.”

===Chinese===

「你的意思是说，你懂得？我不信你能懂得？」

「我懂得的尽够了，」我说，「够我为你的幸福欢欣了。」

「谢谢你，威廉，」她说。「也请你别觉得受了冷落。约拿方才还说，你是世上唯一他不在乎看见我们相爱的活人。」

「历史在重演，」我说，指了指这些屋子。「御前侍从从不会被他的国王王后撇下。」

===Original===

“Oh, he’s a courtier,” cried Adèle, clapping her hands.

“Don’t slander my lieutenant,” said Mansel, putting his head through the floor. “And here’s a piece of good news.” He sank his voice to a murmur. “The car is still out.”

“Which means?” breathed Adèle.

“I hope and believe it means that it’s gone for good, that George and the servants have got it and all or some of its crew. When Rose Noble sent them out, he knew very well that there was nothing to see: he knew we were in the archway and he sent them packing to mislead us and draw us out: I admit he pulled that off, but, unless and until they return, I believe he’s alone.”

===Chinese===

「哦，他是个朝臣，」阿黛尔拍着手喊道。

「不许诽谤我的副官，」曼塞尔说着，把头从地板下探了出来。「还有个好消息。」他把声音压成低语。「汽车还没回来。」

「这意味着什么？」阿黛尔轻声问。

「我盼望并且相信，这意味着它一去不返了：乔治和仆人们截住了它，连同车上全部或一部分人马。罗斯·诺布尔派他们出去时，很清楚外面没什么可看的：他知道我们在拱洞底下，把他们打发走，是为了迷惑我们、诱我们出去：这一手他确实得逞了，我承认；可他们一天不回来，我便相信只剩他孤身一人。」

===Original===

I suppose that I showed my excitement, for he continued at once.

“But please remember Rose Noble’s a host in himself. He may be single-handed, but he’s worth ten ordinary men. And so we must watch and wait. If only we’d known of that passage two nights ago﻿—”

“Then,” said Adèle, “you’d never have known that I loved you. And now, please, I’m hungry. Will one of you give me some food?”

Mansel smiled.

===Chinese===

想必我露出了兴奋，因为他立刻接着说了下去。

「可请记住，罗斯·诺布尔一个人就是一支大军。他也许是孤身一人，可他抵得上十个寻常人。所以我们必须守望等待。哪怕两夜之前我们知道那条走廊——」

「那样的话，」阿黛尔说，「你就永远不会知道我爱上了你。好了，拜托，我饿了。你们哪位给我点吃的？」

曼塞尔笑了。

===Original===

“Feminine influence, William, is a terrible thing. With this girl-child round our necks, we shall forget we’re at war.”

“Forget it,” said I, rising, “for half an hour. While you two lunch, I’ll watch the castle gate.”

“Very well,” said Mansel, hoisting himself to the floor. “But keep in the shadow and report the first sign of life. If Hanbury’s sunk the pinnace, I don’t think what’s left of her crew will come aboard before dark. At least, they’ll be fools if they try. But we can’t hang our lives on guesswork and so we must watch. And, by the time I’ve eaten, I may have some plan.”

But he had not: neither had I.

===Chinese===

「女性化的影响，威廉，是件可怕的事。脖子上挂着这么个女孩儿，我们会忘了自己身在战争。」

「那就忘它半个钟头，」我起身说。「你们两位用午饭，我去看住城堡大门。」

「很好，」曼塞尔说着，把自己撑上地板。「但要待在暗影里，一见人影就报告。汉伯里若是击沉了那条小艇，我想艇上剩下的人，天黑前不会回船。至少，他们要是回来就是蠢货。可我们不能把性命押在猜测上，所以必须守望。等我吃完了，说不定能想出个计划。」

但他没有：我也没有。

===Original===

Through all that afternoon we three might have been alone in the Castle of Gath. If others spoke or moved, we neither saw nor heard them, and, when the light faded, we were still without any grounds on which to base any belief.

Of the ways of the castle Adèle knew far less than we, for she had been brought in blindfold and had never, before we seized her, been beyond the “gallery of stone”: but, had she been able to tell us that a tunnel led out of the chapel to Salzburg itself, the knowledge would not have helped us, for we dared not use the passage, in case it was held.

What remained of the waterfall cord was some sixty feet long, and this we had all ready to help our escape. Could we, therefore, have gained the roof, we could doubtless have reached the spur; but we had no means of ascent, and, though I essayed both chimneys, these were built of hewn stone and only a very small child could have made its way up their shafts.

But for Adèle, Mansel and I would have sallied and might well, I think, have escaped. If the passage was under fire, at least it must be lighted by windows which overlooked the courtyard, and we would have chanced a lot in order to reach the gate. To such a risk, however, we dared not expose Adèle. Here I think we were wise. Unless he could starve us to surrender, Rose Noble’s hope of ransom was gone by the board: once we sallied, therefore, the fellow had nothing to gain by sparing our lives﻿—but much to lose, for he knew very well that we would shoot him at sight. Add to this that we had lately enraged him as never before, and you will see that to run such a gauntlet, with Adèle, so to speak, in our arms, was out of the question.

===Chinese===

整整一个下午，我们三个简直像是独占着加斯城堡。倘使有人说话走动，我们既没看见也没听见；天光暗尽时，我们依旧没有任何依据，足以确立一种判断。

城堡的路径，阿黛尔比我们知道的少得多，因为她是被蒙着眼进来的，被我们夺下之前，从未走出过「石头回廊」：而即便她能告诉我们有一条地道，从礼拜堂直通萨尔茨堡，这知识也帮不了我们，因为我们不敢走那条通道，唯恐有人把守。

瀑布绳剩下的约莫六十英尺长，我们已把它备好，以助脱身。倘若我们能上得屋顶，无疑便能抵达山嘴；可我们没有攀爬的工具，两处烟囱我也都试过了，都是琢石砌成，只有很小的孩子才能钻得上它们的通道。

若不是为了阿黛尔，曼塞尔和我早就杀出去，而且我想十有八九能逃脱。那条通道纵然处于火力之下，至少总该有俯瞰围院的窗子透光，为了抵达大门，我们愿意冒很大的险。可这样的险，我们不敢让阿黛尔来担。在这一点上，我想我们是明智的。除非把我们饿到投降，罗斯·诺布尔勒索的指望已成泡影：因而我们一旦杀出去，这家伙留我们性命便毫无好处——可损失却大得很，因为他很清楚，我们见了他就会开枪。再加上我们新近把他激怒得空前未有，你便明白，怀里差不多抱着个阿黛尔去闯那样的刀山，是万万谈不上的。

===Original===

At last we decided to write a message to George and making it into a parcel, to cast it over the cliff.

So far as my memory serves me, this was how the note ran:

> George.
> The rope has been cut. We are fast in the Closet and the Bedchamber and we have got Adèle. There is a passage connecting the two stone galleries and looking upon the courtyard. You will attack tomorrow﻿—Friday, half an hour after dark. As before, gain the roof: go directly to the door in the southwest tower: guard that: then let fall three ropes to the windows of the King’s Closet: the moment these are in place, *demonstrate*. You will demonstrate by letting down a ladder to one of the windows of the passage and accidentally breaking the glass. Carefully rehearse the demonstration, which must last one minute. Whilst it is going on, you will take up Adèle by one of the ropes: rush her along the roof, lower her down to the spur and run with her for the wood: Chandos and I will follow. Not counting the caretakers, Rose Noble is, I think, alone﻿—except for those who escaped when you stopped the car. Work out the whole attack with the greatest care: it must not fail.

===Chinese===

最后我们决定给乔治写封信，把它包成个包裹，抛下崖去。

就我记忆所及，信是这样写的：

> 乔治。
> 绳索已被割断。我们困在密室与寝宫里，阿黛尔已在我们手中。有一条走廊连接两条石头回廊，俯临围院。你们明日进攻——星期五，天黑后半小时。同前番一样，先上屋顶：直趋西南塔中的那扇门：守住它：然后朝国王密室的窗子放下三条绳：绳一就位，便*佯攻*。佯攻之法：向走廊的一扇窗放下梯子，失手打碎玻璃。把佯攻仔细演练，须持续一分钟。佯攻进行之际，你们用其中一条绳把阿黛尔拉上去：带她沿屋顶疾走，把她缒到山嘴上，携她奔树林：钱多斯与我随后。除看守城堡的人不算，我想罗斯·诺布尔是孤身一人——你们拦车时逃脱的那些人除外。把整个进攻筹划到万分仔细：绝不容失败。

===Original===

We wrapped the note in oiled silk and then in a wet coat of mine, to serve as ballast: this we made into a parcel, and at ten o’clock that night I hurled it over the cliff.

Neither Mansel nor I had expected to sleep at all, for the castle gate had to be watched as well as the doors of the chambers in which we lay; but Adèle insisted on taking her turn with us, so each of us slept for two hours and watched for four.

Adèle, was the first to rest, and I was the last; and I remember how I stole up the stairway at two o’clock, to find the rooms full of moonlight, Adèle in the midst of the doorway with her fair head against the jamb, and Mansel sleeping like a child, with her arms about him and his head in her lap. And, when he was gone to the archway and I would have lain alone, she would not have it so, but made me lie down as he had and pillow my head upon her.

---

===Chinese===

我们把信用油绸裹了，又裹上我的一件湿外套充作压载：把它扎成包裹，当晚十点钟，我奋力把它抛下了悬崖。

曼塞尔和我本都料想根本睡不成，因为城堡大门要看守，我们躺着的两间屋的门也要看守；可阿黛尔执意要同我们轮班，于是每人睡两个钟头，守望四个钟头。

阿黛尔第一个歇息，我最后一个；我记得两点钟时我悄悄上得楼来，见满屋月光，阿黛尔正当门而立，美丽的头靠着门框，曼塞尔睡得像个孩子，被她搂着，头枕在她膝上。待他去了拱洞、我该独自躺下时，她却不肯，叫我像他那样躺下，把头枕在她身上。

---

===Original===

The castle gate was not opened during the night: and this made us certain that those that had left with the car were in Hanbury’s hands.

We, therefore, decided to watch no more from the archway, but only to use it as and when we required. To this end we stopped the keyholes of the door and the gate and wedged a cage-grate in the channel beneath the door, to prevent an entry by stealth while our backs were turned.

And here let me say that before it was light we had all three bathed in the channel and made as fair a toilet as our means would allow. Mansel and I could do no more than shave and make ourselves clean, but Adèle must change her frock for one of a powder blue, and, when we sat down to breakfast, she was as point-device in appearance as though she had just left her bedroom in London Town.

The day passed quietly enough, and, except that one of us was always in the Bedchamber, listening for any sound, we kept no particular watch.

===Chinese===

城堡大门一夜未开：这使我们确信，随车离去的那伙人已落进汉伯里手里。

于是我们决定不再从拱洞守望，只在我们需要时用它。为此我们堵住了门和大门的锁眼，又在门下的水道里楔进一道铁栅，以防我们背过身时有人潜入。

在这里我要说一句：天亮之前，我们三个都在水道里洗了澡，尽了手头的条件，各自收拾得体体面面。曼塞尔和我无非刮刮脸、把自己弄干净，阿黛尔却定要换上一件粉蓝的衣裙；待我们坐下用早饭时，她仪容齐整，仿佛方才走出伦敦城的闺房。

这一天过得够平静，除了始终有一人守在寝宫里听动静，我们没做特别的守望。

===Original===

Adèle and Mansel were happy as the day was long. I have never seen two beings so plainly glad of each other, so easy and natural in their love. There was nothing common or unclean in all their tenderness, and, so far from embarrassing my senses, my acquaintance with such devotion lifted up my heart.

One thing only troubled us, and that was the absolute silence which reigned without our doors.

That Rose Noble should make no sound was natural enough: yet the continuous absence of any sign of life came to insist that we had the castle to ourselves and to tempt us against all reason out of our lair. This temptation we certainly resisted, but with every hour the suggestion that we were alone increased in strength, until, when the evening came, we were all three unsettled and did not know what to think. In a way this did not matter, for, if, indeed, Rose Noble were out of the way, our release by Hanbury must be a simple affair: yet the bare idea of such fortune seemed something sinister, like the counsel of a prophet whose eyes are not straight in his head.

“The truth is,” said Mansel, “we ought to have played his game. We shouldn’t have made a sound for twenty-four hours. Then he’d’ve begun to wonder if we were gone: and at last he’d’ve tried to find out and shown his hand. As it is, the positions are reversed, and he’s fairly got us guessing﻿—which is just what he wants.”

===Chinese===

阿黛尔和曼塞尔从早到晚都快活极了。我从没见过两个人如此明明白白地彼此欢喜，相爱得这样从容自然。他们的温柔里没有一丝鄙俗或不洁；这样的挚爱非但不扰我的耳目，反倒使我心气高昂。

只有一件事让我们不安：门外主宰着绝对的寂静。

罗斯·诺布尔不出声，本很自然：可长久不见一丝生命的迹象，渐渐变成了一个执念，咬定城堡里只剩我们，又引诱我们全然不顾死活地出了巢穴。这诱惑我们自然抵住了，但随着一个个钟头过去，「我们独占城堡」的念头愈发强劲，到傍晚时分，我们三个都心神不定，不知该怎么想。这在一方面无关紧要，因为倘若罗斯·诺布尔真不在了，汉伯里来救我们必是轻而易举的事：可单是这种好运的念头，便显得有些凶险，像一个先知眼神不正时给出的主意。

「实情是，」曼塞尔说，「我们本该照他的牌路打。二十四小时里不该出一点声。那他就会开始疑心我们走了：最后他就会设法查明，亮出自己的牌。如今形势倒了个个儿，他稳稳地让我们猜——那正是他要的。」

===Original===

This was uncommon sense, but, even whilst I agreed, I found myself supposing that Rose Noble was dead or gone and finding the supposition curiously untoward.

For this strange uncertainty of outlook I have never been able to account: I am not given to imagining vain things, or to letting my fancy fly in the face of fact: yet, though I was not uneasy, my mind would not come to rest, but continually dwelled upon the silence and the prosperous tale which it told.

At last the day was over, and dusk came in.

When it was dark, we opened the Closet windows and shut the trapdoor. Then Mansel set me at a window, with Adèle by my side, and himself to watch the Bedchamber until the moment should come.

===Chinese===

这话见识非凡，可即便在点头称是之际，我发觉自己正在设想罗斯·诺布尔已死或已走，又觉得这设想蹊跷得不祥。

这般奇怪的前景不定，我始终无从解释：我不是惯于空想的人，也不惯让想象顶着事实飞：然而，虽则我并不惶惑，我的心却安不下来，总是萦回着那寂静，和它讲述的那番兴隆的故事。

这一天终于过完，暮色进来了。

天黑之后，我们打开密室的窗，关上活板门。随后曼塞尔把我安置在一扇窗边，阿黛尔陪着我，他自己去看守寝宫，直到那时刻来临。

===Original===

The night was most black and still, and I stood leaning out into the thunderous air, straining my ears for the rustle of a rope coming down.﻿ ﻿…

But none came: and, after a long time, I began to feel sick at heart.

I did not move, for the night was before us, and while it was dark we could hope: but Hanbury was always punctual﻿—and now he was late.﻿ ﻿…

I do not know how long I stood there, but suddenly my heart bounded as I heard a movement above.

===Chinese===

夜极黑，极静；我探身立在轰鸣的空气里，竖起耳朵，等一条绳滑下来的窸窣声。……

可始终没有：过了许久，我开始心里发苦。

我没有动，因为长夜在我们面前，只要天还黑着，我们就还有指望：可汉伯里向来守时——如今他迟了。……

我不知道在那里站了多久，忽然，头顶上一声响动，我的心猛地一跳。

===Original===

An instant later I had a rope in my hands.﻿ ﻿…

As I put my weight upon it, another rope brushed my arms, and almost at once, a third.

By my silent direction, Adèle gave one flash with her torch and, in a twinkling, Mansel was by our side.

Himself he tested the ropes: then he bound one about her and lifted her on to the sill.

===Chinese===

转眼间，一条绳已在我手里。……

我把体重压上去时，另一条绳擦过我的手臂，几乎同时，又是一条。

依我无言的指示，阿黛尔用手电打了一下闪光，转眼间曼塞尔已到我们身旁。

他亲手试了试绳：然后把一条捆在她身上，把她抱上窗台。

===Original===

Adèle put her arms round his neck and pressed her face against his.

So we stayed, waiting.﻿ ﻿…

Then came a clatter from the passage and the shiver of broken glass.

“My beautiful darling,” said Mansel, and swung her out of the window and into the night.

===Chinese===

阿黛尔搂住他的脖子，把脸贴上他的脸。

我们就这样待着，等候。……

接着，走廊那边传来哗啦一声，玻璃打碎了，颤出一串碎响。

「我美丽的宝贝，」曼塞尔说着，把她荡出窗口，送进夜里。

===Original===

For a second he watched her rising: then he drew back.

“You next, William,” he said; “as quick as you can.”

At once I leaned out to grope for the other two ropes.

As I did so, a second clatter came from beyond our doors.

===Chinese===

他望着她升上去，望了一秒：随即缩回身来。

「下一个是你，威廉，」他说；「越快越好。」

我立刻探身出去，去够另外两条绳。

正在这时，门外又传来第二阵哗啦声。

===Original===

“Quick,” breathed Mansel. “That demonstration’s too thin.”

Desperately I flung out both arms, sweeping the air﻿—*and found nothing*.

“They’re gone,” I cried, drawing back. “They were﻿—”

Mansel was at the next window, leaning out and craning his neck.

===Chinese===

「快，」曼塞尔低声道。「那佯攻太单薄了。」

我拼命挥出双臂，扫过空气——*却什么也没扫着*。

「绳没了，」我喊道，缩回身来。「刚才明明——」

曼塞尔已到了下一扇窗，探身出去，伸着脖子。

===Original===

As I did the same, a very faint exclamation came down from above.

And then a thick laugh.

“Isn’t that nice?” said Rose Noble.

===Chinese===

我也照做时，头顶上飘下来一声极轻的惊呼。

接着是一声浊笑。

「这不是很妙么？」罗斯·诺布尔说。

## VII We Practise to Deceive / 七 骗局迭起

===Original===
Try as I will, I cannot distinctly remember what then took place, and I think that I acted blindly, as a man in a trance.

I know that we were both in the passage, the main doors of which were fast shut, that our rope was dangling from a window and that Mansel was about to go down, when the beam of a torch illumined his head and shoulders, and a bullet sang past his ear.

I know that the light and the shot both came from directly above, so that, placed as we were, we could not so much as reply.

I know that we were both on the terrace and that Mansel was casting the rope in the vain hope of catching a merlon in the noose he had made.

I know that we were both in the antechamber, that the great door was as we had left it and that, whilst I fought like a madman to shift the bolt, Mansel was kneeling beside me with his head in his hands.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
尽管我百般回想，当时发生的事我怎么也记不真切了；我想，我当时的举动全凭盲目，像一个失魂的人。

我只记得我们两个都在那条通道里，通道的大门紧闭着，我们的绳索正从一扇窗垂下，曼塞尔正要下去，忽然一道手电的光照亮了他的头和肩，一颗子弹贴着他的耳朵呼啸而过。

我只记得那道光和那一枪都来自正上方，以致以我们当时的处境，连还一枪都不能。

我只记得我们两个都在台地上，曼塞尔正把绳索抛出去，徒然指望绳头的活扣能套住一根垛齿。

我只记得我们两个都在前厅里，那道大门还是我们离开时的样子，我疯了一般拼命去拽那门闩，曼塞尔却跪在我身旁，双手抱着头。……

===Original===
At last I felt a touch on my shoulder, and Mansel got to his feet. I followed him into the Dining-room slowly enough. My head was strangely heavy, and I felt shaken and spent. Had he bade me lie down and sleep, I should have done his bidding without a word. The shock of what we had done had left me listless.

Mansel sat down in a chair, and I sat down in another and waited for him to speak.

To be honest, I hoped he would stay silent, for I could think of no comment upon our case which would not be bitter as death and wholly vain: but, when he began to speak, he did so with such composure as fairly shamed me out of a humour so recreant and so mean.

===Chinese===
末了我觉得肩上被碰了一下，曼塞尔站起身来。我跟随他走进餐厅，脚步慢得出奇。我的头沉得古怪，只觉得自己被震散了架，一点力气也没有。他若吩咐我躺下睡觉，我会一言不发地照办。我们方才那一场的震动，把我弄得心灰意懒。

曼塞尔在一张椅子上坐下，我在另一张里坐下，等他开口。

说实话，我倒希望他保持沉默，因为对我们眼下的处境，我想不出一句评语不是苦得要命、又全然无用的：可他一开口，那份镇定简直让我羞愧难当，把我从那样怯懦、那样没出息的心绪里生生拽了出来。

===Original===
“Something, of course,” he said quietly, “has happened to George. I can tell you the time when it happened﻿—ten o’clock yesterday night. He was awaiting a message at the foot of the cliff. No doubt he was being watched. And, when the packet fell down, the enemy laid him out and picked it up. When I say ‘George,’ I mean ‘George and the servants,’ of course. They’re not four any more﻿—four effectives: they may not even be three.﻿ ﻿…

“Point number two﻿—*Rose Noble is not alone*. He may have been yesterday morning, but he’s certainly not alone now. I suppose he took them in by a rope some time last night. Anyway, he’s smarter than I am, and Punter or Casemate has certainly bested George.

“Point number three﻿—*we must get out of this suite*. I should never have split our force: and now, by hook or by crook, I’ve got to join it again. If Carson or George is alive, they’ll attack tonight, and we’ve got to open the gate and let them in. What’s a thousand times worse, if we’re still at his mercy tomorrow, Rose Noble will put on the screw.﻿ ﻿…

“Point number four﻿—*the ‘hands-up’ phase is over*. From now on seeing is shooting, and shooting to kill. Rose Noble’s still hoping to break me, for the sake of doing a deal: but he knows it’s a chance in a million and he’s not going to risk his life by sparing mine.”

===Chinese===
「不用说，」他平静地说，「乔治出了事。出事的时间我可以说给你听——昨天夜里十点。他正在崖脚下等候消息。无疑有人正盯着他。等那包东西坠下去，敌人便把他打翻，把东西捡走了。我说『乔治』，自然是说『乔治和仆人们』。他们不再是四个了——四个能动手的：兴许连三个都不剩了。……

「第二点——*罗斯·诺布尔不是孤身一人*。昨天早上他兴许还是，如今肯定不是了。我猜是昨夜什么时候，他用绳子把他们接进了城堡。无论如何，他比我高明，而且赢下乔治那一手的，必定是庞特或凯斯梅特。

「第三点——*我们必须退出这套房*。我本不该把人马分开：如今不管使什么法子，我都得把力量重新合到一处。卡森或者乔治只要还活着，今夜就会来攻，我们得打开大门放他们进来。更糟一千倍的是，倘若到明天我们还捏在他手心里，罗斯·诺布尔就要拧紧螺丝了。……

「第四点——*『举手投降』的阶段过去了*。从今往后，见人即开枪，开枪便要命。罗斯·诺布尔还指望摧垮我，好做成一笔交易：可他知道那机会微乎其微，他不会为了饶我这条命，去拿他自己的命冒险。」

===Original===
And there, I remember, he happened to cast down his eyes.

We were not without light, for he had the torch in his hand, but this he was holding downward, so that its beam made a circle upon the floor.

For a moment he did not move. Then he was out of his chair and down on his knees.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我记得，就在这时，他恰好垂下了目光。

我们并非没有光，他手里正握着手电，只是电头朝下，光柱便在地板上照出一个圆圈。

有一刻他没有动。随即他离开椅子，跪了下去。……

===Original===
Clean around the table there was a crack in the floor.

It was a very fine cleft and was choked with dust and the wax with which the floor had been rubbed; but these gave way at once to the point of a knife, and then we could see that the floor had been sawn asunder with the finest of saws.

I could scarcely believe that here was another trapdoor, for, for one thing only, the cleft was surrounding the table with a fair two inches to spare, and a trapdoor some eight feet by five seemed out of reason: yet, for some purpose or other, the floor had been cut, and, what seemed to me still more strange, except by the dust and the wax, the cleft had never been stopped.

===Chinese===
环着那张桌子，地板上有一道裂缝。

那是一道极细的罅隙，塞满了灰尘和擦地板的蜡：可刀尖一挑，这些东西立刻让开，于是我们看得分明，这地板是被最细的锯子锯开的。

我几乎不能相信这里又藏着一扇活板门，单说一件事：那道罅隙围着桌子绕一圈，四边还各富余足足两英寸，一块八英尺长、五英尺宽的活板似乎不合常理：然而不管为了什么目的，地板确实被切开过，而在我看来更奇怪的是，除了灰尘和蜡，那道罅隙从来不曾填补过。

===Original===
Mansel was speaking in my ear.

“The table sinks through the floor. I saw it once before in some castle. The idea was to gain privacy. No servants in the room; but the table descended and rose between each course.” He touched the smooth oak beneath the table. “This piece of the floor is really no more than a lift: and, if we can find out its trick.﻿ ﻿…”

I sought to move the table, but it was fixed to the floor.

“That’s right,” breathed Mansel. “The other I saw was fixed. I remember they said it was raised by a system of pulleys and weights. The weights weighed far more than the table, so it couldn’t descend on its own or so much as budge, but had to be hauled down by a windlass between each course.”

===Chinese===
曼塞尔在我耳边说话。

「这桌子是穿地板升降的。我在别的城堡里见过一回。用意在于清静。屋里不用仆人：桌子在每道菜之间降下去、再升上来。」他摸了摸桌下光滑的橡木。「这块地板其实不过是一架升降台：只要咱们摸清它的机关。……」

我试着推那桌子，它却固定在地板上。

「这就对了，」曼塞尔低声道。「我见过的那一张也是固定的。我记得他们说，它是靠一套滑轮和重锤升起来的。重锤比桌子重得多，所以桌子自己降不下去，连动也动不得，每上一道菜，都得用绞盘把它绞下去。」

===Original===
“It’s as firm as a rock,” said I, stooping. “You don’t think it’s locked into place.”

Mansel shrugged his shoulders.

“We must try to find out,” he said. “If we can weight it enough.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「它稳得像块岩石，」我弯下腰说。「你总不至于认为它被锁死在位子上吧。」

曼塞尔耸了耸肩。

「咱们得设法弄清楚，」他说。「要是咱们能把它压得够重。……

===Original===
Then I saw that, with four counterweights, each, let us say, of the weight of the table and lift, these two would seem as much fixed as though they had beneath them a girder to hold them in place﻿—*until there was laid upon them a burden three times their own weight*; but that if we could manage to load them to this extent the table would sink through the floor and open a way of escape.

In silence we lifted the chairs and set them upon the board: they were immensely heavy, but the table stood fast. Ten more chairs we added, bringing them one by one from the other rooms and using our rope to lash the perilous pile. But, though we added our weight, the mass never budged.

And here we were brought to a standstill, for, though there were yet more chairs, we had used all the rope we had and we could not think how to get them on to the top of the structure their fellows made.

===Chinese===
于是我明白了：设若有四具配重，每一具姑且算作桌子加升降台的分量，这两样瞧上去便如底下架了钢梁一般牢牢固定——*直到有三倍于它们自身分量的重负压上身来*；可只要我们能把它们压到这个地步，桌子便会沉下地板，开出一条逃生之路。

我们默不作声地把椅子搬起来搁上桌面：那些椅子沉重无比，桌子却岿然不动。我们又添了十把，一把一把从别的屋里搬来，还用我们的绳索把那险巍巍的一垛捆牢。可尽管再压上我们自己的分量，那一大堆还是纹丝不动。

到这一步我们便一筹莫展了：椅子倒还有富余，绳索却已用尽，再说我们也想不出，怎么把椅子弄上它们同伴垒起的塔顶。

===Original===
Suddenly I thought of the slab which Mansel and I had hidden beneath the King’s bed.﻿ ﻿…

We had dragged this into the chamber, and I was under the table, with my feet braced against the great stretcher, hauling the stone into place, and sitting, as it happened, directly upon the cleft, when I felt a definite movement beneath my seat.

At once I told Mansel, and, after a short consultation, we lay down upon opposite sides and, taking hold of the stretcher, began to pass by inches on to the lift.

As I drew myself on, I felt this beginning to move, and all at once we were sinking into some cold, dark place.

We must have come down with a crash, but the chairs we had piled on the table were overlapping the lift, and, when these were prevented by the floor, the lift, thus relieved of their weight, immediately stopped.

===Chinese===
突然，我想起曼塞尔和我藏在国王床底下那块石板。……

我们把石板拖进屋里，我钻到桌子底下，双脚蹬住那根粗大的横档，把石头拖到位，恰好正坐在那道罅隙上，这时我觉得身下确确实实动了一下。

我立刻告诉曼塞尔，简短商议几句之后，我们便分卧两侧，抓住横档，开始一寸一寸地挪上升降台。

我往前挪着，觉出它开始移动，冷不防我们已沉进一处又冷又黑的地方。

我们本该轰然一声落到底，好在堆在桌上的椅子悬出了升降台的边沿，待它们被周围的地板卡住，升降台卸去了椅子的分量，便立刻停住了。

===Original===
The torch now showed us a cellar, with a door in its eastern wall. About us was the massive cage in which the lift ran, and at each of its corners were a pulley and a rope and a great counterweight of stone. A little to one side stood the windlass, as Mansel had said.

And now, once again, as they say, we had the wolf by the ears.

The floor was but eight feet away, but, if one of us was to descend, the lift would instantly rise and, taking the other up, lock him once more into the Dining-room; while, if both descended at once, the lift would shoot back into place with a shock which would shake the castle and send the twelve chairs crashing to wake the dead.

But, after a little reflection, Mansel found out a way.

On the under side of the lift, right in its middle, was a hook: to this was attached the great rope which the windlass controlled. If we could reach this rope, the trick was ours.

===Chinese===
手电照出一间地窖，东墙上有一道门。我们四周是升降台运行的那座浑重的笼子，四角各有一具滑轮、一条绳索和一块巨大的石制配重。曼塞尔说过的那台绞盘，立在一旁。

而此刻，正如俗话所说，我们又揪住了狼的耳朵。

窖底离我们不过八英尺，可我们中若有一人下去，升降台立刻会升上去，把另一人带回楼上，重新锁进餐厅；若两人同时下去，升降台便会猛地弹回原位，那一震能摇动整座城堡，让十二把椅子轰然砸落，吵醒死人。

但略一思量，曼塞尔便想出了办法。

升降台底面正中有一只吊钩：绞盘控制的那条大绳就系在钩上。只要够得着这条绳，机关便归我们掌握了。

===Original===
Mansel took my left wrist in his hands and lowered me clear of the lift. At once I swung to and fro until I could reach and lay hold of the great iron hook. With my left hand I then laid hold of the edge of the lift, and Mansel climbed round my body and seized the rope. Then we came down the rope together, on to the ground.

We let the lift rise by inches, until it was back in its place, and then, with one accord, we turned to the door.

This was unfastened and brought us directly into what seemed a great hall.

For a moment I thought we were out, for I heard the gurgle of water as plain as could be, but then I perceived that the air was the air of a crypt, dank and something musty and very still.

===Chinese===
曼塞尔双手攥住我的左腕，把我悬放到升降台之外。我立刻来回悠荡，荡到够得着那只大铁钩，一把抓住。随后我左手扒住升降台的边沿，曼塞尔贴着我的身子绕过去，抓住了那条绳。然后我们一同顺绳而下，落到地上。

我们任升降台一寸一寸升回去，直到它归了位，然后我们不约而同地转向那道门。

门没有上闩，把我们径直引进一处像是大厅的地方。

有那么一刻我以为我们到了外面，因为我把汩汩的水声听得真真切切，可随即我便觉出，这里的空气是墓穴里的空气，阴冷潮湿，带点霉味，而且死寂。

===Original===
After listening carefully, we ventured to light the torch.

The place was a kitchen, but had not been used as such for a number of years. There was the huge fireplace, with the chains for the spits hanging down, and a grate like a hayrack to serve a dozen joints. Five great, shuttered windows were looking upon the courtyard, and a doorway, whose step was muddy, was in the same wall.

And on the hearth lay George Hanbury, with his wrists and his ankles bound and a gag in his mouth.

===Chinese===
仔细听了半晌，我们才敢点亮手电。

这地方是一间厨房，只是多年不曾当厨房用了。巨大的壁炉还在，烤肉叉的链条垂着，炉栅大得像个草料架，供得起一打大块的肉。五扇上了护窗板的大窗朝向庭院，同一面墙上还有一道门洞，门前的台阶上沾着泥。

而炉膛前的地上躺着乔治·汉伯里，手腕脚踝都被捆着，嘴里堵着布团。

---


===Original===
That George had been left there to live, if he could, but, if he could not, to die we had not much doubt. He had lain there, gagged and bound, for twenty-four hours: his bonds had never been loosened, he had been given no water, much less any food: he had not so much as been visited. And such as will so use a prisoner are scarcely like to be troubled to find him dead.

Happily George was strong, and his condition of health as fine as ours, and, when we had set him free and had chafed his limbs, he was able to rise and to walk as straight as he pleased. It was clear, however, that he must have food and drink, and, since there remained in the Closet some brandy and bread and meat, we made our way back to the cellar without delay.

By using the windlass, we had the lift down at once, but, when I would have gone up, Mansel put me aside.

“I’m going,” he said. “And please give me full five minutes before you bring me down. Now that one’s got to go back, he may as well cover our tracks: the pile of chairs doesn’t matter, but that slab would make anyone think.”

===Chinese===
他们把乔治撂在那儿，活得了便活，活不了便死，这一点我们几乎不存在疑问。他被堵着嘴捆在那里躺了二十四小时：绳索从未松过，水也没给过一口，更不用说吃食：连个人影也没来看过一眼。而这样使唤俘虏的人，见俘虏死了，多半也不会为此不安。

所幸乔治身子结实，底子同我们一样棒，我们替他松了绑、搓揉过四肢之后，他便能够站起来，爱走多直走多直。然而他显然必须吃喝，密室里还剩着些白兰地、面包和肉，于是我们毫不耽搁，循原路回地窖去。

用绞盘一绞，升降台立刻落了下来，可我正要上去，曼塞尔把我推到一边。

「我去，」他说。「请足足给我五分钟，再把我放下来。既然反正得有人回去一趟，索性把咱们的痕迹也一并抹了：那一垛椅子不打紧，那块石板却会引人起疑。」

===Original===
With that, he mounted the lift, and I hoisted him up.

And while he was gone, George Hanbury told me his tale.

“When the car came out of the castle, I was down by the beechwoods at the foot of the cliff. Rowley was with me, but Carson and Bell and Tester were on guard, within sound of the drive.

===Chinese===
说罢，他登上升降台，我把他绞了上去。

他不在的当口，乔治·汉伯里给我讲了他的经历。

「汽车驶出城堡的时候，我正在崖脚下山毛榉林边上。罗利跟着我，卡森、贝尔和特斯特都在警戒，守在听得见车道动静的地方。

===Original===
“Carson heard the car coming, and the moment it passed he gave chase. I don’t know whether they had expected this, but they did the best they could think of to shake him off. Of course they failed. They didn’t bother much about the foot of the cliff: they certainly went that way, for I saw them go by, but they passed at sixty and never so much as slowed up. The sight of them worried me, but I didn’t see what I could do but stay where I was. Of course Carson could have caught them, but, as Bell and he were alone, he thought he couldn’t do better than cling to their heels. For half an hour they had the devil’s own luck﻿—never a check. Then comes a hairpin bend, and two hundred yards further on a flock of sheep.﻿ ﻿…

“When Carson rounded the corner, Bunch was fifty yards off and *turning his car*. Sheep or no, it was an excellent move. The car has a shortish wheelbase, but a Rolls takes some getting round. Then Carson did well. He stopped, went into reverse and started to back to a turning he’d marked at the top of the hill. Before he could get there, Bunch was coming like hell. There was Punter beside him, and Casemate was back in the car. As they went by, Punter fired full at Carson and hit the brim of his hat.﻿ ﻿…

“This annoyed Carson and Bell, and I must say I’m not surprised. And, as soon as the Rolls was round, they put her along. By this time Bunch had stolen a bit of a start, but they gradually overhauled him, and, choosing a smooth bit of going, Bell took a shot at their tank. He didn’t hit it that time, but he laid it open the next.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「卡森听见汽车过来，等它一驶过便追了上去。我不知道他们料没料到这一手，但他们把想得出的招数全使出来甩脱他。他们当然没能成功。他们并没把崖脚放在心上：那一路他们确实走了，因为我亲眼看见他们驶过，只是时速六十英里一掠而过，连放缓都没有放缓一下。看见他们我心里发急，可除了守在原地，我想不出自己还能做什么。卡森本来追得上他们，但他和贝尔只两个人，便觉得牢牢咬住他们的脚后跟才是上策。足足半个钟头，他们运气好得邪门——一路没有一处阻碍。接着来了个发夹弯，再往前两百码，是一群羊。……

「卡森转过弯时，邦奇已在五十码外，*正在原地调头*。且不管那群羊，这一手实在高明。那部车轴距偏短，可劳斯莱斯调起头来就费些事。接着卡森应付得漂亮。他停住车，挂上倒挡，开始往坡顶一处他记下的岔口倒回去。还没倒到，邦奇已经没命地扑了过来。庞特坐在他旁边，凯斯梅特缩在车后座。两车交会时，庞特照准卡森开了一枪，打中了他的帽檐。……

「这一下惹恼了卡森和贝尔，我得说我不奇怪。劳斯莱斯一调过头，他们便放开车速猛追。这时邦奇已偷出一点先手，但他们一步步追了上来，挑了一段平整路面，贝尔朝对方的油箱开了一枪。那一枪没打着，下一枪却把它打穿了。……

===Original===
“It was now a matter of time and nothing else. Bunch might do another three miles, but he couldn’t do more, so Carson fell back a little, to keep, as he judged, out of range. Considering they’d fired again and made a hole in the screen, I think he was wise.

“They were now not more than six miles from the foot of the cliff, and heading that way. The road was full of bends, so half the time the cars were out of each other’s sight. You can guess what happened. Three miles on Carson rounded a bend to see the car, doors open, by the side of the road. Of course, he put down his foot and went by a blue streak. Two shots were fired, but they didn’t do any harm.

“Then Carson drove back to the spur, and he hadn’t been there ten minutes when I came in. I was as pleased as Punch when I heard his report. I assumed they’d gone out to get food: and now their car was done in, and they were cut off. We fairly picketed that spur. A ferret couldn’t have passed the line we held.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「这时拼的就只是时间，再无别的。邦奇兴许还能撑三英里，但决计撑不过去，卡森便稍稍落后了些，照他的判断，退到射程之外。想想对方又开了火，还在挡风玻璃上打了个洞，我认为他做得明智。

「这时他们离崖脚已不到六英里，正朝那边去。路上净是弯道，两部车有一半时间互相看不见。结局你猜得到。又行了三英里，卡森转过一道弯，看见那部车停在路边，车门敞着。他自然是油门踩到底，风驰电掣地掠了过去。对方打了两枪，却没伤着人。

「随后卡森驶回山嘴，我赶到时，他到那儿还不到十分钟。听了他的报告，我简直乐得像潘趣一般。我以为他们是出去弄吃食的：如今他们的车废了，人也被切断了。我们把那道山嘴布得铁桶也似，一只雪貂也休想钻过我们布下的防线。……

===Original===
“Bell and I visited the beechwoods at three. I hated leaving the spur, but it didn’t seem prudent for one to go out alone. We saw no one, and, when we got back, Carson had nothing to report.

“At last it began to grow dark, and I had to face two fresh facts. The first was this. According to plan, one of the cars must now leave for the foot of the cliff *and stay there perhaps till dawn*. That was awkward enough, but the second was worse. It was a yard of pearls to a bootlace that Punter and Casemate and Bunch would try to get home that night.

“After a lot of reflection, I decided to go alone to the foot of the cliff. I went. I meant to take Tester, but at the last I forgot. I found the rope gone and imagined you’d pulled it up. At ten o’clock something fell down about ten yards from where I stood. As I bent over it, somebody laid me out.﻿ ﻿…

“Well, there you are.

===Chinese===
「三点钟，贝尔和我巡了一趟山毛榉林。我不愿撤离山嘴，可单人出去总欠稳妥。我们没见到一个人，回来时卡森也无可禀报。

「天终于黑了下来，我不得不面对两个新情况。第一个是这样：按照计划，两部车中必须有一部前往崖脚，*或许得一直守到天亮*。这已经够别扭了，第二个更糟。庞特、凯斯梅特和邦奇十有八九会在当夜设法回巢。

「苦苦思量一番之后，我决定独自去崖脚。我去了。本想带上特斯特，临了却忘了。我发现绳子不见了，只当是你们收了上去。十点钟，有个东西坠下来，落在我站的地方十来码外。我俯身去看时，不知哪里冒出来的人把我撂倒了。……

「唉，就是这么回事。

===Original===
“The next thing I knew I was lying in the back of the Rolls, which was doing forty over a wicked road. Then I was taken out and hauled through a ground-floor window into the Castle of Gath.

“Of course it was easy. When the servants heard the Rolls coming, they thought it was me. They probably thought it odd that I should drive on up to Gath, but, before they’d scented the trouble, the fat was burnt.”

As I bent again to the windlass, my mind was full.

===Chinese===
「再恢复知觉时，我正躺在劳斯莱斯的后座上，车在一条坑洼烂路上开着四十英里。随后我被抬下车，从底楼一扇窗子拖进了加斯城堡。

「这当然容易。仆人们听见劳斯莱斯回来，只当是我。他们多半觉得奇怪，我怎么一直把车开上了加斯，可不等他们嗅出祸事，木已成舟。」

我重新俯身去摇绞盘，心里百感翻腾。

===Original===
Not only was Rose Noble’s foresight a fearsome thing, but the fellow was very well served. Punter and Bunch and Casemate had done uncommonly well. But what struck me most of all was that, though their car had been ruined and the Rolls was at their gate, the temptation to take her inside had been withstood. That for this resolution we had Rose Noble to thank, I have no doubt: the others would scarce have renounced so handsome a prize. Yet, had we seen the Rolls in the archway, the sight would have told us that George was out of the running, and Adèle would not have been delivered into the enemy’s hand. This was the pink of strategy﻿—the casting away of a sceptre to win a crown: and I must confess a sudden, craven fear that we should never outwit a man so firm of purpose and so unearthly wise.

The gurgle of water in the kitchen came from a little well. This had been sunk in the floor and was fed by a pipe which clearly ran out of the channel we knew so well. A similar pipe was conducting the overflow. By this simple device, the cooks had always fresh water ready to hand, and, what is more to the point, poor George was able to drink and to bathe his aching jaws. Then we plied him with brandy and made him eat what there was, for, though he made light of his bondage, a man cannot suffer as he had and feel as sound the same night.

While he was eating and drinking, we laid our plans.

===Chinese===
罗斯·诺布尔的先见之明固然可怖，这厮手下人也极其得力。庞特、邦奇和凯斯梅特干得实在出色。而最令我震动的是：他们自己的车已经报废，劳斯莱斯就停在他们门口，把车开进去的诱惑，竟被他们生生忍住了。这份决断要归功于罗斯·诺布尔，我毫不怀疑：换了旁人，断难舍弃这样一件体面的战利品。然而，我们若是望见劳斯莱斯停在拱洞里，那一眼便足以告诉我们乔治已经出局，阿黛尔也就不会被送进敌人手中。这是策略的极致——抛却权杖，去赢王冠：我不由得生出一阵怯懦的恐惧，只怕我们永远斗不过一个意志如此坚定、智虑如此通神的人。

厨房里的汩汩水声出自一口小井。井是凿在地板里的，由一根水管供水，那管子显然接自我们熟得不能再熟的水道。另一根同样的管子排走溢水。靠这个简单的装置，厨子们随时都有活水可用，而更要紧的是，可怜的乔治得以喝上水，还能浸一浸他疼痛的腮帮。随后我们拿白兰地一遍遍喂他，逼他把现成的吃食都吃了，因为尽管他把被俘的苦楚说得轻描淡写，一个人遭了他那样的罪，当夜是决计硬朗不起来的。

他吃喝的当口，我们定下了计划。

===Original===
That the servants were close at hand we had no doubt. Indeed, it seemed certain that they would any moment attack. If they did so before we could reach them, they would assuredly fail, for, for one thing only, they would enter by way of the roof, which the enemy was holding against our escape from the suite. They would thus walk clean into a trap, of which we, below, could not warn them, because we could not climb up. We, therefore, determined to make at once for the gate and, opening this if we could, to bring them in. If we could contrive to do this without being seen or heard, we should for the first time have the advantage of the enemy, for, whilst they were sure that they had but two men to deal with, and those under lock and key, in fact there would be six men free and within their camp.

The kitchen-door was unlocked and yielded without any fuss.

As we looked into the courtyard, hardly daring to breathe, the beam of a torch swept the wall beneath which we stood. It was little more than a flash and came, as before, from above; but it showed that the passage windows were still being watched. We had expected no less, but, although the beam was directed upon the wall, it lighted quite twenty feet of the courtyard itself, and, unless we could cross this belt between the flashes, we could not fail to be seen.

“No good waiting,” breathed Mansel. “We must go across one by one. The first twenty feet on foot and as hard as we can: then, down on your face and crawl the rest of the way. If we go lightly, the water will cover our noise. Chandos first. Wait for me in the porch: I may be a little time coming, because I must shut this door. And now stand by.”

===Chinese===
仆人们就在近旁，对此我们毫不怀疑。的确，他们随时都可能进攻，这几乎是必然的。倘若他们赶在我们与他们会合之前动手，必定一败涂地，单说一条：他们只能打屋顶进来，而敌人正守住屋顶，防我们从套房脱身。他们无异于径直走进一个陷阱，我们在下面却无法示警，因为我们爬不上去。于是我们决定立刻奔大门去，若能打开，便放他们进来。倘若这事能办得不让人看见听见，我们将头一回占敌人的上风：他们满以为要对付的只有两个人，而且锁在屋里插翅难飞，实际上却有六条好汉自由自在，就在他们营垒腹中。

厨房的门没有上锁，一推就开。

我们朝庭院里张望，大气也不敢出，一道手电的光扫过我们头顶的墙面。那不过闪了一闪，和先前一样来自上方；可它表明通道的窗子仍有人盯守。这我们早料到了，只是那道光虽照在墙上，却照亮了庭院本身足足二十英尺宽的一条地带，除非能在光闪的间隙里穿过这条地带，否则我们难免被人看见。

「等无益处，」曼塞尔低声说。「咱们得一个一个过去。头二十英尺放开脚步快跑，越快越好：随后趴下，匍匐爬完剩下的路。咱们脚步放轻，水声自会盖住动静。钱多斯先走。在门廊里等我：我也许要迟一会儿，因为我还得关上这道门。现在各就各位。」

===Original===
Whilst he was speaking, the flashes came and went, but at intervals so irregular that no observation could help us, and we had nothing to do but to take our chance.

At last Mansel gave the word, and I made my dash.

As I fell on my face, a flicker lit up the courtyard.﻿ ﻿…

Ten minutes later we were all three in the porch.

===Chinese===
他说话的当口，那道光闪了又灭，间隔全无规律，观察也无济于事，我们只能听天由命碰运气。

末了曼塞尔一声令下，我便冲了出去。

我扑倒在地时，一道闪光正照亮庭院。……

十分钟后，我们三个都到了门廊里。

===Original===
The wicket-gate was locked, and its key was gone. We, therefore, made ready to open the great gate itself, using the greatest care to make no sound. And here, to our vexation, we met with another check. Though we could feel the great bolts, we could not make out how to draw them, for each was engaged with some catch which we could not release. It was dreadful to stand there fumbling, because of some simple holdfast never devised to embarrass a porter’s hand, but intended to prevent the bolts turning under battery of the gate. Yet show a light we dared not: and, as the minutes went by and the catch still mocked our fingers for want of sight, I began to feel that Fortune was not so much frowning upon us as laughing at us in her sleeve.

Then Mansel gave a short sigh and drew his bolt, and a moment later his fingers were playing with mine.

As the door yielded, he put his mouth to my ear.

“You go and find them,” he said. “Hanbury and I stay here. If you don’t strike them at once, try using the torch. But﻿—”

===Chinese===
小门锁着，钥匙也不见了。我们便准备去开大门本身，万分小心，不敢弄出半点声响。谁知就在这里，令我们懊恼的是，又碰上一道坎。那几根大门闩摸是摸得着，怎么抽却摸不出门道，每根闩都咬在一处我们解脱不开的机括里。站在那儿干摸索，只因一个再简单不过的固定件——它从来就不是为了难为门房的手而设，只是为了防大门遭撞击时门闩转动——实在叫人胆寒。可我们又不敢亮灯：时间一分一分过去，那机括只因我们看不见，兀自嘲弄着我们的手指，我开始觉得，命运女神与其说是在对我们皱眉，不如说是在袖中暗笑我们。

随后曼塞尔短短叹了口气，抽开了他那根闩，转眼他的手指也来帮我的手指了。

门松动时，他把嘴凑到我耳边。

「你去找他们，」他说。「汉伯里和我守在这儿。若不能立刻撞见他们，就试着用手电打信号。不过——」

===Original===
And there, somewhere behind us, a shot rang out.

As we swung round﻿—

“I saw the flash,” said Hanbury. “*Up on the roof.*”

===Chinese===
就在这时，我们身后什么地方响起一枪。

我们猛然回身——

「我看见枪火了，」汉伯里说。「*就在屋顶上。*」

---


===Original===
I ran full tilt into Rowley, who was standing at the foot of the ladders, with a cord in his hand.

“Oh, thank God, sir,” says he. “We thought you were gone.”

I shook him by the shoulder and pointed up to the roof.

“Recall them!” I cried. “Recall them! How can you get them back?”

He was tugging at the cord, like a madman, when another two shots rang out.

===Chinese===
我一头撞在罗利身上，他正站在梯子脚下，手里握着一根绳。

「哦，谢天谢地，先生，」他说。「我们还当你们完了。」

我抓住他的肩膀摇了摇，指着屋顶。

「叫他们回来！」我喊道。「叫他们回来！你怎么把他们叫回来？」

他正像个疯子似的猛拽那根绳，又有两枪响了起来。

===Original===
I could not stand there idle, but began to go up the wall.

I was on the second ladder, when someone above me looked down.

“Is that you, Bell?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Get Carson and come down to the gate.”

“Very good, sir.”

===Chinese===
我不能干站在那里，便开始往墙上爬。

我爬上第二架梯子时，头顶上有人往下看。

「是你吗，贝尔？」

「是我，先生。」

「带上卡森，到大门这儿来。」

「遵命，先生。」

===Original===
I slid and fell back to the ground, to run to the gate.

Mansel was standing, waiting, cool as a man in a garden, regarding his flowers.

“Carson must stay out,” he said. “I daren’t have everyone in. He and Tester must feed us and keep the cars. Send him for food and drink, as soon as he’s down. And two fifty-foot lengths of rope. The others come in.”

Whilst he was speaking, another four shots were fired, and I ran back to Rowley with my heart in my mouth.

===Chinese===
我滑下来，落回地面，朝大门跑去。

曼塞尔站着等候，镇定得像花园里赏花的闲人。

「卡森必须留在外头，」他说。「我不敢把人都放进来。他和特斯特得管我们吃喝、看住汽车。他一下来，就派他去弄吃的喝的。再要两条五十英尺长的绳子。其余的人都进来。」

他说话的当口，又打了四枪，我心提到嗓子眼，跑回罗利那里。

===Original===
Him I sent to the gate and took his place. I think he was glad to go, for the firing was growing hotter, and to stand at the foot of the ladder in the knowledge that those you were awaiting might very well never come down pulled at a man’s nerves.

Such concern will seem out of reason. When six men set out in a body to play with fire, it hardly becomes them to tremble lest someone be burned. But our outlook was not so simple. We feared no more for Carson than Carson feared for himself: but we all of us feared very much for the matter in hand. Our school was not that of Rose Noble. If one of us was wounded, we could not let him lie: he would have to be saved and attended at any cost: how high that cost would be no one could tell, but, placed as we were, we all knew that such a distraction might ruin our enterprise.

It seemed an age before Bell began to descend.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我派他去大门，自己接了他的位置。我想他乐意去，因为枪火越来越紧，而明知自己等候的人很可能再也下不来、还得守在梯子脚下，这种滋味最磨人的神经。

这样的忧心在旁人看来未免过分。六个人结伙出来玩火，便不便吓得发抖，唯恐烧着哪一个。可我们的处境没有那么简单。我们替卡森担心的分量，并不比卡森替自己担心的多：但我们人人都为眼下的正事捏着一把汗。我们受的那套教养，与罗斯·诺布尔的不同。我们中间若有人负了伤，绝不能弃之不顾：无论如何也得把他救下来、给他救治：这代价会有多高，谁也说不上，而以我们的处境，人人都明白，这样一分神便可能坏了我们的大事。

仿佛过了整整一世，贝尔才开始下来。……

===Original===
“Are you hit?” said I.

“Oh, no, sir,” said he. “Nor’s Carson. But, of course, we had to go slow.”

I sent him to Mansel at once.

Then a shot was fired right above me, and Carson came down with a run.

===Chinese===
「伤着了吗？」我说。

「哦，没有，先生，」他说。「卡森也没有。不过我们当然只能慢慢来。」

我立刻派他到曼塞尔那里去。

随后正头顶上打了一枪，卡森飞快地滑了下来。

===Original===
“Come,” said I, and led him away up the spur.﻿ ﻿…

It was well that I did so, for a light leaped out of the darkness, and a bullet went over our heads. To round the picture, I turned and fired back at the torch. No doubt the bullet went wide, but the light was put out.

Then I stopped and gave Carson his orders and told him that we were all safe, and he promised to be at the gate in a quarter of an hour. Then I stole back to the castle, and Mansel took me in.

===Chinese===
「来，」我说，领着他往山嘴上走。……

幸好我这么做了，黑暗中一道光猛地亮起，一颗子弹从我们头顶掠过。为给这幅图画凑个圆满，我转身朝那手电回了一枪。子弹无疑打偏了，可那盏灯灭了。

随后我停下脚步，给卡森交代了命令，告诉他我们都平安，他答应一刻钟后赶到大门。然后我潜回城堡，曼塞尔把我放了进去。

===Original===
Two doorways led out of the archway, one upon either hand. These Mansel set us to watch till Carson should come: “but I don’t think,” said he, “they’ll disturb us: they’ve got their hands full. They’ve the roof to watch, and the passage, and it won’t be light for three hours.”

Here he was right, for Carson came and went, but nobody else; and, though lights flashed on the roof and the passage windows were closely and continually watched, no one came down to the archway or entered the great courtyard.

Carson went heavily away, for, though his part was most dangerous﻿—because, except for Tester, he was alone﻿—and though he would be the sole link between us and the world we knew, he had the true heart of a fighter and could hardly bear to leave us at such a pinch.

===Chinese===
拱洞两侧各有一道门通出去。曼塞尔派我们把住这两道门，等卡森到来：「不过我想，」他说，「他们不会来搅扰：他们自顾不暇。屋顶要盯，通道要盯，而且离天亮还有三个钟头。」

这一点他说对了：卡森来了又去了，再无别人；屋顶上灯光闪烁，通道的窗子也始终被人严密盯守着，却没有一个人下到拱洞，也没有一个人踏进大庭院。

卡森步履沉重地走了，虽然他的差事最险——因为除了特斯特，他孤身一人——虽然他将成为我们与熟知世界之间的唯一纽带，他到底有一颗真正斗士的心，几乎不忍在这样吃紧的关头离我们而去。

===Original===
Early next morning he was to drive into Lass and there to take in supplies which should last us a week. He was then to bestow the two cars as best he could, somewhere beyond the wood and by the crossroads. He was not to move during the day, but by night he was to come to the spur and there wait till one of us met him or else it was dawn.

Then Mansel shut the gate and shot the great bolts and set his face again to the business of reaching Adèle, or, to be more precise, of thrusting between her and Rose Noble, *before the latter knew we were there*.

One thing was plain. Before the night was over, we must either have accomplished our purpose or have gained some room or corner where we could lie hid. Now the only shelter we knew was that of the cellar beneath the dining-room’s floor, but, since our goal was, as ever, the southwest tower, we decided to make for the latter and to trust to striking another and more convenient lair.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，他要驱车进拉斯，在那儿采办够我们吃一个星期的补给。然后把两部车妥善安置在林子外、十字路口附近的什么地方。白天他不得动弹，入夜则须到山嘴去，在那儿等到我们中有人来会他，或者天亮为止。

随后曼塞尔关上大门，插好粗大的门闩，重新把全副心思扑向那桩正事：够到阿黛尔，或者更准确地说，楔进她与罗斯·诺布尔之间，*而且要赶在后者察觉我们到场之前*。

有一件事明摆着。今夜过去之前，我们要么得成事，要么得寻到某间屋子、某个角落藏身。我们知道的唯一避身之处，只有餐厅地板下的那间地窖，可我们的目标一如既往是西南塔楼，于是我们决定直奔后者，指望路上撞见另一处更合用的藏身之地。

===Original===
Now, though we knew next to nothing of the way from the porch to Adèle, we had one valuable clue.

When Casemate had been hounded by Rose Noble to “turn out the car,” he had certainly reached the porch as quickly as ever he could. Now his shortest path, as we knew from the bookseller’s guide, was down the Grand Staircase and across the courtyard. *But Casemate had not crossed the courtyard.* It was, therefore, perfectly plain that that way was shut.

We, therefore, turned to the door in the western wall of the porch, for Casemate had come out of that, and that could, therefore, lead us back to the “gallery of stone.”

===Chinese===
不过，从门廊到阿黛尔那里怎么走，我们虽然几乎一无所知，手上却握着一条宝贵的线索。

当初凯斯梅特被罗斯·诺布尔吆喝着去『把车开出来』的时候，他必定是以最快的速度赶到了门廊。而照书商那本指南，他最短的路径是下大楼梯（Grand Staircase）、横穿庭院。*可凯斯梅特没有穿过庭院。*因此事情明明白白：那条路是封死的。

于是我们转向门廊西墙上的那道门，因为凯斯梅特正是从那里出来的，它便也就能领我们回到『石头回廊』。

===Original===
I could set down our passage in detail, for I remember most clearly every step that we took. The hopes and fears which attended us, the sudden shocks of thankfulness and dismay, the waves of suspense, and relief﻿—all these are engraved upon my memory as letters cut upon a stone. But I think that such a recital would be out of place, for only those that were there could find it moving, and I have not the mind or the skill to trick it out.

And so I will only say that by galleries, stairs and chambers we made our way in the darkness towards the southwest tower. Again and again boards creaked beneath our weight, and sometimes, do what we would, a hinge would whine: we made mistakes in our going and were forced to retrace the steps we had been at such pains to take; and we went at a true snail’s pace and as blind men go, for we dared not use our torches in case their light should betray us and ruin our game.

At break of day we stood in a little lobby that looked out upon the mountains and seemed at that misty moment to command the world.

===Chinese===
我们一路的经过，我本可以逐笔细录，因为我们走的每一步，我都记得清清楚楚。一路相伴的希望与恐惧，骤然袭来的感激与惊惶，一阵阵的悬心与释然——这一切都刻在我的记忆里，如同凿在石头上的字。但我想，这样一篇流水账不合时宜，因为只有在场的人才会为之动容，而我既无这份心思，也无这份笔力去妆点它。

所以我只想说：我们凭着一道道回廊、一段段楼梯、一间间屋子，在黑暗中朝西南塔楼摸去。脚下的地板一次又一次吱呀作响，有时无论怎样小心，铰链还是要呻吟一声：我们走岔了路，只得原路退回，把方才千辛万苦迈出的步子再一步步倒回来；我们走得慢得像蜗牛，像盲人一样摸索着走，因为不敢用手电，生怕那一点光出卖我们、坏了全局。

拂晓时分，我们站在一间小小的过厅里，窗外便是群山，在那雾气迷蒙的一刻，仿佛整个天下都尽收眼底。

===Original===
The place had the look of a guardroom, and so, I am sure, it had served, for a wicket gave directly on to a winding stair, which if a man ascended he came to the roof, but, if he went down, *he came to the “gallery of stone.”* It was, indeed, the stairway of the southwest tower.

So we broke and entered into that jealous keep which for six long days had mocked us and all our works.

The need for caution was now paramount.

We stood at the enemy’s elbow, and he did not know we were there. We had our hand almost upon him; but he had his hand on Adèle. He was unready, but we did not know the ground. If we could strike before he could, the game was ours: but if we were to be behindhand, we had better be sitting at Lass with our hands in our lap.

===Chinese===
这地方一副卫兵室的模样，而且我敢肯定它就是干这个用的，因为一扇小门直通一道旋梯，人若往上走便到屋顶，若往下走，*便到了『石头回廊』*。这正是西南塔楼的楼梯。

就这样，我们破门闯进了那座戒备深严的主堡——长长六天来，它一直奚落着我们和我们的一切作为。

此刻，谨慎成了头等大事。
